# short.win — Economic & Risk Model

*Design document • v0.4.1 (fourth review round; see the changelog) • 2026-09-27; Raydium pool rules synced to the program as built after M4 on 2026-09-28 (changelog #91–#95); post-change confirmation of the observation state after a venue switch PROPOSED on 2026-09-28 (#96, M4R), its keeper coverage and reference cap on 2026-09-29 (#98, M4F-1), the owner's rule that a pool switch carries open positions only if every one fits the new pool's opening caps on 2026-09-29 (#99), and its verification's resolutions on 2026-09-29 (#100: carried fee-scaled caps at `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)`; the first fix's lows bound and `refresh_max_q` withdrawn), and the owner's decisions on that round's verification (#101, FITS: the carried fee caps at most 25 bps; the liquidation spot leg at `max(L_live, L_ema')` and the keeper's per-second liquidity-anomaly coverage), and #101's verification (#102, FITX-1: a genuinely reached low `L_ema` frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks still forces liquidations — exactly those the same LP's withdrawal for good forces; restated as a residual for the owner, no rule change), and the doc sync after M5 on 2026-09-30 (#103: the curve's caps on its reserves at the EMA price, M5-SEC-3; the M5 venue facts; the post-wipe venue-reward bit on the curve; the tier inputs under a negative PumpSwap VQR, PROPOSED), and the doc sync after M6 on 2026-09-30 (#104: PumpSwap and the graduation as built — §10's graduation rows on the fork under M6's graduation rule, the negative-VQR tier inputs built, third-party LP on canonical PumpSwap pools, OWNER-M6R-1), and the owner's decisions on the M6 reports on 2026-09-30 (#105: Raydium's liquidity-push protections on PumpSwap with `D` the burned creation LP's depth, the warm-up seed's falls bounded, no curve opens while PumpSwap's `create_pool` is disabled, the post-graduation exit delay accepted; the normative sim brought to the graduation as built), and the owner's decision on the #105 verification's M6D-1 on 2026-09-30 (#106: PumpSwap credited on its burned LP only, `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10,000 in sizing, the tier and — since its verification's M6E-1 — the exit reference's cap), and M7 on 2026-10-01 (#107: the keeper cost model on the real keeper's measured transactions, O17 as built), and the doc sync after M10 on 2026-10-03 (#111: the rent top-up vests, M10-8C-1; the late depositor's deposit warning; §12 on the real venues and O19's held sandwich accepted; §5.8 on the release build; the gap calibration's premise), and the owner's pre-freeze sign-off on 2026-10-09 (#115: O18 accepted as the v1.0.0 design, every PROPOSED row of §14 ADOPTED; §12.12's residual 16 accepted), and the doc sync after the AI review's fixes on 2026-10-12 (#117: AIR-01 … AIR-24 and the owner's decisions on them; the sims' outputs regenerated) • baseline: [`docs/SPEC.md`](../SPEC.md) v0.3 incl. owner decisions D1–D4 (owner-approved, not modified here)*
*Simulation: [`sim/shortwin_sim.py`](sim/shortwin_sim.py) v0.4.1 (venues, positions, observation/EMA layer, settlement box, exits, scenarios, Monte Carlo, exit fuzz), [`sim/pool_model.py`](sim/pool_model.py) v0.4.1 (lender-pool accounting, property tests, liveness check), the attack scripts in [`sim/attacks/`](sim/attacks/), the parameter gate [`sim/config_check.py`](sim/config_check.py) and the keeper cost model [`sim/keeper_cost.py`](sim/keeper_cost.py). The frozen v0.3 model in `sim/legacy/v0_3/` is used only for "v0.3" comparison rows; "v0.4" comparison rows use the analysis switches of the v0.4.1 sim (`box_obs`, `pprime_raw`, `ref_cap_lo = False`, `liq_reserve = "rent+penalty"`). Standard library only; every run is deterministic (fixed seeds). Every table in this document was regenerated with the v0.4.1 defaults; the generated outputs are in `sim/out/`.*

This document turns the economics of SPEC §2, §4, §5 and §6 into formulas an Anchor program can implement: units, fixed-point types, rounding direction, closed forms, state updates and payout orders. It reports what the simulations say about lenders, borrowers and attackers. `ARCHITECTURE.md` aligns to this version; where the two disagree, this document is the economic source of truth, and `sim/shortwin_sim.py` + `sim/pool_model.py` v0.4.1 are the normative reference implementation for the math crate (they implement the program's formulas exactly, R3A-3; the analysis-only switches are listed in each sim's header).

**Conventions**

- **SPEC**: the value is taken from `docs/SPEC.md` v0.3.
- **OWNER (D1–D4)**: an owner decision recorded in SPEC v0.3 §0/§14 on 2026-09-27. Binding.
- **ADOPTED**: a default the owner adopted from the first-pass questions (Q4–Q24). Still a configurable parameter (§15). **ADOPTED (owner, 2026-10-09, O18)**: a rule §14 listed as PROPOSED, accepted by the owner as the v1.0.0 design (#115); its values stay configurable within the compiled hard bounds (§15) behind the SPEC §11 timelock.
- **PROPOSED**: a new rule or parameter. Every PROPOSED parameter is configurable, has a default and compiled hard bounds (§15), and sits behind the SPEC §11 timelock.
- **UNVERIFIED**: a claim I could not check against a primary source.
- Finding IDs: `C0-n` / `C1-n` (first review), `V1-n` / `V2-n` (second review), `R3A-n` / `R3B-n` (third review), `R4A-n` / `R4B-n` (fourth review, 2026-09-27); `M4-SEC-n` (the M4 security review of the built program), `M4V-n` and `DOC-n` (its verification, 2026-09-28), `M4R-n` (its re-verification, 2026-09-28: the state a venue switch leaves behind), `M4X-n` (the M4R verification), `M4F-n` (its final check, 2026-09-29), `M4G-n` (the verification of the M4F-1 fix, 2026-09-29), `FIT-n` (the verification of #99, 2026-09-29), `FITV-n` (the verification of #100's first fix, 2026-09-29), `FITS-n` (the verification of #100's simplification, 2026-09-29) `FITX-n` (the verification of #101, 2026-09-29), `M5-SEC-n` (the M5 security review, 2026-09-30) and `M6R-n`, `M6-SEC-n` and `M6SK-n` (the M6 reports, 2026-09-30). Appendix A lists the economic side of every round-3 finding; the v0.4.1 changelog below lists the round-4 changes; `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix A records the outcome of every finding of every round.

---

## Changelog v0.4 → v0.4.1 (fourth review round)

The fourth review (findings `R4A-1` … `R4A-10`, `R4B-1` … `R4B-10`, 2026-09-27) verified the v0.4 fixes and found that two of them created new problems: the penalty reserve (#50) blocked honest liquidations, and the settlement box (#51) could be moved by a push followed by "frozen" seconds. Every finding was re-checked before it was fixed; `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix A ("Review resolution — round 4") records the outcome of each one, including the parts rejected with evidence. Every table below and in the changed sections was regenerated with the v0.4.1 sims (`sim/out/`).

| # | Change | Finding | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 74 | **A liquidation reserves nothing in `max_in`** (supersedes #50 for liquidations; SPEC §5.2 literal): it buys back iff `B̂_live ≤ min(⌊B̂_ref·(10⁴ + δ)/10⁴⌋, V − K_s − ert)`. Both reviewers' findings reproduce: the penalty reserve turns 6 of the reviewer's 960 genuine-pump cells and 6 noisy battery rows from liquidation (lender +0.4 … +3.1 %) into S1 (−32 … −70 %). Both reviewers proposed reverting to v0.3's rent-only reserve; R4B-1 on the premise that rent due stays below `K_s`. It does not for aged positions (Tier A at 25 days and 90 % utilisation: rent due 6.04 SOL against `K_s` 0.25 SOL), so rent-only has the same dead band: on the new 1,655-run battery it loses one aged liquidation (C, 90 %, 6×/40 s with ±0.3 % noise: S1 −46.1 % instead of +0.6 %), and 3–4 more at in-bound maximum rates. No reserve has no dead band. The cost is in the held model on aged positions (Tier A at 90 %: lenders + protocol −8.02 SOL, against −1.92 with rent-only); fresh positions are identical under both rules. The exact liveness invariant R4B-1 asked for now holds: a liquidatable, buyback-allowed position with a fresh reference never waits while `B̂_live ≤ B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` | R4A-1, R4B-1 | §8.2, §12.2, §12.6 |
| 75 | **Expiry keeps rent and the expiry fee senior** (unchanged from v0.4; nothing is urgent, and an expiry that waits becomes a liquidation if the price keeps rising). No battery row changes with any expiry reserve rule. `ε` (`exec_tol_bps`) now enters only the expiry bound | R4A-1 (expiry part) | §8.2, §8.4 |
| 76 | **Liveness battery extended to 1,655 runs** (`config_check.py`, the governance CI gate): the 65 core rows; the same rows with deterministic per-second price noise of ±0.3 % and ±1 % (3 seeds each), so live and reference differ within a second as on a real pool; the reviewer's 960-cell genuine-pump grid (8 markets × 5 multiples × 6 durations × 2 sizes × keeper latency 1 and 2 s); 240 aged-position runs (rent due above `K_s`). Re-deriving τ and κ on it: τ = 6 loses 83 runs and κ = 2,250 loses 38, so `EMA_TAU_S = 5` and `kappa_liq_bps ≥ 2,500` stay, now for reasons independent of any reserve | R4A-1, R4B-1 | §5.7, §15 |
| 77 | **Compiled bounds re-derived on the full battery for every parameter with a liveness effect** (the v0.4 corner check covered 7): settlement keeper terms capped at their SPEC / ADOPTED values (`settle_keeper_fixed ≤ 0.01 SOL`, `_bps ≤ 100`, `_cap ≤ 0.25 SOL`; larger values lose 4–31 runs); `liq_persist_n ≤ 3` (4 loses 58 runs); the SPEC §4 opening and liquidation CRs become per-tier compiled **minima** (lower values lose 109–362 runs); the relational check `liq_persist_n + kappa_growth_n ≤ 7` is now implied by the bounds. The scan compares sizing parameters at equal position size (their v0.4 "failures" were the size effect of `K_s`'s fixed part). All 128 corners of the core box and a stress corner with every scanned parameter at its adverse extreme pass the 1,655 runs | R4A-4 | §5.7, §15 |
| 78 | **Settlement-box medians over bucket extremes**: each ring entry stores its bucket's `(lo, hi, llo, lhi)` instead of the clamped `(obs, lobs)`; `P_lo = min(P_ema, lower median of the lows)`, `P_hi = max(P_ema, upper median of the highs)`, likewise `L`. A "frozen" second — the honest keeper's observation plus an attacker observation just beyond `P_ema`, which leaves `obs = P_ema` — keeps its honest low and high, so push-then-freeze no longer moves the box: the (M) flips need 5 attacker-only seconds instead of 1 push + 4 frozen seconds (v0.4: +2.37 to +5.58 SOL), and the refutation delays S1 by 8 s instead of indefinitely. New residual, disclosed: a paid contrary observation every second widens the box and defers S1 (like the V1-2 veto) or an S3 amount on an executable venue | R4B-2 | §2.1, §5.2, §5.4, §5.6, §12.10, §12.11 |
| 79 | **Liquidation reference also capped at `lo_x·(1 + κ_eff)`**: a pushed-and-frozen `P_ema` no longer lifts the 2 % bound (v0.4: `B̂_ref` ×1.156 and the Tier A borrower's remainder 19.49 → 0.22 SOL; v0.4.1: ×0.999, the atomic-sandwich loss of 1.90 SOL). No battery row changes | R4B-2 | §8.2 |
| 80 | **FV's `p'` is decided on the box corner it is evaluated at**: the locked penalty, or the expiry fee if the position is expired and `CR(V_eff, B̂_FV) ≥ L` at that corner. The robust-spot leg no longer enters, so one contrary observation cannot switch the reading (v0.4: lenders −2.9 % … −7.3 % on the flag-S3 cases for a 0.02–0.71 SOL veto; v0.4.1: no change) | R4A-3 | §5.6, §8.6 |
| 81 | **Immediate part of an S2/S3 take = `min(x, ⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due)`**; impact, fees and the penalty above it vest like a lump. A token whale's `[deposit → sol_settle → withdraw → claim]` took 90 % of the premium over market value (7 % premium: 31.5 SOL on a 500 SOL position); now 0. This also removes the reconstruction of `B̂_FV` from FV that made the sim's lump differ from ARCH's formula by 1 lamport in 76 of 86 settlements. Since M10 (finding M10-8C-1, #111) the rent top-up inside that immediate part — `max(0, min(x − lump, R_due) − credited_for(pos))` — vests too, and S1 vests its own. *(Superseded by AIR-21 for S1 and by AIR-09 for a venue that cannot execute, #117: S1 also vests its take above `⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due`, and on a halted venue the tokens are priced at 0, so only `R_due`, but for its top-up, stays immediate.)* | R4B-6, R4A-5 | §8.6, §9.4 |
| 82 | **Lock expiry calendar**: the aggregate lock term lives in day buckets keyed by `lock_until`, which is rounded up to the bucket end, so an expired lock leaves the aggregate at its `lock_until` without its holder being touched (v0.4: an idle lender's expired lock inflated everyone else's allowance (sum 1.15·T), and the idle lender got nothing). Lock terms are floored, so the aggregate never exceeds the lenders' own locks. I-P4 is now asserted on the state the program sees (each lender touched alone): 92 of 954 sampled states violated it under v0.4 (worst 2.72·T), 0 now | R4A-2, R4B-3 | §9.3, §13 |
| 83 | **Locks cover each position's S3 tail**: `lock_until = ⌈(max(now, max_release) + lock_grace_s)/1 d⌉·1 d` with `max_release` = the latest `deadline + max_migration_pause_s + grad_grace_s + s3_wait_s` (the position's snapshot values) of any position opened. No relational check with governance values is needed | R4A-8, R4B-8 | §9.3 |
| 84 | **Value-weighted vest end**: `vest_end' = now + ⌊(vest_bal·(vest_end − now) + x·lump_vest_s)/(vest_bal + x)⌋` (rounded down since AIR-02, #117; the ceiling first built moved the end 1 s per dust lump, any number of times per transaction). A 1-lamport sweep every minute left 63 % of a lump released after 6 h (v0.4), now 100 % (98 % with the ceiling) | R4B-5 | §9.4 |
| 85 | **Deposits wait for the post-wipe sweep**: whenever `S = 0` (while a full-wipe record is live, and since AIR-08, #117, also after the last lender's exit), `deposit` requires that `sweep_trader` and `claim_venue_rewards` have run since `S` reached 0 (both permissionless; a depositor bundles them). The wiping keeper's `[sol_settle → deposit(2 raw units) → claim_venue_rewards]` took the whole pre-wipe cashback (v0.4), now the wiped lenders get it | R4A-7 | §9.5 |
| 86 | **Exit forfeits the unreleased vest to whoever remains** (disclosed; the "last lender is paid the vest" rule is kept for `S → 0` only). The reviewer's threshold flush was tested and rejected: it lets a whale that deposits 199× just before a lump take 99.5 % of it atomically | R4B-4 | §9.4 |
| 87 | **Consecutive attacker-only seconds** quantified: 5 in a row (of 8 ring entries) flip a capped S2 into an uncapped S1 on the drained-RA model cases (+2.37 … +5.58 SOL for 1.1–2.0 SOL); with one honest second in between, none up to 6. This is the degraded-mode (censored or absent keeper) exposure; §5.6 now says "5 of 8 in either direction" | R4A-9 | §5.6, §12.6, §12.11 |
| 88 | Keeper batches are **v1 transactions** (a v0 transaction with the protocol ALT fits 4 PumpSwap / CPMM or 5 curve cranks, not 6); `keeper_cost.py` shows the v0 fallback (k = 4: +50 % base fees per market-day) | R4B-9 | §5.8 |
| 89 | Text fixes: the keeper floor formula of #63 (`⌊π·split⌋ + min(max(0, K_s − ⌊π·split⌋), remainder)`), the S3 condition in §10.3 ("liquidatable **or expired**", global hard cap), R3A-8 (a) "resolved pending O15" | R4A-10 | #63, §10.3, Appendix A |
| 90 | **Doc sync after M0b** (no economic change; from the `shortwin-math` README's documentation items): **one source of parameter validation** — `shortwin_sim.validate_params` now also checks the penalty split's protocol and keeper parts (≤ 5,000) and every per-tier bound (`cr_open_bps ≤ 100,000`, penalty, duration, impacts, S2 slippage, rates, minimum rent), so the program's `params::validate` is that function exactly; `params::validate_econ` keeps only the bounds of parameters the sim does not model (§15; before, the sim accepted configurations the program refused). §2.2's overflow table adds σ's numerator, the impact caps' `X²·(10⁴ + ι)` and the tier threshold; §4 states the D4 margin with an undefined EMA quote (the spot quote alone, as the sim); §5.6 "sustained" uptrend; §6.1 the integer cap forms; §8.6, §14, §17 O15 as recorded (S2 and S3, SPEC §5.3); §9.4 the vest end after a governance decrease of `lump_vest_s` | M0b notes | §2.2, §4, §5.6, §6.1, §8.6, §9.4, §14, §15, §17 |
| 91 | **Raydium eligibility as built after M4** (doc sync; owner item O18): no clause reads `λ_now` or the live SOL reserve any more. A listing, a zero-position adoption and a list-only market's adoption need the **locked-depth floor** `⌊⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋·10⁴/λ_min⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol`; an adoption that carries open positions — a collection window's candidate, on a Raydium market or on a list-only market with positions — needs the **window floor**, its locked SOL itself, `⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol`, when a proposal opens or improves the window and again at the execution. `P_ref` is the market's `P_ema`, or the candidate's spot on a market without a price. v0.4.1's `λ_now ≥ λ_min` let a standing unlocked deposit keep every alternate pool ineligible, so a halted market's positions stayed frozen at the pre-halt `P_ema` until S3; its live `R ≥ pool_min_real_sol` moved with a swap inside one transaction; the `/λ_min` floor alone moved open positions onto fully locked pools of 12.5–25 SOL without any manipulation (M4V-1). A pool with `λ < λ_min` is eligible when its locked SOL clears the floor; sizing stays bounded by `D/λ_min` | M4-SEC-1, M4-SEC-3, M4V-1 | §1.3, §2.1, §6.2, §10.3, §14, §15 |
| 92 | *(Superseded by #96: its written sample was a forcing path, M4R-1.)* **Adoption liquidity as built**: an adoption writes `L_new` into `L_ema` and every ring entry — `min(L_rec, L_live, ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)` for a collection window's adoption (`L_rec` the candidate's `√(X·Y)` at the proposal that recorded it, `L_live` at the execution), `min(L_live, L_ema, ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)` otherwise — and `L_slow = min(L_slow, D)` (v0.4.1: `min(√(X·Y)_new, current)` for both, from the adopting instruction's own sample). `venue_switch.py` (b), (c): a deeper candidate's box is narrow from the first second (v0.4.1: wide for 18 s after the switch); a deposit around either sampling instruction changes nothing (the M4 review's record-only rule `min(L_rec, cap)`: `L_ema` 1.995× the pool's depth, box wide 24 s); a withdrawal around either pushes `L_new` down to `D` at most (`1 − λ`; box wide 0 s at λ 99.78 %, 25 s at λ 25 %), a delay; the exit is the same in every row | M4-SEC-5, M4-SEC-6, M4V-2 | §2.1, §5.2, §10.1 |
| 93 | *(The adoption part is superseded by #96: the warm-up seed reaches such a pool's depth at the gate, and M4's written cap was also a forced-liquidation band, §12.12.)* **Eligible pools with `λ < λ_min` re-checked** (ARCH §4.4's L-push bound `1 − λ ≤ 50 %` holds only for `λ ≥ λ_min`): an honest fast path onto one writes the cap `D/λ_min`, below its depth, so the box stays wide in `L` for 20 s after the switch (exits unchanged). An LP-wrapped sample reaches `D`, a push of `1 − λ` (75 % at λ 25 %, 90 % at λ 10 %); an attacker-only second moves `L_ema` by at most −8.3 % (the clamp), and the box medians need 4–5 of 8 seconds, as for the price. Sizing on `D/λ_min` makes the lowest state any push reaches — every unlocked LP withdrawn — no worse relative to sizing than at λ = 50 % (CR after the full drain 2.01 / 1.99 / 1.98 at λ 50 / 25 / 10 %; S2 at ×125.9 / ×124.7 / ×124.7): **no liquidation, S1 or S2 can be forced**. Delay: a contrary LP observation every second (no swap fee) holds the S2/S3 amount spread at ≈ `(1 − λ)·Q/X_D` — 113–196 bps at λ 25–10 % on 25 bps pools (λ 50 %: 75–109), 253–672 bps on 1 % pools — above `fv_dev_bps`, so an S3 on an executable venue waits while it is repeated; §5.6's "moves `L` too little to matter" is corrected | M4V-3, DOC-3 | §5.6, §10.5 |
| 94 | Text: λ = 50 % is `λ_min`, no longer "the worst pool the market can adopt" (§1.3, §11.1); the λ = 50 % drain rows stay the worst case relative to sizing up to the open's own price impact (§11.2, §11.7); `pool_lock_min_bps` is the divisor of the locked depth (eligibility floor, sizing cap, adoption cap), not a required lock share, and `pool_min_real_sol` is a floor on locked SOL (§14 N11, §15); §5.1 lists `execute_pool_switch` among the instructions that observe | DOC-3 | §1.3, §5.1, §11, §14, §15 |
| 95 | *(Folded into #96's post-change confirmation: the same 8-bucket warm-up, now a countdown that also re-seeds `P_ema`.)* **First-open warm-up after a price-starting adoption** (PROPOSED): a Raydium market whose price state a Raydium listing or a list-only market's first adoption started at the candidate's own spot opens only once the EMA has integrated more than `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` = `RING_LEN` (8) buckets since that start, so at least 8 recorded after `pool_seen_at` (`EmaWarmingUp`); the reference keeper observes such a market every second at the end of its listing window. Without it, `[buy, propose_pool, sell]` started `P_ema` at a pushed spot (6.39× the true price on the reproducer), and an hour later, with no crank in between, `[buy, open_short, sell]` opened a short on the captured sub-floor pool and pinned the market to it | M4V-5 | §5.8, §6.2, §14, §15, §17 |
| 96 | **Post-change confirmation of the observation state** (PROPOSED, M4R; owner item O18; replaces #92's adoption liquidity and folds #95's first-open warm-up into one mechanism). As built after M4, a fast-path adoption seeded the exits from single-instruction samples — `L_new = min(L_rec, L_live, D/λ_min)` — and a `P_ema` frozen during the halt: an unlocked LP of the candidate withdraws around a sampling instruction (`L_new = D`), then holds `L_ema` there with one withdraw-wrapped crank per second (directional aggregation freezes an EMA that has an observation on its own side every second), and **liquidates positions within ≈ `q/x` of `cr_liq` that are healthy on the pool as it trades** (M4R-1; the LiteSVM reproducer 24–26 s after the adoption, the sim 21–41 s); and the window floor judged at the stale `P_ema` admits, after a dump, a pool with only `floor·√(P_live/P_ema)` of real locked SOL (M4R-2). **The rule:** (1) every Raydium adoption reads **no sample** of the new pool: `L_ema` and every ring entry's `l`, `llo`, `lhi` := the anchor `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`, `L_slow = min(L_slow, D)`, `P_ema` and the ring prices carried, and the state is marked unconfirmed (`ema_warm = EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` = 8, `ema_guard = 1`; a Raydium price start is marked the same way); (2) each of the next 8 integrations re-seeds, over the post-change entries only, `P_ema = min` of their bucket lows (since #97 also of the largest v0.4.1 step from the previous `P_ema`) and `L_ema = min(max lhi, ⌊lomed(lhi)·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)` — no blend, `L_slow` untouched; (3) while `ema_warm > 0` on an executable venue `liquidate` and `expire` fail (`EmaWarmingUp`), `sol_settle` evaluates nothing and Raydium opens wait; a halted venue is never gated; (4) while `ema_guard = 1` the exit reference reads `L_b = max(L_live, min(L_ema, L_mlo), L_slow)` (and since #97 `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)`), cleared at the first integration after the warm-up with `L_ema ≤ 1.01·L_mlo` (at the gate on an honest pool); (5) a proposal that opens or improves a window judges the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)`. `attacks/switch_warmup.py`: in the M4R-1 grid (q 0.40 / 0.74 %, four wrap variants) no forced exit (as built: every withdraw-wrapped row from 1.98× / 1.97× up to the honest threshold 2.01×, liquidated +21 to +41 s after the adoption); 1,200 targeted random runs: 0 forced (as built 30 of 615 honest-open runs); `λ < λ_min` candidates: no forced band (as built forced at 2.00–2.03×); `L_ema` = the pool's depth at the gate for λ 10–99.78 % and the box narrow from then on (as built wide 20–26 s at λ < λ_min: M4V-3, M4-SEC-6 resolved); a per-second capital deposit freeze no longer blocks the reference (RA → 630 SOL: liquidation at +61 s, lender +0.834 SOL; without the guard S1 at +123 s, −17.785 SOL); after a mild dip during the halt the stale `P_ema` no longer forces a liquidation at +2 s; an honest dump before the window opens keeps a thin pool out (M4R-2; one inside the window is adopted at `P_ema`, #97). Exits resume at adoption + 9 s with the keeper cranking every second, usually earlier than as built (2.2×: +9 vs +13 s; 3× S1: +49 vs +61 s). New state: two header bytes of `Market` (`ema_warm`, `ema_guard`, zero on existing accounts); `fp_lambda_bps` retired; no new parameter, error or event; `REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS` = 100 compiled. Residuals: §12.12 | M4R-1, M4R-2, M4V-3, M4-SEC-6 | §2.1, §5.2, §5.4–§5.6, §5.8, §6.2, §8.2, §10.1, §10.3, §10.5, §12.12, §14, §15, §17 |

A bucket's low is honest only if an honest observation landed in its second, and `L_mlo` belongs to the attacker once 5 of the 8 newest ring entries are attacker-only (a second nobody observed adds no entry). As built at M4R the keeper observed a healthy carried position every second only while `ema_warm > 0` (duty 9), then every 30 s (duty 1), and duty 2's bursts compared prices only. | 99 | *(Restated by #100: its "Residuals restated" held at 25-bps candidates and 120-s pumps, not at a 1 % candidate's fit limit in a 30-s pump — FIT-2, closed by #100's carried fee-scaled caps at `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)`; the proposal's `X_cand` is not manipulation-resistant — FIT-1, the guarantee is the execution's `X₀`; and a closed large position keeps the mark — FIT-3, a disclosed residual.)* **Switch only if all fit** (OWNER decision, 2026-09-29; SPEC §14; owner items O17, O18). The verification of #98 (M4G) showed that its keeper duties closed M4F-1 only with a perfect keeper, and found the non-atomic sibling: **M4G-1** — while an LP-wrapped crank is repeated in every keeper-covered second the frozen `L_ema` neither rises nor decays, so every second the keeper misses is another attacker-only step (×7/6); isolated misses (never three in a row, so no degraded mode) added up, and four of them ended a genuine pump in S1 on the fork (lenders −10.056 SOL, the borrower zeroed); **M4G-2** — the liquidity trigger let anyone start a 60-s per-slot burst with one `[deposit ≥ 2 % of the LP counter, crank_price, withdraw]` for a transaction fee: 25–150× below the keeper's cost of the burst, on every covered market with or without positions (1.10–1.87 SOL per market-day unbatched against the attacker's 0.007–0.029 SOL for one trigger a minute; the example O17 budget of 0.03 SOL gone in under an hour, then no observation at all); **M4G-3** — a real deposit held through the flat phase after the guard's honest clear and pulled at a genuine pump's first second is never foreign, so no coverage sees it, and the lagging `L_ema` / `L_slow` (capped at 2× the depth) blocked a carried position of 3–6 % of the new pool's `X` (S1, −10.056 SOL on the fork). All three need a carried position larger than the new pool lets anyone open. **The rule** (§6.1, §6.2, §10.3): a pool adoption that carries open positions — a fast-path window's on a Raydium market, or a list-only market's window with open positions — is allowed only if every open position fits the candidate's normal opening caps, evaluated as an open on it would be right after the adoption: on its conservative `X₀ = min(X_cand, ⌊min(L_slow, D)·10⁹/isqrt⁺(p)⌋, ⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(λ_min·isqrt⁺(p))⌋)` — `L_slow` capped at the candidate's locked depth `D` as the adoption leaves it, so `X₀ ≤ D/√p`, its locked token reserve — `q_max ≤ min(impact cap, fee-scaled size cap)` and `ΣQ ≤ min(OI cap, fee-scaled OI cap)` with `X₁ = X₀` (nothing is sold into the candidate), the impact caps of the tier the market would have on the candidate, judged at `p = min(P_ema, P_cand)` (the M4R-2 floor's reference) **and** at `p = P_ema`, the stricter binding (a lower price credits more tokens, so a dump wrapped around the instruction cannot loosen the fit). A proposal also reads the candidate's live token reserve `X_cand` (its own transaction); the execution does not, so neither an unlocked LP's withdrawal nor a buy around it can cancel a window that fits. A proposal whose candidate fails is refused (`CarriedPositionsTooLarge`, appended); an execution whose candidate fails cancels the window, and the chain stays armed. `q_max = min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)`: the new `Market.max_q_hwm` is the high-water mark of `q` over the positions opened since `open_positions` last returned to 0 (raised by `open_short`, reset to 0 when the last position exits) — O(1) and conservative by design — and `ΣQ = ΣB_Q`. For a position at a λ 50 % incumbent's size cap that means a candidate whose locked depth covers the incumbent's `L_slow`, its whole depth. If no candidate fits, the positions stay on the failed pool — healthy ones frozen at the pre-halt `P_ema` until their deadline — and exit through S3 at fair value (D3, O15): the intended outcome. Zero-position adoptions and listings are unaffected. **Keeper** (§5.8): the liquidity-triggered bursts of #98 are reverted (duty 2 compares prices only again); duty 9's per-second observation while `ema_guard = 1` on a market with positions and §8.2's reference cap stay, as defense in depth; the fast path proposes only a candidate that fits. **Residuals restated** (§12.12 (11)): a carried position is now no larger than one opened on the new pool right after the switch, so the M4F-1 freeze — held from the adoption, or started after the guard's clear with the keeper back on its 30-s cadence (M4G-1's limit: every second after the clear but the keeper's is attacker-only) — and M4G-3's held deposit pulled at or just before the pump only delay the liquidation: 0–1 s (0–1.4 % of price), lenders +0.102 to +0.835 SOL, for RA's position carried onto candidates at the fit limit (λ 99.78 % pools of 314–2,510 SOL at `q/x` 0.73–0.75 %, a λ 50 % pool of 5,000 SOL at 0.37 %), identically under the #99 keeper, the keeper as built at M4R and without the cap. Part 4d's oversized rows (`q` 2.94–5.90 % of the candidate's `X₀` against the 0.75 % cap) open no window and settle S3 at the deadline (+29.96 d; lenders 19.786 SOL for `q` worth 18.469 SOL at the frozen `P_ema`, +7.1 %). **Sims:** `shortwin_sim.py` `carried_x0`, `carried_q_bound`, `carried_fit_ok`, `Market.carried_fit` and `Market.{open_positions, sum_q, max_q_hwm}` (`open` registers a position, `try_exit` releases it); `fast_path_events` checks the window floor and the fit at the proposal and at the execution; `FAST_SCENARIOS` gains a 120 % candidate at index 1 (the adopting replay the vectors trace), and `scenario_tables` continues the refused rows to their S3; `venue_switch.py` applies the rule to M4R in (b), (c) and (d1) and replays the 120 % candidate in (a); `switch_warmup.py` gains part 11, and parts 4d / 4e relabel #98's keeper and 4d adds the #99 keeper's column (parts 1–10 study the adoption's mechanics and force it). **Output diffs:** `full.txt` (only the fast-path section: the 50 % and 25 % rows frozen, the 120 % rows, the S3 table), `venue_switch.out` ((a) on the 120 % candidate; (b), (c) the RA / RB / RC candidates whose locked depth is below the incumbent's refused under M4R; (d1)'s carried rows), `switch_warmup.out` (4d's header and last column, 4e's header, part 11); every other output byte-identical; `config_check.py` prints `OK`. Vectors: `risk/exits.json` and `settle/trace_attacks_3.json` regenerated (`FAST_SCENARIOS`). A 25-bps incumbent's positions therefore keep the 0.75 % class the sims validated on any candidate: FIT-2's positions (100 / 76 % of #99's limit, 2.469 / 1.879 % of `X₀`) open no window and settle S3 at fair value at the deadline (`switch_warmup.py` 11d (i): +6.96 d, lenders 8.389 / 6.380 SOL for `q` worth 7.883 / 6.000 SOL at the frozen `P_ema`, +6.4 / +6.3 %; the fork's `freeze::fit2_a_position_at_the_one_percent_fit_limit_is_not_adopted_and_settles_in_s3`), and a position at the new fit limit (0.75 % of `X₀`) on the 1 % candidate is liquidated within 0–1 s of the control under every push (11d (ii); `freeze::fit2_a_carried_position_at_the_new_fit_limit_is_liquidated_under_the_pushes`). **Residual (§12.12 residual 15):** `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` changes nothing when the incumbent's fee is at least the candidate's, so a 1 %→1 % switch still carries up to the candidate's 2.47 % impact cap on a margin posted on the deeper incumbent's cost: at 70–100 % of that limit a 4×/30 s pump under the freeze after the clear or the held deposit ends in S1 (11d (iii): lenders −5.424 to −7.666 SOL, the control liquidated), at ≤ 65 % it is liquidated within 1 s, and 45-s and slower pumps within 0–3 s. **The first fix, withdrawn (FITV-1 – FITV-3):** (a) a **lows bound** on the exit reference's EMA terms, `L_slow` included (`min(…, ⌊L_mlo·(10⁴ + 100)/10⁴⌋)`), closed FIT-2 but let an unlocked LP force the liquidation of a position that is healthy at the pool's depth (FITV-2): withdraw-wrapped cranks in the quiet seconds of the keeper's 30-s cadence drag `L_ema` and every bucket low to the locked depth `D`, and a withdraw-wrapped liquidation — its spot leg at the drained `L_live`, its EMA leg at the dragged `L_ema` — then fit an exit bound the lows had lowered to `D` (the fork: forced at 2.05× where the honest keeper never liquidates, λ 50 %, 1 %; at 2.00× for λ 25 % and 1.90× for `λ_min` 2,000; its overpay on a due liquidation also scaled with `1/λ_min`, FITV-3). Removing it restores #98's `L_b = max(L_live, min(max(L_ema', L_slow), ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋))`, whose `L_slow` term (V2-14) still credits the depth the pool had: `switch_warmup.py` 5b, the same drag and wrapped liquidation under the keeper's real cadence, forces nothing after drags of 30 s–10 min (λ 50 %), up to 1 h (λ 25 %) or up to 3 h (`λ_min` 2,000) — but `L_slow` integrates the dragged `L_ema` (τ_slow = 1 h), and a drag held 0.79 h (λ 50 %, 2.05×), 1.80 h (λ 25 %, 2.00×) or 3.19 h (`λ_min` 2,000, 1.90×) before the pump opens the same band (2.01–2.07× forced against the honest 2.078× at λ 50 %): the pre-existing steady-state class, §12.12 residual 10, now quantified at 1 %. (b) **`refresh_max_q`** (FIT-3's fix: anyone lowered `max_q_hwm` to the open positions' largest `q` over a chunked scan) kept its scan in one shared, unauthenticated slot, so anyone could reset it between the keeper's chunks for one transaction fee and keep the market pinned (FITV-1: 400 griefer transactions against 815 keeper transactions, no switch); it is removed with the keeper's refresh duty, and the mark again resets only when `open_positions` reaches 0 (its error 6103 is kept reserved, `ReservedScanOutOfOrder`, never returned). **FIT-3 (a residual, §6.1, §12.12 residual 14):** a borrower who opened a large position — even one closed a second later (0.013401 SOL on the fork) — keeps every candidate thinner than that position's cap refused while any position is open; the positions then exit through S3 at fair value, each at its own deadline (the §10.3 timer; at most the tier's term, 30 / 14 / 7 d at A / B / C), or are carried once those exits bring `ΣQ` under the candidate's per-position cap (`q_max = min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)`): 11e, RA's three positions of a quarter of the cap opened on days 0 / 10 / 20 and halted on day 21 — adopted at +210 s without the griefer; with it S3 at +9.00 and +19.00 d and the last position carried at +19.00 d. **FIT-1 (a residual, stated; §6.1, residual 13):** the proposal reads the candidate's live `X_cand`, which binds after a genuine pump of the candidate during the halt; `[sell ≈X_cand tokens, propose_pool, buy back]` lifts it inside the transaction, and the execution never reads it (so that no withdrawal or buy around it cancels a window). The guarantee is the execution's `X₀ = min(L_slow', ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)/√P_ema`: carried `q` up to `λ·√(P_cand/P_ema)` × a native open's cap (the fork: 1.085 / 1.109 / 1.366 at `r` 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.9 against the bounds 1.094 / 1.223 / 1.377), liquidated under the pushes within 1 s of the control; the proposal keeps `X_cand` (it binds the honest flow). | 101 | *(Restated by #102: its (c) "nothing is forced" holds for drags built from attacker-only seconds and for withdrawals inside the liquidation, not for a genuinely reached low — a withdrawal held for 3–20 s, then frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks, which the per-second coverage holds but cannot lift (FITX-1): on the fork both FITS-2 reproducers and the 25-bps position at its open cap are forced again, in the sim bands of 1–5 hold levels at half the cap and below and at 25 bps and, after a 25-min – 1-h freeze, at 0.79–1 × the cap, each exactly a liquidation the same LP's withdrawal for good also gets; its "Left" understated it.)* **The verification of #100's simplification (FITS-1 – FITS-3)** (PROPOSED with #96–#100; the owner's decisions of the FITS round; owner items O17, O18). **FITS-1 — a 1 %→1 % switch at the impact cap (#100's residual 15):** `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` is the candidate's own fee when the incumbent's is at least as high, so #100 still carried up to the candidate's 2.47 % impact cap, on a margin posted on the deeper incumbent's cost. On the fork (the 2,500-SOL λ 50 % incumbent and the 320-SOL λ 99.78 % candidate, both at 1 %, FIT-2's pushes, a genuine 4×/30 s pump) carried positions at **60–100 %** of that limit (1.48–2.46 % of `X`) ended in S1 (lenders −4.716 to −7.824 SOL) where the control was liquidated: wider than the sim's 70–100 %, which #100 stated. **The fix (owner decision):** the carried fit's fee-scaled caps take `φ = min(φ_inc, φ_cand, CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM)`, **`CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM` = 2,500** (a new compiled constant, PROPOSED; §6.1, §15): every carried position is in the ≤ 0.75 %-of-`X₀` class the switch sims and the fork validated under every push, whatever the fees; a larger one is not carried and settles S3 at fair value (the accepted fallback, §12.12 residual 12). `switch_warmup.py` 11d (iii): the 1 %→1 % positions at 50–100 % of #100's limit open no window and settle S3 at +6.96 d (lenders +7.0–7.2 % against `q` at the frozen `P_ema`); one at the new limit (0.749 % of `X₀`) is liquidated within 0–1 s of the control under every push and pump speed. | 106 | **The owner's decision on OWNER-M6D-1: PumpSwap is sized on its burned LP only** (2026-09-30; SPEC §14 M6D-1; ARCH changelog 57; owner item O18) — option (a) of the #105 verification's M6D-1. **(1) The rule** (§6.1, §6.2; N34): every sizing term that divides a canonical PumpSwap pool's `D` by `λ_min` takes **`PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10,000** (compile-time; `λ_min` = 100 %) instead of `pool_lock_min_bps`: `X₀ = min(X_now, ⌊L_slow·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋, ⌊D·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋)` (the `D` term `⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(10⁴·isqrt⁺(P_ema))⌋` exactly), and with it the per-position, market-OI, fee-scaled, wallet and utilisation caps on `X₀ + q`; the tier's `R_eff ≤ max(⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋ − max(VQR, 0), 0)`, so `Λ` counts the burned LP's real SOL only. A third-party deposit, held for any time, adds nothing to a PumpSwap position's size, the market's caps or the tier (`pumpswap_lp.py` 3 (b): 1.00× after 1× / 3× / 7× held 1–4 h, #105 1.63× / 2.00× / 2.00×). A canonical pool's lock is its burned LP, so `λ_min` has no eligibility role there; Raydium keeps `pool_lock_min_bps` everywhere. **(2) The exit reference's cap is `D` too** (§8.2; N34; the #106 verification's M6E-1, closed in this row's fix pass): `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋` at PumpSwap's `λ_min` = 100 %, so `L_b = max(L_live, min(max(L_ema', L_slow), D)) = L_live` on a canonical pool (`D ≤ L_live`), and a PumpSwap price start's anchor likewise (`min(√(X·(Y + VQR)), D)`, §5.2) — the owner's rule that every term crediting `D/λ_min` takes 100 %, and M4F-1's own: the cap is "never more depth than sizing credits". The decision was first written with the cap kept at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋` = `2D`, on the argument that with the positions sized on `D` the two caps give the same exits: true of every liquidation row ((12) (f) (i)–(iii): a buyback on the drained pool costs `(1 − c/2)/(1 − c) − 1` over a reference at twice its depth, 1.27 % at the impact cap's `c` = 2.47 %, inside `δ_liq` = 2 %), not of expiry (`δ_exp` = 1 %, crossed above `c` = 1.96 %, ≈ 80 % of the cap): a deposit of 2–3× the pool held for the hour before a deadline and pulled at it held such an expiry up for 31–56 min ((12) (f) (iv): 3× +3,326 s, 2× +1,874 s, pulled 10 min early +2,355 s; the fork's `pamm_it::lock::part_3f_*` +3,347 s) — a free extension with the lenders' tokens out that anyone, the borrower included, gets with capital alone, no attacker-only second — and at a governed `pool_lock_min_bps` below the default the kept cap was 3D–5D and M6D-1's S1 came back through the parameter ((12) (f) (v): at 3,333 / 2,000 a 3× deposit pulled at a 5× / 10× pump, −31.1 / −52.3 % of `Q`, the borrower zeroed; the fork at 2,000 −39.1 %). At `D` every such expiry lands at +1 s and `pool_lock_min_bps` reaches no PumpSwap term (`VenueKind::lock_min_bps`, one `λ_min` per venue). What the EMA terms gave on PumpSwap (V2-14's layer) was a deeper reference for a buyback wrapped in an LP's own withdrawal; priced at the drained pool it lands in the same second, the borrower's share of `V` at most 0.1 points lower, the lenders +4.1–4.2 % ((12) (f) (iii)). **(3) Result** ((12) (e)): every row #105 ended in S1 is liquidated, at +21–67 s, the lenders +4.1–4.2 % of `Q`'s value; the fork (`pamm_it::lock::part_3e_*`) +59 s, the control +61 s, the lenders +4.20 %. **Cost:** a pool with genuine third-party LP is sized at λ of its live depth (e/acc 0.93×, a pool with a third of its LP third-party 0.67×); **Changed in v0.4.1 — for `shortwin-math`** (the exact delete / change / add list is ARCH §9 M0b): `RingEntry` stores the bucket's `(lo, hi, llo, lhi)` instead of `(obs, lobs)`; the box's medians (lower of the lows, upper of the highs); FV's `p'` per evaluated state; `max_in` split into a liquidation form without reserve and the expiry form with rent and fee; the liquidation reference's `lo_x·(1 + κ_eff)` cap; the S2/S3 lump `x − min(x, ⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due)`; in `pool`, the lock expiry calendar, `max_release`, floored lock terms, the value-weighted vest end and the post-wipe deposit gate; the risk quote above the fee hard cap charges one combined fee at the cap (the sims' form).

**Changed after M4 — for `shortwin-math`** (#91–#95; as built): `pool_select::{locked_floor_ok, position_floor_ok, adoption_liquidity}`, `observe::VenueChange::RaydiumLocked { slow_cap: D }`, and the PROPOSED `consts::EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` with `caps::price_warmed_up`. The sim's counterparts are `locked_floor_ok`, `position_floor_ok` and `Market.adopt_pool`.

**Changed by M4R — for `shortwin-math`** (#96; PROPOSED): `observe::{Ema.warm, Ema.guard (Ema._pad[0..2]), mark_restart, warmed, Observation::new_raydium}` and the warm-up branch and guard check of `integrate`; `bounds::ref_liquidity(l_live, &Ema, &Ring)`; `SettleInputs.warmed` (`settle::evaluate` a no-op while warming on an executable venue); `consts::{EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS: u8, REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS}`; `caps::price_warmed_up` and `pool_select::adoption_liquidity` removed. The sim's counterparts are `Market.{adopt_pool, _integrate_warmup, _guard_check, mark_restart, gated, exit_reference}`, `window_floor_ok`, `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` and `REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS`; `Market.fast_switch` keeps the v0.4.1 rewrite for the crate's differential vectors (`VenueChange::Raydium`) only, and `FAST_SCENARIOS` replays `adopt_pool`. Regenerated vectors: `settle/trace_attacks_3.json` (the `venue_switch/RA` trace: its adoption is the op `rewrite t R l_new slow_cap`, then 8 warm-up rolls) and `risk/exits.json` (rows sampled from `FAST_SCENARIOS`).

**Changed by M4F-1 — for `shortwin-math`** (#98; PROPOSED): `bounds::ref_liquidity(l_live, &Ema, &Ring, depth_cap: Option<u128>)` computes `max(l_live, min(max(L_ema', L_slow), cap))`, and `bounds::ref_depth_cap(D, λ_min)` = `pool_select::depth_at_lambda`. The program passes `Some(ref_depth_cap(Market.pool_d, pool_lock_min_bps))` on a Raydium market and `None` on the other venues. The sim's counterpart is `Market.ref_depth_cap`. Regenerated vectors: the settle traces' `ref` op carries the cap as a new last token (`trace_attacks_1.json`, `trace_attacks_3.json`, `trace_random_1.json`, `trace_scenarios_1.json`); no `L_b` value, decision or other line changes. On their λ = 50 % pools the cap is the live depth to λ's whole-bps floor (0.9998×), and `L_b` already took the live depth there; after the 50 % drain it is 2× the live depth, above `L_slow`. There is also a new `oracle/units_ref.json` (500 `ref_liquidity` cases: the guard on or off, the cap binding or not, `λ_min` over its compiled range). Every other generated vector regenerates byte-identical.

**Changed by #99 — for `shortwin-math`** (OWNER decision; as the code builds it): the carried-position fit of an adoption, `caps::carried_fit(q_max, ΣQ, &CarriedCaps)` with `q_max = caps::carried_q_bound(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)` = `min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)` (`ΣQ` alone for a zero mark), and `caps::position_cap`; the program fills `CarriedCaps.x0` with `caps::conservative_x0(X_cand, min(L_slow, D), p, (D, λ_min))` (without `X_cand` at the execution) at `p = min(P_ema, P_cand)` and at `P_ema`. The sim's counterparts are `carried_x0`, `carried_q_bound`, `carried_fit_ok` and `Market.carried_fit`. Every other generated vector regenerates byte-identical.

**Changed by #100 — for `shortwin-math`** (PROPOSED): `caps::open_fee_mark(mark, φ)` — `Market.min_open_fee_ppm` after an open at the one-way fee `φ`: `φ` for the first open after the market emptied (`mark` = 0), else `min(mark, φ)` — and `caps::carried_phi(min_open_fee_ppm, φ_cand)` = `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` (`φ_cand` alone for a zero mark), the fee `CarriedCaps` passes to `fee_size_cap` / `fee_oi_cap`; `bounds` is #98's again (the first fix's `bounds::ref_lows_cap` is gone). **Changed by #101 — for `shortwin-math`** (PROPOSED): `caps::CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM` (2,500) and `caps::carried_phi(min_open_fee_ppm, φ_cand)` = `min(φ_inc, φ_cand, CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM)` (`min(φ_cand, CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM)` for a zero mark); the CR-liquidatability spot leg repriced on `max(l_live, L_ema')`, `L_ema'` being `bounds::ref_liquidity`'s guarded EMA term (`min(L_ema, L_mlo)` while `ema.guard = 1`) without `L_slow` or the cap, in the program's `liquidate` / `expire` assessment and `sol_settle`'s `engine::arming::inputs` and in the keeper's `decide` (`risk::spot_leg` itself is unchanged: it takes the quote). **Changed by #105 — for `shortwin-math`** (the owner's decisions of 2026-09-30; the code step builds them): `observe::price_down_bound(p_ema, dt, clamp_bps)` = `⌊(P_ema·τ + ⌊P_ema·10⁴/(10⁴ + C)⌋·dt)/(τ + dt)⌋` (the mirror of `price_up_bound`: ×11/12 at `dt` = 1, ×6/7 at `dt` = 2) and the warm-up branch of `integrate` seeding `P = max(min(min lo, price_up_bound), price_down_bound)` (M6-SEC-1; the I18 hook recomputes both); PumpSwap's locked depth is `pool_select::locked_depth(Pool.lp_supply, lp_mint.supply, X, Y + VQR)` — the Raydium function on the pool's own LP counter and its LP mint (M6R-1) — and the program passes it where it passes Raydium's: `caps::conservative_x0(X_now, L_slow, p, Some((D, λ_min)))`, `bounds::ref_liquidity(…, Some(ref_depth_cap(pool_d, λ_min)))`, and `TierVenue::PumpSwap` gains `{ locked_depth, lambda_min_bps }` with `R_eff ≤ ⌊max(⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋ − max(VQR, 0), 0)·10⁴/λ_min⌋` (the locked SOL less the virtual reserve). The sim's counterparts are `Market._integrate_warmup`, `CPPool.{lock_bps, locked_depth, add_liquidity, remove_liquidity}`, `Market.{locked_depth_venue, conservative_x, ref_depth_cap, _warmup_slow_cap, _graduate, _restart_on}` and `CPPool.boost_tick` (the ELF's rounding). **Regenerated vectors** (every generator except `gen_mainnet`; the other 31 files unchanged): `quotes/caps.json` — the PumpSwap example markets and `conservative_x0` rows (A, B, C) carry `raydium_lock = (D, λ_min)` (the field keeps its name; `D` is the whole depth on these pools, so `expected` moves only where `λ_min < 10⁴` makes `D/λ_min` bind: 19 A / B rows), market C's rows move by the boost rounding (`x_now`, `p_ema`, `l_slow` in the last digits), and the 235 PumpSwap `tiers` rows carry a `lock` drawn from their own stream (`liquidity` changes in 92 rows, the tier in 11); `quotes/sim.json` — market C's repriced rows (the boost rounding); `risk/exits.json` — the samplers draw again over the changed record sets (the graduation's restart and tx3 without the keeper's exit, the boost rounding; `exit_fuzz` and ECON §13's statistics unchanged); `settle/fuzz_1.json`, `fuzz_2.json`, `fuzz_3.json` — the fuzz's market-C traces (the position's `q` and the amounts, the boost rounding; 56 / 54 / 20 traces); `settle/trace_attacks_1.json`, `trace_attacks_3.json`, `trace_random_1.json`, `trace_scenarios_1.json` — the `ref` op's cap token on PumpSwap markets (`-` → `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`, 2× the depth: no `L_b` value or decision changes), market C's traces (the boost rounding), and the `venue_switch/D@90 %` graduation trace, whose transition is now `obs t 1` (the roll without a sample) then the op `rewrite t R anchor anchor` (v0.4.1: `rewrite t g l`) and 8 warm-up rolls. `settle_vectors.rs` already parses every op form; `oracle/gen_oracle.py`'s `unit_rewrite` keeps v0.4.1's graduation (`Market._switch_to_pool`) for `VenueChange::Graduation`'s unit vectors.

**Changed by #106 — for `shortwin-math`** (the owner's decision on OWNER-M6D-1; the code step builds it): no function changes — `caps::conservative_x0` and `TierVenue::PumpSwap` already take `λ_min` as an input. A new compile-time constant `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS: u32 = 10_000` (proposed in `consts`, beside `CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM`), which the program passes on PumpSwap: `caps::conservative_x0(X_now, L_slow, P_ema, Some((pool_d, PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS)))` and `TierVenue::PumpSwap { locked_depth: pool_d, lambda_min_bps: PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS }` (the wasm module's venue-1 inputs likewise); since the fix pass (M6E-1) also `bounds::ref_depth_cap(pool_d, PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS)` = `pool_d` and a PumpSwap price start's anchor at `D` (the program's `VenueKind::lock_min_bps`). The sim's counterparts are `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` and `Market.lock_min_bps`. **Regenerated vectors** (every generator except `gen_mainnet`; the other 38 generated files byte-identical): `quotes/caps.json` only — `gen_caps.py`'s `lock_of` records the sizing `λ_min` (`Market.lock_min_bps`, first named `sizing_lock_min_bps`) and its PumpSwap tier rows draw `lock = (D, PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS)`: the `conservative_x0` rows on the PumpSwap markets carry `raydium_lock = (D, 10,000)` (119 of 300 rows change: A 45, B 33, C 41; `expected` moves in 46 of them, where `D/√P_ema` now binds); the example markets' `raydium_lock` carries 10,000 (12 of 32 rows: A, B, C and the lagging D@90 % / D@97 % markets, which graduated to their pools) with no `x0` or cap change (`D ≥ L_slow` on these λ = 1 pools); the 222 PumpSwap `tiers` rows draw `D` at `λ_min` 10,000 from their own stream (`liquidity` changes in 120 rows, the tier in 17). `caps_sim.rs` and `tier_arch.rs` take `λ_min` from the rows and pass unchanged (the crate's 307 tests). **Fix pass (M6E-1):** `settle/trace_attacks_1.json`, `trace_attacks_3.json`, `trace_random_1.json`, `trace_scenarios_1.json` — the `ref` lines' `cap` on graduated PumpSwap markets (9 lines, `2D` → `D`; `L_b` unchanged); `settle_vectors.rs` reads the cap from the rows and passes unchanged.

**Changed by #111 — for `shortwin-math`** (M10-8C-1, WP-6 batch 2; as built): `pool::rent_top_up(principal, rent, credited)` = `max(0, min(principal, rent) − credited)`; `pool::repay` vests `lump + top-up`; `pool::settle(…, sol_in, rent)` takes the position's `R_due` and returns `Settled { rec, vested }` (`vested` = `min(lump, sol_in)` + top-up, which `EpochRolled.vest_flushed` counts; since AIR-07, #117, `SolSettle.vest_flushed` too, with or without a roll, when the settlement flushes); the sim's counterparts are `Pool._rent_top_up` / `_true_up`. **Vectors:** the pool group `pool_ext_topup` (`pool_vectors::ext_rent_top_up_vests`: `R_min`-sized top-ups and the scripted JIT whale). `shortwin-math-wasm` (batch 3) adds `RentBasis.lenderCredit`, the credit the next prologue books, for the web's deposit warning.

The round-3 brief asked for two things at once: fix every finding, and remove complexity that the reviewers showed buys no safety. Items marked **(simplification)** delete mechanisms; the list of what to delete from the math crate follows the table. Earlier changelogs are in Appendix B.

Re-running the reviewer's `dutch*.py`: 0 of 144 genuine-move cells change; 1 of 96 near-band cells liquidates 2 s later at a higher price (lenders +2.2 % instead of +4.5 %); a fixed 1 % turns 3 drain-then-pump liquidations into S1, so 2 % is the compiled minimum. The atomic first-second sandwich doubles (Tier A +1.50 SOL, v0.3 +0.71) but no longer ramps (after 4 s: +1.50 instead of +3.86), and blocking the spot leg can no longer widen the bound | decision 1; R3A-7; R3A-11 (c) | §8.2, §12.2, §12.9 |

Found by the full attack suite under the fixed bound: in the held model the attacker bought the vault down to `V − K_s − ert` and truncated the penalty (Tier A lenders + protocol −2.06 SOL).
