---

## 0. Findings in brief

1. **All three venue families are constant-product pools** (§1). Buyback cost has a closed form, and an immediate sell-then-buy of `Q` costs only the fee pair (2.53 % of proceeds on the curve).
2. **The SPEC §4 table holds under D4.** Enforcing the opening CR after fees at spot and at the EMA costs borrowers 0.8–3.9 % of notional more than the "initial margin" column for small positions and up to 17.6 % at the size cap, where the literal EMA leg charges the open's own price impact (§4).
3. **The exit sandwich is bounded by a fixed 2 % over the reference** (v0.4). Under the atomic model a keeper sandwiching a liquidation gains at most +1.50 SOL over an honest keeper on a capped Tier A position (borrower −2.02 SOL), whether it acts at once or waits; expiry: +0.35 SOL; lenders lose nothing. Blocking the spot leg can no longer widen the bound, and (v0.4.1) neither can a pushed-and-frozen EMA (§12.2, §12.9).
4. **Atomic-bundle EMA manipulation does not work** when any other observation lands in the same second — the in-house keeper provides one every second near thresholds (§5.8). Near-threshold, fresh-position and OI-basket attacks cannot force any exit under that model (§12.3–12.5).
5. **Held pumps remain the residual.** An attacker who holds the pool at a manipulated price for whole seconds, with nobody trading against it, looks like a genuine pump. A keeper can take the remainder of a borrower who is already being liquidated (Tier A: +8.36 SOL over an honest keeper, borrower −19.5 SOL), and lenders and protocol lose the part of the penalty — and, on positions whose rent due exceeds the settlement reward, of the rent — that the bought-down vault no longer covers (Tier A −3.34 SOL; −4.54 SOL on a position aged 25 days at 90 % utilisation). Before AIR-22's lower-median growth (#117), with which two held seconds do what took three, these read +5.66, −2.06 and −8.02 SOL. v0.4's claim that lenders are never worse off rested on a penalty reserve that blocked honest liquidations in genuine fast pumps and did not hold on aged positions (since AIR-22 it only trims the loss on fresh ones too); v0.4.1 removes it (§8.2). Low-fee Raydium baskets stay marginally profitable (+0.5 to +8.5 SOL at the OI cap). The same numbers are the exposure of a market the keeper stops covering (degraded mode, §5.8) (§12.6).
6. **Liquidations keep working in fast pumps.** Tier C liquidates a 10×/60 s pump at 2.93× and Tier D at 3.29×. A liquidatable position whose buyback fits within 2 % of the reference never waits (v0.4.1: no reserve in the liquidation bound). The simulated-safe parameter region is narrow and compiled: τ = 5 s (a constant), κ ≥ 25 %, persistence ≤ 3 updates, a growth window of ≤ 4 steps, δ ≥ 2 %, SPEC CRs as minima; it is checked on 1,655 runs that include per-second price noise, the reviewer's genuine-pump grid and aged positions (§5.7, §15).
7. **Settlement decisions are robust to manipulated seconds** (settlement box; v0.4.1 takes its medians over the ring's bucket lows and highs). S1 pays lenders `V − K_s` and needs every S1 leg — including the EMA leg on the box — to hold; S2/S3 pay `min(V − K_s, FV)` with FV on EMA reserves, and only when the amount agrees within 1 % across the box. Moving a decision needs 5 attacker-only seconds of the ring's 8; seconds that also hold an honest observation count for nothing, so v0.4's push-then-freeze is closed along with the v0.3 S2 → S1 flip and refutation push. A paid contrary observation every second can still defer S1 or an S3 on an executable venue (§5.6, §12.10–12.11).
8. **Gap risk is unchanged and still borne by lenders.** Lenders start losing only when a gap outruns the whole cushion (≈ 2.1× A, 2.6× B, 3.1× C, 4× D); at 10× they lose 78 / 73 / 68 / 58 % of the lent tokens' value (§11.5). Whether rent compensates this depends on uncalibrated gap frequencies (§11.6).
9. **Graduation carries a deterministic +45.66 % push** from the proportional boost; positions opened at 80–90 % curve progress are liquidated normally on the completing buy or by the boost push (§10.1).
10. **Lender accounting is live and bounded**: every realised lamport is claimable by someone after wind-down (stranding 68.12 SOL → 0.00002 SOL on the reviewer's test), repeated claims pay 0, lumps and the premium of a settlement's take over market value (S2/S3, and S1 since AIR-21) vest over 6 h so just-in-time deposits capture none of them atomically, no lock outlives the positions it stands for (including their S3 tail), and an expired lock stops counting for everyone at its expiry, not at its holder's next touch (v0.4.1). A run before an S1 still gains the runner 4.3 % over fair value at 90 % utilisation and up to 11.1 % at 50 %, and a lender who exits forfeits its unreleased vest to whoever stays (§9).
11. **Keepers are the protocol's operating cost.** Per-second coverage of every market costs more than a beta market earns; coverage is targeted and budgeted by the owner, and a market the keeper cannot cover pauses opens visibly (§5.8).

---

## 1. Venue facts (verified)

### 1.1 Pump.fun bonding curve (program `6EF8rrecthR5Dkzon8Nwu78hRvfCKubJ14M5uBEwF6P`)

Unchanged from v0.1 and re-verified by the fact-check (C1 "verified OK").

- The curve is "based on Uniswap V2 and uses synthetic x and y reserves" [S1]. State: `virtual_token_reserves` (vt), `virtual_quote_reserves` (vs), `real_token_reserves` (rt), `real_quote_reserves` (rs), `complete` [S1, S3]. Virtual and real reserves move by the same amounts, so `vt − rt = 279,900,000,000,000` and (non-mayhem SOL curves) `vs − rs = 30,000,000,000` are constant [T1, T4]; mayhem curves have other offsets [T2, T3, T5, T12] and are rejected (ADOPTED Q6).
- Live `Global` [A1]: initial virtual tokens 1,073,000,000,000,000; initial virtual SOL 30,000,000,000; initial real tokens 793,100,000,000,000; supply 10¹⁵ (6 decimals); `pool_migration_fee` 15,000,001.
- **Buy, exact out:** `net = ⌊Q·vs/(vt − Q)⌋ + 1`; protocol and creator fees `⌈net·bps/10⁴⌉` on top [S4]. `amount ≤ rt` [S2].
- **Sell, exact in:** `gross = ⌊Q·vs/(vt + Q)⌋`; fees deducted [S4].
- **Fees:** pump `FeeConfig` has one tier: 95 bps protocol + 30 bps creator = **1.25 %** each way [A2].
- **Completion and migration:** the buy that makes `rt = 0` sets `complete`; `migrate_v2` is permissionless [S1]. Migration seeds the canonical pool with 206,900,000,000,000 tokens and `rs − pool_migration_fee` lamports [T7, T8].
- **M5 re-verification (2026-09-29; R-PUMP Update U1–U10) — facts that move money:** (1) when the creator vault is empty, pump tops it up to `Rent.minimum_balance(0)` = **650,240** lamports from `user` on buys **and sells**, a sale's top-up taken **before** the proceeds are credited, so on the curve `S` = proceeds − 650,240 then (an open whose `S_exp` cannot absorb it within `exec_tol_bps` fails `ProceedsBelowMin`); (2) every pump buy accepts an undocumented trailing **cap byte** — set to 1, an oversized buy fills exactly `rt` at the exact-out price instead of reverting; short.win and its helper never send it, so "a buy above `rt` reverts" (§10.2) still holds for them; (3) `create_v2` allocates 125-byte curves, and pump grows a shorter curve to 125 B on its first v2 trade, `user` topping up its rent reserve; (4) pump resolves the curve's fee tier on the mint's **live** supply, `⌊vs·supply/vt⌋` (this model and the build use 10¹⁵; no quote differs while pump's `FeeConfig` has one tier); (5) anyone can complete a curve and run `migrate_v2` in **one** transaction, roughly self-financing (the completer keeps the `rt` tokens), and completion to migration took 0–14 slots in 20 mainnet samples; (6) all 1,610 non-mayhem migrations of 2026-09-27 – 09-29 follow the boost rule of §1.2 (none of 492 mayhem pools is boosted). Money-path consequences are in ARCH §2.4 rule 3, §3.12 and §5.4.
- ★**pump's 2026-10-08 / 10-13 builds (the pump-final re-pin; `tests/fixtures/README.md`):** curves are 166 bytes (`create_v2`, and every trade grows a shorter one, its rent reserve net of the fee buckets), with `depth` @141, `initial_virtual_quote_reserves` @142 (`vs − rs = ivqr`) and the post-complete fields `pcB` / `pcQ` @150 / @158; v3 trades (`sell_v3`, `buy_v3`, `buy_exact_quote_in_v3`) keep the protocol and creator fees in the curve's buckets, outside `rs`; a `buy_v3` past `rt` completes the curve by **synthetic migration** (§3.1, §10.2), both parts at the curve tier of the live supply's market cap; v3 refuses cashback coins (6094), so short.win lists live cashback curves no longer (owner D1 (a)). short.win trades v3 since 1.0.1: no creator-vault top-up any more (item (1) is v2's), and the live-supply tier (item (4)) now prices its synthetic exits (known issues KI-77).

### 1.2 PumpSwap AMM (program `pAMMBay6oceH9fJKBRHGP5D4bD4sWpmSwMn52FMfXEA`)

- **Buy, exact base out:** `quote_in = ⌈Y·b/(X − b)⌉`, `Y = real quote + virtual_quote_reserves`; lp, protocol and creator fees `⌈quote_in·bps/10⁴⌉` on top; the LP fee stays in the pool [S6, T9–T11].
- **Sell, exact base in:** `⌊Y·b/(X + b)⌋` minus fees; **payouts are capped by the real quote reserve** (error `InsufficientRealQuoteReserves`), so the price has a floor `VQR/X` that sellers cannot go through [R-VEN §1.4] — **while `VQR ≥ 0`**.
- **Negative VQR (from 2026-09-30; PPD `cb188ce0`, R-VEN Update G2).** VQR drifts after the boost, downward too, and pump announced that it can be **negative**: it guarantees `0 ≤ quote_vault + VQR ≤ u64::MAX` and keeps the pricing formula (`Y = real + VQR`). With `VQR < 0` the real SOL **exceeds** the SOL that prices trades, the floor above and the real-reserve cap cannot bind (a payout is below `Y`, hence below the real reserve), and every quote is still exact on `Y`. The pool reader computes `Y` signed and rejects only a sum outside `[0, u64::MAX]` (ARCH §4.1); the tier input that read real SOL alone (Q19) overstates such a pool (§6.2). Fresh canonical pools are 271 bytes and trade as such (R-VEN Update G4).
- **Fees:** canonical pools 125 bps (2/93/30) below a 420 SOL market cap, then 25 tiers down to 30 bps at ≥ 98,240 SOL [A3]. Note that the tier just above 420 SOL is (20 lp / 5 protocol / **95 creator**) = 1.20 %.
- **Boost (C1-13).** `migrate_v2` moves part of the migrated SOL into a boost vault and books the same amount as `virtual_quote_reserves`, so the price is unchanged at migration; pump's boost authority then spends the vault on zero-fee buy-and-burn (~40 calls, vault empty after ~6 min) [R-VEN §1.4]. On both pools I checked, **`VQR = ⌊migrated_sol × 2069/10⁴⌋` to the lamport** (32UVHz1W…: 17,584,505,333; 8DY32U41…: 17,584,505,348; mainnet slot 450,832,464–469) [A5], and the research note finds the same rule on **all 130 non-mayhem migrations** decoded between 2026-09-25 and 09-27 (115 SOL, 9 USDC, 6 custom-quote pools) [R-VEN §1.4]. The rule is inferred from data; the program source is closed (UNVERIFIED as a rule). ★**Corrected on pump's 2026-10-08 / 10-13 ELFs (pump-final G0, G0b):** the boost is `⌊X₁·Y₁/10¹⁵⌋` on the pool `migrate_v2` creates (`X₁` the live `curve_base_ata`), which equals `⌊migrated × 2069/10⁴⌋` exactly when `X₁` is the standard 206.9e12; a synthetic pool part or a token donation moves it (§3.1). Consequence (sim): VQR = 17,584,505,348 lamports (20.69% of migrated SOL); after the zero-fee buy-and-burn the price is 1.4566x the graduation price (constant-product: (1 + 0.2069)^2 = 1.4566), independent of the migrated amount.
- **Third-party liquidity on canonical pools (#104, #105; R-VEN Update H11).** The LP a canonical pool mints at creation is burned by `migrate_v2`, but the pool is not closed to other LPs: anyone can `deposit` into a canonical pool and `withdraw` again — on the dumped ELF with the boost vault still funded (13.98 SOL), on the mature and the cashback pool alike (`BoostPoolLiquidityUnsupported` was never raised) — and VQR scales with the deposit (a mainnet deposit moved e/acc's VQR by exactly `⌊VQR·base_in/base⌋`, which is the "VQR drift" above). 5 of 127 actively traded canonical WSOL pools carry third-party LP (e/acc 7.02 % of `lp_supply`). (v0.4.1 read R-VEN §1.4 as "canonical pools have 100 % burned LP and boost pools reject deposit/withdraw: LP drains are impossible on canonical PumpSwap pools"; that was false.) So the deposit/withdraw-wrapped vectors of §10.5 and §12.12 (M4F-1, FITS-2, FITX-1) and a deposit held to inflate sizing, the tier or `Λ` apply to PumpSwap as to Raydium — a withdrawal can drain only the third-party part, the burned creation LP stays. **The owner's decision of 2026-09-30 (M6R-1, #105; SPEC §14):** the Raydium protections apply. A canonical pool's locked depth is its burned LP's, `D = ⌊λ_bps·isqrt(X·(Y + VQR))/10⁴⌋` with `λ_bps = ⌊(Pool.lp_supply − lp_mint.supply)·10⁴/Pool.lp_supply⌋` (`lp_supply` counts every LP unit minted less every unit `withdraw` redeems, so `lp_supply − circulating supply` is LP that can never be redeemed: λ = 1 at creation, 92.98 % on e/acc); a deposit or withdrawal scales `X`, `Y + VQR` and `lp_supply` together and leaves `D` unchanged, as on Raydium. `D` caps the exit reference (§8.2; `D/λ_min` under #105, `D` itself since #106's M6E-1), `D` bounds `L_slow` itself through a graduation's warm-up (§5.2), and the liquidation spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` and the keeper covers liquidity anomalies on markets with positions (§5.5, §5.8), as on Raydium (§12.12 (12)). **Sizing is on the burned LP only (the owner's decision of 2026-09-30 on M6D-1, #106; SPEC §14):** the size cap and the tier take `D` itself (`λ_min` = `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 100 % on PumpSwap, §6.1, §6.2), so a third-party deposit, held for any time, adds nothing to a position's size, the market's caps or the tier — nor (M6E-1) to an exit's reference, capped at `D` too, so `L_b = L_live` on a canonical pool and a deposit pulled at a deadline holds no expiry up (§8.2, §12.12 (12) (f) (iv)). Under #105 `D/λ_min` let a deposit held for about an hour add up to 2× the burned depth's cap, and a position sized on it gapped into S1 when the LP left as a genuine pump started (M6D-1, §12.12 (12) (e)); a canonical pool's lock is its burned LP, so `λ_min` has no eligibility role there, and a pool with genuine third-party LP is sized at λ of its live depth (e/acc 0.93×).
- **Admin switches (#104, R-VEN Update H4, on the dumped ELF):** `disable_flags` bit 3 halts buys (every exit: no route, the S3 chain), bit 4 sells (every open), bit 0 `create_pool` — and with it pump's `migrate_v2`, so graduation stalls and the market takes the stuck-migration path (§10.3) — bits 1 / 2 `deposit` / `withdraw`, bits 5–7 nothing. **Since M6-SEC-3 (owner decision of 2026-09-30, #105)** bit 0 also refuses opens on every curve market (`GraduationHalted`): a position opened while it is set could not graduate; positions opened before it keep that exposure (a healthy one frozen until its deadline, then S3 at fair value, §10.3).
- **The 09-30 negative-VQR mechanism (#104, R-VEN Update H2; unreleased, not deployed on 2026-09-30):** pump's new v2 trades keep the protocol and creator fees in the pool, counted in two buckets; VQR = boost − buckets, negative on a never-boosted pool's first v2 trade, restored by a permissionless sweep; sells are capped at `vault − buckets`. Pricing stays `Y = vault + VQR`; the payable SOL becomes `vault − buckets`.

### 1.3 Raydium AMM v4 and CPMM (owner decision D2)

The economic model uses a generic constant-product pool with configurable fees on the input. Its structure matches the verified research note `docs/research/raydium-amm.md` [R-RAY], which reproduces 388 AMM v4 and 285 CPMM mainnet swaps to the raw unit (v0.2.1 alignment, #26):

- **CPMM** (`raydium-io/raydium-cp-swap@59fb845a`; deployed since slot 452,376,460 as `@bdeee23`, branch `feat/creator-fee-share`, whose swap path is the same — M10): the trade fee is charged on the **input** amount, `trade_fee = ⌈input·rate/10⁶⌉`; exact-out swaps gross the input up with `⌈post_fee·10⁶/(10⁶ − rate)⌉`; the curve rounds exact-out input up (`checked_ceil_div`) [R1]. Pricing reserves are the vault balances **minus accrued protocol, fund and creator fees** [R2]; the counters can be large (one pool: 792.6 SOL in the vault, 547.1 SOL of reserves) [R-RAY §2.4]. Fee rates come from the pool's `AmmConfig`: all 21 live configs have trade fees from 0.005 % to 4 % and carve protocol 12 % and fund 4 % out of the trade fee (these only move the excluded counters, not the trader's cost). The creator fee (0.05 %–1.495 % by config) applies only if the pool enabled it at creation (launchpads such as LaunchLab), on the input or the output side [R-RAY §2.5, §3.3]. Since the 2026-10 redeploy `AmmConfig.creator_fee_share_rate` (offset 116, carved from padding; 0 on all 21 configs at slot 452,488,420) lets Raydium keep `⌊fee·rate/10⁶⌋` of a pool's accrued creator fee when it is collected (§12.8); the trader's fee and the pricing reserves are unchanged. No virtual reserves.
- **AMM v4** (`raydium-io/raydium-amm@d26944bf`): the trader pays `swap_fee_numerator/denominator` of the input, read per pool (25/10⁴ on every sampled pool); the whole fee stays in the vault, and the protocol later removes 12/100 of the constant-product growth through `withdrawpnl`, which only moves `need_take_pnl`; pricing reserves are `vault − need_take_pnl`; exact-out input is grossed up with a ceiling division [R3; R-RAY §2.2–2.3]. No creator or fund fee; SPL-Token mints only.
- **LP is withdrawable** on both unless burned; the main pools of the D2 target coins have 92–99.8 % of their LP permanently burned [R-RAY §6.4]. Drain scenarios apply to the unlocked part. Since M4 no pool rule reads the locked share `λ` itself (#91): eligibility is a floor on the pool's locked SOL at `P_ema` (§6.2) and sizing is bounded by the locked depth over `λ_min` (§6.1), so a pool with `λ < 50 %` can be adopted, and a full drain still leaves at least `λ_min` of the depth any position opened on it was sized on (§10.5).
- Simulation defaults (examples, not facts about any pool): AMM v4 25 bps with 12 % of the fee leaving the reserves (a slight overstatement of the between-withdrawals state, in which the whole fee stays); CPMM 25 bps with 16 % leaving (protocol 12 % + fund 4 %, the value of every live config). The example pools have a permanently locked LP share λ = 50 % = `λ_min` (v0.4: the worst pool the market could adopt). An eligible pool may now have a lower λ; it is sized on the same `D/λ_min`, so its full drain lands at the same depth relative to sizing and the λ = 50 % rows remain the worst case up to the open's own price impact (CR after a full drain 2.01 at λ 50 %, 1.99 at 25 %, 1.98 at 10 %; §10.5); only the settlement box's `L` side sees the difference (§10.1, §10.5). The sim tracks LP units, so unlocked LP can be withdrawn only down to the locked part (drains beyond it are labelled model cases).

### 1.4 What the model takes from this

All venues are **x·y = k pools**. The fee rates are parameters φ read live from the venue's fee state, never cached. Pump venues add fees on the SOL leg; Raydium takes them from the input. For CR, S1/S2 and FV the protocol only needs `B̂(q; X, Y)`, an executable-cost quote on a (real or hypothetical) reserve state.


---

## 2. Units, fixed point, rounding, overflow (R3A-12, R3B-3, decision 8)

### 2.1 Numerics table (normative for `shortwin-math`)

Every quantity that must match the sims bit for bit. "Floor"/"ceil" are the rounding of the last division; `isqrt` is the floor integer square root and `isqrt⁺` the ceiling one. The sims implement exactly these forms.

| Quantity | Program type | Scale / unit | Formula and rounding |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Q, S, M, V, R, B̂, π, e, K_s, K_e, ert` | u64 | raw token units / lamports | as stated per formula; quotes and fees **up**, payouts and cuts **down** |
| `X, Y` pricing reserves | u64 (PumpSwap `Y = real + VQR`, VQR i128 in the pool account → u128 intermediate) | raw / lamports | read from venue state (ARCH §4.1) |
| `P` (spot, `P_ema`, `obs`, bucket `lo`/`hi`, `P_rs`, `P_ref`, `P_mlo`, `P_mhi`) | u128 | WAD (lamports per raw unit × 10¹⁸) | `P = ⌊Y·WAD/X⌋` |
| `L` (live, `L_ema`, `lobs`, bucket `llo`/`lhi`, `L_slow`, `L_mlo`, `L_mhi`) | u128 | √(raw·lamports) | `L = isqrt(X·Y)` (`X·Y < 2¹²⁸`, u128 square root) |
| `reprice(L, P)` | u128 → (u128, u128) | — | `sp = isqrt⁺(P)`; `X = ⌊L·10⁹/sp⌋`; `Y = ⌈L·sp/10⁹⌉` — so `Y/X ≥ P` and every reprojected quote is an upper bound (R3B-7 c) |
| EMA step | u128 | WAD / √ | `obs = clamp(P_ema, b_lo, b_hi)` then `clamp(obs, ⌊P_ema·10⁴/(10⁴ + C)⌋, ⌊P_ema·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)`; `P_ema ← ⌊(P_ema·τ + obs·dt)/(τ + dt)⌋`, `dt = min(now − ema_ts, dt_max)`; `L_ema`, `L_slow` likewise |
| `RingEntry` | `ts` i64; `p, l, x, y, lo, hi, llo, lhi` u128 | — | pushed at `integrate`; `(x, y) = reprice(l, p)` cached (decision 3); **v0.4.1 (R4B-2): `(lo, hi, llo, lhi)` = the integrated bucket's price and liquidity extremes, replacing the clamped `obs`, `lobs`** |
| ring medians `P_mlo`, `P_mhi`, `L_mlo`, `L_mhi` | u128 | — | `P_mlo` = **lower** median of the ring's 8 `lo` (the 4th smallest, index `⌊(n − 1)/2⌋` of the sorted values); `P_mhi` = **upper** median of the 8 `hi` (index `⌊n/2⌋`); `L_mlo`, `L_mhi` likewise over `llo`, `lhi` |
| `g` sustained growth | i128 intermediate → u32 | bps per second | per step `⌊(P_i − P_{i−1})·10⁴/(P_{i−1}·Δt_i)⌋`; `g = max(0, lower median (index ⌊(n−1)/2⌋) of the last n = kappa_growth_n step rates)` (AIR-22, #117; the minimum before) |
| `κ_eff` | u32 | bps | `min(kappa_liq_bps, kappa_base_bps + (τ + 1)·g)` |
| `P_ref` (liquidation) | u128 | WAD | `lo_x = ⌊lb_lo·(10⁴ + g·(now − lb_sec))/10⁴⌋`; `P_ref = min(max(P_ema, lo_x), ⌊P_ema·(10⁴ + κ_eff)/10⁴⌋, ⌊lo_x·(10⁴ + κ_eff)/10⁴⌋)` (last term v0.4.1) |
| `P_ref` (expiry) | u128 | WAD | `min(P_ema, min_i p_i, lb_lo)` |
| σ (S2 slippage) | u32 | bps | `⌊n·WAD·10⁴/(Q·P)⌋ − 10⁴`, `n` the pre-fee input of the quote and `P = ⌊Y·WAD/X⌋` of the same state |
| CR | u32 | bps | `⌊V_eff·10⁴/B̂⌋` |
| bound, `max_in` | u64 | lamports | `⌊B̂_ref·(10⁴ + δ)/10⁴⌋`; expiry only (v0.4.1): `⌊B̂_ref·(10⁴ + ε)/10⁴⌋`, `π_ref = ⌈expiry_fee_bps·B̂_ref/10⁴⌉` |
| φ one-way venue fee | u32 | **ppm** (pump bps × 100 exactly; AMM v4 `⌈num·10⁶/den⌉`; CPMM `t + c`) | caps `⌊cap_x100·φ·X/(100·10⁶)⌋`; fee gates compare `φ` with `cap_bps·100` (no rounding, R3A-3 iii) |
| `λ` locked LP share | u32 | bps | `⌊(lp_counter − lp_supply)·10⁴/lp_counter⌋` |
| `D` locked depth | u128 | √ | `⌊λ_bps·isqrt(X·Y)/10⁴⌋` |
| locked SOL, `R_eff` term | u64 | lamports | `⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋`; Raydium `⌊locked·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋`; PumpSwap (#105, #106) `max(locked − max(VQR + buckets, 0), 0)` (the buckets since #112) (`⌊·10⁴/PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS⌋` with `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10⁴) |
| eligibility floors (§6.2; M4) | — | lamports | `locked = ⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋`; locked-depth floor (listing, zero-position, list-only adoption) `pool_min_real_sol·λ_min_bps ≤ locked·10⁴`, i.e. `⌊locked·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol`; window floor (an adoption that carries open positions) `locked ≥ pool_min_real_sol`, at a proposal with `P_ref = min(P_ema, P_cand)` (M4R-2, #96), `P_cand = ⌊Y·WAD/X⌋` of the candidate's pre-trade state |
| adoption anchor (§5.2; M4R) | u128 | √ | `cap = ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋` into `L_ema` and every ring entry's `l`, `llo`, `lhi`; `L_slow ← min(L_slow, D)`; a Raydium price start `L = min(√(X·Y), cap)` (#96; M4 as built wrote `min(L_rec, L_live, cap)`, #92) and `L_slow = min(L, D)` (AIR-13, #117) |
| warm-up seed (§5.2; M4R) | u128 | as `P_ema`, `L_ema` | over the `n = k + 1 ≤ 8` post-change entries (this bucket and the `k = 8 − ema_warm` newest ring entries): `P_ema = max(min(min lo, ⌊(P_ema·τ + ⌊P_ema·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋·dt)/(τ + dt)⌋), ⌊(P_ema·τ + ⌊P_ema·10⁴/(10⁴ + C)⌋·dt)/(τ + dt)⌋)` (the largest v0.4.1 step up from the previous `P_ema`, M4X-1, `observe::price_up_bound`; and since M6-SEC-1, #105, the largest step down, `observe::price_down_bound`); `H` = their `lhi` ascending, `L_ema = min(H[n−1], ⌊H[⌊(n−1)/2⌋]·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)` (a `mul_div` that saturates at `u128::MAX`, then the `min`); `ema_warm`, `ema_guard` u8 |
| reference guard (§8.2; M4R) | — | — | clears iff `L_ema·10⁴ ≤ L_mlo·(10⁴ + REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS)` (exact integers, `REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS` = 100) at an integration with `ema_warm = 0` after it; while it is set the steady step feeds `L_slow` with `min(lobs, L_mlo)` (M4X-2), `L_mlo` over the ring after the push |
| conservative token reserve for caps | u64 | raw | curve (#103, M5-SEC-3): `vT` while `P_ema ≤ P_spot = ⌊vQ·WAD/vT⌋`, else `min(vT, x_ema)` with `x_ema = ⌊isqrt(vT·vQ)·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋`, and the Tier D guard's `rT_guard` = `rT`, resp. `min(rT, max(0, x_ema − (vT − rT)))`; AMMs `min(X_now, ⌊L_slow·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋)`; Raydium also `⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(λ_min_bps·isqrt⁺(P_ema))⌋`; PumpSwap (#105; `λ_min` = `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10⁴ since #106) also `⌊D·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋` |
| `K_s`, `K_e` | u64 | lamports | `min(fixed + ⌊V·bps/10⁴⌋, cap, V)`; `min(fixed + ⌊B̂_ref·bps/10⁴⌋, cap, remainder)` |
| penalty split | u64 | lamports | keeper `⌊π·3,000/10⁴⌋`, protocol `⌊π·2,000/10⁴⌋`, lenders the rest |
| FV | u64 | lamports | `B̂_FV + ⌈p'·B̂_FV/10⁴⌉ + R_due`, `B̂_FV` a risk quote on `reprice(L, P)`; `p'` = locked penalty, or the expiry fee if expired and `⌊V_eff·10⁴/B̂_FV⌋ ≥ L` **on the same `(P, L)`** (v0.4.1) |
| settlement immediate part and lump (S1, S2, S3) | u64 | lamports | `immediate = min(x, ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due)`, `P′ = P_ema`, or 0 when the venue cannot execute; `lump = x − immediate` (vests; v0.4.1 for S2/S3; S1 since AIR-21 and `P′ = 0` since AIR-09, #117) |
| box amount check | u64 | lamports | settle iff `(x_hi − x_lo)·10⁴ ≤ fv_dev_bps·x_lo` (exact integers) |
| `U` | u32 | bps | `⌈B_Q·10⁴/(T + B_Q)⌉` |
| rent basis `J0, J1, J2` | u128 | WAD·s | `J0 += P̃·Δt`; `J1 += ⌈P̃·min(U, k)·Δt/10⁴⌉`; `J2 += ⌈P̃·max(U − k, 0)·Δt/10⁴⌉`, `P̃ = min(P_ema, P_ACC_MAX)` before this instruction's roll |
| `rent_accrued`, `R_min` | u64 (U256 intermediate) | lamports | `⌈Q·(b·ΔJ0 + s1·ΔJ1 + s2·ΔJ2)/(10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌉`; `⌈Q·P_ema·(b·10⁴ + s1·min(U, k) + s2·max(U − k, 0))·T_min/(10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌉` |
| lender rent credit | u64 (U256 intermediate) | lamports | `⌊(Σ_j A_j·δJ_j + rem)/(10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌋`, `rem` carried in `Market.lender_credit_rem` (AIR-03, #117), `A_j = Σ_open Q·coef_j·(10⁴ − p_pos)`; true-up `credited_for = ⌈Q·Σ_j coef_j·ΔJ_j·(10⁴ − p_pos)/(10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌉` |
| shares | u128 (U256 intermediate) | shares | mint `⌊a·(S + VS)/(T + B + VA)⌋`; burn value `⌊s·(T + B + VA)/(S + VS)⌋` |
| `acc`, `acc_r` | i128 / u128 | WAD lamports per share | `Δ = ⌊(x·WAD + carry)/S⌋`, remainder kept in `carry` |
| `pending`, `real` | i64 / u64 (U256 intermediate) | lamports | `+= ⌊shares·Δacc/WAD⌋` (floor toward −∞ for signed) |
| locks | u128 (U256 intermediate) | shares | own lock `⌈lock·Π/lock_Π⌉`; term `⌊lock·WAD/Π⌋` into the day bucket of `lock_until` (v0.4.1: floor); `locked = min(⌊Σ_live buckets·Π/WAD⌋, S)`; `Π ← ⌈Π·B'/b_min⌉` |
| `lock_until` | i64 | s | `⌈(max(now, max_release) + lock_grace_s)/86,400⌉·86,400` (v0.4.1) |
| vest release | u64 | lamports | `⌊vest_bal·(now − vest_last)/(vest_end − vest_last)⌋`, all of it once `now ≥ vest_end`; a lump `x` sets `vest_end = now + ⌊(vest_bal·(vest_end − now) + x·lump_vest_s)/(vest_bal + x)⌋` after the release (v0.4.1; rounded down since AIR-02, #117) |

**Order of operations inside one instruction** (ARCH §3.0): (1) rent accrues on `P_ema` **before** this instruction's roll; (2) the completed bucket is integrated (the roll); (3) this instruction's pre-trade observation is recorded; (4) the body reads the **post-roll** EMA state — including the D4 margin's EMA leg, which uses the newest ring entry's cached `(x, y)` (R3A-12).

### 2.2 Overflow: where 256-bit intermediates are needed (R3B-3; corrects v0.3's table)

Bounds used: `Q, X ≤ 2⁶⁴ ≈ 1.8·10¹⁹`; `Y ≤` total SOL supply `≈ 6·10¹⁷` lamports; `P ≤ Y·WAD ≈ 6·10³⁵`; `L ≤ 3.3·10¹⁸`; `S ≤ R_MAX·(T + B + VA) ≤ 1.8·10²⁵`; `τ = 5` (a constant), `dt ≤ 2` (compiled bound, §15).

| Product | Worst case | Type |
|---|---|---|
| `Q·Y` in every quote and sale (incl. σ's pre-fee input `n`) | `1.1·10³⁷ < 2¹²⁸ = 3.4·10³⁸` | **u128** |
| `L·10⁹`, `L·isqrt⁺(P)` in `reprice` | `3.3·10²⁷`, `2.5·10³⁶` | **u128** |
| `X·Y` under `isqrt` | `1.1·10³⁷` | **u128** (no U256 square root anywhere) |
| fees `n·φ` (ppm), CR `V·10⁴`, caps `X·φ·cap_x100` | `< 10³⁰` | **u128** |
| EMA `P_ema·(τ + dt)`, `L_slow·(τ_slow + dts)` | `4.2·10³⁶`, `3·10²³` | **u128** |
| FV, bounds, box check, waterfalls | u64 sums | **u64/u128** |
| rent basis increments `P̃·Δt` and the cumulative `J_i` | `J_i ≤ P_ACC_MAX·3.2·10⁹ s` (100 years) | **u128** with `P_ACC_MAX = 10²⁹` (a price of 100 SOL per raw unit); opens are refused while `P_ema > P_OPEN_MAX = 10²⁷` |
| `Q·coef·ΔJ`, `A_j·δJ_j`, `Q·P_ema·r·T_min` | `> 10⁴⁰` | **U256** |
| shares and accumulators: `a·(S + VS)`, `s·(T + B + VA)`, `shares·Δacc`, `lock·Π` | up to `3·10⁴⁴` | **U256** |
| σ denominator `Q·P`, κ and g products `P·(10⁴ + κ)`, `ΔP·10⁴` | overflow only above `P ≈ 2·10³⁴` | **u128 fast path, U256 fallback** (`mul_div` checks the product) |
| σ numerator `n·WAD·10⁴` | exceeds `2¹²⁸` once `n > 3.4·10¹⁶` lamports, inside the bound `Y ≤ 6·10¹⁷` | **u128 fast path, U256 fallback** |
| impact caps (§6.1) `X²·(10⁴ + ι)` and `X²·10⁴` | exceed `2¹²⁸` once `X > ≈ 1.8·10¹⁷`; the radicand `⌊X²·(10⁴ + ι)/10⁴⌋` itself once `X > 2⁶⁴/√(1 + ι)` | **u128 fast path, U256 product and quotient**; the root stays u128: `⌊√q⌋` from `⌊√⌊q/4ᵏ⌋⌋` (the smallest `k` that fits) plus its `k` low bits, checked against `s² ≤ q` (no U256 square root) |
| tier threshold (§6.2) `Λ·price·10^(expo+6)` against `θ_usd_e6·10⁹` | `θ_usd_e6·10⁹ < 2⁹⁴`; the left side `< 10³¹` for any realistic pool (`Λ ≤ 10¹⁴` lamports, a Pyth price `≤ 10¹³`, `\|expo + 6\| ≤ 4`) | **u128 fast path, U256 fallback** (a comparison, no division) |

Implementation rule for the math crate: every `mul_div` takes the u128 path when `a.checked_mul(b)` succeeds and falls back to a limb-based 256/128 division (never bit-by-bit) otherwise; ARCH §8.1 sets per-primitive CU budgets as an M0 gate. With the cached reprojection (§2.3) a liquidation evaluates at most `liq_persist_n` + 3 quotes per position instead of up to 18 reprojection-plus-quote pairs.

### 2.3 `reprice` and the cached ring reprojection

`reprice(L, P)` is the constant-product state with liquidity `L` at price `P` (§2.1). On the curve `√(vT·vQ)` is constant, so `reprice(L_curve, P)` is the curve itself at `P`; its real token reserve follows from the constant offset `vT − rT`. Every ring entry stores `(x, y) = reprice(l, p)` computed once at `integrate`; the EMA leg, the settlement box corners that coincide with ring states, and the D4 margin read the cached pair. The cache depends only on the market, never on a position (decision 3).

### 2.4 Position record (locked at open)

Extends SPEC §10 (ADOPTED Q9, C0-8, V1-5): `Q, S, M, V, N_open, fee_open, J_open[3], R_min, t_open, deadline, entry_price`; locked tier terms `L, penalty, σ_max, expiry_fee, rate curve (base, slope1, slope2)`; **`rent_protocol_bps`** (v0.4, R3A-5/R3B-9); settlement and keeper terms (`K_s` fixed/bps/cap, `K_e` fixed/bps/cap, penalty split); exit-bound and persistence terms (**`δ_liq`**, `δ_exp`, `κ_max`, `κ_base`, `kappa_growth_n`, `liq_persist_n`, `settle_persist_n`, arm gap); execution and settlement terms (`ε` — the expiry bound's tolerance since v0.4.1 —, effective `ert` — 0 on Raydium —, `ref_max_age_s`, `fv_dev_bps`, `venue_fee_hard_cap_bps`, `s3_wait_s`, `grad_grace_s`, `max_migration_pause_s`; the last three also raise the market's `max_release = deadline + their sum`, which bounds the lenders' locks, §9.3); settlement arming state `(s_armed, s_count, s_since, s_last)`. Removed in v0.4: `delta_liq_min/slope/max`, `liq_persist_s`, `settle_persist_s`, `fv_liq`, `settle_s2_first`. τ is a compile-time constant since v0.4 (§5.7); `dt_max`, the clamp and the slow-liquidity EMA parameters are market-level (params-version rule; forced adoption after `ema_force_delay_s` — their compiled ranges keep a forced adoption inside the simulated-safe region, R3B-6). `util_kink_bps` carries its own version and is adopted only with no open position; a forced EMA adoption does not advance it (R3A-9).

---

## 3. Pricing primitives

### 3.1 Executable buyback cost `B̂(q)` — closed forms

**Fees on the SOL leg** (pump curve, PumpSwap; `ε₁ = 1` on the curve, 0 on PumpSwap):

```
n(q)  = ⌈q·Y/(X − q)⌉ + ε₁
B̂(q) = n + Σ_i ⌈n·φ_i/10⁴⌉                       φ_i: lp, protocol, creator (creator only if set)
```

The curve charges `⌊·⌋ + 1`, so `B̂` exceeds the charge by at most one lamport per fee component (asserted in the sim: charge ∈ [B̂ − 5, B̂]). ★pump's v3 buys charge the same: `⌈·⌉` off an exact division and `⌊·⌋ + 1` on one (every one of the 400 exact divisions constructed in the re-pin's gate G0 took `⌊·⌋ + 1`, v2's charge; `tests/fixtures/README.md`).

**★Synthetic quote `B̂_v3`** (since short.win 1.0.1; pump-final §5.2; owner D3) for a live curve with `q > rt`, what one `buy_v3` charges by pump's synthetic migration:

```
φ_s     = the curve tier at its pre-trade market cap on the mint's LIVE supply (both parts; every other curve fee
          keeps the fixed 10¹⁵, known issues KI-77)
n₁      = ⌈rt·vs/(vt − rt)⌉ + 1                       leg 1: the curve's rt, ε₁ = 1, fees φ_s
(X_s, Y_s) = (curve_base_ata − rt, rs + n₁ − fee_m)   the would-be pool (live leg; 206.9e12 tokens on EMA states)
n₂      = ⌈Y_s·q₂/(X_s − q₂)⌉ + 1, q₂ = q − rt         leg 2, ε₁ = 1, fees φ_s;  B̂ = +∞ unless q₂ < X_s
B̂_v3    = n₁ + Σ⌈n₁·φ_i/10⁴⌉ + n₂ + Σ⌈n₂·φ_i/10⁴⌉
```

pump charges the pool part `⌈Y_sᵖ·q₂/(X_s − q₂)⌉` (exact division too) on `Y_sᵖ = rs + n₁ᵖ − fee_m`, so `0 ≤ B̂_v3 − charge ≤ (1 + Σ⌈φ_i/10⁴⌉) + (Δ₂ + Σ⌈Δ₂·φ_i/10⁴⌉)` with `Δ₂ = 1 + ⌈q₂/(X_s − q₂)⌉`: ≤ 7 lamports for `q₂ ≤ X_s/2`, which the per-market cap keeps every position at (`q ≤ ≈ 4.99e13` against `X_s ≥ 2.069e14`); +1 … +4 measured. `B̂_v3` is continuous at `rt` and 0.8–96 % below `B̂_str` for straddle-size positions (≤ +2.4 bps in the dip region). The pool part completes the curve and pump writes `pcB = q₂`, `pcQ = n₂ᵖ` (`rs` excludes `pcQ`); `migrate_v2` then seeds `(curve_base_ata, rs + pcQ − fee_m)`.

**★The migration boost (corrected, P0/P1 of pump-final, measured on pump's 2026-10-08 and 10-13 ELFs):** `VQR = ⌊X₁·Y₁/10¹⁵⌋` on the pool `migrate_v2` creates, `X₁` the live `curve_base_ata` and `Y₁ = rs + pcQ − fee_m`. With the standard `X₁ = 206.9e12` that is §1.2's `⌊migrated × 2069/10⁴⌋`; a synthetic pool part (`X₁ < 206.9e12`) or a token donation (`X₁` larger) moves it — the design's `⌊(Y − pcQ)·2069/10⁴⌋` held only without a donation (a 3e12 donation after the completion: 17,852,426,396 against 17,584,506,040). The pool's effective reserve stays `Y₁` (vault + VQR), so the boost moves no price; only the later buy-and-burn does (§10.1).

**Fees on the input** (Raydium AMM v4 / CPMM; rate `f` in ppm incl. an input-side creator fee):

```
n(q)  = ⌈q·Y/(X − q)⌉
B̂(q) = ⌈n·10⁶/(10⁶ − f)⌉
```

`B̂ = +∞` (no quote) if `q ≥ X`, the curve is complete, or the pool cannot execute. **Straddle quote** for a live curve with `q > rt`: `B̂_str(q) = B̂_pool(q)` on the deterministic post-migration pool `(206.9 M, rs + n_curve(rt) − fee)` — the executed ≥ 3-transaction bundle path (V1-9). ★Since 1.0.1 `B̂_str` prices only a **migrating** market's states, on the pool `migrate_v2` will create (`+ pcQ`, and the live `curve_base_ata` after a synthetic pool part, §5.3); a live curve past `rt` prices `B̂_v3` (above). Exact per-venue forms are normative in ARCH §4.3.

**Two fee views.** *Execution quotes* (`B̂_live` in close affordability, "normal buyback allowed" and the `B̂_live ≤ max_in` check) use the live fee. *Risk quotes* — every CR, the S1/S2 legs, `B̂_ref`, refutation, the penalty base and FV — take `min(φ_live, hard cap)`, the one-way fee capped at the position's locked `venue_fee_hard_cap_bps` (for pump venues the capped fee is charged as one combined component), and on PumpSwap also at most the tier at `P_ema`'s market cap (AIR-10, #117: the pump-fees tier rises as the market cap falls, so a sale earlier in the same transaction raised every risk quote of `[sell, liquidate]` and liquidated a position the honest state holds healthy; the tier at `P_ema` is resolved on the live mint supply, so a same-transaction burn still raises it — a residual the owner accepted on 2026-10-12, known issues). **Hypothetical states use the live fee tier** (R3B-7 b): a PumpSwap quote on `reprice(L_i, P_i)` uses the tier of the live market cap, not of the hypothetical one (the sims do the same).

Sale proceeds at open: pump venues `g = ⌊q·Y/(X+q)⌋`, `S = g − Σ⌈g·φ_i/10⁴⌉`; Raydium `q_n = q − ⌈q·f/10⁶⌉`, `S = ⌊Y·q_n/(X + q_n)⌋`.

### 3.2 Round-trip retention and the curve round trip

```
ρ := S/B̂_open ≈ (1 − φ_sell)/(1 + φ_buy)   (pump venues)      ρ ≈ (1 − f)²   (Raydium)
```

ρ = 0.9753 (curve, 125 bps), 0.9821 (PumpSwap 90 bps), 0.9950 (Raydium 25 bps). Measured by the sim on the curve state of R-PUMP §4.2:

- sell 10,000,000,000,000 raw then buy it back: proceeds 462,410,820, buyback 474,117,427 lamports -> round-trip cost 2.53% of proceeds (fees only; impact cancels). Buying Q on the pre-sale state costs 485,764,634 -> 5.05% (the same-state bid/ask spread for size Q).

The first-pass research note's "5.05 % round trip" priced both legs on the pre-trade state; it is the same-state spread, not the round-trip cost (C1-6).

### 3.3 Notional, CR and the execution venue

| Name | Definition | Used for |
|---|---|---|
| `N_open` | `B̂_spot(Q)` on post-open reserves | open fee, reporting |
| `N_mark(t)` | `Q·P_ema/WAD` | UI |
| `B̂_live` | `B̂(Q)` on the live reserves of the canonical venue | CR spot leg, S1, execution |

`R_due = max(rent_accrued, R_min)`, `V_eff = V − R_due`, `CR = V_eff·10⁴/B̂`.

Execution (D1, D2): the canonical constant-product pool is traded directly (PumpSwap for pump graduates, Raydium AMM v4/CPMM otherwise). Jupiter (v1.1) may only be used if it delivers `Q` for no more than the on-chain bound of §8.2; any saving goes to the borrower (ADOPTED Q20).

**Liquidation price** (UI and keepers only; constant product with invariant K, fee φ, target `b = V_eff/L`): `s = (−bQ + √(b²Q² + 4(1+φ)·K·Q·b))/(2(1+φ)·√K·Q)`, `P_liq = s²`. With `b = V − K_s` it gives the S1 price.

---

## 4. Opening: margin (D4), fee, opening CR

Procedure (atomic): prologue (rent accrual, roll, observation) → caps (§6) → CPI sell `Q` with the borrower's `min_proceeds` → reload → quotes → margin and fee.

```
B̂_spot  = B̂(Q; live post-open reserves)
B̂_ema   = B̂(Q; x, y of the newest ring entry)          # EMA reserves after this instruction's roll (R3A-12); D4 literal
M        = ⌈CR_open,T · max(B̂_spot, B̂_ema) / 10⁴⌉ − S   # OWNER D4
                                                        # B̂_ema undefined (e.g. Q ≥ x): B̂_spot alone (sim Market.open)
fee_open = ⌈25·B̂_spot/10⁴⌉                              # SPEC 0.25 % of notional, on top of M, to protocol
V = S + M,  R_min per §2.1
```

Open guards (PROPOSED): refuse opens while the market is younger than `market_warmup_s` (50 s), `|P_spot/P_ema − 1| > 10 %`, `P_ema > P_OPEN_MAX` (§2.2), during the post-graduation boost window (ADOPTED Q15), after a venue program upgrade until re-validated (ADOPTED Q13), below `min_notional` 0.1 SOL (ADOPTED Q11), above the per-market beta cap 25 SOL of open notional (ADOPTED Q21), and while the market's opens are paused (including keeper degraded mode, §5.8).

The literal D4 EMA leg costs `Q` at the pre-sale EMA price and so charges the borrower a second time for its own impact at the size cap. The shifted reading (owner option O2) is **not built**; its margin is shown for the owner only (analysis column).

## 5. Price observation, EMA, persistence and settlement (SPEC §5.1, §5.3, §9)

### 5.1 Observation buckets — PROPOSED (unchanged)

Every instruction that carries the market's venue accounts (open, close, liquidate, expire, sol_settle, crank_price, propose_pool, execute_pool_switch) runs a prologue **before any CPI trade**. `deposit` and `withdraw` roll a completed bucket but record no observation; `claim_sol`, `cover_deficit`, `sync_lender` and `add_margin` do not touch market state.

```
observe(now):
    if b_sec is set and now > b_sec:  integrate(now)            # roll the completed second into the EMA
    update the S3 chain (s3_no_route) and, on Raydium, the fast-path chain (pool_fail)   (§10.3)
    if the venue is executable (or a migrating curve, whose final state is still recorded):
        add (P_spot, √(X·Y)) of the PRE-TRADE state to the bucket of second `now`
```

Every instruction of the second records (there is no one-per-slot limit). A venue that cannot execute records nothing (V2-5); a fee above the hard cap does not stop observation, because the pool still trades.

### 5.2 EMA integration — PROPOSED

```
integrate(now):                                           # at most once per second
    if ema_warm > 0: integrate_warmup(now); return        # M4R (PROPOSED, #96): the 8 integrations after a restart
    obs  = clamp(P_ema, b_lo, b_hi), then clamp to [P_ema/(1 + C), P_ema·(1 + C)]      # C = 100 %
    lobs = same for liquidity (L_ema, b_llo, b_lhi)
    dt   = min(now − ema_ts, dt_max)                      # dt_max = 2 s
    P_ema = (P_ema·τ + obs·dt)/(τ + dt);  L_ema likewise  # τ = 5 s
    ring.push(now, P_ema, L_ema, x, y = reprice(L_ema, P_ema), b_lo, b_hi, b_llo, b_lhi)  # v0.4.1: bucket extremes
    sin  = min(lobs, L_mlo) if ema_guard = 1 else lobs    # M4X-2: L_mlo = lower median of llo after the push
    L_slow = (L_slow·3,600 + sin·dts)/(3,600 + dts), dts = min(now − slow_ts, 60)
    guard_check()
    lb_lo, lb_hi, lb_sec = bucket; clear the bucket

integrate_warmup(now):                                    # M4R post-change confirmation (PROPOSED, #96)
    k = 8 − ema_warm                                      # post-change entries already in the ring (0..7)
    E = this bucket and the k newest ring entries         # n = k + 1 ≤ 8; never an entry from before the restart
    dt = min(now − ema_ts, dt_max)
    P_ema = max(min(min over E of lo,                     # fresh, on the side no push can force an exit from,
                    (P_ema·τ + P_ema·(1 + C)·dt)/(τ + dt)),   # rising by at most the largest v0.4.1 step (M4X-1)
                (P_ema·τ + P_ema/(1 + C)·dt)/(τ + dt))    # and falling by at most one (M6-SEC-1, #105)
    H = the lhi of E, ascending
    L_ema = min(H[n−1], ⌊H[⌊(n−1)/2⌋]·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)     # the highest bucket high, bounded by (1 + C) × the lower median
    ring.push(now, P_ema, L_ema, x, y = reprice(L_ema, P_ema), b_lo, b_hi, b_llo, b_lhi)
    ema_ts = now; ema_count += 1; ema_warm −= 1           # no blend; L_slow and slow_ts unchanged
    guard_check()
    lb_lo, lb_hi, lb_sec = bucket; clear the bucket

guard_check():                                            # M4R reference guard (§8.2)
    if ema_guard = 1 and ema_warm = 0 and L_ema·10⁴ ≤ L_mlo·(10⁴ + 100):  ema_guard = 0     # L_mlo = lower median of llo
```

- **One-second lag.** The EMA an instruction uses never contains that instruction's own observation, so `[pump → liquidate → dump]` cannot feed its own trigger.
- **Directional aggregation.** The EMA moves up only if every observation of a second was above it, down only if every one was below it. Self-test: single integration after an atomic 3x bundle alone in its second: EMA +16.67% (bound +16.7% at dt=1, +28.6% at dt=2, tau=5s); with an honest observation in the same second: +0.00%
- **Step bounds** (τ = 5 s, C = 100 %): +16.7 % / −8.3 % per 1 s step, +28.6 % / −14.3 % at `dt = 2 s`.
- **The freeze cuts both ways** (V1-2): one contrary observation per second freezes the EMA or fails the robust-spot leg; §12.9 prices this veto.
- **Initialisation and venue changes** (v0.4, R3B-7 a, R3A-11 d; Raydium: the post-change confirmation, PROPOSED, M4R, #96; the graduation as built, M6, #104, #105). At creation `P_ema = P_spot`, `L_ema = L_slow = √(X·Y)`. **At graduation** (M6 as built; kept and accepted by the owner on 2026-09-30, OWNER-M6R-2 / -4, #105; ARCH §2.5) the prologue of the first instruction that reads the valid canonical pool, after the roll of the curve's last bucket, rewrites `L_ema` and every ring entry to the pool's `lp_supply` (its `isqrt(X·(Y + VQR))` at creation; no sample of the pool is read), sets `L_slow = min(L_slow, lp_supply)`, clears both route chains and restarts the observation state exactly as a Raydium adoption does (`ema_warm = 8`, `ema_guard = 1`; `P_ema` carries over); that instruction records nothing. A PumpSwap market's first observation after a listing or re-detection starts its price like a Raydium price start (`L = min(√(X·(Y + VQR)), ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)` at PumpSwap's `λ_min` = 100 %, i.e. `D` — #106, M6E-1; the first warm-up integration re-seeds `L_ema` from the pool's own bucket — marked). **During a PumpSwap warm-up every executable observation caps `L_slow` at the pool's locked depth `D`** (M6R-1, #105; §1.2): the Raydium adoption's `L_slow = min(L_slow, D)`, taken at the first instruction that reads the LP mint, because a deposit between `migrate_v2` and the transition raises `lp_supply` (as built `L_slow` then kept the curve's value, ≈ 1.35× the pool's depth, for about an hour; `attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` part 2). At every Raydium adoption (listing-window replacement, zero-position adoption, fast path, a list-only market's window) the adoption **reads no sample of the new pool**. With `D` its locked depth and `cap = ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋` the most depth sizing ever credits it with (§6.1):

  ```
  L_ema = cap;  every ring entry's l, llo, lhi = cap, (x, y) recomputed;  the open bucket cleared   (the anchor)
  L_slow = min(L_slow, D)                  never a sample, never raised
  P_ema and the ring prices carry over     (stale after a halt: the warm-up replaces them)
  ema_warm = EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS (8), ema_guard = 1                                                 (mark_restart)
  ```

  A **Raydium price start** — a Raydium listing, or a list-only market's first adoption, which starts the pool's price state as a creation does — writes `P_ema = P_spot`, `L_ema = min(√(X·Y), cap)`, `L_slow = min(L_ema, D)` (AIR-13, #117: as an adoption leaves it; `L_slow = L_ema` let a deposit wrapped around the start seed `L_slow`, which the warm-up does not re-seed, at up to `D/λ_min`, 2D at the default) and is marked the same way. The next 8 integrations re-seed `P_ema` and `L_ema` from the new pool's own post-change buckets (`integrate_warmup` above). While `ema_warm > 0` on an executable venue no keeper exit runs and no Raydium or PumpSwap open (§5.5, §5.6, §6.2); while `ema_guard = 1` the exit reference reads `min(L_ema, L_mlo)` (§8.2). A market that never changes venue keeps `ema_warm = ema_guard = 0` and runs exactly the v0.4.1 integration.

  *Why.* v0.4.1 set both liquidity EMAs to `min(√(X·Y)_new, current)` from the adopting instruction's own sample: an unlocked LP of the candidate could withdraw around it and write only `D` into the ring (M4-SEC-5), and a deeper candidate kept the old, thinner `L_ema` (box wide 18 s, M4-SEC-6). M4 as built wrote `min(L_rec, L_live, cap)`, the smaller of two samples taken in two transactions (#92): a deposit could no longer push it up, but a withdrawal around either sample still wrote `D`. The M4 re-verification (M4R-1) showed that this is not a delay: after the adoption the unlocked LP holds `L_ema` at `D` with one withdraw-wrapped crank per second — directional aggregation freezes an EMA that has one observation on its own side every second — and a position within ≈ `q/x` of `cr_liq`, healthy on the pool as it trades, becomes liquidatable on the EMA leg and on a withdraw-wrapped spot leg (the LiteSVM reproducer: liquidated 24–26 s after the adoption, never without the wrap; §12.12). The carried `P_ema`, frozen while the incumbent could not execute, added a second forced path: after a mild dip during the halt the stale-high `P_ema` let the candidate's LP holder liquidate a healthy position 2 s after an honest adoption (§12.12). The anchor reads nothing any party can wrap, and the warm-up seed reads only bucket **highs** for `L` and bucket **lows** for `P`, the sides on which a wrapped sample cannot force an exit. A bucket low is a wrapped sample too when nobody else observed that second: the M4R verification (M4X-1) found that 8 attacker-only warm-up seconds of `[buy, crank_price, sell]` seeded `P_ema` at the pump at once, and a `[buy, liquidate, sell]` at the gate then liquidated a position at any threshold while the pool traded flat (§12.12 (8)). The price seed therefore also rises by at most the largest step the v0.4.1 integration can take, `×(τ + dt·(1 + C))/(τ + dt)` (9/7 at `dt = 2`, 7/6 at `dt = 1`): attacker-only warm-up seconds reach no higher than the same seconds in steady state (where a pump of `(1 + C)×` in each gives exactly that step), and a genuine move during the halt is still tracked by the gate (2.38× at the 5th integration). **Since M6-SEC-1 (owner decision of 2026-09-30, #105) it also falls by at most the largest step down, `×(τ + dt/(1 + C))/(τ + dt)`** (6/7 at `dt = 2`, 11/12 at `dt = 1`; `observe::price_down_bound`, the mirror of `price_up_bound`: the steady-state EMA falls no faster either). The seed was the lowest post-change bucket low, so one low recorded in any warm-up second — a `[sell, crank_price, buy]` next to the keeper's crank sets that bucket's low, which the keeper's own observation cannot lift — set `P_ema` for the rest of the warm-up, and the EMA leg then climbed back by at most one step a second: after a graduation, where the carried positions come onto a fresh pool at their full size, that delayed keeper exits by ≈ `ln(P_pool/P_low)/ln(7/6)` s (the M6 security review). Now the low stays in `E` but pulls the seed by at most one step per remaining integration, ≤ ×0.4986 over the 8, from the **running** seed: a genuine dump is tracked at the steady-state rate (to 0.50× by the gate, the rest after it), and a seed that lags a fall forces no exit — the spot leg reads the live price (§5.5), opens are refused by the spot/EMA check while it lags (§4), and the lagging-high `P_ema` only makes caps smaller and rent a little dearer, as in steady state. **Residual:** after a graduation whose completing buy jumped the price, the carried `P_ema` itself lies below the pool (ECON's D@80 %: 0.555× at the transition) and climbs at ≤ ×7/6 per integration, so a low recorded in the first one or two warm-up seconds is within the step bound of the still-low seed and is reached as before (§12.12 (12): a 0.25× low in the 1st second, the D@80 % exit +28 s instead of +12 s, as built +28 s; in the 4th / 8th second +22 / +13 s instead of +28 s, +22 / +14 s before AIR-22, #117; the lenders whole in every row). Its properties (proptested; ARCH §8.1):

  - **(P1)** If any warm-up bucket holds an honest observation `(p*, H)`, then `P_ema ≤ max(p*, the down-step chain from the carried P_ema)` — `P_ema ≤ p*` once the seed has had the integrations to fall to it (since M6-SEC-1; before, `P_ema ≤ p*` at once). A withdraw-wrapped sample in a bucket that also holds an honest observation lowers only that bucket's `llo`, which the seed never reads.
  - **(P2)** If at least one warm-up bucket is honest, `L_ema ≥ min(H_honest, (1 + C)·D)`, because every sample's `√(X·Y) ≥ D`: for `λ ≥ 1/(1 + C)` (50 % at the defaults) `L_ema ≥ H_honest` unless every warm-up bucket is attacker-only.
  - **(P3)** If at most 4 of the 8 buckets carry a sample above `H_max`, `L_ema ≤ ⌊H_max·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋`.
  - **(P4)** Constant honest buckets give exactly `L_ema = H` and `P_ema = min(max(p*, the down-step chain), the up-step chain)` from the carried `P_ema` — `p*` whenever `p*` lies between (11/12)⁸ ≈ 0.4986× and (7/6)⁸ ≈ 3.4× the carried `P_ema` (at `dt = 1`).
  - **(P5)** No warm-up integration raises `P_ema` by more than the largest v0.4.1 step from the previous one (M4X-1), nor — since M6-SEC-1 — lowers it by more than the largest step down, whatever the samples.

  While the reference guard is set (§8.2), `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)` instead of `lobs` (M4X-2): the guard caps only the `L_ema` term of the reference, and under the capital deposit freeze of §12.12 (4) the frozen, pushed `L_ema` would otherwise climb into `L_slow` — `+1/τ_slow` of the gap per second, 1.4–2.6× the pool's depth after 10–30 min — which the reference reads unguarded (a later genuine pump then ended in S1 with a lender loss instead of a liquidation). With the guard clear, and on every market that never switched, the step is v0.4.1's. The guard's clear test and this input both read `L_mlo`, so they are only as honest as the buckets' lows. A deposit cannot lower a bucket's low below an honest observation in the same second, but it sets the whole low of a second nobody else observed, and `L_mlo` is the attacker's once 5 of the 8 newest entries are such seconds. The guard therefore holds only while the keeper observes every second, which duty 9 requires for as long as `ema_guard = 1` on a market with positions (§5.8; M4F-1, #98: with the 30-s idle cadence after the gate the guard cleared at ≈ +15 s under a deposit freeze and the reference blocked, §12.12 (4)). Once the guard has cleared, a deposit push can reach the reference again, as it can on a market that never switched; since #99 every carried position fits the new pool's opening caps (§6.1), so the push only delays its exit, as it delays one opened on the pool (§12.12 (11): 0–1 s).

  An honest warm-up therefore ends with `L_ema` equal to the pool's depth for every λ (1.000 at the gate for λ 10–99.78 %, the box narrow from then on; §10.1 (b), (c)) and `P_ema` equal to its price, and the ring holds only post-change buckets. The rewrite to the anchor still runs, and the ring's `L` is read during the warm-up only where nothing is gated: on a venue that cannot execute (a new pool that halts within the warm-up; S3 then reads the partly anchored ring, §12.12). Without any rewrite the ring medians would still carry the old venue's liquidity, which left the settlement box more than 1 % wide in `L` for 3 s (v0.4.1's bucket-extremes box: 4 s) after a graduation (§10.1, `attacks/venue_switch.py`).

### 5.3 EMA reprojection

The EMA state prices the venue **at the EMA price with EMA liquidity**, `B̂_ema(Q) = B̂(Q; x, y)` of the newest ring entry (§2.3). On the curve this is the curve itself at `P_ema` (with `B̂_str` if `Q > rt`; ★since 1.0.1 `B̂_v3` on a live curve, at the live supply's fee and the standard 206.9e12 would-be pool, and `B̂_str` only on a migrating market's states, on the pool `migrate_v2` will create — `+ pcQ`, and the live `curve_base_ata` after a synthetic pool part, the one place a token donation counts; §3.1, §8.6). `L_slow` feeds tiering (§6.2), the caps' conservative token reserve (§6.1) and the exit references (§8.2).

### 5.4 History ring and robust spot

- **Ring:** the last 8 integrations `(ts, P_ema, L_ema, x, y, lo, hi, llo, lhi)`. The liquidation EMA leg, the expiry reference, the sustained-growth estimate and the settlement box (via the medians of the bucket extremes, v0.4.1) read it. After a Raydium venue change or price start (M4R, #96) the 8 warm-up entries hold the **seeds** of §5.2 (`p`, `l` the running seed, `x, y` their reprojection) next to their buckets' true extremes; after the 8th the ring holds no entry from before the change, so every reader sees only the new pool's buckets. During the warm-up nothing on an executable venue reads them (§5.5).
- **Robust spot:** `P_rs = min(P_live, lb_lo)`, valid only if `lb_sec ≥ now − ref_max_age_s` (2 s). Without a fresh completed bucket the spot leg cannot be confirmed (the keeper cranks one second and acts the next).
- **Sustained EMA growth** `g` and `κ_eff`: §2.1. A single held second produces one step; a genuine trend produces three.

### 5.5 Liquidatable (SPEC §5.1)

```
spot leg:  venue executable ? CR(V_eff, B̂(Q; reprice(L_spot, P_rs))) < L : true         (P_rs fresh; SPEC: CR_spot := 0)
           L_spot = max(L_live, L_floor)     (#101, FITS-2 (a); L_floor = min(L_ema, L_mhi) while ema_guard = 1 — AIR-17, #117: the
                                             upper median of the bucket liquidity highs; it was L_ema' = min(L_ema, L_mlo) —, else
                                             L_ema; uncapped)
EMA leg:   the newest liq_persist_n (= 3) ring entries all have CR(V_eff, B̂(Q; x_i, y_i)) < L       (v0.4: no seconds condition)
liquidatable ⇔ (spot leg ∧ EMA leg) ∨ now ≥ deadline
gate (M4R, #96): while ema_warm > 0 on an executable venue, liquidate and expire fail (EmaWarmingUp) before either leg is read
```

**The spot leg's liquidity** (PROPOSED, #101, FITS-2 (a)). Through #100 the spot leg repriced the robust spot on the live `√(X·Y)`, which an unlocked LP lowers inside its own transaction: `[withdraw all, liquidate, deposit]` made the leg true at the drained depth for a position healthy at the pool's depth. The EMA leg then had to be dragged too (withdraw-wrapped cranks in the keeper's quiet seconds, §12.12 (5b)), or already true — after a genuine spike and retreat `P_ema` lags above the spot for a few seconds, the EMA leg holds while the spot leg vetoes, and the wrapped liquidation removed the veto (§12.12 (5b) (v): forced at 2.01–2.07× under a 2.078× threshold). The leg now reads `L_spot = max(L_live, L_floor)`: a withdrawal lowers `L_live` but not `L_floor`, so the leg needs the EMA liquidity itself to have fallen, genuinely or through attacker-only seconds. **The guarded floor (AIR-17, #117).** While the post-change guard is set the floor is `min(L_ema, L_mhi)`, `L_mhi` the upper median of the ring's bucket liquidity **highs**; it was the exit reference's guarded term `L_ema' = min(L_ema, L_mlo)`, on the lows, and a bucket low is the minimum of its second, so an unlocked LP's `[withdraw, crank_price, deposit]` in 4 of every 8 seconds set `L_mlo` to the drained depth even with the keeper observing every second (and kept the guard from clearing), and a liquidation wrapped in the LP's withdrawal was judged at that depth — forcing positions in the spike-and-retreat band. One honest observation keeps a bucket high at the pool's depth, and lowering `L_mhi` takes 5 of 8 attacker-only seconds, which the keeper's per-second coverage under the guard denies; a deposit push lifts the floor to at most `L_ema`, so it defers, never forces. Outside the guard `L_floor = L_ema`, as before. The keeper's per-second liquidity-anomaly coverage denies the attacker-only seconds after the first (§5.8 duty 2), but it holds `L_ema` where it is and cannot lift it: a low reached genuinely — the unlocked LP's withdrawal held for a few seconds — and then frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks stays in the leg, and forces exactly the liquidations the same LP's withdrawal for good forces (FITX-1, #102; §12.12 (5b) (vii), residual 10; an owner decision, O18). It is venue-independent — PumpSwap markets have it too, which the owner's M6R-1 decision (2026-09-30, #105) relies on now that canonical pools take third-party LP (§1.2). It is the same leg in every decision that reads CR-liquidatability (ARCH §3.6–§3.8): `liquidate` (step 3), `expire`'s `UseLiquidate` check — so a withdrawal inside the transaction cannot turn an expiry into a penalty-paying liquidation either (a held drain, frozen, can, where it could force the liquidation: residual 10) —, and `sol_settle`'s branch between "not liquidatable" (disarm on the box) and the refutation / S1 / S2 legs, and its S3-due test on an executable venue (the flag or the fee cap). The exit bound's `L_b` (§8.2), the S1 / S2 legs, the refutation and "not liquidatable" keep their liquidities: `L_b` already takes the deepest term; the S1 / S2 legs also need the box corner `(P_lo, L_hi)`, which a withdrawal in the same transaction cannot move — inside an unlocked LP's `[withdraw, sol_settle, deposit]` that corner is their only veto (AIR-20, §5.6); the refutation reads `(P_hi, L_lo)` and `min(L_live, L_lo)`, and "not liquidatable" `(P_hi, L_lo)`, where a withdrawal only makes the borrower-favourable outcome harder. **Cost.** A deposit can raise `L_spot` and so delay the leg, by at most the position's own depth effect (≈ `(1 − L_live/L_floor)·q/x` in price; `L_b`'s cap bounds the reference, not this leg). A genuine withdrawal delays nothing where the EMA leg binds — a flat or rising market, whose newest ring entry already sits at `(P_ema ≤ P_rs, L_ema)` — and otherwise until `L_ema` has decayed (each integrated second closes 1/6 of the gap `L_ema − L_live`, τ = 5 s at `dt` = 1: 90 % of it in ≈ 13 s, 99 % in ≈ 25 s at the keeper's per-second cadence near a threshold): in the sims +0–3 s after a 0.25–0.5 % dip at the withdrawal, +0–8 s with ±1 % per-second noise (+0–10 s at `9aade3e`; §12.12 (5b) (vi)).

**The post-change gate** (PROPOSED, M4R, #96; the graduation as built, M6). For the `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` = 8 integrations after a Raydium venue change or price start — and since M6 the `→ PumpSwap` graduation and a PumpSwap price start (§5.2) — the exits wait: `liquidate` and `expire` fail with `EmaWarmingUp` (after the venue is found executable, before the fresh-bucket check), `sol_settle` evaluates nothing (§5.6) and Raydium and PumpSwap opens are refused (§6.2; OWNER-M6R-2, kept 2026-09-30). After a graduation the keeper's exit on a carried position lands 8–12 s after the migration (§10.1: +10 s on D@80 %; T + 8 with the straddle's tx3 bucket, §10.2) — accepted by the owner on 2026-09-30 (M6R-4, SPEC §14). A venue that cannot execute is never gated, so a new pool that halts within the warm-up reaches S3 as before. With the keeper cranking every second from the adoption on, the 8th warm-up integration lands at adoption + 9 s, and every exit that is due then executes in that second (§10.3, §12.12); positions have already waited ≥ 3 min for the fast path (`pool_fast_route_s + pool_fast_window_s`), so the extra seconds are cheap. The gate does not change the legs (`persist::liq_ema_leg` is unchanged): it only keeps them from being read while the ring still holds anchors and stale prices.

The ring integrates at most once per second, so 3 entries always span ≥ 2 s; the separate `liq_persist_s` added nothing (decision 2). The route-broken flag and the fee hard cap never make the spot leg true (V2-1); every CR here is a risk quote (§3.1). `L` is the position's locked liquidation CR.

### 5.6 SOL settlement: one rule for every decision (the settlement box) — PROPOSED (v0.4; medians v0.4.1)

`K_s = min(10,000,000 + ⌊V·100/10⁴⌋, K_cap, V)`, `K_cap` = 0.25 SOL (ADOPTED Q4). `ert` is the position's effective venue-side rent tolerance (6,500,000 lamports on pump venues, 0 on Raydium). σ(q; X, Y) is SPEC's slippage (§2.1). σ_max = 20 % (Tier D 30 %).

**The rule.** A settlement decision may rest on EMA evidence only if manipulated seconds could not have produced that evidence. One attacker-only second moves `P_ema` by up to +16.7 % / −8.3 % and `L_ema` alike. v0.4 bounded it with the lower median of the ring's 8 clamped observations `obs`; the fourth review showed that a second which also holds the honest keeper's observation can still be used: if the attacker adds an observation just beyond the (pushed) `P_ema`, the bucket straddles `P_ema` and `obs = P_ema`, so one attacker-only push followed by 3–4 such **frozen** seconds moved the median onto the pushed value (R4B-2). v0.4.1 therefore takes the medians over each ring entry's **bucket extremes**: a frozen second contains the honest observation, so its bucket low is at most the honest price and its bucket high at least the honest price. Raising the lower median of the lows, or lowering the upper median of the highs, needs 5 of the 8 entries, and only an attacker-only second can raise a low or lower a high. Every EMA-based settlement decision is evaluated on the **settlement box**

```
P_mlo = lower median of the ring's bucket lows,  P_mhi = upper median of the bucket highs  (L_mlo, L_mhi likewise)
P_lo = min(P_ema, P_mlo),  P_hi = max(P_ema, P_mhi),  L_lo = min(L_ema, L_mlo),  L_hi = max(L_ema, min(L_mhi, cap))
           cap = the market's depth cap — D on Raydium and PumpSwap, none on the curve (AIR-15, #117; L_hi = max(L_ema, L_mhi) before)
(the box is the EMA point alone if nothing has been integrated for more than ring_len seconds and the venue
 cannot execute now: a venue that records no observations cannot have been pushed since, and a halted venue's
 S3 must not be blocked; on an executable venue the program's lender instructions integrate a pushed bucket
 without recording, so there the medians always apply — M2-SEC-1)
```

and requires the decision to come out the same everywhere in it. Because B̂ and σ increase with `P` and decrease with `L` (§2.1), "everywhere in the box" means "at the box corner least favourable to the decision":

```
S1 legs    ⇔ B̂_live + ert > V − K_s  ∧  B̂(Q; reprice(L_live, P_rs)) + ert > V − K_s  ∧  B̂(Q; reprice(L_hi, P_lo)) + ert > V − K_s
S2 legs    ⇔ σ_live > σ_max  ∧  σ(Q; reprice(L_live, P_rs)) > σ_max  ∧  σ(Q; reprice(L_hi, P_lo)) > σ_max
           (the S1/S2 legs read the raw L_live, not L_spot, so inside an unlocked LP's [withdraw, sol_settle, deposit] only the
            box corner (P_lo, L_hi) vetoes: AIR-20, a residual documented and pinned, no behaviour change, #117)
refuted    ⇔ B̂(Q; reprice(L_lo, P_hi)) + ert ≤ V − K_s  ∧  σ(Q; reprice(min(L_live, L_lo), P_hi)) ≤ σ_max
not liquidatable (disarms) ⇔ EMA leg fails  ∧  CR(V_eff, B̂(Q; reprice(L_lo, P_hi))) ≥ L          (and not expired)
amount (S2, S3): x(P, L) = min(V − K_s, FV(P, L)), FV's p' decided at the same (P, L)           (v0.4.1, R4A-3)
                 settle only if  x(P_hi, L_lo) − x(P_lo, L_hi) ≤ fv_dev_bps·x(P_lo, L_hi)/10⁴;
                 pay x(P_ema, L_ema)                                                          (D3 literal: EMA reserves)
normal buyback allowed ⇔ B̂_live(execution fee) + ert ≤ V − K_s  ∧  σ_live ≤ σ_max            (then §8.2)
                         ★1.0.1: on a live curve past rt, B̂_live and σ are B̂_v3's (pump-final C5): a straddle-size
                         position above σ_max goes to S2 at once instead of waiting for a bundle (known issues PUMP-2026-10-13-3)
```

**Arming (one eligibility clock for S1 and S2, V2-4 as amended).**

- The first evaluation at which the S1 legs or the S2 legs hold arms the position (`s_count = ema_count`, `s_since = now`).
- Only box-robust evidence disarms: `refuted`, or "not liquidatable" as above. **Every other evaluation within `settle_arm_gap_s` of the last keeps an armed position's clock running** (`s_last = now`) — a failing live or robust-spot leg, a stale bucket, a spot-leg veto (R3A-6 b).
- Evaluations more than `settle_arm_gap_s` (10 s) apart re-arm it: a lapsed clock is not kept by any evaluation, it re-arms with a fresh count at the next one whose S1 or S2 legs hold (AIR-16, #117: as built the "not liquidatable, not expired" branch refreshed a lapsed clock with its old count, so the next evaluation settled at once, skipping the persistence window — and anyone can force that branch with a dump or an LP deposit that fails the spot leg).
- Settlement executes when the S1 or S2 legs hold now and `ema_count − s_count ≥ settle_persist_n` (40 updates, hence ≥ 39 s; `settle_persist_s` is gone). The kind is chosen then: **S1 if the S1 legs hold** (box included), else S2; an S2 whose amount is not box-consistent waits (no state change).
- S3 (no route, §10.3) needs no arming; its amount obeys the same box check.
- **While warming** (M4R, #96): on an executable venue with `ema_warm > 0` (§5.2, §5.5) `sol_settle` evaluates nothing — no arming or disarming, no clock update, no S1/S2/S3 — and returns `Ok` after its prologue, whose observation stands. S3 cannot be due then, because the adoption cleared the no-route chain (and a price start has no position). A venue that cannot execute is never gated.

**What the rule closes and what it leaves.** The corner choice keeps honest liveness where it matters: in a sustained uptrend `P_ema ≤ P_mlo` (the EMA lags more than the median of the last 8 bucket lows), so the S1 corner is `P_ema` itself and S1 timing is unchanged (in a ramp's first ≈ 8–11 s, after a step that fills 4 of the 8 entries, or in a steady ramp of ≈ 9.5–16.7 %/s, where the EMA's steady lag `(1 + g)/(1 + 6g)` is smaller than the median's `(1 + g)⁻⁴`, the S1 corner is `P_mlo`, which delays S1 but never makes it easier); in a genuine reversal `P_ema ≥ P_mhi`, so refutation reads `P_ema` itself. **`p'` on the box (R4A-3).** FV prices an expired position with the expiry fee only if it is not CR-liquidatable; v0.4 read that from the raw "CR-liquidatable" flag, whose robust-spot leg one contrary low observation (the V1-2 primitive, not an attacker-only second) fails, and both box corners then used the manipulated `p'`. v0.4.1 decides `p'` at each evaluated state: a disagreement between corners shows up as an amount difference and the settlement waits. Evidence (§12.10, §12.11):

- *S2 → S1 flip* (R3A-1): a lender-keeper's 1–2 pushed seconds plus `[pump → sol_settle → dump]` no longer produce an uncapped S1 (v0.3: +2.37 to +5.58 SOL for lenders on the drained RA cases); neither does one push followed by up to 10 frozen seconds (v0.4: +2.37 to +5.58 SOL after 4 frozen seconds).
- *Refutation push* (R3A-6 a, R4B-2): a borrower's one-second dump before maturity, or a dump plus 4 frozen seconds repeated every cycle, delays S1 by at most 8 s (v0.3, and v0.4 under push-then-freeze: no S1 within 600 s at 3 % beyond the boundary).
- *Amount push* (V2-9): a one-second push up or down moves an S2/S3 amount by at most `fv_dev_bps` (±1 %); on the realistic case — Emergency flag set, healthy position expires, S3 capped hard — +0.70 SOL for a lender push instead of +10.48 SOL without the box. A contrary observation before an S3 no longer changes `p'` (v0.4: −2.9 % to −7.3 % for lenders).
- *Residual — consecutive attacker-only seconds* (R4A-9): **5 of 8 in either direction** move a median; on the drained-RA model cases 5 consecutive attacker-only seconds flip a capped S2 into S1 (+2.37 to +5.58 SOL for 1.1–2.0 SOL of fees), none of 6 with one honest second in between. Only a censored or absent keeper (degraded mode, §5.8) leaves 5 consecutive seconds to an attacker.
- *Residual — deferral by widening* (v0.4.1): a contrary observation in every second (a paid round trip; on Raydium also an unlocked LP holder's `[withdraw, crank, re-deposit]`, which costs no swap fee and holds the box's low `L` at the locked depth `D`: an S2/S3 amount spread of ≈ `(1 − λ)·Q/X_D`, 75–109 bps at λ 50 % and 113–196 bps at λ 25–10 % on 25 bps pools, 253–672 bps on 1 % pools, §10.5 — corrected after M4: v0.4.1 said it "moves `L` too little to matter") keeps the box wide on one side. It cannot flip a decision, but it defers S1 (like the robust-spot veto of §12.9) and an S3 amount on an executable venue for as long as it is paid: indefinitely at a 1 % round trip per second, which costs 0.002 SOL/s (RC) to 0.21 SOL/s (A); a 3 % round trip every second delays an S1 2 % beyond its boundary from 60 s to 225 s on Tier A at 0.81 SOL/s (`refute_push.py` (d)). A pushed EMA alone defers an S2/S3 by ≈ 13 s per attacker-only second (§12.11). A deferred S3 on an executable venue can still be expired or liquidated normally when the route works for short.win. **Measured on real pools (M10, G-E4; CPMM USELESS and PumpSwap, the real LP `deposit` / `withdraw`):** `[deposit LP, sol_settle, withdraw LP]` every 20 s from a gap past the S1 boundary until the honest S1, and in 3 of its last 5 s, leaves the box honest; wrapped in each of the last 5 s (5 consecutive ring buckets with an LP-wrapped observation) it doubled the box's `L_hi` as built, and the S1 still lands in the honest second (57 / 58 s) with the honest amounts — this residual at work, no flip (dense wraps widened `L_hi`; only sparse ones left it unchanged). **Since AIR-15 (#117) deposit wraps widen `L_hi` at most to the market's depth cap** (`L_hi = max(L_ema, min(L_mhi, cap))`, cap = `D` on Raydium and PumpSwap): a deposit raises its second's liquidity high, which honest records in that second cannot lower, so wraps in 4 of 8 seconds set `L_mhi` at will and deferred the S1/S2 legs and the S2/S3 amount check for no swap fee; `D` is deposit- and withdraw-invariant, so no capital-only bundle moves `min(L_mhi, cap)`, and `L_ema` is never capped (the box keeps the EMA point). In `refute_push.py` (d) the Raydium rows widened by an LP add/remove of 2× `L` now settle S1 2 s sooner (RA at 59 s instead of 61 s, RC at 62 s instead of 64 s).

The literal "lender-adverse corner for everything" reading of the brief was tested and rejected: for refutation it is the corner that makes refutation *easiest* (a borrower push would disarm), and for the amount it lets a borrower's one-second push lower FV by up to 8 %. "The same decision on the whole box" is the one rule that closes both directions.

If spot says "cannot buy back" but neither S1 nor S2 is confirmed, the position waits. `ert` enters the S1 threshold so that no band exists within the position's locked fee cap in which neither a buyback (budget `V − K_s − ert`) nor S1 is possible; above that cap, S3 settles (the S3 chain counts a live fee above `min(global cap, the market's lowest locked cap)` as no route, and a fee above the position's own locked cap is no route for it — AIR-19, #117, §10.3).

### 5.7 Parameter choice: lag versus manipulation, and the simulated-safe region (R3B-6)

The EMA must be fast enough that liquidations track genuine pumps and slow enough that manipulation costs more than it earns. Manipulation resistance comes from aggregation, persistence and the bounds (§12); the choice of τ, κ, persistence and δ is driven by liveness. Reading: τ = 5 s with κ = 25 % is what liquidates Tier C (2.93×) and D (3.29×) in a 10×/60 s pump; τ = 8 s or κ = 15 % (the reviewer's suggested bounds) or 5 updates of persistence each turn them into S1 losses (−68 % / −58 %), as does the critic's EMA-only 16.7 % bound. A fixed 1 % bound liquidates every pump here but fails after LP drains (§12.2). Tier A (cushion 1.6×) cannot survive a 10×/60 s pump with any setting.

**The battery (v0.4.1, R4A-1, R4B-1, R4A-4).** v0.4 checked its bounds on 65 runs in which the price was set once per second, so the live quote always equalled the one-second-old reference, and on 7 parameters. Both reviewers showed that this hid the dead band of the penalty reserve (§8.2). `config_check.py` — the governance CI gate — now runs 1,655:

| part | runs | what it adds | default outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| core | 65 | the lag-study pumps above, gaps, 3×/60 s and 2×/20 s pumps, 50 % Raydium LP drains followed by pumps | 43 liquidations, 12 S1, mean lender −8.35 % of Q |
| noise | 390 | the core rows with deterministic per-second price noise ±0.3 % and ±1 %, 3 seeds each: live and reference differ within a second, as on a real pool | 267 liquidations, 72 S1, −8.27 % |
| grid | 960 | the reviewer's genuine-pump grid: 8 markets × 4–15× × 20–100 s × size 1 and 0.5 × keeper latency 1 and 2 s | 437 liquidations, 523 S1, −36.22 % |
| aged | 240 | positions aged close to their maximum duration at 80 / 90 % utilisation (rent due far above `K_s`), then a ramp, with ±0.3 % noise | 156 liquidations, 84 S1, −14.88 % |

A proposal fails if any run that liquidates, expires or stays open under the defaults becomes an S1 or stays open although liquidatable ("liveness"); a run that leaves lenders more than 1 % of the value of `Q` worse is reported as a lender-income warning for the owner. Sizing parameters (fee-scaled caps, λ_min, impact caps, γ) are compared at equal position size, because a smaller position is S1-prone only through `K_s`'s fixed part (v0.4's scan counted that size effect as a failure: `pool_lock_min_bps` = 10,000 and `fee_size_cap_x100` = 100 lose nothing at equal size).
