- **τ and κ** keep their v0.4 bounds (τ the constant 5 s, κ ≥ 25 %), now on this battery rather than because of the withdrawn penalty reserve: τ = 6 loses 83 runs, κ = 22.5 % 38 (6–8× pumps over 40–50 s on Tier C, D and RC, in the grid and among the aged runs). The growth window would pass at 5 but stays compiled at [3, 4].
- **Persistence** tightens to `liq_persist_n ≤ 3` (4 loses 58 runs, mostly noisy fast pumps), which makes v0.4's relational check `liq_persist_n + kappa_growth_n ≤ 7` redundant.
- **Settlement keeper terms** are capped at their SPEC / ADOPTED values (0.01 SOL, 1 %, 0.25 SOL): any larger `K_s` widens the S1 region (`B̂ + ert > V − K_s`) and loses fast-pump liquidations of half-size positions first.
- **SPEC CRs become per-tier compiled minima**: lower opening or liquidation CRs lose 102–362 runs (109–362 before the AI review's fixes, #117, which also move `delta_liq_bps` 150 / 100 to 27 / 0 rows from 28 / 0 and 34 / 1, and the noise part's mean lender P&L from −8.28 % to −8.27 %).
- **Rates at 0** lose 2–5 aged runs (less rent erosion, so liquidation triggers closer to the S1 boundary). Rates are SPEC fee parameters and keep their compiled range; the gate rejects such a proposal.
- `exec_tol_bps` = 0, `penalty_bps` = 2,000 and `pool_lock_min_bps` = 10,000 now lose nothing (they failed in the reviewer's v0.4 check through the penalty reserve or the size effect).

**Scan** (`--scan`: every parameter with a liveness effect at each compiled extreme alone, on core, noise and aged, 695 runs per value). Every value not listed passes — `curve_oi_gamma_bps`, `delta_exp_bps`, `delta_liq_bps`, `ema_clamp_bps`, `ema_dtmax_s`, `exec_rent_tol`, `exec_tol_bps`, `expiry_fee_bps`, `expiry_keeper_bps`, `expiry_keeper_cap`, `expiry_keeper_fixed`, `fee_oi_cap_x100`, `fee_size_cap_x100`, `fv_dev_bps`, `grad_grace_s`, `impact_mkt_bps`, `impact_pos_bps`, `kappa_base_bps`, `kappa_growth_n`, `kappa_liq_bps`, `liq_persist_n`, `liq_slow_dtmax_s`, `liq_slow_tau_s`, `max_liq_slip_bps`, `max_migration_pause_s`, `min_rent_s`, `penalty_bps`, `pool_lock_min_bps`, `rate_base_bps`, `rate_s1_bps`, `rate_s2_bps`, `ref_max_age_s`, `rent_protocol_bps`, `s3_wait_s`, `settle_arm_gap_s`, `settle_bps`, `settle_fixed`, `settle_persist_n`, `util_cap_bps`, `util_kink_bps`, `venue_fee_hard_cap_bps` — and these do not:

| parameter | value | rows worse than the default (liquidation -> S1, or lender -1 % of Q) |
|---|---|---|
| `settle_cap` | 0 | (rejected by validate_params: settle_fixed <= settle_cap) |
| `util_kink_bps` | 9500 | (rejected by validate_params: util_kink_bps <= util_cap_bps) |
| `util_cap_bps` | 0 | (rejected by validate_params: util_kink_bps <= util_cap_bps) |
| `delta_exp_bps` | 300 | (rejected by validate_params: delta_liq_bps >= delta_exp_bps) |
| `expiry_keeper_cap` | 0 | (rejected by validate_params: expiry_keeper_fixed <= expiry_keeper_cap) |
| `cr_liq_bps` | 10500 | (rejected by validate_params: tier A: cr_open >= 20,000 and cr_liq >= 12,500 (SPEC minima)) |
| `cr_liq_bps` | 100000 | 0 liveness / 118 lender -1 %: B pump 3x/60s -> liquidation; C pump 3x/60s -> liquidation; D@0% pump 3x/60s -> liquidation (+115 more) |
| `penalty_bps` | 0 | 0 liveness / 306 lender -1 %: A gap 2x -> liquidation; A pump 3x/60s -> liquidation; A pump 2x/20s -> liquidation (+303 more) |
| `rate_base_bps` | 0 | 2 liveness / 2 lender -1 %: D@0% aged pump 6x/40s noise 0.3% s1 U90 -> liquidation; D@0% aged pump 6x/40s noise 0.3% s3 U90 -> S1; D@50% aged pump 6x/40s noise 0.3% s1 U90 -> liquidation (+1 more) |
| `rate_s1_bps` | 0 | 5 liveness / 4 lender -1 %: RC drain 50%+2.0x/600s noise 0.3% s3 -> open; RC drain 50%+2.0x/120s noise 0.3% s3 -> open; D@0% aged pump 6x/40s noise 0.3% s1 U90 -> liquidation (+6 more) |
| `rate_s2_bps` | 0 | 2 liveness / 32 lender -1 %: A aged pump 3x/60s noise 0.3% s1 U90 -> liquidation; A aged pump 3x/60s noise 0.3% s2 U90 -> liquidation; A aged pump 3x/60s noise 0.3% s3 U90 -> liquidation (+31 more) |
| `cr_open_bps` | 0 | (rejected by validate_params: tier A: cr_open >= 20,000 and cr_liq >= 12,500 (SPEC minima)) |
| `cr_open_bps` | 100000 | 13 liveness / 231 lender -1 %: A gap 2x -> open; A pump 3x/60s -> open; A pump 2x/20s -> open (+241 more) |

`cr_liq_bps` = 100,000 and `cr_open_bps` = 100,000 lose only lender income (earlier liquidation on a smaller penalty base; healthier positions that never liquidate); `penalty_bps` = 0 likewise. **Since #105** `cr_open_bps` = 100,000 also flags 13 liveness rows, all D@50 %'s 10×/60 s pump (plain, with noise and aged): a position opened at a 10× CR becomes liquidatable only after the pump has completed the curve (at 5.84×), the graduation's warm-up gate (§10.1, OWNER-M6R-4) holds the keeper's exit for 8 s of a steep ramp, and the run settles S1 at ≈ 10× (through #104, under v0.4.1's graduation, it was liquidated at 7.94×, lenders +4.2 to +4.8 % of `Q`; the default configuration liquidates it at 3.29×, lenders +1.7 to +2.3 %); the lenders end **better** off than under the default configuration in all 13 (+4.6 to +5.7 %; within −0.2 / +1.4 points of v0.4.1's graduation), so the flag is the gate's mechanical "liquidation → S1", not a loss. The default configuration is unaffected (`config_default.txt`: `OK`). **Corners** (`--corners`): every corner of the core box that passes the relational checks — κ ∈ {25 %, 30 %}, persistence ∈ {2, 3}, growth window ∈ {3, 4}, `dt_max` ∈ {1, 2}, clamp ∈ {50 %, 100 %}, bucket age ∈ {1, 3}, δ ∈ {2 %, 5 %}: 128 configurations — on the full 1,655 runs: core-box corners that pass the relational checks (tau = 5 s): 128; failing on the full battery (1655 runs each): 0. A stress corner with every scanned parameter at its liveness-adverse compiled extreme at once (κ 25 %, persistence 3, growth 4, `dt_max` 1, clamp 50 %, bucket age 1 s, δ 2 %, the settlement keeper terms at their maxima, `L_slow` τ 1 day, `fv_dev` 0.5 %, ε 0, hard cap 3 %, σ_max 5 %, maximum rates) loses 0 runs. What this does **not** cover: combinations beyond the core box other than that one stress corner, and price paths outside the battery (§17). "The compiled bounds are the simulated-safe region" means exactly this.

Defaults (PROPOSED, all tiers): **τ = 5 s (constant), dt_max = 2 s, C = 10,000 bps, ring 8, liq_persist_n = 3, settle_persist_n = 40, arm gap 10 s, κ_max = 25 %, kappa_growth_n = 3, δ_liq = 2 %, δ_exp = 1 %, fv_dev = 1 %.**

### 5.8 Keepers: duties, cost model, degraded mode (R3B-5, decision 10)

**Duties** (off-chain, PROPOSED; the manipulation resistance of §12's atomic model assumes them):

1. At least one observation **per second** on every **covered** market (open positions and lenders' value at risk ≥ `var_floor_lamports`; owner decision A of 2026-10-01, "The global keeper budget" below) with a position whose live CR is below 1.3·L, whose deadline is within 60 s, or that is armed, and wherever `|P_spot/P_ema − 1| > 2 %`; otherwise at least every 30 s for markets with open positions (after a venue switch, duty 9 keeps them per second while the reference guard is set). **Below the floor a market gets the 30-s idle cadence only** (its exits still go when the program accepts them: the keeper reads the market again in the seconds after an idle crank while a position is near an exit, #110).
2. After any observation it did not make whose bucket **price** (`lo` / `hi`) deviates from the spot it read in that second by more than 2 %, observe that market **every slot for 60 s** (atomic bundles then have no second of their own, and the settlement-box deferral of §5.6 gets no consecutive attacker-only seconds). **Liquidity anomalies (#101, FITS-2 (b)):** on a market **with open positions**, after an observation it did not make whose bucket **liquidity** (`llo` / `lhi`) deviates from the `√(X·Y)` it read in that second by more than 2 % (`burst_dev_bps`), observe that market **every second** (not every slot) for the next `liq_cover_s` (10 s, keeper config), extended while such deviations recur; a market without positions never gets liquidity-triggered coverage, and price bursts are unchanged. An LP-wrapped crank (`[withdraw all, crank_price, deposit]`, or a deposit around it) moves no price, and in a second nobody else observed it sets the bucket's liquidity alone: repeated in the keeper's quiet seconds (29 of every 30), it drags `L_ema` to the drained depth `D` at 1/6 of the gap per second, and a keeper observation in a dragged second cannot lift it (its value is clamped into the bucket's `[D, H]`, where `L_ema` already is). That drag, with the spot leg at the drained `L_live`, forced liquidations of positions healthy at the pool's depth at the defaults (FITS-2; §12.12 (5b)). Under this coverage the first wrapped second is the only attacker-only one; every later second shares its bucket with the keeper's honest observation, which holds `L_ema` where it is (one step, ≈ ×11/12), and an attacker that pauses until the window lapses gives back 1/6 of the gap in each honest covered second. At `9aade3e` that one step forced nothing; since AIR-14's reference cap at `D` (#117) it forces one level (2.06× against the honest 2.065× at 0.79 × the cap, §12.12 (5b) (iii)). **Cost:** one keeper crank per attacker transaction while the wrapping is sustained (1:1); an isolated trigger buys `liq_cover_s` cranks (10:1 in transactions unbatched, ≈ 1.7:1 batched at zero priority; measured in M7, #107: 8 cranks above the control, 8:1 / 1.3:1) — against 150:1 for #98's liquidity bursts (60 s per slot, on every covered market; M4G-2), which #99 reverted. **What is left:** a covered second in which the keeper's observation does not land is another attacker-only step, and the steps add up while the attacker keeps wrapping (the M4G-1 class, downward: §12.12 (5b) (iv), a half-cap 1 % position forced at 2.03× against a 2.039× threshold after 3 isolated misses in an hour; since AIR-14's cap at `D` a cap-sized one at 2.07× against 2.078× after a single miss and at 2.01–2.07× after 20, where at `9aade3e` only 20 forced it, at 2.06–2.07×); such misses are not consecutive, so they do not trigger degraded mode by themselves. And the coverage holds `L_ema` where it is — it cannot lift a low the attacker reached **genuinely**, by withdrawing for real for a few seconds (nothing foreign then: the keeper reads the same drained depth) before it starts wrapping: that low stays frozen at 1:1 cost and forces what the same LP's withdrawal for good forces (FITX-1, #102; §12.12 (5b) (vii), residual 10). Since #99 no carried position is larger than the new pool's opening caps, so a push that reaches the reference after a switch's guard has cleared only delays an exit, as for positions opened on the pool (§12.12 (11)). **PumpSwap markets (M6R-1, owner decision of 2026-09-30, #105):** canonical pools take third-party LP (§1.2), so the same coverage runs on PumpSwap markets with open positions — never on those without — at the same 1:1 cost (the keeper's comparison with its own `√(X·(Y + VQR))` is venue-independent as built). On a λ 50 % PumpSwap pool a drag of withdraw-wrapped cranks forces a half-cap position at 2.020–2.035× against the honest 2.040× (a quarter-cap one at 2.002–2.017× against 2.022×) under the #99 keeper, whichever spot leg, and nothing under this coverage (`attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` part 3 (d); §12.12 (12)). **Budget (decision A, 2026-10-01):** price bursts and liquidity-anomaly coverage are discretionary charges on the global budget — the market's own entitlement, then the free pool, never another market's entitlement — and run on covered markets only.
3. Liquidate at the first executable second (Jito bundle with tip); `expire` when the expiry bound is met.
4. Call `sol_settle` every second for armed positions.
5. Observe every market without a route at least every 30 s (continuous S3 chain, §10.3); on Raydium, run the fast path (§10.3), proposing only a candidate that every open position fits (#99, §6.1); with none, the chain carries the positions to S3.
6. Call pump `migrate_v2` top-level as soon as a curve completes (ADOPTED Q16); `claim_venue_rewards` and `sweep_trader` right after a full wipe (so pre-wipe cashback reaches the wiped epoch's lenders, §9.5; since v0.4.1 deposits also wait for this sweep, R4A-7) — on the curve as built (M5-SEC-1) the bundle `[pump claim_cashback(user = trader), sweep_trader]`, the sweep setting both post-wipe bits. As built in M5 the keeper's `migrate_v2` duty is off by default until M6's `→ PumpSwap` transition, and the post-wipe and `sweep_trader` duties are not built yet (both instructions are permissionless; a depositor's client can send them). **As built in M6 (#104):** `migrate_v2` is on again; moving a curve market onto its pool (the transition crank and the pool's first bucket) is a keeper duty after anyone's migration; after a full wipe the keeper sends, in the same second, `[create ATA(trader, WSOL), pAMM claim_cashback, sweep_trader]` on PumpSwap, `[pump claim_cashback, sweep_trader]` on the curve, `sweep_trader` alone with nothing to claim; the weekly cashback claim and `sweep_trader` on stray trader balances run as duty 13 (PROPOSED ops knobs); PumpSwap markets without positions are cranked through their warm-up (8 cranks per graduation or listing, OWNER-M6SK-2). **M6 security review (fixed):** on PumpSwap the post-wipe bundle starts with pAMM `init_user_volume_accumulator(payer = keeper, user = trader)` whenever the trader's pAMM UVA is missing (M6-SEC-2: pAMM `claim_cashback` fails without it, and anyone can fund the UVA's WSOL ATA without it — the donation then reaches the wiped lenders); `deposit` also waits for the venue-reward bit on a `PumpMigrating` market (M6-SEC-5: a full wipe settled S3 during the migration pause no longer lets the next epoch take the curve-era cashback).
7. While an S3 on an executable venue is due, observe that market **once per second** (not per slot): the settlement box of v0.4.1 (§5.6) takes the medians of bucket lows and highs, so several honest observations at different prices within one second widen it for no benefit.
8. (M4V-5, with the PROPOSED warm-up of §6.2) Observe a Raydium market without positions inside its listing window every 60 s, and **every second** in its last `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS + 2` seconds and after it until the price state has warmed up (`ema_warm = 0`), so the first open after a listing or a list-only adoption reads `P_ema` from honest buckets rather than the spot the adoption started it at (about 70 cranks per listing).
9. (M4R, #96; M4F-1, #98) After a `PoolSwitched`, on any market with open positions, observe **every second while `ema_warm > 0` or `ema_guard = 1`**: through the warm-up (8 integrations, adoption + 9 s) and after it until the reference guard clears (at the gate on an honest pool). Send no `liquidate` or `expire` before `ema_warm = 0` (they fail `EmaWarmingUp`; `sol_settle` is harmless). One honest observation in a warm-up bucket keeps that bucket's low and high honest, which is all the seed needs (§5.2 (P1)–(P3)); a warm-up without any honest bucket is the degraded mode of §12.12.

   **The reference guard and M4X-2's input guard hold only while every second carries an honest observation** (§5.2, §8.2). Both read `L_mlo`, the lower median of the 8 newest buckets' liquidity lows. A deposit-wrapped crank in a second nobody else observed sets that bucket's low, and 5 such buckets of 8 move `L_mlo`: the guard clears and the reference reads the pushed `L_ema` and `L_slow`. As built at M4R the keeper fell back to the 30-s cadence at +9 s; a per-second `[deposit k·LP counter, crank_price, withdraw]` then cleared the guard at ≈ +15 s. Under this duty a freeze held from the adoption keeps the keeper at an observation every second for as long as the freeze lasts, and the guard stays set (the reference at the honest lows). After the guard has cleared — honestly at the gate, or once the keeper misses 5 of 8 guarded seconds — the keeper is back on duty 1's cadence and an attacker owns its idle seconds, as on any market. That blocked the liquidation of carried positions of 3–6 % of the new pool's `X` (§12.12 (4); M4F-1, M4G-1, M4G-3). Since #99 a carried position fits the new pool's opening caps, and the same push delays its liquidation by 0–1 s (0–1.4 % of price; §12.12 (11)), as it delays a position opened at the caps in steady state (part 4e: 0–2 s). Cost: about 9 cranks after an honest switch; under a freeze from the adoption, one crank a second for as long as the attacker pays its own transaction every second and ties up about the pool's size in capital. The degraded mode below applies when the budget cannot cover it.

`sol_settle`, `liquidate`, `expire` and `close_short` carry the venue accounts, so each of them **is** that second's observation; the keeper never sends a separate crank in a second in which it already acts. Several markets' `crank_price` instructions share one transaction (one signature). A `crank_price` keeps its per-market accounts inline (6 keys on CPMM, 5 on the curve, 7 on PumpSwap since #105's LP mint; ARCH §6.4), so a v0 transaction with the protocol ALT holds only a few; **keeper batches are v1 transactions** (4,096 B, 64 inline addresses, no ALT; live since slot 447,120,000), with the v0 batch as the fallback. **Measured (M7, #107; the real keeper's builder, `tests/bench/m7_keeper`)** — this replaces v0.4.1's byte estimate:

| venue | v1 max (B / accounts) | v1 batch of 6 (B / accounts) | v0 + protocol ALT, max (B) | v0 + protocol ALT + a table per market, max (B / accounts) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| PumpSwap | 8 (2,338 / 63) | 1,840 / 49 | 3 (1,003) | 8 (779 / 64) |
| AMM v4 | 9 (2,208 / 59) | 1,566 / 41 | 4 (1,109) | 9 (806 / 60) |
| CPMM | 9 (2,249 / 60) | 1,604 / 42 | 4 (1,114) | 9 (816 / 61) |
| curve | 11 (2,445 / 64) | 1,520 / 39 | 5 (1,181) | 10 (891 / 60) |
| mixed (AMM v4, CPMM, PumpSwap, curve) | 9 (2,382 / 64) | 1,734 / 46 | 4 (1,121) | 8 (764 / 59) |

The keeper keeps `batch_v1` = 6 (a larger v1 batch write-locks more markets, so one congested market holds up more observations) and `batch_v0` = 4 size-packed (a PumpSwap v0 batch is cut at 3). That v1 transactions land on mainnet is still UNVERIFIED (ARCH U25).

**Cost model** (`python3 sim/keeper_cost.py`; ★#107: on measured inputs, U27 closed). Inputs: base fee 5,000 lamports per signature (mainnet `getFeeForMessage`, slot 450,897,283); priority price scenarios 0–100,000 µlamports/CU (mainnet `getRecentPrioritizationFees` on pAMM, a PumpSwap pool and AMM v4 over slots 450,897,105–255: the lowest landed fee was 0 in ≥ 75 % of slots, spikes to 1.31 lamports/CU; 100,000 is O17's ceiling); **CU per instruction measured on the real keeper's transactions** (`services/keeper-litesvm` `m7_cost`, `tests/bench/m7_keeper/results.json`, the costliest measured per kind) — the **requested** limit `⌈consumed × 1.1⌉`, on which the priority fee is paid, so a transaction costs `5,000 × signatures + ⌈requested × price / 10⁶⌉` — in place of v0.4's estimates (crank 35 k, `sol_settle` 90 k, `liquidate` 220 k); the keeper's measured **cadence** (a quiet market one crank per 30 s, 2,880 a day; per second near a threshold; an armed position 43 `sol_settle` calls; a foreign price deviation 116 cranks at the harness's 2 ticks a second, ≈ 150 at 2.5 slots a second; an isolated LP-wrapped trigger 8 cranks above the control; a PumpSwap warm-up 8). The script cross-checks its constants against `results.json`. ★#111 (M10): re-measured on the M10 release build (`shortwin.so` `c4398d01…`, the hash `out/keeper_cost.txt` recorded then; since #112 `bbef1075…`, and since the KI-66 full / KI-67 batch of 2026-10-08 `4d19c582…`); every table below mirrors `out/keeper_cost.txt`. Against M7 the requested CU moved by crank +17…+40, `sol_settle` +4…+109, `liquidate` −408…−473 and `expire` −226…+243 (the mainnet Token-2022 ELF in the harness and the M10 program batches: the rent top-up's vesting, the extras denylist); the per-market-day costs moved by at most 0.0004 SOL, and no reading below changes. ★#112 (M10 program batch 4): re-measured on `shortwin.so` `bbef1075…` with the pump venues' 2026-10-02 redeploys and PUMP-2026-10-1's reads: against #111 PumpSwap's requested CU moved by crank +238, `sol_settle` +178, `liquidate` / `expire` +738 (+428 / +428 from the redeployed PumpSwap, the rest the bucket reads) and the curve's `liquidate` / `expire` by +321 / +322 (the redeployed pump); the per-market-day costs by at most 0.0021 SOL (PumpSwap at 100,000 µL/CU, every position near a threshold: 0.4280 → 0.4300 batched, 0.7880 → 0.7900 unbatched), and no reading below changes. ★#119 (pump-final P8, 2026-10-16): re-measured on short.win **1.0.1** (`72c82879…`: the AI review's fixes and pump's v3 curve adapter, on pump's 2026-10-13 ELFs; `results.json` names it): the curve's `liquidate` / `expire` fell to **147,131 / 144,314** requested CU (−44,706 / −44,701, −23.3 %; one `buy_v3` with 10 TradeExtras in place of `buy_v2` with 20, 1,122 B against 1,452 B — pump-final §12's 5 % re-baseline trigger), its crank +755 and `sol_settle` +1,226 / +1,899; PumpSwap's crank −288, `sol_settle` +1,812 / +2,563, `liquidate` / `expire` +1,862 / +1,995; Raydium's crank −283 / −282, `sol_settle` +279…+1,035, exits −27…−77. The per-market-day costs moved by at most 0.0065 SOL (the curve at 100,000 µL/CU, every position near a threshold: 0.3950 → 0.4015 batched, 0.7550 → 0.7615 unbatched; PumpSwap 0.4300 → 0.4275), and the readings below move by rounding only.

*(Superseded as the primary control by the global keeper budget — owner decision A of 2026-10-01, #109, below; kept for the comparison.)* **The O17 budget as built (#107; ARCH §7.1 duty 8):** `per_market_daily_lamports` = 30,000,000 (0.03 SOL a UTC day, PROPOSED, O17's upper bound) is charged with the market's share of its `crank_price` transactions (a batch's fee split equally among its markets); the cranks exits depend on — a position near a threshold, armed or warming up, a liquidatable or expired position waiting for a fresh reference or its bound, the S3 chain, a market without a route — are charged but **never throttled**; exits, pool selection, graduation moves, `crank_sol_usd` and `set_coverage_pause` are neither charged nor throttled; an exhausted budget stops the rest (the idle cadence, bursts, liquidity-anomaly and guard coverage, warm-ups) and, on a market with open positions, is **degraded mode at once** (below). What exhausts it (OWNER-M7-2: one LP-wrapped trigger every 11 s on top of the quiet cadence of a market with a healthy position; the market's opens then stay paused until the UTC day rolls over + 600 s, exits continue; LP-wrapped triggers need LP accounting — PumpSwap and Raydium only: the curve rows #107 listed cannot occur, the pump curve takes no deposit, M7-SEC-2):

**Price bursts after M7-SEC-2 (#108):** a duty-2 per-slot burst now needs a **reverted** ≥ 2 % excursion — the bucket's extreme off the keeper's reads of that second and the one before, and off its next read (ARCH §7.1 duty 2) —, i.e. a price round trip of ≈ 1 % of the token reserve paying `2·φ·δ` in fees; as built at #107 one plain `crank_price` (5,000 lamports) after anyone's honest ≥ 2 % move started it (the review's reproducer: 51 such cranks, 0.000255 SOL, spent the 0.03-SOL budget and paused opens for the UTC day). The keeper's cost of a burst is 116 cranks (PumpSwap, unbatched):

| pool SOL reserve | fee one way | attacker: one round trip | keeper unbatched at 0 uL/CU (attacker / keeper; budget) | keeper unbatched at 100,000 uL/CU (attacker / keeper; budget) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 100 SOL | 25 bps | 0.00496 | 0.00058 (8.5x; 27 bursts = 0.134 SOL to spend the day's budget) | 0.00106 (4.7x; 4 bursts = 0.020 SOL to spend the day's budget) |
| 100 SOL | 100 bps | 0.01980 | 0.00058 (34.1x; 27 bursts = 0.535 SOL to spend the day's budget) | 0.00106 (18.7x; 4 bursts = 0.079 SOL to spend the day's budget) |
| 1,000 SOL | 25 bps | 0.04951 | 0.00058 (85.4x; 27 bursts = 1.337 SOL to spend the day's budget) | 0.00106 (46.8x; 4 bursts = 0.198 SOL to spend the day's budget) |
| 1,000 SOL | 100 bps | 0.19800 | 0.00058 (341.4x; 27 bursts = 5.346 SOL to spend the day's budget) | 0.00106 (187.3x; 4 bursts = 0.792 SOL to spend the day's budget) |

Under the per-market budget as built at #107–#108 the quiet cadence alone took 88 % of the day's budget at the ceiling unbatched, so a few round trips exhausted the rest; **decision A of 2026-10-01 (OWNER-M7-13) moves bursts to the global budget** (below).

**The global keeper budget (owner decision A of 2026-10-01, SPEC §14 M7-13 / M7-14; #109; normative in ARCH §7.1 duty 8; PROPOSED defaults, §15, owner item OWNER-M7-17, adopted by the owner with O18 on 2026-10-09, §14 N25).** One daily budget `G` (`global_daily_lamports`, 1 SOL) replaces the per-market budgets as the primary control and funds markets **in order of their lenders' value at risk**:

- **Value at risk** `V_m = ⌊ΣB_Q·min(P_ema, P_ACC_MAX)/WAD⌋` lamports (`Market.borrowed_q`, `Market.ema_price`; U256 intermediate). It is what the lenders are owed in the protocol's own smoothed price — the product on which rent accrues (§7) and a settlement's immediate token value `⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋` (§8.6; S2/S3, and S1 since AIR-21; 0 on a venue that cannot execute since AIR-09, #117) —, it rises as a pump carries positions toward their thresholds, and no trade moves it within a transaction. Open notional (`Σ N_open`) is frozen at each open's quote and lowered ≥ 0.9× by a sale-wrapped open (M7-11, §6.1); lender counts are free to inflate with wallets (M7-SEC-9). The caps bound it: `V` ≤ ≈ `7.5·φ` of the pool's conservative SOL depth (≤ 11 % at the 150-bps fee cap; §6.1) and `Σ N_open` ≤ 25 SOL in beta.
- **Covered** markets — open positions and `V ≥ var_floor_lamports` (1 SOL) — are ranked by `V`. Each is funded an **entitlement** of `(1 + reserve_margin_bps)` × its quiet day at its own per-crank charge (2× by default; before its first crank the keeper's measured average, or `⌈5,000 / batch⌉` at a start, re-estimated at its first crank: #110) in rank order at 00:00 UTC — at most once a UTC day: a market that leaves and re-joins gets nothing more, and what a join takes from lower-ranked markets pays no burst and returns to them when it leaves (#110) —; the rest of `G` is the **free pool**. **Below the floor** a market with positions gets the 30-s idle cadence only (no per-second coverage of any kind, read at that cadence — and, while a position is near an exit, in the seconds after an observation while its reference is fresh, so the exit lands the second after the idle crank: #110), drawn from the free pool within `tail_share_bps` (20 %) of `G` — except the crank an exit waits on, which is exit-critical (#110); markets without positions keep their warm-ups and listing windows (duties 6, 8) from the free pool.
- **A charge** (a market's share of a crank transaction) draws on the market's own entitlement, then the free pool, then — exit-critical and baseline cranks only, **never bursts or liquidity-anomaly coverage** — the entitlements of lower-ranked markets, lowest first (exit-critical: at most one crank per market a second, a burst's further cranks discretionary: #110); what is still uncovered is refused (the market is degraded at once: opens paused, exits continue), except an exit-critical crank — or a warm-up of a market without positions, which its listing or graduation pays for ≥ 100× over — which is sent and booked to an alerted overdraft (OWNER-M7-6 (a), lender safety first). Exits, pool selection, graduation moves, `crank_sol_usd` and `set_coverage_pause` stay uncharged.

A burst storm degrades only its target: it spends the target's entitlement and the free pool, never another market's entitlement, so the other markets keep their baseline. Degrading one chosen market costs 29–33× the as-built per-market budget's bursts (1,567 against 48 at zero priority, 434 against 15 at the ceiling); once a storm has spent the free pool, each further market costs only its own entitlement (9 / 27 bursts). All 20 markets together cost 8.6 / 4.7 SOL of round trips on 100-SOL 25-bps pools against 4.8 / 1.5 SOL as built; that total is about `G`'s worth of keeper bursts against `n` × 0.03 SOL as built, so the global budget matches the per-market design protocol-wide at `n` ≈ 33 covered markets per SOL of `G` (OWNER-M7-17).

**LP-wrapped liquidity-anomaly triggers** (duty 2, OWNER-M7-2) under the same budget: 8 keeper cranks per isolated trigger, at most one per 11 s and market; cells: triggers / the attacker's wrapped cranks (SOL) / market-hours at one trigger per 11 s:

A trigger costs its attacker one transaction against the keeper's 8 cranks, so the free pool is cheap to drain this way (≈ 0.1–0.7 SOL a day, spread over ≥ 3 markets for the unbatched case, ≥ 18 for the batched one); after it each market keeps 120 (unbatched) to 720 (batched) triggers' worth. Against the per-market budget as built (390 triggers, 0.0019 SOL per market against an unbatched keeper at zero priority, the as-built table above) the first market costs 58×; the residual is OWNER-M7-2.

**Junk markets below the floor** (M7-SEC-8's economic side, OWNER-M7-14): listing ≥ 0.035 SOL each (ARCH §3.2: account rent at `(len + 128)·5,080` plus the 0.005 fee, 0.035–0.038 SOL; the review's ≈ 0.047 took the older rent rate); a min-notional (0.1 SOL) Tier D position on a self-lent junk market costs its attacker ≈ 0.0008 SOL a day (renewed every 72 h: the open fee and 2 × 1 % of venue fees, plus the protocol's 15 % of 100 % APR); the keeper gives such a market its idle cadence from the tail only:

| priority price (uL/CU) | keeper per junk market-day, k = 6: junk markets that fill the tail | unbatched |
|---|---|---|
| 0 | 0.0024: 84 markets, attacker 2.94 SOL once + 0.066 SOL a day | 0.0144: 14 markets, attacker 0.49 SOL once + 0.011 SOL a day |
| 1,000 | 0.0025: 80 markets, attacker 2.80 SOL once + 0.063 SOL a day | 0.0145: 14 markets, attacker 0.49 SOL once + 0.011 SOL a day |
| 10,000 | 0.0036: 56 markets, attacker 1.96 SOL once + 0.044 SOL a day | 0.0156: 13 markets, attacker 0.46 SOL once + 0.010 SOL a day |
| 100,000 | 0.0143: 15 markets, attacker 0.53 SOL once + 0.012 SOL a day | 0.0263: 8 markets, attacker 0.28 SOL once + 0.006 SOL a day |

The keeper's spend on junk below the floor is at most `tail_share_bps` of `G` (0.2 SOL a day) in idle cranks however many markets are listed — as built it was 0.0024–0.0263 SOL per junk market-day with no bound on their number —, and junk below the floor never touches a covered market's entitlement; beyond the tail its idle cranks are refused and the junk markets degrade (their own opens paused). The same cap applies to honest markets below the floor, which then degrade too — but their exits do not stop: the crank an exit waits on (an expired position, or one whose EMA leg holds with its live CR below `L`, waiting only for a fresh reference) is exit-critical and overdraws beyond the tail, a few cranks per position that exits, which junk cannot sustain without being liquidated or expired itself (#110; as built at #109 it was refused like any idle crank).

**Junk markets above the floor that outrank a real market** (exit-critical hovering): to take a covered market's coverage, junk must rank above it — hold more value at risk `V_J > V_R` on each junk market — and spend `G` through cranks the keeper cannot refuse: a Tier D position held near its threshold (live CR < 1.3·L, the price `h = cr_open/(1.3·L)` = 1.76× its open) makes the keeper crank every second all day (C). Per junk market the notional `V/h`, the capital `(cr_open − 1)·N` of margin plus a `2·N` lender deposit (U = 50 %), and a day's cost the open fee and `2·φ` (1 %) on `N` every 72 h plus the protocol's 15 % of 287.5 % APR on `V`:

| V_R | priority (uL/CU) | C, keeper per junk market-day | junk markets | their notional at open | attacker capital | attacker per day | keeper per day | keeper / attacker per junk market-day |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 | 14 | 8.0 | 39.8 | 0.076 | 1.008 | 13.2x |
| 1 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 | 3 | 1.7 | 8.5 | 0.016 | 1.283 | 78.5x |
| 5 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 | 14 | 39.8 | 199.1 | 0.381 | 1.008 | 2.6x |
| 5 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 | 3 | 8.5 | 42.7 | 0.082 | 1.283 | 15.7x |
| 25 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 | 14 | 199.1 | 995.3 | 1.906 | 1.008 | 0.5x |
| 25 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 | 3 | 42.7 | 213.3 | 0.409 | 1.283 | 3.1x |

Such an attack degrades every market ranked below `V_J` (opens paused; their exit-critical cranks continue on the overdraft), never one above it. The pool depth the caps need for `V_J` comes on top — `V` ≤ ≈ `7.5·φ` × the conservative SOL depth —, so a junk curve market holds at most ≈ 3–11 SOL of `V` (7.5 × 1.25 % of its 30–115 SOL of virtual SOL, §1.1), and `V_J` = 25 SOL needs ≈ 222 SOL of locked or burned pool depth even at the 150-bps fee cap. The keeper's daily cost exceeds the attacker's below ≈ 13 SOL of `V` at zero priority (13× at 1 SOL) and at every beta-size `V` at the ceiling (78.5× at 1 SOL, 3.1× at 25 SOL); what bounds it is the attacker's capital, ≈ 2.8 SOL per SOL of `V` on every junk market, and that it reaches only the markets ranked below.

**With bursts (#110, M7D-4).** Exit-critical is one crank per market a second; a burst's further cranks are discretionary — the junk market's own entitlement and the free pool only, never another market's entitlement nor what its own join took from lower-ranked markets (M7D-3). Junk hovering **and** bursting on its own pools (one round trip a minute, 1,440 a day) therefore drains the free pool and its own entitlements with the bursts while the hovering still has to take the 20 real markets' entitlements (`keeper_cost.py`: `n = max(⌈20·E/C⌉, ⌈F₀/(D − E)⌉)`, `F₀ = G − 20·E`; C exit-critical and D discretionary per junk market-day; a round trip on the junk's own pool costs `2·δ·V/7.5` of fees whatever the fee — the depth the caps need for `V` —, all lost at the full fee, only the venue protocol's 5 bps of 100 on a self-LP pool):

| V_R | priority (uL/CU) | C / D, keeper per junk market-day | junk markets (hovering -> and bursting) | attacker capital | attacker per day (full fee / self-LP pool) | keeper per day |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 / 0.4032 | 14 -> 3 | 8.5 | 11.443 / 0.608 | 1.134 |
| 1 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 / 0.7350 | 3 -> 2 | 5.7 | 7.628 / 0.405 | 1.342 |
| 5 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 / 0.4032 | 14 -> 3 | 42.7 | 57.127 / 2.955 | 1.134 |
| 5 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 / 0.7350 | 3 -> 2 | 28.4 | 38.085 / 1.970 | 1.342 |
| 25 SOL | 0 | 0.0720 / 0.4032 | 14 -> 3 | 213.3 | 285.550 / 14.686 | 1.134 |
| 25 SOL | 100,000 | 0.4275 / 0.7350 | 3 -> 2 | 142.2 | 190.367 / 9.791 | 1.342 |

Bursting cuts the junk markets needed from 14 / 3 to 3 / 2 and their capital ≈ 4.7× / 1.5×, at the price of the round trips: at `V` 1 SOL 0.61 / 0.41 SOL a day on a self-LP pool — about half the keeper's 1.13 / 1.34 SOL —, 11.4 / 7.6 SOL at the full fee; at 25 SOL 14.7 / 9.8 SOL a day self-LP. As built at #109, before M7D-4, every slot of a burst on a hovering market was exit-critical (≈ 2.5 cranks a second, never throttled: ≈ 1.08 SOL a junk market-day), so one junk market spent `G`. That the free pool is drainable by bursts on any covered market is OWNER-M7-17 (3): (a) accept it — it is what makes a storm on one real market cost 1,567 / 434 round trips —, or (b) confine bursts to their own market's entitlement (hovering junk then needs 14 / 3 again, but a storm degrades a real market after 9 / 27 round trips on its own pool).

**The floor's price for honest small markets** (OWNER-M7-17): a market with less than 1 SOL of `V` gets the 30-s keeper of §11.3, where a Tier C or D position in a 10×/60 s pump ends in S1 (lenders −67.7 % / −58.3 % of the borrowed tokens' terminal value) instead of being liquidated (+0.9 % / +1.7 % at 1 s); a 5× gap is S1 at every cadence. Those rows model a 30-s keeper that liquidates whenever the program accepts. Since #110 (M7D-1) the keeper does: an exit needs a completed bucket at most `ref_max_age_s` (2 s) old, so after each idle crank, while a position is near an exit, the market is read again in the seconds that reference stays fresh and the exit goes then (measured: a 0.3-SOL position after a ×2.3 jump is first liquidatable — the EMA following the 30-s cranks only — 297 s after it and liquidated that second; covered, 11 s; an expiry lands at the deadline's first idle crank + 1 s); and the crank it waits on is exit-critical, never refused once the tail is spent (M7D-2). As built at #109 no below-floor position was liquidated or expired at all — each read came 30 s after the last observation and found its reference stale —, and a covered market's cascade stopped where its `V` fell below the floor, so the floor's price was the lenders' whole position, not §11.3's 30-s rows.

| C | 200 % | 0.1370 | 0.0205 | 0.1164 |

| priority price (uL/CU) | coverage cost (SOL/day) | A | B | C | D |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1,000 | 0.0756 | 368 SOL | 184 SOL | 92 SOL | 46 SOL |
| 10,000 | 0.1076 | 523 SOL | 262 SOL | 131 SOL | 65 SOL |
| 100,000 | 0.4275 | 2,081 SOL | 1,040 SOL | 520 SOL | 260 SOL |

Reading (#107, measured): covering every market every second costs 0.43 SOL per market-day unbatched and 0.072 SOL batched in v1 transactions at zero priority (0.10–0.11 at 10,000 µlamports/CU, 0.35–0.43 at the 100,000 ceiling; the v0 fallback 0.09–0.14 at zero priority), against 0.005–0.04 SOL per day of protocol rent (0.03–0.23 SOL of lender rent) for a market at the 25 SOL beta cap; per-second coverage of every market pays for itself only above ≈ 180–520 SOL of open notional on Tier A/B markets at 1,000–10,000 µlamports/CU. Targeted coverage (duty 1: per second only near thresholds) costs **0.0024 SOL per market-day batched for a quiet market (0.0144 unbatched)** and 0.0059–0.0088 batched with 5 % of the day near a threshold at 0–10,000 µlamports/CU (0.029–0.035 at the ceiling) — v0.4.1's estimate was 0.003–0.03; armed positions, deviation bursts and liquidations cost fractions of a millisol each. Liquidity-anomaly coverage (duty 2, #101) runs only on markets with open positions and costs the keeper what the attacker pays while it is sustained (1:1 per second unbatched; at priority prices above zero the attacker's larger transaction costs more than the keeper's crank); an isolated trigger costs the keeper 8 cranks (3.6–8.0× the attacker's transaction unbatched, 1.3–2.0× batched). **The operating budget (O17, decided 2026-09-30):** every market with open positions is covered at targeted cadence, the ceiling is 100,000 µlamports/CU, and an exhausted budget on a market with positions is degraded mode (below), never a quiet stop (M4G-2); the per-market default 0.03 SOL covered a quiet market's day 2.1–12.5× over batched (1.1–2.1× unbatched) at 0–100,000 µlamports/CU. **Since 2026-10-01 (decision A, #109) one global budget** funds the covered markets in order of value at risk (the block and tables above): 1 SOL a day funds 208 covered markets' entitlements at zero priority batched and 35 at the ceiling (from a keeper start or restart too since #110: the batched first-crank estimate, re-estimated at each market's first crank), keeps each market's entitlement out of reach of every other market's bursts and of every lower-ranked market, and caps junk below the floor at 0.2 SOL a day. **Open (owner, ARCH §10):** OWNER-M7-17 (its options (3) and (4); the global budget's defaults adopted by the owner with O18 on 2026-10-09, §14 N25, #115), OWNER-M7-2 (LP-wrapped triggers drain the free pool for ≈ 0.1–0.7 SOL a day), OWNER-M7-6 (exit-critical cranks stay never throttled, beyond `G` on an alerted overdraft: a market held near a threshold all day costs 0.072 SOL a day batched, 0.43 unbatched at zero priority, 0.35–0.43 / 0.71–0.79 at the ceiling); OWNER-M7-7 (confirm 0.03 SOL) is superseded by OWNER-M7-17.

**Degraded mode (PROPOSED).** Coverage lapses when the budget is exhausted, the keeper is down, or blocks are contested. Without an honest observation in a second, an attacker's atomic bundle achieves what the held model assumes at the atomic cost: the §12.6 held-model numbers become that market's exposure. Throttling must therefore be visible, not silent:

1. **Opens pause.** New opens are paused on a market whose coverage SLO (duties 1–2) the keeper cannot meet, with reason code `KeeperCoverage`; the market page shows it, and the pause lifts when coverage returns. Closes, liquidations, settlements, withdrawals and claims are never paused. **As built (M7, #107; O17 approved the role on 2026-09-30, SPEC §11, §14):** the program's `set_coverage_pause` (ARCH §3.1) lets a separate `coverage_authority` key set or clear one market's `paused_coverage` flag, which only `open_short` reads (`CoveragePaused`); it moves no funds and is inert until Governance installs the key with a timelocked `Authorities` change. The keeper's designated pauser sends it in the tick a market **with open positions** degrades — an outage over `COVERAGE_GAP_S` (40 s since 2026-10-01; `CoverageGap`), 3 consecutive missed required seconds, or a charge the global budget refuses (decision A, #109) — and clears it after 600 s of restored coverage; a flag it finds set (a restart, another instance) is lifted only through that recovery; without the key, or with the role unset or rotated, it pages the Emergency multisig, whose `set_pause` is then the path. `coverage_authority` sets and clears the flag; **since the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 (OWNER-M7-3 (b), SPEC §11, §14 M7-3) the Emergency multisig (`pause_authority`) may also clear it — never set it** —, so a lost or hijacked coverage key can no longer hold a market's opens paused for the `Authorities` timelock (≥ 86,400 s): each of its sets is undone by one Emergency clear; nothing moves funds, and opens resume 5 s after any clear (`COVERAGE_CLEAR_DELAY_S`). A pause costs 10,000–10,893 lamports (two signatures, 8,091–8,110 CU on 1.0.1, 8,085–8,104 at M10; the table above), a degraded episode two of them. The drill (the keeper's `coverage_pause` scenarios, every CI run): opens fail `CoveragePaused` while liquidations, closes, `add_margin`, settlements, withdrawals and claims land. **#108 (the M7 security review):** a clear takes effect for opens only `COVERAGE_CLEAR_DELAY_S` (5 s) later, so the key cannot bracket an open on a paused market in one transaction (M7-SEC-3), and the keeper alerts a clear it did not send and an open that lands while it holds the market degraded; its timing tolerates the gap between its `confirmed` read and its transactions' landing (M7-SEC-1): as built at #107 an idle crank landing one second later than the previous one relative to its read was a 31-s `CoverageGap` and degraded mode, and a recovery never completed while the sending ticks waited for their confirmations — a quiet market with one healthy position was paused 99.7 % of two hours under 0–1-s landing jitter; now 0 s in two hours under 0–2-s jitter with slow ticks.
2. **On-chain signal.** Each integration that follows a gap of more than `COVERAGE_GAP_S` = **40 s** (owner decision of 2026-10-01, OWNER-M7-12; was 30 s) on a market with open positions emits `CoverageGap{market, gap_s, open_positions}`; indexers and the UI show coverage per market. **#108:** at the keeper's 30-s idle cadence the landing jitter makes 31–32-s gaps on healthy markets (measured: 79 of 241 integrations in two hours under 0–2-s jitter), which the 30-s constant reported as gaps; at 40 s none is emitted, every `CoverageGap` is a real outage (the keeper degrades on each), and indexers need no tolerance. The constant has no economic effect (§15).
3. **Existing positions** keep their locked rules (A26). Their exposure during degraded mode is disclosed as the held-model bounds of §12.6. Right after a venue switch whose 8 warm-up seconds the keeper misses entirely it is §12.12's degraded mode (the attacker can then seed `L_ema = D`). After a switch whose guard-set seconds the keeper misses (duty 9), a deposit freeze can clear the guard; since #99 the carried positions fit the new pool's caps, so it delays their exits as in steady state (§12.12 (11)), and the §8.2 cap bounds the pushed reference at `λ/λ_min` of the depth.
4. **Not adopted: raising persistence in degraded mode.** Persistence is a locked per-position term, so a raise could reach only new positions, which degraded mode pauses anyway; and 5 updates already break Tier C liveness (§5.7).

---

## 6. Caps and tier assignment

### 6.1 Size caps

With `X₀` the conservative token reserve before the open (§2.1: curve `vT`, or `min(vT, x_ema)` while `P_ema > P_spot` — #103, below; AMMs `min(X_now, ⌊L_slow·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋)`, Raydium and — since #105 (M6R-1) — PumpSwap also `≤ ⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(λ_min_bps·isqrt⁺(P_ema))⌋`, with `λ_min_bps` = `pool_lock_min_bps` on Raydium and `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10,000 on PumpSwap since #106 (OWNER-M6D-1), i.e. `⌊D·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋` there — now implemented in the sim, R3A-3 ii) and `X₁ = X₀ + Q`. The impact caps' closed forms below are real-valued; the normative integer forms are the sim's `max_q_impact`, `isqrt(⌊X₀²·(10⁴ + ι_pos)/10⁴⌋) − X₀`, and `max_total_q_market`, `X₁ − isqrt(⌊X₁²·10⁴/(10⁴ + ι_mkt)⌋)`, which the flooring inside the root can put one raw unit above the real-valued market bound:

| Cap | Rule | Closed form |
|---|---|---|
| Per position (SPEC) | buying `Q` back from post-open reserves moves price ≤ ι_pos (5 %; D 3 %) | `Q ≤ X₀(√(1+ι_pos) − 1)` → 2.470 % / 1.489 % of X₀ |
| Per position, fee-scaled (PROPOSED) | `Q ≤ 3.00·φ·X₀` (φ in ppm, §2.1; on the pump venues `φ = min(φ_live, φ at P_ema)` since #108, M7-SEC-5) | binds when φ < 0.82 % (D: 0.50 %) |
| Per market (SPEC) | buying all outstanding back moves price ≤ ι_mkt (20 %; D 10 %) | `ΣQ ≤ X₁(1 − 1/√(1+ι_mkt))` → 8.713 % / 4.654 % of X₁ |
| Per market, fee-scaled (PROPOSED) | `ΣQ ≤ 7.50·φ·X₁` | binds when φ < 1.16 % (D: 0.62 %) |
| Utilisation (SPEC) | `B_Q + Q ≤ 0.9·(T + B_Q)` | |
| Per wallet (ADOPTED Q10) | 25 % of the market OI cap | per-(wallet, market) PDA — per key, so 4 wallets fill the OI cap: a UX limit, not a self-dealing bound (M7-SEC-9; no bound here relies on it, §12.5's basket uses the full OI cap) |
| Tier D graduation guard | `ΣQ + Q ≤ γ·(rT_guard + Q)`, γ = 50 % | `rT_guard` = the curve's real tokens at `P_ema` when that is the smaller (#103) |
| Minimum / beta (ADOPTED Q11, Q21) | `N_open ≥ 0.1 SOL`; Σ N_open per market ≤ 25 SOL during beta | |

**The curve's cap reserves (#103; M5-SEC-3; ARCH §4.6).** The curve's own `L = isqrt(vT·vQ)` does not move with a curve trade (§2.3), so the curve at `P_ema` is `reprice(L, P_ema)`, with real tokens `x_ema − (vT − rT)`. v0.4.1 sized a curve open on the live `vT` and guarded Tier D with the live `rT`; an opener that wraps its open in its own sale, `[sell(δ), open(q), buy(δ)]`, lifts both by `δ` for the open only — the open's 10 % spot–EMA check lets `δ` reach ≈ 5 % of `vT`, the whole `rT` near completion — so the graduation guard stopped nothing (the M5 reproducer: `ΣQ/rT` 0.87 → 0.89 against γ 0.50 in one transaction on ECON's D@90 % curve). Both caps now read the curve at the EMA price whenever that is the smaller one: `X₀ = min(vT, x_ema)` and `rT_guard = min(rT, max(0, x_ema − (vT − rT)))` while `P_ema > P_spot`, the live `(vT, rT)` unrounded otherwise — the rule PumpSwap and Raydium already had (`min(X_now, reprice(L_slow, P_ema).X)`), applied to the curve. Nothing changes at a steady price (every ECON scenario opens at `P_ema = P_spot` and every §11 and §12 table except §12.5's basket is unchanged); consecutive capped curve opens now wait for the EMA to follow each open's own sale, as on PumpSwap: §12.5's held S1-forcing basket holds 3 positions / 4.23 SOL on D@0 % (was 4 / 4.68) and 4 / 7.11 SOL on D@50 % (was 4 / 7.42), and the fork's basket fills 3 positions at 4.279 % of `vT` instead of 4 at 4.654 %. **Residual (not changed):** `N_open` — the open fee and the notional limits — is still quoted on the post-open live reserves, so the same-transaction sale can lower it by up to ≈ 10 %; the round trip costs ≈ 2.5 % of `δ` to save ≈ 10 % of a 0.25 % fee, so it does not pay for the fee, and the D4 margin already takes `max(B̂_spot, B̂_ema)`. **For the launch guard it pays on thin pools (M7-SEC-4, #108; OWNER-M7-11 — accepted and disclosed by the owner on 2026-10-01, SPEC §14 M7-11, recorded under ARCH O19; #109):** each wrapped open records ≥ 0.90 of its honest quote (measured 0.9096 on the mock), so a market holds up to ≈ 1/0.9 of `market_max_notional` in honestly quoted notional (measured 27.00 SOL against the 25-SOL guard, +8.0 %), and a buy before the open undercuts `min_notional` by ≈ 9 %; the review's fix (`N_open` at `max(B̂_spot, B̂_ema)`), which would close it at a cost to every honest open of its own price impact (+0.02 / +0.22 / +0.82 % of `N_open` and of the fee for 0.1 / 1 / 3 SOL on a 1,000-SOL 25-bps pool, +0.002 / +0.022 / +0.080 % on a 10,000-SOL 1 % one; `m7_sec_it`), is not adopted: the guard is exceedable by at most ≈ 11 % of honestly quoted notional, disclosed. Sim: `Market.curve_cap_reserves`; program: `engine::caps::curve_reserves`.

**PumpSwap: sized on the burned LP's locked depth `D` (M6R-1, #105; OWNER-M6D-1, #106; the owner's decisions of 2026-09-30; §1.2).** Canonical PumpSwap pools take third-party LP, so a locked-depth term bounds `X₀` as on Raydium, with `D` the depth of the burned creation LP (`λ` from `Pool.lp_supply` and the LP mint, §1.2) — and **since #106 at `λ_min` = `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 100 %**: `X₀ = min(X_now, ⌊L_slow·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋, ⌊D·10⁹/isqrt⁺(P_ema)⌋)`, the burned LP's token reserve at `P_ema`, and with it every cap on `X₀ + q` (per position, market OI, fee-scaled, wallet, utilisation) and the tier (§6.2). A canonical pool's lock is its burned LP, so `λ_min` has no eligibility role there; Raydium keeps `pool_lock_min_bps`. **At the open** nothing a single transaction can do inflates the caps, the margin, the tier or `Λ`: `X₀`'s live term `X_now` is dropped by the minimum with `L_slow/√P_ema` and `D/√P_ema` (which a deposit does not move), the tier's `R_eff = min(R_now, R_slow, …)` likewise (§6.2), the margin prices `max(B̂_spot, B̂_ema)` on the ring's cached EMA reserves (§4), the fee tier reads the price, not the depth (`attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` 3 (c): a 3× deposit wrapped around the open, the position at the honest cap, 1.00×); the residual is the curve's `N_open` one above. **A deposit held** for minutes to hours pushes `L_slow` (τ = 1 h), and `D/√P_ema` keeps it out of sizing (3 (b): after 1× / 3× / 7× the pool held 1–4 h, `q/X` after the withdrawal **1.00×** the honest cap; #105's `D/λ_min` allowed 1.63× / 2.00× / 2.00×, M6 as built 1.63–7.86×). **Why not `D/λ_min` (M6D-1, found by the #105 verification):** that bound is Raydium's guarantee — at least `λ_min` of the depth a position was sized on stays — and a position sized on a held deposit whose LP leaves in the first second of a genuine pump is then on a pool with half (at `λ_min` 50 %) the depth it was sized on while `L_slow` (τ 1 h) still credits the deposit: the exit reference sits at the cap `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`, every liquidation fails its exit bound and the position gaps into S1 (§12.12 (12) (e), #105's column: 3× held 1 h, the open at 95 % of the 2.00× cap, a 5×/120-s pump — S1 +102 s, lenders −29.3 % of `Q`'s value, the borrower zeroed; 10×/120 s −50.8 %; the fork −37.9 %). Sized on `D` every such row is liquidated at +21–67 s, the lenders +4.1–4.2 % ((12) (e), #106). **Cost:** a pool with genuine third-party LP is sized at λ of its live depth (e/acc 0.93×, a pool with a third of its LP third-party 0.67×). **Left (the margin, not the size):** the open's margin prices the buyback on the pool as it is, so a position opened while a deposit is held posts a little less margin than the same `q` on `D` — at Tier C's edges ((12) (f)) a hold at 2.00× liquidates where the control stays open and a 3× one-second gap settles S1 instead of a liquidation with the borrower zeroed, the lenders whole in both (residual 16). On the example pools A / B / C (λ = 1, no third-party LP) `D` is the whole depth, no `D` term binds and no table changes.

Caps are checked at open, and at a pool adoption that carries open positions (#99, below); they never force a close. **Why fee-scaled caps:** manipulating a constant-product pool for one second costs about φ times the liquidity moved, while the payoff of forcing and sandwiching exits grows with the size at risk; 25 bps Raydium pools are 3.6–5× cheaper to manipulate than pump venues. Held-model basket attack (§12.5) by ratio (re-run on v0.4.1; positions below the 0.1 SOL minimum notional are no longer counted):

| fee_oi_cap_x100 | market | OI cap notional (SOL) | positions / margin (SOL) | held basket net (SOL) | borrower margin lost (SOL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| off | A | 217.82 | 6 / 280.87 | -101.61 | 182.83 |
| off | B | 87.13 | 6 / 162.85 | -49.69 | 132.42 |
| off | RA | 217.82 | 14 / 250.23 | +138.00 | 217.96 |
