**OWNER** visibility: this tightens SPEC's size caps. On PumpSwap the held basket attack is unprofitable even without the market cap, so `fee_oi_cap_x100 = 1000` would leave PumpSwap A/B unchanged at the cost of a larger Raydium residual (RA +16.7 SOL); `500` removes the Raydium residual at the cost of about half of the PumpSwap Tier A/B OI capacity.

| tier | pos. impact | max Q per position | mkt impact | max total Q (OI cap) | per-wallet (25% of OI cap) | S2 slippage | Q/X at which S2 triggers |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 5% | 2.470% of X0 | 20% | 8.713% of X | 2.178% of X | 20% | 16.67% of X |
| B | 5% | 2.470% of X0 | 20% | 8.713% of X | 2.178% of X | 20% | 16.67% of X |
| C | 5% | 2.470% of X0 | 20% | 8.713% of X | 2.178% of X | 20% | 16.67% of X |
| D | 3% | 1.489% of X0 | 10% | 4.654% of X | 1.163% of X | 30% | 23.08% of X |

**Carried positions: switch only if all fit (OWNER decision, 2026-09-29; #99; SPEC §14).** A pool adoption that carries open positions — a fast-path collection window on a Raydium market, or a list-only market's window with open positions (§6.2, §10.3) — is allowed only if every open position fits the candidate's normal opening caps, evaluated as an open on it would be right after the adoption:

```
X₀(p) = min( X_cand, ⌊min(L_slow, D)·10⁹/isqrt⁺(p)⌋, ⌊D·10⁹·10⁴/(λ_min_bps·isqrt⁺(p))⌋ )     conservative_x on the candidate;
        L_slow capped at its locked depth D as the adoption leaves it, so X₀ ≤ D/√p    (X_cand: a proposal only)
q_max = min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)                                                 (ΣQ alone while max_q_hwm = 0)
φ     = min(φ_inc, φ_cand, CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM) = carried_phi(min_open_fee_ppm, φ_cand)    (#100, FIT-2; the cap #101, FITS-1;
        CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM = 2,500 compiled; min(φ_cand, 2,500) while the mark = 0)
per position:  q_max ≤ min( isqrt(⌊X₀²·(10⁴ + ι_pos)/10⁴⌋) − X₀,  ⌊fee_size_cap_x100·φ·X₀/(100·10⁶)⌋ )
per market:    ΣQ    ≤ min( X₀ − isqrt(⌊X₀²·10⁴/(10⁴ + ι_mkt)⌋),  ⌊fee_oi_cap_x100·φ·X₀/(100·10⁶)⌋ )      (X₁ = X₀)
both at p = min(P_ema, P_cand) and at p = P_ema (the stricter binds), at the proposal and at the execution
```

`X_cand` is the candidate's live token reserve and `P_cand` its pre-trade spot in the instruction; `φ` the fee-scaled caps' fee, **`min(φ_inc, φ_cand, CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM)`** (#100, FIT-2; #101, FITS-1): `φ_cand` the candidate's one-way fee (ppm) and `φ_inc` = `Market.min_open_fee_ppm`, the low-water mark of the one-way venue fee paid by the opens since `open_positions` last returned to 0 — the fee the open positions' caps were computed and their margin posted at (`open_short` sets it at the first open after the market emptied and lowers it at every later one; the exit of the last open position resets it with `max_q_hwm`); it is the incumbent's fee unless that changed or a position was carried from an earlier pool, so a position carried onto a higher-fee pool stays in the size class of the fee it was margined at (0.75 % of `X₀` at 25 bps, not the 2.47 % impact cap of a 1 % pool), and a lower-fee candidate's own fee binds; **`CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM` = 2,500** (25 bps; a compiled constant, PROPOSED, `caps::CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM`) caps it whatever the two fees, so every carried position is in the ≤ 0.75 %-of-`X₀` class that the switch sims and the fork validated under every push — a switch between two 1 % pools included, where `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` is 1 % and the fit limit was the 2.47 % impact cap on a margin posted on the deeper incumbent's cost (FITS-1, §12.12 residual 15); a larger position is not carried and settles S3 at fair value (the accepted fallback, residual 12); the impact caps do not read `φ`; `ι_pos` / `ι_mkt` those of the tier the market would have on the candidate (§6.2; A, B and C share 5 % / 20 % at the defaults, so the tier matters only if governance separates them); `ΣQ = ΣB_Q` the outstanding quantity; `max_q_hwm` the high-water mark of `q` over the positions opened since `open_positions` last returned to 0 (`Market.max_q_hwm`: `open_short` raises it, the exit of the last open position resets it to 0). The check is O(1) and conservative by design: a large position that has closed keeps the mark until the market empties (FIT-3, a disclosed residual: one borrower's round trip at the incumbent's size cap keeps every candidate thinner than that cap refused while any position stays open; the positions then exit through S3 at fair value, each at its own deadline, or are carried once those exits bring `ΣQ` under the candidate's per-position cap, §12.12 residual 14 and (11) (e); #100's first answer, a permissionless `refresh_max_q` scan, was withdrawn because anyone could reset its shared scan state between the keeper's chunks, FITV-1). The market caps use `X₁ = X₀`: nothing is sold into the candidate, so an open's `ΣB_Q + q ≤ OI_cap(X₀ + q)` is taken at `q = 0`. A proposal whose candidate fails is refused (`CarriedPositionsTooLarge`); an execution whose candidate fails cancels the window, and the fast-path chain stays armed. **If no candidate fits, the positions stay on the failed pool and exit through the S3 chain at fair value** (§8.6, D3; O15): a healthy position is frozen at the last `P_ema` until its deadline (§10.3; the frozen `FAST_SCENARIOS` rows: lenders +6.9–7.1 % against `q` at that price). Zero-position adoptions and listings are unaffected. The sim's form is `carried_x0` / `carried_fit_ok` / `Market.carried_fit`.

*What it means.* An open right after an adoption is sized on the candidate's locked depth: the adoption caps `L_slow` at `D` and never raises it (§5.2), so `X₀ ≤ D/√p`. A position at the size cap of a λ 50 % incumbent (0.75 % of its `X` at 25 bps) therefore moves only onto a candidate whose locked depth covers the incumbent's `L_slow` — its whole depth: ≥ 111 % of its reserves at λ 90 %, ≥ 200 % at λ 50 % (§10.1, §10.3, §10.5); a thinner candidate carries a proportionally smaller position (RA's position at an eighth of its cap fits a 314-SOL λ 99.78 % pool). A carried position is thus no larger, relative to the new pool, than a position opened on it at the time of the switch — ≤ 0.75 % of its live `X` at λ ≈ 1 and 25 bps, ≤ 0.375 % at λ 50 % — **when the candidate trades at or below `P_ema`**. After a genuine pump of the candidate during the halt its live `X_cand` binds, and only the honest proposal reads it (FIT-1, below): the guarantee is the execution's `X₀`, up to `λ·√(P_cand/P_ema)` × a native open's cap.

*Why these inputs.* Every price is judged twice, at the M4R-2 floor's `min(P_ema, P_cand)` and at `P_ema`: a lower price credits more tokens to the same depth, so a dump wrapped around a proposal or an execution cannot loosen the fit's **depth terms** (`L_slow'`, `D`), and the check at `P_ema` binds. The execution reads `L_slow`, `D` and `P_ema` only — no `X_cand` — so no unlocked LP's withdrawal or buy wrapped around it can cancel a window that fits (the lever M4X-3 kept out of the execution's floor, §12.12 residual 4, which a borrower whose short went underwater during the halt would pull, to settle at the frozen, lower `P_ema`). A proposal reads `X_cand` too, and **that term is not manipulation-resistant** (FIT-1, #100): it binds when the candidate trades above `P_ema` (`X_cand ≈ D/(λ·√(r·P_ema))` at `P_cand = r·P_ema`, under the locked-depth term `D/√P_ema`), and `[sell ≈X_cand tokens into the candidate, propose_pool, buy them back]` lifts it inside the transaction for two swap fees. No rule that reads the candidate's reserve or spot can be protected within one transaction, and a band on `P_cand` is met by dumping only to `P_ema`; reading `X_cand` at the execution as well would bring back the withdrawal / buy griefing and still not stop a dump wrapped around the execution. The proposal keeps `X_cand` (it binds every honest proposal, the keeper's included), and the stated guarantee is the execution's: `q ≤` the caps on `X₀ = min(L_slow', ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)/√P_ema`, which is up to `λ·√(P_cand/P_ema)` × a native open's cap on the adopted pool — about 1.4× at the carried shorts' own ≈ 2× liquidation threshold (the fork: q/cap 1.085 / 1.109 / 1.366 at `r` 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.9, against 1.094 / 1.223 / 1.377). After a genuine pump during the halt the position is also that much nearer its liquidation; after a genuine dump the fit is stricter (§12.12 residual 13).

*What it buys.* The switch-specific exposure of M4F-1, M4G-1 and M4G-3 — a reference pushed up by capital that blocks the liquidation of a carried position of 3–6 % of the new pool's `X` until S1 — needs a position larger than the new pool's caps. The rule with the fee-scaled caps at the candidate's own fee did not make every push a delay: a 25-bps incumbent's position could move onto a 1 % candidate at its 2.47 % impact cap, on a margin posted on the incumbent's cheaper cost, and a deposit held and pulled at a 4×/30 s pump (`L_slow` crediting it for about an hour) or a freeze after the guard's clear ended it in S1 (FIT-2). With the carried fee caps at `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` (#100) that position is refused and settles S3 at fair value, and at the new fit limit (0.75 % of `X₀`) the same pushes delay its exit by 0–1 s, as they delay a position opened on the pool (§12.12 (11) (d)). That left a switch whose incumbent fee is at least the candidate's: a 1 %→1 % switch still carried up to the 2.47 % impact cap, and under those pushes in a 4×/30 s pump carried positions at 70–100 % of that limit ended in S1 in the sim and at 60–100 % on the fork (FITS-1). **Since #101 the carried fee caps take at most 25 bps** (`CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM`): every carried position, whatever the fees, is in the 0.75 %-of-`X₀` class; the 1 %→1 % positions above that are refused and settle S3 at fair value, and one at the new limit is liquidated within 0–1 s of the control under every push (§12.12 (11) (d) (iii)). The cost is adoptions refused between high-fee pools for positions between 0.75 % of the candidate's `X₀` and its impact cap, which then exit through S3 (residual 12).

### 6.2 Tier function (re-evaluated on every interaction; applies to new opens)

```
D  if venue = pump curve (live or migrating)
else Λ = 2·R_eff                                         (ADOPTED Q19: real SOL only, no VQR/boost; the D bound on Raydium and, #105, PumpSwap: D itself since #106)
     age = now − graduated_at                            (ADOPTED Q18)
     C  if age < 24 h  or  Λ < θ_B  or  the oracle cache is stale (ADOPTED Q8)
     A  if Λ ≥ θ_A  and  age ≥ 7 d
     B  otherwise
```

Inputs: `R_eff = min(R_now, R_slow)` with `R_slow = Y(reprice(L_slow, P_ema)) − VQR_live` (≥ 0); **under a negative VQR (PROPOSED, #103; delegated to the builder; built in M6, #104 — `pamm_it::negvqr`, `pumpswap_negvqr_e2e`; owner item O18):** each PumpSwap input is the smaller of its real and its effective SOL — `R_now = min(quote_vault, quote_vault + VQR)`, `R_slow = Y(reprice(L_slow, P_ema)) − max(VQR_live, 0)` — the conservative reading of Q19 once real SOL can exceed the SOL that prices trades (§1.2); while `VQR ≥ 0` it is Q19's real SOL exactly, so no pool before 2026-09-30 and no sim table changes; ★since pump's 2026-10-02 redeploy (#112, PUMP-2026-10-1, built in M10 program batch 4) `R_now = min(vault − buckets, vault + VQR)` and every `max(VQR, 0)` here reads `max(VQR + buckets, 0)` — v2 trades keep their protocol and creator fees in the vault as buckets (`Pool.protocol_fees` @271, `creator_fees` @279), take them off the VQR (`VQR = boost − buckets`) and pay no sale out of them, so `min(vault, effective)` and `max(VQR, 0)` overstated payable SOL by `min(buckets, boost)`; with no buckets (every SOL pool at slot ≈ 452.995 M, and the sim, whose traders are v1) the reading is unchanged; on Raydium also `R_eff ≤ ⌊D·√P_ema/10⁹⌋·10⁴/λ_min_bps` (locked SOL divided by λ_min, V2-8; rounding in §2.1); **on PumpSwap since #105 (M6R-1)** `R_eff ≤ ⌊max(⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋ − max(VQR_live, 0), 0)·10⁴/λ_min_bps⌋` with `λ_min_bps` = `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10,000 since #106 (OWNER-M6D-1), i.e. `R_eff ≤ max(⌊D·isqrt(P_ema)/10⁹⌋ − max(VQR_live, 0), 0)` — the burned LP's locked SOL less the virtual reserve (real SOL only, Q19; exact at λ = 1, conservative by `(1 − λ)·VQR` otherwise), so a held deposit lifts `Λ` not at all (under #105 by up to `λ/λ_min`, 2×: M6D-1, §6.1, §12.12 (12) (e)), and a deposit wrapped around the open lifts nothing (`R_now` is dropped by the minimum, and it even lowers `R_slow` through `VQR_live`). Age: PumpSwap first on-chain observation of the pool, AMM v4 listing time, CPMM `min(listing, max(open_time, T_CLAMP))`.

**Age from listing (O13, extended, R3B-12).** A token that graduated to PumpSwap months ago, or a CPMM pool whose `open_time` is before `T_CLAMP`, but is listed today gets `graduated_at` = listing time: it is Tier C for 24 h and at most Tier B until day 7 — higher margins, lower caps and shorter durations than SPEC §4's criteria would give it. This is conservative and applies equally to AMM v4 (unusable `pool_open_time`), PumpSwap and pre-`T_CLAMP` CPMM pools; markets created before graduation observe the transition live and are unaffected. The UI's "tier and why" shows it; owner item O13 now covers all three.

**Raydium pool selection (economic side, decision 7; as built after M4, #91–#95; the M4R rules PROPOSED, #96).** A market adopts a different Raydium pool without a failure only while it has **no open position** (with the 25 % depth margin, 5 % inside the listing window); the one-hour pending switch (`pool_switch_delay_s`) is gone. Open positions therefore see a pool change only through a collection window — the fast path on incumbent failure (§10.3), or a list-only market with positions — only onto a pool on which every one of them fits the opening caps (#99, §6.1), and every change rewrites the ring's liquidity to the anchor and restarts the observation state (§5.2).

**Eligibility** reads neither `λ_now` nor the live SOL reserve (M4-SEC-1, M4-SEC-3). Besides `venue_executable` and the fee cap, with `locked = ⌊D·isqrt(P_ref)/10⁹⌋` the pool's locked SOL at `P_ref` = the market's `P_ema` (the candidate's own spot on a market without a price):

```
listing, zero-position or list-only adoption (no position moves):  ⌊locked·10⁴/λ_min⌋ ≥ pool_min_real_sol   (locked-depth floor)
collection window's candidate (its adoption moves open positions): locked ≥ pool_min_real_sol              (window floor, M4V-1)
                                                                   and every open position fits its caps (#99, §6.1)
```

The locked-depth floor is exactly the `D` term of `R_eff` above, so a pool it admits can also be opened on; it keeps v0.4.1's lasting guarantee (at least `λ_min` of the floor stays locked whatever the unlocked LPs do) and also admits fully locked pools of 12.5–25 SOL and pools with `λ < λ_min` whose locked SOL is large enough. The window floor is checked when a proposal opens or improves a window and again at the execution (the `/λ_min` form moved open positions onto fully locked 13–20 SOL pools without any manipulation, M4V-1). **Where it is judged (M4R-2, PROPOSED, #96).** `P_ema` does not move while the incumbent cannot execute, so a floor judged at `P_ema` alone — M4 as built, at the proposal and at the execution — admits, after a dump during the halt, a pool whose real locked SOL at the live price is only `floor·√(P_live/P_ema)`: 50 % of the floor after a 4× dump, 25 % after 16× (the M4R re-verification; ARCH's "slightly under a floor" understated it). A proposal that opens or improves a window therefore judges the window floor at `p_win = min(P_ema, P_cand)`, `P_cand` the candidate's pre-trade `⌊Y·WAD/X⌋` in the proposing instruction; the execution keeps `P_ema`, and every failure there cancels as before. After an honest dump the proposal is refused (`PoolTooThin`) and no window opens (`switch_warmup.py` (6): the 15.0- and 7.5-SOL pools of a 4× / 16× dump refused; as built adopted); after a pump `P_ema` is the lower price and nothing changes. **Residual (disclosed, §12.12):** a swap by the proposer moves only its own `P_cand`, so a dump wrap can only hurt its own proposal, but the thin pool's backer can pump-wrap its window-opening proposal back to the frozen price (0.03–0.04 SOL in the sim), which restores the M4 judgment at `P_ema` — never looser; the warm-up then lowers `P_ema` towards the live price (§5.2; since M6-SEC-1, #105, by at most one v0.4.1 step per integration — 1.86× the pool at the gate after a 4× dump, 7.44× after 16×, `switch_warmup.py` (6) — and the steady-state EMA after it, onto the pool within about a minute), so once the adopted pool's listing window has run (`PoolListWindow`) opens, tiers and `R_eff` see the pool as it is and a new open is refused `PoolTooThin`; no exit is forced while the seed lags (the spot leg reads the live price). So open positions move only onto a pool whose locked SOL alone reached `pool_min_real_sol` at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` when the proposal that opened or last improved the window ran, unless the pool's backer paid for that wrap, and at `P_ema` at the execution — and, since #99, whose opening caps every one of them fits at the same prices (§6.1). A genuine dump **inside** the 60-s window, after that proposal, is judged only at `P_ema` and adopted (M4X-3, disclosed with §12.12 residual 4; never looser than M4 as built): the execution does not re-read the candidate's spot, because that one-instruction sample would let a dump-wrapped execution either hold every other proposal (a revert, the M4V-4 freeze) or cancel windows until S3 (a cancel). Neither floor moves with an unlocked deposit or withdrawal, and on a market with a price the listing floor and the execution's floor do not move inside one transaction; a standing unlocked deposit can no longer keep every alternate pool ineligible (v0.4.1's `λ_now ≥ λ_min` clause froze a halted market's positions at the pre-halt `P_ema` until S3, M4-SEC-1). Sizing is unchanged: every open is bounded by `D/λ_min` (§6.1 and `R_eff` above), whatever `λ` is.

**First open after a price start** (PROPOSED, M4V-5, unified with the post-change confirmation by M4R, #96; `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS`, §15). On a market without a price — a Raydium listing, or a list-only market's first adoption — the floor is judged at the candidate's own spot, which `[buy, create_market or propose_pool, sell]` can push, and the listing or adoption starts `P_ema` there. Such a market has no position; the start marks its observation state unconfirmed (§5.2), and its first open waits until `ema_warm = 0` — `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` = `RING_LEN` (8) integrations since the start, every one of a bucket recorded after it — as well as the listing window; the reference keeper observes it every second at the end of its listing window. The warm-up re-seeds `P_ema` towards the lowest post-start bucket low, so a start price pushed **up** falls as soon as one warm-up bucket holds an honest observation — since M6-SEC-1 (#105) by at most one v0.4.1 step per integration (§5.2 (P1), (P5): ×11/12 at `dt` = 1, ≤ ×0.4986 by the gate), the rest through the steady-state EMA over the listing window, the safe side where the spot/EMA check refuses opens (before M4R the 8 buckets moved `P_ema` only 77–93 % of the way); one pushed down recovers by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration (§5.2 (P5): ×7/6 to ×9/7 each, the rest over the listing window's observations) — the safe side, where the spot/EMA check and `R_eff` refuse opens — and the rest of the listing window integrates normally. The first open therefore no longer reads a price the adopting transaction chose: a capturer has to fill all 8 warm-up buckets with attacker-only seconds and then hold the pushed price against every observation of the listing window (a round trip in each second the keeper observes, §5.2's freeze) instead of pushing it once.

### 6.3 USD thresholds (ADOPTED Q8)

θ_A = $500 k and θ_B = $100 k of pool liquidity are converted with a cached Pyth SOL/USD price — since #108 (M7-SEC-7) at `price − conf`, the low end of its confidence interval, so the uncertainty never tiers a market more generously; the boundary is fuzzy by ≈ ±0.5 % (any newer verified print since the sponsor's last 0.5 % update can be cranked). If the cache is older than `oracle_stale_s` — **600 s**, its floor, since the owner's decision of 2026-10-01 (OWNER-M7-15, SPEC §14 M7-15; was 3,600 s) — graduated markets tier as C (fail-safe): a stalled feed during a 5–10 % SOL move can tier a market near θ one level too generously for at most 10 minutes. The keeper cranks `crank_sol_usd` every ≤ 30 s and alerts at 300 s (§15). Exits never read the oracle. The sponsored feed updates on a 55 s heartbeat or a 0.5 % move, so the crank accepts updates up to `oracle_max_age_s = 120 s` old; only tiering of new opens depends on it.

---

## 7. Rent

SPEC §6.1 kinked curve; minimum rent fixed at open; 85/15 split; no protocol share in settlements.

| tier | base | slope1 (per 100% U) | slope2 (per 100% U) | rate@80% | rate@90% (cap) | rate@100% | min rent @80% | max-duration rent @80% | max-duration rent @90% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 10% | 50.00% | 1000% | 50% | 150% | 250% | 0.006% | 4.11% | 12.33% |
| B | 25% | 93.75% | 1500% | 100% | 250% | 400% | 0.011% | 3.84% | 9.59% |
| C | 50% | 187.50% | 3000% | 200% | 500% | 800% | 0.023% | 3.84% | 9.59% |
| D | 100% | 375.00% | 5500% | 400% | 950% | 1500% | 0.183% | 3.29% | 7.81% |

**Implementation (normative; the sims now use exactly this form, R3A-3).** The position locks its rate curve and, since v0.4, its protocol share. Three market-level basis indices integrate the curve in O(1):

```
k = util_kink_bps (market-level, own version; adopted only with no open position — R3A-9)
accrue(Δt):  J0 += P̃·Δt ;  J1 += ⌈P̃·min(U, k)·Δt/10⁴⌉ ;  J2 += ⌈P̃·max(U − k, 0)·Δt/10⁴⌉      P̃ = min(P_ema, P_ACC_MAX), before the roll
rent_accrued(pos) = ⌈Q·(base·ΔJ0 + slope1·ΔJ1 + slope2·ΔJ2)/(10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌉            (ΔJ since open)
lender credit per accrual = ⌊(Σ_j A_j·δJ_j + rem) / (10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌋,   A_j = Σ_open Q·coef_j·(10⁴ − rent_protocol_bps_pos),
                            rem carried in Market.lender_credit_rem (AIR-03, #117)
true-up at exit: credited_for(pos) = ⌈Q·Σ_j coef_j·ΔJ_j·(10⁴ − rent_protocol_bps_pos)/(10⁴·10⁴·31,536,000·WAD)⌉
```

**Protocol share per position (R3A-5, R3B-9).** v0.3 applied the global `rent_protocol_bps` at every accrual and again at the true-up, so a governance change while a position was open left lenders an entitlement with no SOL behind it (15 % → 30 %: a phantom receivable of 0.075 SOL on the reviewer's 0.5 SOL example) or took `receivable` belonging to other positions. v0.4 snapshots it at open and folds `(10⁴ − p)` into `A_j`, so continuous credits and the per-position true-up describe the same money; the pool fuzz changes the value at random and asserts `receivable = 0` whenever no position is open (§9.6). A governance change applies to new positions only (A26 is now accurate). Because the accruals' credits sum to the floor of their exact total (the remainder carried between accruals, AIR-03, #117) while each true-up ceils per position, `receivable` never goes negative before the clamp `receivable −= min(credited_for, receivable)`, which ARCH §3.3 states explicitly. As first built each accrual floored its credit alone and kept no remainder: a market accrued every second (any permissionless `crank_price`) lost up to a lamport per market-second, and all of the continuous rent of a position crediting under a lamport a second (a 0.1 SOL Tier A position at 15 % APR: 34,932 lamports a day), which reached `lender_sol` with no entitlement behind it and stayed unclaimable.

---

## 8. Exits: execution bounds and waterfalls (SPEC §2.2, §5.2–5.3, §6.2–6.3)

Notation: `V` vault lamports, `R = R_due`, `p` locked penalty bps, `K_s` settlement reward (§5.6), `ert` effective venue-side rent tolerance (6,500,000 lamports on pump venues — the worst PumpSwap buy with every optional account missing costs 5,811,520 — and 0 on Raydium), `ε` venue rounding tolerance of the expiry bound (10 bps).

### 8.1 Close (borrower)

Preconditions: a route exists and `B̂_live + R + ert ≤ V`. `max_in = min(max_buyback_cost, V − R − ert)`. Closing while liquidatable is allowed with no penalty. Waterfall: `B` (measured spend) → `R` (85 % lenders / 15 % protocol, the position's split) → remainder to the borrower.

### 8.2 Keeper-initiated buybacks: reference price and bound — PROPOSED

Applies to `liquidate` and `expire`. Requires a fresh completed bucket (`lb_sec ≥ now − 2 s`).

```
L_b   = max(L_live, L_ema', L_slow)                                  # deepest liquidity -> lowest reference cost
        L_ema' = min(L_ema, L_mlo) while ema_guard = 1, else L_ema     # M4R reference guard (PROPOSED, #96); L_mlo = lower median of llo
      on Raydium (M4F-1, PROPOSED, #98) and PumpSwap (M6R-1, owner decision 2026-09-30, #105):
L_b   = max(L_live, min(max(L_ema', L_slow), D))                      # D = the pool's locked depth; L_live never capped;
        the cap is D itself on PumpSwap (#106, M6E-1: PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS = 100 %) and, since AIR-14 (#117), on Raydium
        (⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋ = 2D at the default before): D <= L_live there, so L_b = L_live; no governed
        parameter enters an open position's exit bound (AIR-23)
lo_x  = lb_lo·(1 + g·(now − lb_sec))
liquidation:  P_ref = min( max(P_ema, lo_x), P_ema·(1 + κ_eff), lo_x·(1 + κ_eff) );   δ = δ_liq = 2 %   (last term v0.4.1)
expiry (not CR-liquidatable):  P_ref = min(P_ema, min_i p_i, lb_lo);  δ = δ_exp = 1 %
B̂_ref  = B̂(Q; reprice(L_b, P_ref))                                   (risk quote)
liquidation (v0.4.1):  max_in = min( B̂_ref·(1 + δ_liq),  V − K_s − ert )                       (no reserve; SPEC §5.2)
expiry:                max_in = min( B̂_ref·(1 + δ_exp),  V − K_s − ert,  max(V − R − π_ref − ert, B̂_ref·(1 + ε)) ),
                       π_ref = ⌈expiry_fee_bps·B̂_ref/10⁴⌉                                     (rent and fee senior)
execute iff B̂_live (execution quote) ≤ max_in;  otherwise the position waits (keeper retries next second)
```

- **Reference price.** For expiry nothing is urgent, so the reference is the lowest recent robust price. For liquidation it must follow a genuine fast pump: the last-second low extrapolated by one second of *sustained* EMA growth, capped at `P_ema·(1 + κ_eff)`. **v0.4.1 (R4B-2)** also caps it at `lo_x·(1 + κ_eff)`: a `P_ema` pushed by one attacker-only second and then frozen (§5.6) has no sustained growth, so the reference stays at the honest bucket low instead of the pushed EMA (v0.4: `B̂_ref` ×1.156 and the Tier A borrower's remainder 19.49 → 0.22 SOL, the attacker net −15.7 SOL; v0.4.1: `B̂_ref` ×0.999, borrower −1.90 SOL, the ordinary atomic sandwich; `attacks/ptf_sandwich.py`). In a genuine pump `P_ema ≤ lo_x`, so the new term never binds; no row of the 1,655-run battery changes.
- **Fixed δ (v0.4, decision 1).** v0.3 widened the bound 1 %/s from 1 % to 5 % over consecutive executable seconds (the Dutch clock). The reviewer's liveness runs and ours (§12.2) show the ramp never changed a genuine outcome at 2 %; a fixed 1 % does (it turns three drain-then-pump liquidations into S1), so the bound is a fixed 2 % with a compiled minimum of 2 %. It costs the borrower more when a keeper acts at once (Tier A atomic sandwich: borrower −2.02 SOL instead of −0.95) and less when keepers wait (−2.025 instead of −5.11 after 4 s; −2.022 before AIR-22), and it removes the V1-1 blocking attack class, the ring's `lo` field and the executable-run scan.
- **A liquidation reserves nothing; an expiry keeps rent and fee senior (v0.4.1, R4A-1, R4B-1).** v0.4 reserved rent *and* the penalty in the liquidation bound (`max(V − R − π_ref − ert, B̂_ref·(1 + ε))`), so that the held-model attacker could not buy the vault down to `V − K_s − ert`. Any reserve creates a dead band `V − R − π_ref − ert < B̂_live ≤ min(B̂_ref·(1 + δ), V − K_s − ert)` in which a liquidation that is due can execute only if `B̂_live ≤ B̂_ref·(1 + ε)` and the S1 legs are false. In a genuine fast pump the live cost is a few tenths of a percent above the one-second-old reference, so the keeper waits and the position gaps into S1. v0.3's rent-only reserve has the same band whenever the rent due exceeds `K_s` — which aged positions reach (Tier A at 25 days and 90 % utilisation: 6.04 SOL against 0.25 SOL). The trade-off on the 1,655-run battery (§5.7) and the held model (§12.6):

| liquidation bound | extra S1 on the 1,655-run battery | its worst row (lender P&L vs no reserve) | held model, fresh Tier A: lenders + protocol | held model, Tier A aged 25 d at 90 %: lenders + protocol | parameter |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4: rent and penalty reserved | 35 | RC pump 10×/60 s with ±0.3 % noise: S1 −69.7 % instead of liquidation +1.1 % | −2.94 SOL | −9.18 SOL | `ε` load-bearing |
| v0.3: rent reserved (the reviewers' proposal) | 1 (and 3–4 more at in-bound maximum rates) | C aged 6 d, 90 %, pump 6×/40 s with ±0.3 % noise: S1 −46.1 % instead of +0.6 % | −3.34 SOL | −9.18 SOL | `ε` load-bearing for aged positions |
| **v0.4.1: no reserve (SPEC §5.2)** | **0** | — | −3.34 SOL | −4.54 SOL | none |

(The held-model columns are `sandwich.py`'s last section under the lower-median growth (AIR-22, #117), with which two held seconds open `κ_eff` and the attacker's best plan changes with the rule. With the minimum they read +0.46 / −5.72, −2.06 / −1.92 and −2.06 / −8.02 SOL: the rent-only reserve then kept an aged position's rent out of the attacker's reach, and now does not. The battery columns were measured with v0.4.1's growth rule.)

The liquidation bound is now the complement of S1 exactly: every liquidatable, buyback-allowed position with a fresh reference executes as soon as `B̂_live ≤ B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` (I-E11). What it costs: in the last few percent before S1, where the vault cannot cover the whole waterfall anyway, a sandwiching keeper can take rent and penalty up to the bound's slack (I-E3), and a held-model attacker can buy the vault down to `V − K_s − ert` (§12.6). An expiry is never urgent — if the price keeps rising it becomes a liquidation — so it keeps rent and the expiry fee senior; no battery row changes with any expiry reserve rule.
- **`V − K_s − ert`** keeps the buyback region disjoint from S1 (§5.6).
- **The reference guard after a venue change** (PROPOSED, M4R, #96). `L_b` takes the deepest liquidity so that quiet-second LP withdrawals cannot lower the reference (V2-14). Right after a venue change that direction is the risky one. A capital holder who deposits around one crank every second from the adoption on (a deposit freeze: no swap fee, about the pool's size in both assets) holds a pushed `L_ema` up. A reference at the pushed depth stays below the live cost, so `B̂_live ≤ max_in` never holds and the position gaps into S1 (the M4R study on RA → 630 SOL at λ 99.78 %, a 3×/120 s pump: S1 at +123 s, lenders −17.785 SOL, without the guard — before AIR-14, #117, whose reference cap at `D` now bounds that push on Raydium by itself; §12.12 (4)). While `ema_guard = 1` the EMA term is capped at `L_mlo`, the lower median of the ring's bucket liquidity lows. The guard is set by every Raydium venue change or price start and cleared at the first integration after the warm-up with `L_ema ≤ 1.01·L_mlo`, i.e. at the gate on an honest pool. The guard also covers the `L_slow` term through its input: while it is set, `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)` (§5.2; M4X-2). The M4R verification found that the guard on the `L_ema` term alone was not enough. Under the freeze every integration fed the frozen `L_ema` into `L_slow`, which `L_b` reads unguarded, and after 10–30 min `L_b = L_slow` sat at 1.4–2.6× the pool's depth: the same block, reached slowly and kept for about an hour after the freeze stopped (`τ_slow` = 1 h). A genuine pump after 30 min of freeze ended in S1 with lenders −12.6 SOL (RA → 314 SOL), and on the fork −10.06 SOL with the borrower zeroed. With the keeper observing every second it is now a liquidation, after any length of freeze (§12.12 (4)); the same freeze then liquidates at +61 s at 1.732× (lenders +0.834 SOL; §12.12 (4)). A market that never changes venue never sets the guard, and after an honest switch it is clear before the first exit. What it leaves: a withdraw contest while the guard is set (§12.12).
- **The guard holds only while every second carries an honest observation** (M4F-1, #98). `L_mlo` is the lower median of the 8 newest buckets' `llo`, and a bucket's low is honest only if an honest observation landed in its second: a deposit cannot lower a bucket's low below an honest observation in the same second, but it sets the whole low of a second nobody else observed (a second without any observation adds no ring entry). Once 5 of the 8 newest entries are attacker-only, `L_mlo` is the deposit's depth; the guard clears (`L_ema ≤ 1.01·L_mlo`), `L_slow`'s input `min(lobs, L_mlo)` no longer bounds it, and the reference reads the pushed `L_ema`. As built at M4R the keeper observed a healthy carried position every 30 s after the gate, and its duty-2 bursts compared prices only, so the attacker owned 29 of every 30 seconds. The guard cleared at ≈ +15 s and the genuine pump ended in S1: lenders −12.553 to −14.092 SOL in `switch_warmup.py` part 4d (q 2.9–5.9 % of the candidate's `X`), and −10.056 SOL with the borrower zeroed on the fork (q/x 3.12 %). The keeper duty that keeps the guard honest (§5.8): **every second while `ema_guard = 1`** on a market with positions (duty 9), so no guarded bucket is attacker-only. #98's second duty, **liquidity-aware bursts** after the guard has cleared, is reverted by #99: a trigger cost one transaction fee (M4G-2), and with an LP-wrapped crank repeated in every covered second each second the keeper missed was another `L_ema` step (M4G-1); a real deposit held and pulled at a pump is never foreign at all (M4G-3). #101 brings back a narrower duty for the withdraw drag (FITS-2): per second, not per slot, for 10 s after a foreign liquidity deviation on a market with open positions, extended while they recur (§5.8 duty 2); against a deposit freeze after the clear it holds `L_ema` at its first step too. After the clear a push reaches the reference again, as on a market that never switched; what made it block was a carried position larger than the new pool's caps, which #99 no longer adopts, and — on a higher-fee candidate at #99's first fit limit, in a fast pump — a margin posted on the old pool's cheaper cost, which #100's carried fee caps at `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` keep out (§6.1). At the fit limit the same freeze, or the held deposit, delays the liquidation by 0–1 s (§12.12 (11)); a 1 %→1 % switch at its impact cap was the exception until #101 capped the carried fee caps at 25 bps (§6.1; §12.12 residual 15). Engine P's other rows assume a keeper that cranks every second, so they show the guard's effect under full coverage.
- **The reference cap** (PROPOSED, M4F-1, #98; defense in depth). **Since AIR-14 (#117) the Raydium cap is `D` itself, as PumpSwap's (M6E-1, below), so `L_b = L_live` above it** (`D ≤ L_live` on the pool the exit reads): at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋` = 2D, on a pool locked above `λ_min` (a λ ≈ 1 pool, honest depth `D`), a deposit held for about an hour and pulled at a position's deadline left `L_b` near 2D over a pool drained to `D`, so the expiry buyback sat ≈ 1.2 % above `B̂_ref`, over `δ_exp` = 1 %, and `expire` failed `ExitBoundNotMet` until `L_slow` decayed (tens of minutes; M6E-1's hold, on Raydium). The cap also reads no governed parameter any more, so a `pool_lock_min_bps` change cannot move an open position's exit bound (AIR-23; it read the parameter live at every exit: 2D → 5D at 2,000). What it gives up is V2-14's layer above `D`: a buyback wrapped in an unlocked LP's own withdrawal is priced on the drained pool, at worst at `D`, although Raydium sizing may have credited the position up to `D/λ_min`. In §11.2's scenario table this moves only the penalty base of the Raydium drain-then-pump runs, at the same second and buyback (`drain 50% + pump 2x/120s`: the borrower −0.0152 / −0.0106 SOL on RA / RB, the lenders +0.0076 / +0.0053, the keeper +0.0046 / +0.0032). In the attack scripts it moves more (§12.12). (4d)'s forced adoptions are liquidated under every keeper, and a genuine withdrawal at a 1 % pool's open cap no longer waits for `L_slow`. Residual 10 widens: V2-14's layer also kept a liquidation wrapped in the LP's withdrawal out of its bound, so a drag now forces one level and missed keeper seconds and a held drain force more ((5b) (iii), (iv), (vii)). The rest of this bullet describes the cap as built through M4F-1: on Raydium the EMA terms of `L_b` were capped at `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`: `D` is the pool's locked depth (`Market.pool_d`, refreshed by the exiting instruction's own prologue), and `D/λ_min` is the most depth sizing ever credits a Raydium pool with (§6.1's size cap, the tier's `R_eff`, the adoption anchor; on PumpSwap sizing credits `D` itself since #106, and so does the cap since M6E-1, below). `L_live` is never capped, and `D` is deposit- and withdraw-invariant (up to λ's whole-bps floor), so no capital-only bundle moves the cap. A push of `L_ema` or `L_slow` then lifts the reference by at most `λ/λ_min` over the live depth: ≤ 2× at `λ_min` = 50 %, and nothing on a λ = 50 % pool, whose cap is its live depth. On a pool with `λ ≥ λ_min` a genuine withdrawal leaves `D/λ_min` at or above the pre-withdrawal depth, so the cap leaves V2-14's protection unchanged. On a pool with `λ < λ_min` the reference credits at most `D/λ_min`, the depth the position was sized on, after a drain (§10.5: lender P&L +0.02 / +0.03 points). Alone it did not close M4F-1 (with the 30-s cadence the forced-adoption q 2.9 % rows are liquidated and the q 5.9 % rows still end in S1 at a 2× reference, part 4d; at `D` since AIR-14 every row is liquidated). #99 argued that a fitting position (`q ≤ c·X₀`, `X₀ ≤ D/√P`, `c` 0.75 % at φ = 0.25 %, at most 2.47 %) is then below what any reference can block, from `(1 − c·λ_min)/(1 − c) − 1 < δ`; that bound reads `q/X` at the adoption, while in a pump `q/X_live` grows with `√P` and the EMA lags, and it left out the margin a carried position posted on the old pool's cost. FIT-2 found the counterexample — a 25-bps incumbent's position at a 1 % candidate's 2.47 % impact cap, S1 in a 4×/30 s pump under a held deposit or a freeze after the clear — which #100 closes on the size side: the carried fee caps take `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)`, and since #101 at most 25 bps, so a 1 %→1 % switch carries no more than that class either (§6.1; §12.12 (11) (d)). **PumpSwap since #105 (M6R-1):** canonical pools take third-party LP (§1.2), and `D` is the burned creation LP's depth, deposit- and withdraw-invariant as on Raydium, so the same cap applies: a deposit held k× the pool for an hour and pulled in the second a genuine 3×/120-s pump starts left the reference at 4–8× the pool's depth and the position open (the liquidation blocked) as built after M6, and is liquidated with the honest control now (`attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` 3 (a): L_b capped at 2.00× the depth, liquidation +73 s at 2.22×; §12.12 (12)). Under #105 a position sized while the deposit was held (up to 2× that cap) was not: pulled at a genuine pump, the cap at 2× the drained depth blocked every liquidation and the position gapped into S1 (M6D-1, §12.12 (12) (e)). **#106 (OWNER-M6D-1, 2026-09-30) closes that on the size side, and its verification's M6E-1 on the reference side:** PumpSwap sizing takes `D` itself (§6.1), and so does this cap — `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋` at PumpSwap's `λ_min` = 100 % is `D`, and as `D ≤ L_live` on a canonical pool the reference reads the live pool, `L_b = L_live`. #106 was first written with the cap kept at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋` = `2D`, since a position sized on `D` has `c = q/X_D ≤` 2.47 % and a buyback on the drained pool is within `(1 − c/2)/(1 − c) − 1` = 1.27 % of a reference at twice its depth, inside `δ_liq` = 2 % (the #99 argument); the sims confirm it for every liquidation row (§12.12 (12) (f) (i)–(iii): the same exit second and price at `2D` and at `D` over a 120-cell pulled-deposit grid, the (d) drag table and a due liquidation wrapped in the attacker's own withdrawal). It fails twice. **The expiry** bound is `δ_exp` = 1 %, crossed above `c` = 1.96 % (≈ 80 % of the cap): a deposit of 2–3× the pool held for the hour before a deadline and pulled at it kept `L_slow` above twice the drained depth and held the expiry of such a position up for 31–56 min ((12) (f) (iv): 3× +3,326 s, 2× +1,874 s, pulled 10 min early +2,355 s; the fork +3,347 s, 3,346 refusals `ExitBoundNotMet`) — a free extension for the borrower with the lenders' tokens out, no attacker-only second needed, a genuine LP leaving at the deadline does it too. **A governed `λ_min`** below the default made the kept cap 3D–5D, and a deposit pulled at a genuine pump gapped a position sized on `D` into S1 again ((12) (f) (v): −31.1 / −52.3 % of `Q` at 5× / 10× over 120 s; the fork at 2,000 −39.1 %, the borrower zeroed) — M6D-1's loss through a parameter. At `D` every such expiry lands at +1 s, the liquidations keep their seconds, and `pool_lock_min_bps` reaches no PumpSwap term. What it gives up is V2-14's layer on PumpSwap (quiet-second withdrawals cannot lower the reference): a buyback wrapped in an LP's own withdrawal is priced on the drained pool — at worst the burned depth `D` the position was sized on — landing in the keeper's own second with the borrower's share of `V` at most 0.1 points lower and the lenders whole ((12) (f) (iii)). The curve has no `D` (its `L` is virtual, no LP exists) and no cap.
- **Not adopted: a lows bound on the EMA terms** (#100's first fix for FIT-2, withdrawn; FITV-2). Bounding `max(L_ema', L_slow)` also by `⌊L_mlo·(10⁴ + 100)/10⁴⌋` stopped the reference crediting a pulled deposit, but it gave up V2-14's `L_slow` term. Withdraw-wrapped cranks in the quiet seconds of the keeper's 30-s cadence drag `L_ema` and every bucket low to the locked depth `D` (a keeper observation in a dragged second cannot lift them: its value is clamped into the bucket's `[D, H]`, where `L_ema` already is), and a withdraw-wrapped liquidation — its spot leg at the drained `L_live`, its EMA leg at the dragged `L_ema` — then fit a bound lowered to `D`: an unlocked LP forced the liquidation of a position healthy at the pool's depth (the fork: at a 2.05× hold, which the honest keeper never liquidates, λ 50 %, 1 %; `switch_warmup.py` (5b): 2.01–2.07× forced against the honest 2.078×, and 1.82–1.95× at `λ_min` 2,000). Without it `L_b`'s `L_slow` term keeps the reference at the depth the pool had, so the same wrapped liquidation fails the bound wherever `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 > δ` — for as long as `L_slow` has not followed the dragged `L_ema` (τ_slow = 1 h): a drag held 0.8–3.2 h before a pump reaches the same band (§12.12 (5b), residual 10). That was not all (FITS-2): where `δ` covers the drained cost — at 1 % a position below about half the cap, at 25 bps any — the wrapped liquidation fits the bound with `L_slow` still at the pool's depth, so a 30-s drag already forced a band below the threshold, and at 1.9 % of `X` a 25-min drag did (the fork). A bound on the highs (`L_mhi`) would keep V2-14 but not stop a deposit-wrapped crank each second after a pull; FIT-2 is closed on the size side instead (§6.1), and the drag on the legs and the keeper's coverage (next bullet). *(Since AIR-14, #117, the Raydium cap is `D`, so `L_b`'s `L_slow` term no longer keeps that layer either: the reference reads the drained pool, as PumpSwap's has since M6E-1. What stops a withdraw-wrapped liquidation of a healthy position is then the spot leg at `max(L_live, L_floor)` and the keeper's per-second coverage (next bullet); the keeper's FITV-2 contest still forces nothing on the fixed program — the honest keeper liquidates nothing and all 390 of the attacker's wrapped liquidations are refused (the review's README).)*
- **The liquidation legs against a withdrawal in the same transaction** (PROPOSED, #101, FITS-2; §5.5, §5.8 duty 2). The forcing needs two things: a spot leg that the wrapped liquidation's own withdrawal makes true, and an EMA leg made true by a drag (or by a genuine spike's lag). (a) The spot leg now reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` (§5.5): the withdrawal no longer makes it true, which alone closes the spike-and-retreat case (§12.12 (5b) (v): forced at 2.01–2.07× under #100, nothing now) but not the drag, which drags `L_ema'` itself. (b) The keeper covers a market with open positions every second while foreign liquidity-deviating observations recur (duty 2): the drag gets its first second and holds `L_ema` there (≈ 0.917 of the depth), and `L_slow` follows no further. Together they forced nothing in `switch_warmup.py` 5b (i)–(v) at `9aade3e`, drags built from attacker-only seconds: every pool, hold level and drag of 30 s to 3 h (and none within 8 h at the probed hold level), positions at 0.15–1 × the open cap (the λ 99.78 % pool's one level at its threshold is the attacker's attempt landing first in its second, under every rule: nothing is withdrawn); #100 forced 2.01–2.07× (1 h), 1.94–2.03× (3 h), and 2.01–2.03× after 30 s at half the cap. #101 left `L_b` unchanged. Since AIR-14 (#117) `L_b`'s cap is `D`, so the drag's one step forces one level, 2.06× against 2.065× at 0.79 × the cap (§12.12 (5b) (iii)). What is left is the keeper's missed covered seconds (duty 2; §12.12 (5b) (iv), residual 10); a low reached genuinely — the LP's withdrawal held a few seconds — then frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks, which the coverage holds but cannot lift and which forces exactly what the LP's withdrawal for good forces (FITX-1, #102; (5b) (vii), residual 10; O18); and, for a genuine withdrawal, a delay: none where the EMA leg binds (a flat or rising market), +0–3 s after a 0.25–0.5 % dip at the withdrawal, +0–8 s under ±1 % per-second noise (+0–10 s at `9aade3e`; §12.12 (5b) (vi)).
- **Invariant I-E1:** measured spend `B ≤ max_in`, and the venue part `≤ B̂_ref·(1 + δ)`. **I-E11 (v0.4.1):** a liquidatable, buyback-allowed position with a fresh completed bucket never waits while `B̂_live ≤ ⌊B̂_ref·(10⁴ + δ_liq)/10⁴⌋`.

v1.1 Jupiter routes are held to the same `max_in`.

### 8.3 Normal liquidation

Preconditions: liquidatable (§5.5), normal buyback allowed (§5.6), venue not migrating, bound satisfied (§8.2).

| Step | Amount | To |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | buy `Q` with `max_in` → measured `B` | venue; `Q` tokens → pool |
| 2 | `r = min(R, V − B)` | 85 % lenders / 15 % protocol (position's split) |
| 3 | `π = min(⌈p·base/10⁴⌉, V − B − r)`, **`base = min(B̂_live, B̂_ref)`** (risk quotes) | 30 % keeper, 20 % protocol, 50 % lenders (rounding remainder to lenders) |
| 4 | **keeper floor** `top = min(max(0, K_s − ⌊0.3·π⌋), V − B − r − π)` (v0.4, R3B-10) | keeper, paid by the borrower |
| 5 | `V − B − r − π − top` | borrower |

### 8.4 Expiry (SPEC §4 "Duration")

Precondition: `now ≥ deadline` and not CR-liquidatable (if both, §8.3 applies). Steps 1–2 as §8.3, then:

| Step | Amount | To |
|---|---|---|
| 3 | `e = min(⌈200·base/10⁴⌉, V − B − r)`, `base = min(B̂_live, B̂_ref)` | 100 % lenders (SPEC); vests (§9.4) |
| 4 | `K_e = min(1,000,000 + ⌊10·B̂_ref/10⁴⌋, 50,000,000, V − B − r − e)` (PROPOSED) | keeper, paid by the borrower |
| 5 | remainder | borrower |

### 8.5 Keeper incentives (R3B-10)

The SPEC keeper share of a liquidation is `0.3·p·base ≈ 0.3·p·V/L`; the settlement reward is `K_s = 0.01 SOL + 1 %·V` (capped at 0.25 SOL). Below a vault of ≈ 5.0 SOL (Tier A), 1.8 (B), 1.7 (C) or 1.4 (D), `K_s` is larger: a third-party keeper earns more by letting a small position gap into S1 (a lender loss) than by liquidating it, and at the 0.1 SOL minimum notional a Tier A liquidation paid ≈ 0.0024 SOL, less than a 220 k CU transaction at congested priority prices. v0.4 floors the liquidation keeper reward at `K_s` whenever the borrower has equity left, paid from that equity (step 4 of §8.3): liquidating never pays less than settling, and never less than 0.01 SOL. The top-up is at most `K_s − 0.3·π`, which the borrower would lose entirely in S1; it is zero above the crossover vaults — in none of the 36 liquidations of the §11 scenarios (positions at the size cap) — and applies to small positions: in a 10×/10 min pump at 10 % of the cap it tops the keeper up by 0.0049 SOL (C), 0.0086 SOL (D@0 %), 0.0009 SOL (RA) and 0.0087 SOL (RC). Small positions still depend on the in-house keeper for prompt action (disclosed).

### 8.6 SOL settlement S1 / S2 / S3 (OWNER D3)

Preconditions: liquidatable (or expired), and S1 or S2 confirmed on one clock with persistence (§5.6), or S3 (§10.3).

```
keeper:   K_s
lenders:  S1:      x = V − K_s                                  (never capped; SPEC-literal)
          S2, S3:  x = min(V − K_s, FV(P_ema, L_ema)),  only if the amount agrees within fv_dev_bps on the settlement box (§5.6)
          FV(P, L) = B̂_FV + ⌈p'·B̂_FV/10⁴⌉ + R_due,   B̂_FV = B̂(Q; reprice(L, P)) at φ_FV = min(φ_live, venue_fee_hard_cap_bps)
               (on PumpSwap also at most the tier at P_ema's market cap, AIR-10, #117)
          p' = locked penalty, or the locked expiry fee if the position is expired and
               CR(V_eff, B̂_FV) ≥ L at the same (P, L)                          (SPEC §4, §5.3; O15: S2 and S3; v0.4.1 on the box, R4A-3)
          stuck migration: B̂_FV prices the deterministic post-migration pool at P
          ★1.0.1 (pump-final §5.4): the pool migrate_v2 will create — (X₁, Y₁) with X₁ = 206.9e12 without a pool part
          (bit-identical), the live curve_base_ata after one, Y₁ = rs − fee_m + pcQ — at p' = p, or after a pool part
          p' = ⌈⌈p·Y₁/Y₀⌉·X₀/X₁⌉ (ρ = P(X₁,Y₁)/P(X₀,Y₀); lender side), at that pool's capped PumpSwap tier; FV undefined
          (lenders get the whole take) when pcB / pcQ are not both set or vQ − rQ ≠ ivqr (known issues PUMP-2026-10-13-1)
borrower: V − K_s − x;   protocol: nothing (SPEC §6.3)
of any take (S1, S2, S3), min(x, ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due) is immediate but for its rent top-up, and the rest — impact, fees,
penalty — vests like a lump: lump = x − min(x, ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due), P′ = P_ema, or 0 when the venue cannot execute
(S1 since AIR-21 and P′ = 0 since AIR-09, #117). The rent top-up max(0, min(x − lump, R_due) − credited_for(pos)), R_due
above the rent credited while the position was open, vests with it (§9.4; v0.4.1, R4B-6; M10-8C-1)
```

**S2 is unreachable at default parameters for capped positions** (R3B). σ ≈ Q/(X − Q) grows with √P, so on the live state:

```text
- S2 reachability at the per-position cap (price multiple at which each first holds, live state):
  A: S1 at x2.09, S2 at x48.0
  C: S1 at x3.08, S2 at x48.0
  D@0%: S1 at x3.99, S2 at never (curve completes first)
  RA (all unlocked LP drained): S1 at x2.01, S2 at x125.9
  RC (all unlocked LP drained): S1 at x2.96, S2 at x125.9
```

S1 always comes first. S2 stays one predicate, built and tested (the exit fuzz reaches it through σ_max overrides, §13), and the amount check matters in practice for **S3 on an executable venue** — the Emergency route-broken flag or a venue fee above the hard cap (ARCH §5.3) — and for the stuck-migration S3. The drained-pool S2 cases of v0.3 remain as model cases (the LP lock broken, `attacks/fvcap.py`):

| market | LP drained | move | outcome | V - K_s (SOL) | FV at EMA executable cost (SOL) | lenders get (SOL) | borrower gets (SOL) | S1 lenders' take == V - K_s |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 70% | x2.0 | S1 | 127.491 | 140.439 | 127.491 | 0.000 | yes |
| A | 80% | x1.9 | S1 | 127.491 | 141.809 | 127.491 | 0.000 | yes |
| A | 80% | x2.0 | S1 | 127.491 | 150.055 | 127.491 | 0.000 | yes |
| B | 80% | x2.0 | S2 | 63.684 | 61.318 | 61.318 | 2.366 | n/a (S2) |
| RA | 80% | x2.0 | S1 | 37.620 | 41.069 | 37.620 | 0.000 | yes |

| market | LP drained | move | outcome | V - K_s (SOL) | FV (SOL) | lenders get (SOL) | borrower gets (SOL) | lenders' take / value of Q at spot |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RA | 95% | x1.4 | S2 | 37.620 | 33.048 | 33.048 | 4.573 | 1.278 |
| A | 95% | x1.0 | S2 | 127.491 | 120.267 | 120.267 | 7.225 | 2.045 |

Every S1 pays lenders exactly `V − K_s`; the cap binds only in S2/S3 when the vault exceeds fair value.

**`p'` for expired, not CR-liquidatable positions (R3A-8 a; owner decision O15, SPEC §0, §5.3, §14).** D3 names the "liquidation penalty" in FV; SPEC §4 says an expired position pays "a 2 % expiry fee instead of the full liquidation penalty". The owner recorded the expiry fee for a position settled in **S2 or S3** only because it expired ("the same reasoning applies to S2 as to S3", SPEC §5.3): the reading under which an S2/S3 of an expired position pays lenders what a normal expiry would have paid (the reviewer's alternative resolution of R3A-8 a). The sim applies it to every S2 and S3 amount (`Market.fv_parts`); ECON §13's exit fuzz pays one S2 of an expired Tier A position at the expiry fee. **Which positions count as CR-liquidatable is decided on the settlement box (v0.4.1, R4A-3):** each evaluated state `(P, L)` uses the penalty iff `CR(V_eff, B̂_FV(P, L)) < L`, never the raw flag, whose robust-spot leg one contrary low observation fails. In v0.4 that observation, in the second before an honest keeper's `sol_settle(S3)`, switched an expired, CR-liquidatable position to the expiry fee on both corners (lenders −3.12 SOL on Tier A for a 0.71 SOL veto, −0.09 SOL on D@0 % for 0.02 SOL); now the amount is unchanged (`fv_push.py` (V)). Evidence, with the Emergency flag set 30 minutes before a healthy position's deadline:

At the expiry-fee reading a lender-keeper that races the honest keeper to `sol_settle(S3)` takes the tokens' executable value in SOL plus the 2 % fee and rent — the same as the expiry, paid in SOL instead of tokens — and the borrower loses only `K_s − K_e` more. At the penalty reading the same race moves 3–8 % of `B̂` from the borrower to lenders (Tier A: 1.82 SOL), which rewards lenders for any no-route condition. The owner confirmed the expiry-fee reading for S2 and S3 (O15).

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
