**Fee ceiling in FV (PROPOSED, OWNER visibility).** Venue fees are admin-set and can be raised immediately, to almost 100 % on Raydium. Above `venue_fee_hard_cap_bps` (500 bps) the route counts as broken for S3 and FV uses the ceiling; between the open cap (150 bps) and the ceiling FV follows the live fee (owner option O5: `min(current fee, fee at open)`).

### 8.7 Open fee

`fee_open = ⌈0.25 %·N_open⌉`, paid on top of `M` straight to the protocol; not collateral, not refundable.

---

## 9. Lender-pool accounting

Reference model with fuzzed invariants and a liveness check: [`sim/pool_model.py`](sim/pool_model.py) v0.4.1 (normative for `shortwin-math::pool`).

### 9.1 State

Market: `T` unborrowed tokens (internal counter), `B_Q` tokens lent out, `S` shares (u128), `acc` entitlement per share (i128, WAD), `acc_r` realised allocation per share (u128, WAD), their carries, `receivable`, the rent aggregate `A_j` (§7), share `epoch` and per-epoch records, `wipe_live` and **the post-wipe sweep flags** (v0.4.1), lock state `Π`, `Π_epoch`, `b_min` and **the lock expiry calendar** (day buckets of lock terms and their live sum `lock_norm`, v0.4.1), **`max_release`** (latest `deadline + S3 tail` of any position opened; v0.4 `max_deadline`), **`vest_bal`, `vest_last`, `vest_end`** (v0.4). MarketVault: `lender_sol`, **`unmatched`** (v0.4 pot), `protocol_fees`. Per lender: `shares, snap, pending (i64), snap_r, real, claimed, epoch, lock, lock_Π, lock_epoch, lock_term`, **`lock_until`** (v0.4). Removed: `unassigned_sol`.

### 9.2 Deposits and share value

```
deposit a:    minted = ⌊a·(S + VS)/(T + B_Q + VA)⌋, T += a            (VS = 1,000, VA = 1)
share value:  (T + B_Q + VA)/(S + VS) tokens + SOL entitlement via acc
```

Internal counters ignore donations (first-depositor attack: a victim's 10⁹ deposit redeems 10⁹).

### 9.3 Pro-rata withdrawal with bounded locks (SPEC §2.4; C0-9, V2-3, R3B-1)

A lender may withdraw only its pro-rata part of unborrowed inventory, computed over the shares that still have a claim on it (the exact form of ARCH §3.3 and `pool_model.free_allowance`, R3A-13):

```
lock_i(now)  = 0 if now ≥ lock_until_i or lock_epoch_i ≠ Π_epoch, else min(⌈lock_i·Π/lock_Π_i⌉, shares_i)
locked       = min(⌊lock_norm·Π/WAD⌋, S),  lock_norm = Σ of the calendar buckets not yet expired            (v0.4.1)
u_i          = shares_i − lock_i(now)
allowance_i  = min( ⌊u_i·T/max(S − locked, u_i)⌋,  value of shares_i,  T )                          (tokens)
withdraw s:   tokens = ⌊s·(T + B_Q + VA)/(S + VS)⌋ ≤ allowance_i, ≤ T; requires pending_i ≥ 0 (§9.4)
              new lock_i = lock_i + ⌈min(s, b_max)·(u_i − b_max)/b_max⌉ (b_max = shares that burn allowance_i)
              lock_until_i = max(lock_until_i, ⌈(max(now, max_release) + lock_grace_s)/1 d⌉·1 d)          (v0.4.1)
              a live lock's term leaves its bucket; the new term ⌊new lock_i·WAD/Π⌋ enters bucket lock_until_i/1 d
max_release  = max over positions opened of (deadline + max_migration_pause_s + grad_grace_s + s3_wait_s),
               each with the position's snapshot values                                                   (v0.4.1)
on each resolution of q borrowed tokens, B' = B_Q − q:
    if B' = 0: Π_epoch += 1 (all locks and every bucket clear)
    elif locks exist and B' < b_min: Π ← ⌈Π·B'/b_min⌉, b_min = B'                                   (net reductions only, V2-3)
the first lock while no lock is live (lock_norm = 0, a new lock era): b_min = B_Q and Π = WAD            (AIR-12, #117)
a bucket leaves lock_norm when its day ends, i.e. exactly at the lock_until of every lock in it           (v0.4.1)
```

**Why `lock_until` (R3B-1).** v0.3's locks decayed only when `B_Q` fell below its minimum since the first lock of the Π epoch and cleared only at `B_Q = 0`. In a market whose borrowing stays at or above that level — steady or growing demand, the success case — a lender that once took its free part stayed locked for good, even after every position that existed at lock time had closed. Every position open at lock time resolves by its deadline plus its own S3 tail — a migration pause, the graduation grace and the S3 wait, at the position's snapshot values — so no lock needs to outlive `max_release + lock_grace_s`. v0.4 used the deadline alone: with the three timers at their compiled maxima (1 day each) and `lock_grace_s` at its minimum (1 h), a lock expired on day 30.04 while its position could stay open in `PumpMigrating` until day 33 (R4A-8, R4B-8). Folding each position's tail into `max_release` needs no relational check against governance values, which change after a position snapshots them; `lock_grace_s` (1 day) then covers keeper latency and exits that wait for their bound.

**Why an expiry calendar (R4A-2, R4B-3).** Every lender's allowance divides `T` among the shares that are not locked, using the aggregate `locked`. In v0.4 an expired lock left that aggregate only at its holder's next touch, and the program cannot touch every lender; until then every other lender's denominator was too small, the sum of allowances exceeded `T`, and the first withdrawers took the idle lender's pro-rata part — the C0-9 run, reopened for exactly the lenders R3B-1 meant to release. The fuzz could not see it because `check()` touched every lender first. v0.4.1 keys each lock term by its expiry day: `lock_until` is rounded up to the end of its day, the aggregate drops a bucket when that day ends, and the lender's own view expires at the same second. Lock terms are floored, so the aggregate never exceeds the lenders' own (ceiled) locks — v0.4's ceilings let two lenders with one unlocked share each draw all of `T` when everyone else was locked down to rounding dust. On-chain this is a ring of `LOCK_BUCKETS = 128` day buckets (ARCH §2.3.6); `lock_until` is at most 90 d (compiled maximum duration) + 3 d (S3 tail) + 7 d (`lock_grace_s`) + 1 d ahead. `check()` now asserts I-P4 on the state the program sees, with only the withdrawing lender touched:

| model | E's lock_until (day) | free T | F gets | G gets | E gets |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4 (expiry leaves the aggregate at the holder's touch) | 31.06 | 1,000,000,000,000 | 500,000,000,000 | 500,000,000,000 | 0 |
| v0.4.1 (day-bucket expiry calendar) | 32.00 | 1,000,000,000,000 | 400,000,000,000 | 400,000,000,000 | 200,000,000,000 |

(E, F and G deposit equally; E takes its free part at 50 % utilisation; positions roll so Π stays; on day 33 nobody has touched E; F, G, then E withdraw the most they may.) Over the reviewers' lock-heavy operation mix (6 seeds × 4,000 operations, sampled every 25), the states in which the allowances as the program sees them sum above `T` + dust:

| model | sampled states | states with sum(allowances) > T + dust | worst sum / T |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4 (touch-time expiry, ceil'd terms) | 954 | 87 | 3.00 |
| expiry calendar, ceil'd terms | 954 | 0 | 0.00 |
| v0.4.1 (calendar, floored terms) | 954 | 0 | 0.00 |

(Recomputed on the AI review's pool model, #117: AIR-12 restarts Π at each lock era's first lock, and AIR-07's drained-pool gate refuses some of the mix's deposits (102 of 5,910). As measured for v0.4.1, with Π kept across lock eras, the first two rows read 92 / 2.72 and 1 / 1.27. The floored terms stay: they keep the aggregate at or below the lenders' own locks whatever Π does.)

The day buckets lengthen a lock by less than a day (it ends at the end of its `lock_until` day).

**Why Π / `b_min` is kept** (decision 6: keep it only if it matters). The reviewer's scenario and a fading-interest one, showing the lender's allowance as a share of its pro-rata part of unborrowed inventory:

| market path | lock rule | after 24 h | after 7 d | after 30 d | after 32 d |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| growing, always busy | v0.3 (PI / running minimum only) | 0% | 0% | 0% | 0% |
| growing, always busy | v0.4 (PI + lock_until) | 0% | 0% | 0% | 100% |
| growing, always busy | v0.4 without PI (lock_until only) | 0% | 0% | 0% | 100% |
| interest fades (100 of 900 stays out) | v0.3 (PI / running minimum only) | 94% | 94% | 94% | 94% |
| interest fades (100 of 900 stays out) | v0.4 (PI + lock_until) | 94% | 94% | 94% | 100% |
| interest fades (100 of 900 stays out) | v0.4 without PI (lock_until only) | 0% | 0% | 0% | 100% |

In a busy market only the time bound helps; when interest fades, Π returns 94 % within a day to the lender whose lock set `b_min`, while `lock_until` alone keeps it at 0 for a month. A later lock in the same era inherits that `b_min` and decays only once `ΣB_Q` falls below it, otherwise at `lock_until`. This over-locks, so I-P4 holds; the value side is KI-13 (AIR-11: by design, no change). Both are kept. Since AIR-12 (#117) the first lock of each lock era — taken while no lock is live, `lock_norm = 0` — restarts Π at `WAD` together with `b_min = ΣB_Q`; kept, Π multiplied every era's decay into the next while a small position kept `ΣB_Q` above 0, down to 1 by the sixth era of `pool_model.lock_era_demo`, after which locks no longer shrank when their borrowers repaid, only at `lock_until`.

Run test (two equal lenders, 90 % utilisation, then the whole borrowed inventory SOL-settled for 50 % of its token value; 1 raw token = 1 lamport):

| withdrawal rule | tokens A withdrew before S1 | A final value | B final value |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.1 first come, first served | 200,000,000,000 | 599,999,999,999 | 500,000,000,000 |
| v0.4 pro-rata + lock | 100,000,000,000 | 573,684,210,526 | 526,315,789,473 |

**Residual (disclose).** With fungible shares a runner still gains over its fair value; the gain depends on utilisation, and churn of other positions cannot raise it:

| utilisation | no churn | churn |
|---|---|---|
| 90% | +4.3% | +4.3% |
| 80% | +7.4% | +7.4% |
| 60% | +10.7% | +10.7% |
| 50% | +11.1% | +11.1% |
| 30% | +9.5% | +9.5% |

Exact pro-rata needs a separate share class for withdrawn lenders' borrowed claims (a withdrawal queue, owner item O10).

### 9.4 Lender SOL: entitlement, realised allocation, pot and vesting (R3A-2, R3B-2)

**Decision: option (a), SPEC-faithful and now live.** SPEC §2.4 says "rent accrues to the pool continuously". The brief offered (a) keep continuous rent entitlement and fix liveness with a MarketVault pot plus linear vesting of lump credits, or (b) one realised accumulator with linear release of all lender SOL. (a) is chosen because it can be made provably live, bounded and O(1) (below), and because (b) changes who earns rent in a way the sims show is materially worse for long-term lenders (table below). (b) is recorded as owner option O16.

```
accrue:        lender credit (§7) → acc (entitlement) and receivable;  vest release → acc AND acc_r
repay/settle:  credited = credited_for(pos);  receivable −= min(credited, receivable)
               principal: repay = the paid lender rent (rent = principal);  settle = sol_in − lump (rent = R_due, paid first)
               top-up = max(0, min(principal, rent) − credited)                     (M10-8C-1; pool::rent_top_up)
               Δ = (principal − top-up) − credited → acc   (repay: ≤ 0, only a shortfall is debited at once)
               principal − top-up → acc_r (realised, allocated to the shares holding now)
               LUMPS + top-up → vest: lender half of the penalty, expiry fee, the part of a settlement's take (S1,
               S2, S3) above ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due, P′ = P_ema, or 0 when the venue cannot execute (impact, fees,
               penalty; v0.4.1; S1 since AIR-21, P′ = 0 since AIR-09), and the rent paid above what was credited
               while the position was open (M10); the rest of a take is immediate but for the top-up
               a settlement that leaves ≤ 1/1,000 of the token claims it found: the whole vest → acc, acc_r (AIR-07)
sweeps, cashback: → vest
vest:          release ⌊vest_bal·Δt/(vest_end − vest_last)⌋ first; a lump x then sets
               vest_end = now + ⌊(vest_bal·(vest_end − now) + x·lump_vest_s)/(vest_bal + x)⌋    (value-weighted, v0.4.1;
                                                                                            floor since AIR-02)
touch(lender): pending += shares·Δacc; real += shares·Δacc_r;
               surplus = (real − claimed) − max(pending, 0) > 0  →  real −= surplus; MarketVault.unmatched += surplus      (v0.4)
claim_sol:     own = real − claimed;  draw = min(unmatched, max(0, pending − own));  unmatched −= draw; real += draw
               pay = min(pending, real − claimed, lender_sol);  pending −= pay; claimed += pay
cover_deficit: pending += x, real += x (that lender)
withdraw:      requires pending ≥ 0
```

- **Why a realised allocation at all.** Entitlement accrues continuously, but rent is paid only at exit; paying claims from any SOL in the vault would let a lender claim rent that is still a receivable (C0-13) and escape a later shortfall. Each lender therefore claims from SOL allocated to its shares when it arrived (`real`) — memoryful, so a second call pays 0 (V2-2).
- **Why the pot (R3A-2).** Realised SOL is allocated to the shares that hold when it arrives, entitlement to the shares that held while it accrued. A lender that withdrew between accrual and payment keeps an entitlement it could never claim, while newcomers receive realised SOL beyond their entitlement. v0.3 recycled that surplus into `acc_r` (to the other *current* shares, dividing by `S − shares`), which never reaches a 0-share lender: the reviewer's wind-down test stranded 68.12 SOL of 51,169 SOL (worst seed 15.35 SOL). v0.4 moves the surplus into one MarketVault counter that any lender may draw from, up to its own deficit. It needs no division and no keeper recycling duty (`claim_sol` already writes `MarketVault`); a surplus reaches the pot at the surplus lender's next touch, and `sync_lender` stays permissionless for lenders that never return. ★Behind a rebase record (§9.5) only the position's owner converts it (KI65-REV-SEC1, owner decision of 2026-10-07); anyone else's `sync_lender` is the **pot-only touch** (KI-66, owner decision of 2026-10-07, seventh round; `pool::touch_pot`, `pool_model.Pool._touch_pot`): it accrues the lender's `pending` / `real` up to the values the record froze for its epoch (`acc_end`, `acc_r_end`) and then — **KI-66 full** (owner decision of 2026-10-08, eighth round) — along the lender's **forward chain**: the floor of its shares through the later records (`q_{E+1} = ⌊s/den_E⌋`, `q_{j+1} = ⌊q_j·num_j/den_j⌋`, 0 through a full wipe), which no conversion order can lower, accrued to each later record's ends and to the current `acc` / `acc_r` by cumulative floor (`⌊q·end/WAD⌋ − ⌊q·snap/WAD⌋`, so any number of touches sums to one floor), parking on a live wipe record (a current or parked lender's own accrual stays per touch, `⌊s·(end − snap)/WAD⌋` as since M1, so each touch between two credits may floor away up to 1 lamport of its entitlement and of its realised SOL: known-issues KI-68); then its surplus moves to the pot. It converts no shares, writes no `conv_rem` and leaves the epoch and the lock, so it orders no conversion; a second one moves nothing; the owner's later conversion takes the chain's accruals back exactly (§9.5), so its `pending` ends bit-equal to the owner converting alone, and `real + unmatched` too (`pool_model.fwd_differential`: 2,000 random pools with 6,376 windowed third-party touches, every lender bit-equal once the owners converted). A surplus holder that never returns therefore no longer keeps the deficit lenders waiting, neither for what it held at the roll (`ki65_it`: A, short 2,827,173,914 lamports, is paid in full once a third party's touch of C fills the pot with 4,434,782,609; C's owner converting later is paid as had it converted first) nor for what its unconverted shares earn after the roll (`pool_model.pot_chain_demo`, pinned in `ki65_it`: A, short 201,209,837 with the seventh round's held-only touch, is paid in full by the chain's 201,209,836; `pool_model.pot_liveness_table`, 2,000 random pools with absent lenders, 654 of them with an absent lender behind a rebase record: no present lender unpaid above dust, 9 lamports in total, against 8 runs and a worst 2,134,755,812 with the hold alone (the eighth round's measurement) and 13 runs, a worst 1,830,229,138 without the pot-only touch). **What stays with the owner** is the latent rounding unit: an owner's shares are its chain's floor or up to one more per record, and that unit's earnings after the record reach the pot only at the owner's conversion (`pool_model.latent_residual_table`: 3,000 residue-tuned pools, 31 runs above dust, worst 727,760 lamports, 0 when every owner returns; the floor design's 2 × 20,000 adversarial pools, 1 run owed more than 10,000 lamports beyond what every owner returning leaves, 16,229 in total, worst 14,894; there is no general small bound when `S` is tiny; known-issues KI-66). **Over-move bound:** a chain whose owner sits a unit above it moves at most `(σ − q)·max(0, a − r)` more per epoch than the owner's own touch would (`a`, `r`: one share's entitlement and realised SOL in that epoch; measured: 5 of 15,756 touches, 1 lamport each, `pool_model.py`'s 5,000-pool differential line); the held-only touch over-moved up to `σ·(…)` on the same measure. The owner draws it back from the pot like any deficit lender. Someone must send the touch: the web's claim prepends the pot-only syncs the claimant needs (eighth round; FRONTEND §3.6), and an operator sends them too (ARCH §7, ops item 13). First-come priority among deficit lenders is accepted.
- **Liveness invariant (asserted).** After every position resolves, the vest is released and every lender is synced, sequential claims pay out `lender_sol − dust`: `Σ_i max(pending_i, 0) ≥ lender_sol − dust` by the entitlement identity, `Σ_i (real_i − claimed_i) + unmatched = lender_sol − dust` by the realised identity, and each lender's own allocation never exceeds its entitlement after the move to the pot. `pool_model.check()` and the fuzz run the full wind-down on a copy every 2,000 operations. Dust is per-touch flooring plus SOL credited to rebase-orphan shares (§9.5).
- **Bounded.** A claim never exceeds the lender's own entitlement; the pot never goes negative; `Σ(real − claimed) + unmatched + vest_bal ≤ lender_sol` and `claimed ≤ real` for every lender (I-P2).
- **Lumps vest (R3B-2).** Rent is credited continuously, so a just-in-time depositor earns ≈ 0 of it; every lump used to be credited to the shares existing when it arrived and was claimable at once, so any holder of the token could bundle `[deposit → expire / liquidate / claim_venue_rewards / sweep_trader → withdraw → claim_sol]` and take its pro-rata share. With linear vesting over `lump_vest_s` (6 h), the atomic bundle gets nothing and a whale must hold its shares through the window, exposed like any lender, to earn `h/6` of its pro-rata lump share.
- **The rent top-up vests too (M10, finding M10-8C-1).** Rent is credited continuously, but an exit within `min_rent_s` of the open pays `R_due = R_min`, above what accrued, and a settlement pays `R_due` whole (no protocol share) against a credit that is net of the protocol share. v0.4.1 credited that difference at once, so the atomic bundle took its pro-rata part: a whale's `[deposit 9×, liquidate, claim_sol, withdraw]` around the liquidation of a ≈ 3 SOL position opened a minute before took ≈ 9/10 of it (LiteSVM, M10 WP-8c: 128,972 of the lenders' 145,549 lamports of its `R_min` of 171,233). As built since M10 (the owner's decision of 2026-10-02, SPEC §14 M10-8C-1) the positive part `max(0, min(principal, rent) − credited_for(pos))` (`pool::rent_top_up`) vests with the lump on every exit path — close, liquidation, expiry and S1/S2/S3, where the settlement's immediate part pays `R_due` first; the credited part stays immediate, and a shortfall is still debited at once. The atomic bundle gets 0 again (`lender_real_it::finding_m10_8c_1_*`: an early liquidation and an early close give the whale 0; on an S2 two minutes after the open it is paid at most its share of the extinguished tokens' value — for the tokens it gave up — and none of the rent); a whale holding `h` takes `h/lump_vest_s` of its share of the top-up, as of a lump, and a lender that leaves within `lump_vest_s` of the exit forfeits its part of the top-up to the stayers, as for lumps (R4B-4). The fuzzer's JIT capture oracle bounds a bundle's gain around a liquidation, expiry or settlement by that rule (ARCH §8.4).
- **The premium of an S2/S3 take vests too (v0.4.1, R4B-6).** An S2/S3 pays `x = min(V − K_s, FV)`, and FV is an executable buyback cost at EMA reserves: it exceeds the market value `Q·P` by impact and fees, in S2 by more than σ_max by definition. v0.4 paid `B̂_FV + R_due` immediately, so a token whale could bundle `[deposit → sol_settle → withdraw → claim_sol]` (the S3 timer is public and `sol_settle` permissionless) and take its pro-rata share of that premium. Now only `min(x, ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due)` — the zero-impact value of the extinguished tokens at the EMA price plus the rent, which was entitled to the lenders who held while it accrued — is immediate; the rest vests: `lump = x − min(x, ⌊Q·P′/WAD⌋ + R_due)` on S1, S2 and S3, with `P′ = P_ema`, or 0 when the venue cannot execute (#117). **S1 (AIR-21).** v0.4.1 kept S1 immediate on the argument that it pays `V − K_s < B̂ + ert`, a loss against the buyback cost; but `B̂` carries the buyback's impact and fees, so an S1 take can exceed the tokens' zero-impact value plus the rent, and a just-in-time deposit bundled around the S1 took its pro-rata share of that excess at once (LiteSVM `air_21_*`: a take of 8,949,208,683 lamports against 8,892,623,822; the 9× whale was paid 50.9 M above its share of the tokens' value, now nothing above it). A genuine S1 loss (`V − K_s ≤ ⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due`) has no lump: it stays immediate but for its rent top-up (below). **A venue that cannot execute (AIR-09).** Nothing is observed there: `P_ema` stays at its pre-halt value and the box collapses to the EMA point, so nothing checks that price against the market, and a holder of cheap tokens bundled `[deposit, sol_settle, claim_sol, withdraw]` around an S3 to swap its share of `Q` for SOL at the stale price (KI-24's adverse selection). The tokens are therefore priced at 0 there: the whole token part of the take vests, and only `R_due` stays immediate, but for its top-up (below; S1 and S2 run on an executable venue only). The top-up is different: it is rent no lender was entitled to before the exit (`R_min` above the accrued rent within `min_rent_s`; past it, the protocol share that a settlement's `R_due` does not carry, about `rent_protocol_bps` of the rent), so vesting it moves only that amount to the lenders who stay through `lump_vest_s`, as for every lump; credited at once, an S1 within `min_rent_s` would hand a JIT depositor its pro-rata share. ★**B2-2, kept as built (the owner's decision of 2026-10-03):** every settlement's top-up vests this way, so S1 is immediate but for it (KI-52) *(superseded by AIR-21 for S1's take above `⌊Q·P_ema/WAD⌋ + R_due`, above)*. A whale at 9× the pool around an S2/S3 of the last open position (Q worth 500 SOL at `P_ema`):

| premium of B_FV over Q.P_ema | model | take above Q.P_ema (lamports) | whale's atomic gain (lamports) |
|---|---|---|---|
| 3.5% | v0.4 | 27,850,000,000 | 15,749,999,999 |
| 3.5% | v0.4.1 | 27,850,000,000 | 0 |
| 7.0% | v0.4 | 45,700,000,000 | 31,499,999,999 |
| 7.0% | v0.4.1 | 45,700,000,000 | 0 |
| 20.0% | v0.4 | 112,000,000,000 | 89,999,999,999 |
| 20.0% | v0.4.1 | 112,000,000,000 | 0 |

- **The vest end is value-weighted (v0.4.1, R4B-5).** v0.4 restarted the 6 h window for the whole balance on every lump, so a 1-lamport donation plus a permissionless `sweep_trader` every minute turned the linear release into a roughly exponential one (τ ≈ 6 h). Now a lump moves the end by its share of the balance, and a dust lump not at all (the floor; AIR-02, #117: v0.4.1 rounded up, so every lump of 1 lamport or more moved the end exactly 1 s later — `sweep_trader` is permissionless, has no minimum and rent is checked per transaction, so 1,000 one-lamport sweeps in one transaction moved it 1,000 s, and one a second held it `lump_vest_s` away: 63.2 % released at 6 h, 98.2 % at 24 h). The end never moves earlier while `lump_vest_s ≥ vest_end − now`; after a timelocked decrease of `lump_vest_s` below the remaining span a lump pulls it earlier, to a weighted point between the old end and `now + lump_vest_s` (it costs about the vest balance itself, so it is no practical grief). A 10 SOL lump, then a 1-lamport sweep every k seconds:

| model | k (s) | released after 6 h | after 24 h |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4 (every lump restarts the window) | 3600 | 66.5% | 98.7% |
| v0.4 (every lump restarts the window) | 600 | 63.7% | 98.3% |
| v0.4 (every lump restarts the window) | 60 | 63.3% | 98.2% |
| v0.4.1 (value-weighted end, rounded up) | 3600 | 100.0% | 100.0% |
| v0.4.1 (value-weighted end, rounded up) | 600 | 99.8% | 100.0% |
| v0.4.1 (value-weighted end, rounded up) | 60 | 98.4% | 100.0% |
| AIR-02 (value-weighted end, rounded down) | 3600 | 100.0% | 100.0% |
| AIR-02 (value-weighted end, rounded down) | 600 | 100.0% | 100.0% |
| AIR-02 (value-weighted end, rounded down) | 60 | 100.0% | 100.0% |

- **An exit forfeits the unreleased vest (disclosed, R4B-4).** Vesting pays lumps to whoever holds shares during the window, so a lender that leaves forfeits its unreleased part to the lenders who stay — to a dust holder, if only dust stays: an honest lender with all shares but 1,000 raw units that leaves 10 minutes after 3 SOL of lumps gets 0.083 SOL and the dust holder 2.917 SOL. The "last lender is paid the vest" rule applies only when the withdrawal takes `S` to 0 (the SOL would otherwise have no lender). The reviewer's suggested fix — release the vest to the current shares before any withdrawal that leaves less than 1 % of `S` — is **rejected**: it is the JIT snipe again for a whale large enough to trigger it (199× the pool around a 3 SOL lump: the whale takes 2.985 SOL atomically, against 0 without the flush). A standing dust position that captures lumps must be alone in the market for up to 6 h after the real lenders leave; that is the normal wind-down of a short-lived memecoin market, so the frontend warns a lender that exits with an unreleased vest. ★A lender keeps its part by waiting until the market's `vest_end` (at most `lump_vest_s` after the latest lump; §9.5's lender guidance); in a pool that rebased to a few raw token units, the vest the last real lenders forfeit goes to rebase-orphan shares and is claimable by nobody (§9.5, KI-65).

The last table is also the case against option (b): under (b) the whale that holds for 6 hours takes 90 % of the position's rent *and* lump (2.94 SOL of 3.27), because all lender SOL is released to whoever holds shares during the window; under (a) it takes 90 % of the lump only (1.09 SOL) and none of the rent, which was entitled to the lenders who held while it accrued.

**Owner option O16 — option (b), one realised accumulator.** All lender SOL (rent paid at exit, lumps; settlement principal immediate) enters `vest` and is released into a single accumulator; claims pay `min(pending, lender_sol)`. It removes `acc_r`, `real`, `claimed`, `unmatched`, `receivable`, true-ups, negative pending and `cover_deficit`, and is trivially live (the fuzz runs it too). Trade-off: rent goes to whoever holds shares during the release window rather than over the position's life, so just-in-time capital earns rent it did not fund (table above), and SPEC §2.4's "rent accrues to the pool continuously" holds for the pool but not per lender. A longer window reduces the capture per hour held but delays every lender's income.

Shortfalls (`Δ < 0`) arise only when a position exits with less than `R` of equity left: an expiry reserves rent against any sandwich, a liquidation does not (v0.4.1, §8.2), so in the last few percent before S1 a sandwiching keeper can add to a shortfall up to the bound's slack. A shortfall is debited pro-rata to current shares, so a lender who joined after the rent was credited bears part of it (disclosed); a lender with `pending < 0` must `cover_deficit` before withdrawing.

- **The late depositor's exposure (M10; the owner's decision of 2026-10-03: a deposit warning, no program change).** The debit is not bounded by what the late lender ever received or deposited: in the fuzzer's FUZZ-M7-24H-3 (an AMM v4 market, 3 days without a keeper on a near-cap position, ≈ 3,640 SOL of credited receivable) a lender that deposited afterwards and took ≈ 59 % of the shares owed ≈ 1,044 SOL after the position's short exit, having received 0 (since AIR-07's drain gate that exact deposit, into a pool with nothing unborrowed, is refused `PoolDrained`; the exposure remains for a deposit made while more than 1/1,000 of the claims is unborrowed: the fuzzer's revised reproducer owes 1,028.408 SOL at 1.98 ‰ unborrowed, 99.8 % utilisation); until it covers, the earlier lenders' positive `pending` can be paid only from its cover, and if it abandons its tokens that SOL is never paid out. A receivable-aware share price was not adopted. Instead the deposit review shows the receivable the deposit meets — the stored `receivable` plus the credit its own prologue books first (`RentBasis.lenderCredit`) — and this deposit's part of it should none of it be paid, `⌈receivable·shares/(S + shares)⌉` at the pool's size after the deposit (more if others withdraw first). It warns (expanded, and repeated in the review) when that part is ≥ 1 % of the deposit's value at `P_ema` (`UNPAID_RENT_WARN_BPS` = 100) or ≥ 1 SOL (`UNPAID_RENT_WARN_LAMPORTS`, the only threshold without a price), and says a resulting `pending < 0` must be covered (`cover_deficit`) before withdrawing (`lender.unpaidRent`, FRONTEND §3.6 / §4.2). The thresholds are web constants (`apps/web/src/config/disclosures.ts`), not parameters; the owner confirmed 1 % / 1 SOL on 2026-10-03 (fourth round).
- **What the fuzzer checks (M10's fuzz follow-ups).** Its deficit tripwire bounds every deficit, before the wind-down covers it, by the lender's pro-rata share of the shortfall the landed exits realised (from the exits' events and the observed receivable, cross-checked against each position's `rent::credited_for`), plus 0.001 SOL. It is tight when no rent is credited after the exit (FUZZ-M7-24H-3's reproducer, revised for AIR-07's drain gate: a 1,028.408-SOL deficit at 999,998 ppm of its bound; 1,044.467 SOL at 99.9998 % before); later credits pay back part of a deficit, so the bound is looser there. The `stale_receivable` action reaches deficits of up to 6.7 SOL on AMM v4 and 2.1 SOL on CPMM in 10-minute runs, and 0.01 SOL once on PumpSwap in 5 minutes (`tests/fuzz/README.md`; ARCH §8.4).

| model | A claimed | A pending after shortfall | A could exit | B claims now | B pending left | sol_vault | receivable |

| v0.4 | 500,000,000,000 | 1,688,356,163 | True | 501,688,356,164 | 0 | 1,688,356,165 | 0 |

### 9.5 Share epochs, post-wipe credits and `S = 0` (C0-6, V2-12, R3A-10)

Tokens per share only fall (settlements extinguish token claims and keep shares).

```
after every settlement:
    if S > 0 and 1,000·(T + B_Q) ≤ the token claims T + B_Q it found:  flush vest_bal into acc/acc_r     # AIR-07 (#117): a settlement
                                       # that leaves ≤ 1/1,000 of the token claims it found pays the whole vest to the current shares
    if T + B_Q = 0 and S > 0:  flush vest_bal into acc/acc_r;  roll(num = 0, den = 1)          # v0.4: the wiped lenders keep their unreleased lumps
    elif S + VS > R_MAX·(T + B_Q + VA):  roll(1, 1,000^k)
roll(num, den):  epochs[e] = (acc, acc_r, num, den, S); e += 1; S = ⌊S·num/den⌋; every calendar bucket scaled; acc = acc_r = 0
lender touch:    for each epoch behind: pending/real from the record; shares = ⌊shares·num/den⌋; lock and lock_term scaled
credit while S = 0:   live full-wipe record → acc_end += x·WAD/S_old (V2-12);   otherwise → protocol_fees (v0.4)
deposit while S = 0: requires sweep_trader and claim_venue_rewards since S reached 0       (v0.4.1, R4A-7: while the wipe
                     record is live; AIR-08, #117: whenever S = 0, also after the last lender's exit)
deposit while S > 0, B_Q > 0 and 1,000·T ≤ T + B_Q: refused (PoolDrained)                   (AIR-07's residual, #117)
last lender leaving (withdraw of all remaining shares): the unreleased vest is released to it first (v0.4)
```

- **`S = 0` without a wipe (R3A-10).** If every lender withdrew, a later trader sweep or venue cashback used to go to `unassigned`, which the next depositor took in full, even with a dust deposit (and `claim_venue_rewards` is permissionless). No lender exists to own such SOL, so v0.4 sends it to `protocol_fees` and drops `unassigned`:

| model | dust depositor claims | former lender claims | to protocol |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.3 | 1,000,000,000 | 0 | 0 |
| v0.4 | 0 | 0 | 1,000,000,000 |

  **A deposit into an emptied pool waits for the sweep (AIR-08, #117).** v0.4.1 gated a deposit on the post-wipe sweeps only while a full-wipe record was live (R4A-7, below). On a market every lender left by ordinary withdrawals (`S = 0`, no wipe record; `LastSharesBorrowed` keeps `B_Q = 0` there) a dust deposit landed first, so the sweep vested the SOL on the trader side — venue cashback claimed into it, donations — to the new shares instead of crediting the protocol, and the last-lender flush (R3B-2) paid it all back: `[deposit(dust), claim_cashback, sweep_trader, withdraw]` in one transaction took it. Now a deposit needs the sweep bits **whenever `S = 0`**: the sweeps set them whenever `S = 0`, and the withdrawal that takes `S` to 0 clears them, so the SOL that reached the trader meanwhile goes to the protocol first (`pool_model.empty_pool_gate_demo`, the same market, deposit first):

| model | first step | attacker gets (lamports) | to protocol (lamports) |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4.2 (no gate after the last exit) | the dust deposit lands first | 1,000,000,000 | 0 |
| AIR-08 (a deposit waits for the sweep after the last exit) | deposit refused (WipeSweepPending) | 0 | 1,000,000,000 |

  A new market still starts with both bits set (nothing can have reached its trader); `sweep_trader` also runs the 0-lamport venue-reward sweep when `S = 0` without a wipe record and the trader holds no claimable cashback, so a pump market emptied by withdrawals can always take a deposit again (the M5-SEC-1 argument). The random-history drivers bundle a 0-lamport sweep after the last exit (no RNG drawn), so §9.6's runs, the lock-heavy mix and the stranding table keep their op streams.

- **Post-wipe credits (V2-12, R3B-2).** The full-wipe record takes the unreleased vest at the wipe and every credit while `S = 0`: V2-12 / R3B-2 after a full wipe: old epoch's lenders +4,000,000,000 lamports (3 SOL unreleased vest + 1 SOL post-wipe sweep); dust depositor that arrives first and triggers a 1 SOL sweep: 0 at once, 2,777,777 in total after a lender joins a minute later (that lender: 997,222,222); a lender with 0 shares 6 epochs behind caught up in one touch: True **v0.4 left one gap (R4A-7):** the first deposit finalised the record, so the keeper that executed the wiping `sol_settle` could land `[sol_settle → deposit(2 raw units) → claim_venue_rewards]` atomically and, if nobody else deposited within the vesting window, receive all of the pre-wipe cashback. v0.4.1 makes a deposit into a live wipe record wait until `sweep_trader` and `claim_venue_rewards` have run since the wipe (both permissionless, so a depositor bundles them; they credit the record while `S = 0`); on Raydium markets, which have no venue cashback, only the trader sweep is required, and a venue that cannot execute does not block deposits (nothing can be claimed from it then). **As built (M5-SEC-1):** `deposit` cannot tell whether the venue executes, so it requires the venue-reward bit on every pump-curve and PumpSwap market; on the curve `sweep_trader` sets that bit itself whenever the trader's pump UVA holds no claimable cashback (its lamports above rent), and pump's permissionless `claim_cashback` bundled before it empties the UVA into the trader — so the bit never waits for a venue that cannot execute, and an organic full wipe on a live curve (a gap into S1 after the lenders withdrew every free token) no longer locks the market's deposits, and hence its opens, while the curve lives (the M5 reproducer: `WipeSweepPending` after 30 days of sweeps). PumpSwap's cashback sits in pAMM's UVA WSOL account; **as built in M6 (#104)** `sweep_trader` reads it and sets the bit when it is missing or empty, and pAMM's permissionless `claim_cashback` bundled before it moves any balance into `ATA(trader, WSOL)`, which the sweep credits — the same no-dependence property (`pamm_it::gates::t_cashback_*`). **A market that left pump (AIR-05, #117)** keeps its UVA's claimable cashback, so the bit stays required: a curve market that graduated elsewhere moves to `None` and may adopt a Raydium pool, while its trader's pump UVA and the curve-era cashback in it survive both moves (pump's `claim_cashback` is permissionless and venue-independent). The requirement used to follow the current venue kind, so anyone's `[sol_settle (wiping), sweep_trader, deposit]` ended the wipe record before that cashback was swept and a later `[claim_cashback, sweep_trader]` vested it to the new lenders; now it follows the UVA (every market with `pump_uva` set), and `sweep_trader` reads that UVA off the pump venues too and sets the bit when it holds nothing claimable, as on the curve. SOL that reaches the trader after the first deposit comes from the new epoch's own activity or from donations, and vests to the new shares:

| model | first step after the wipe | dust depositor gets (lamports) | wiped lenders get (lamports) |
|---|---|---|---|
| v0.4 (deposit finalises the record at once) | ATT deposits first | 1,000,000,000 | 0 |
| v0.4.1 (deposit waits for the post-wipe sweep) | deposit refused (WipeSweepPending) | 0 | 1,000,000,000 |

The keeper still sweeps right after a full wipe (§5.8 duty 6); it is no longer the only protection.
- **A settlement that leaves dust claims pays the vest to the shares that held them (AIR-07, #117).** v0.4.1 flushed the unreleased vest to the old shares only at a full wipe (`T + B_Q = 0`). A run leaves the allowance's floor (1 raw token: `T` ended at 1 in all 300 random pools of the AI review's impact check), and a dust deposit before the last settlement leaves its own, so the epoch rebased instead of wiping and the unreleased vest went to whoever deposited next (`[deposit(1), sol_settle, deposit(X)]`: 4,999,993,333 of a 5 SOL lump; with no attacker 3,999,992,004 of 4 SOL to the next depositor). Now **a settlement that leaves at most 1/1,000 of the token claims it found pays the whole vest — its own lump and rent top-up included — to the current shares**, as a full wipe does (`pool::VEST_FLUSH_RATIO`; `SolSettle.vest_flushed` reports every flush, with or without a roll, and `EpochRolled.vest_flushed` too when the epoch rolls, ARCH §3.10). The ratio bounds a just-in-time deposit around such a settlement to 1/1,000 of the flush whatever the pool's history (its own tokens stay as claims: 4,995,003 lamports of a 5 SOL lump for 10⁹ around a settlement of 10¹²); a larger deposit blocks the flush and takes nothing at once (R4B-6's rows are unchanged). Flushing at every roll was rejected (it reopens R4B-6 near the `R_MAX` bound: a 9× whale around a 2 % settlement took 4.5 of a 5 SOL lump atomically), and so was a fixed dust floor treated as a full wipe (a pre-deposit just above the floor bypasses it, and it leaves `T > 0` with `S = 0`). **The residual and the drained-pool gate (the gate kept by the owner on 2026-10-12):** a deposit above 1/999 of the claims made before the settlement stopped the flush, and a later deposit at the collapsed share price took the vest by holding through it (3·10⁹ before and 3·10¹⁰ after a settlement of 2·10¹² lent, held 6 h: 4,613,534,243 lamports of a 5 SOL lump). So `deposit` refuses with `PoolDrained` (ARCH §3.11, 6108) while `S > 0`, `B_Q > 0` and `1,000·T ≤ T + B_Q` — the only states a flushing settlement can start from, since a settlement leaves `T` alone — and no deposit can stand between a pool and its flush. Any repayment above 0.1 % of `B_Q` reopens deposits; `S = 0` is exempt (no vest to keep). The cost falls on honest depositors: in the sims 26 of 6,653 deposits in §9.6's fuzz at the 90 % cap (22 of 6,653 in release mode; 234 of 6,607 at a 100 % cap, out of reach on-chain, `util_cap_bps ≤ 9,500`), 102 of 5,910 in the lock-heavy mix (§9.3) and 378 of 26,749 in the stranding table (§9.4) are refused, and while a pool is drained no newcomer can supply exit liquidity (at the program's caps the state needs a run first: the lenders took the unborrowed part down to ≤ 0.1 %). With the gate a just-in-time deposit around any settlement captures nothing (#59). `pool_model.rebase_vest_demo` (lamports; JIT rows: the deposit's atomic gain beyond its tokens' value):

| case | model | outsider gets | old lenders get |
|---|---|---|---|
| attack: 1 raw unit deposited before the last settlement, 10^6 after | v0.4.2 (flush at a full wipe only) | 4,999,993,333 | 45,000,006,664 |
| organic: two lenders withdrew all they could at 90 % (1 raw token left), 10^6 deposited after | v0.4.2 (flush at a full wipe only) | 3,999,992,004 | 46,000,007,993 |
| JIT: 1e+09 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | v0.4.2 (flush at a full wipe only) | -1 | 1,004,000,998,994 |
| JIT: 1e+10 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | v0.4.2 (flush at a full wipe only) | -2 | 995,099,009,900 |
| residual: 3e9 (above 1/1,000) deposited before, 3e10 after, held 6 h | v0.4.2 (flush at a full wipe only) | 4,613,534,243 | 45,386,465,756 |
| attack: 1 raw unit deposited before the last settlement, 10^6 after | AIR-07 (flush when at most 1/1,000 of the claims is left) | 0 | 49,999,999,998 |
| organic: two lenders withdrew all they could at 90 % (1 raw token left), 10^6 deposited after | AIR-07 (flush when at most 1/1,000 of the claims is left) | 0 | 49,999,999,999 |
| JIT: 1e+09 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | AIR-07 (flush when at most 1/1,000 of the claims is left) | 4,995,003 | 1,003,996,003,996 |
| JIT: 1e+10 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | AIR-07 (flush when at most 1/1,000 of the claims is left) | -2 | 995,099,009,900 |
| residual: 3e9 (above 1/1,000) deposited before, 3e10 after, held 6 h | AIR-07 (flush when at most 1/1,000 of the claims is left) | 4,613,534,243 | 45,386,465,756 |
| attack: 1 raw unit deposited before the last settlement, 10^6 after | AIR-07 + no deposit while drained (residual fix) | 0 | 50,000,000,000 |
| organic: two lenders withdrew all they could at 90 % (1 raw token left), 10^6 deposited after | AIR-07 + no deposit while drained (residual fix) | 0 | 49,999,999,999 |
| JIT: 1e+09 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | AIR-07 + no deposit while drained (residual fix) | 0 | 1,005,000,000,000 |
| JIT: 1e+10 deposited just before the settlement of 1e12, out at once | AIR-07 + no deposit while drained (residual fix) | 0 | 1,005,000,000,000 |
| residual: 3e9 (above 1/1,000) deposited before, 3e10 after, held 6 h | AIR-07 + no deposit while drained (residual fix) | 0 | 50,000,000,000 |

- **Rebase-orphan dust.** A rebase converts each lender with `⌊shares/1,000^k⌋` and `S` with `⌊S/1,000^k⌋`, leaving up to one orphan share per lender; SOL credited to orphan shares is claimable by nobody. While a real lender holds shares it is at most `(lenders in the epoch)/S_new` of later credits, with `S_new ≥ 1,000·(T + B_Q)`: negligible unless the pool holds a few raw token units, which only the fuzz reaches (§9.6).
- ★**The rebase-orphan vest (M10; the final tree's 24 h fuzz, FUZZ-M10-24H-A).** That bound assumes a real lender stays. If every real lender exits while a vest runs, the orphans are all of `S`: the last real withdrawal leaves `S > 0`, so the last-lender flush (R3B-2; only a withdrawal that takes `S` to 0 since R4B-4, §9.4) cannot fire, and the crank releases the whole unreleased vest to the orphans, where it stays in `lender_sol`, claimable by nobody — as does every later credit until a lender deposits again. In the fuzz's triaged reproducers (the five crash files of run 3's FUZZ-M10-24H-A): 0.054 SOL on the curve (2 orphan shares, `S` = 2, after every lender left during a vest); where a lender stayed with dust it could not withdraw, one orphan of 1,621–4,294 shares took its `1/S`, 0.012–0.100 SOL (on AMM v4 its 1/4,294 of a 429-SOL vest). These are examples, not the measured bound: the largest orphan credit in the final tree's 24 h runs was 217,508,010 lamports (≈ 0.218 SOL) of `lender_sol` in one wind-down — run 4's oracle accepts `lender_sol` above its 0.01-SOL cap only up to the orphan shares' realised SOL — and it is run 3's sixth FUZZ-M10-24H-A failure, which got no crash file because the fuzzer keeps 5 per signature; its shape (a lender staying, or every lender gone during a vest) is not yet classified (an owner item, KI-65). Only a market whose pool rebased reaches it (token claims fell ≥ 10⁶-fold against shares: a pool of a few raw token units); the bound is the vest unreleased at the last real lender's exit, plus any credit before the next deposit. It is the spec's I-P6 dust ("rounding plus rebase-orphan credits", §9.4), not a defect; a program fix needs on-chain orphan tracking (counting the orphans out of `S` for the flush), which is not built — an owner item (KI-65). **Lender guidance:** to keep its part of a vest — and, in such a pool, to keep the vest from the orphans — a lender waits until the market's `vest_end` before withdrawing. `vest_end` is at most `lump_vest_s` after the latest lump (the value-weighted end, §9.4, while `lump_vest_s` has not been lowered since): 6 h by default, up to 604,800 s (7 days) at the compiled bound (the fuzz's curve case ran at that bound: 603,728 s of vest remained at the lenders' exit).

| model | new deposit 1e14 mints | new lender pending after 1,000 SOL lump + rent (lamports) | next deposit after 2nd wipe would mint | > u128 | epochs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| v0.1 (no epochs) | 1.000e+31 | 0 | 1.000e+45 | True | 0 |
| v0.4 (share epochs) | 1.000e+17 | 1,000,042,500,000 | 1.000e+17 | False | 2 |

- ★**The forward chain (KI-66 full, owner decision of 2026-10-08).** Since KI-65 a lender's conversion through a rebase record rounds cumulatively (`conv_rem`; I-P1′: `Σ shares = S` once every lender converted). The pot-only touch's forward chain (§9.4) changes none of that — a third party writes no share, `conv_rem`, epoch or lock (KI65-REV-SEC1), so I-P1′ is unchanged. A lender's chain state (`LenderPosition.fwd`, ARCH §2.3.6): **none**; **HOLD** — the pot-only touch accrued the lender's own epoch `E` to the record's ends and its chain covers `E + 1 … fwd_epoch` on `fwd_shares` (`⌊s/den_E⌋`, then the floor through each later record; the lender's own snapshot is implicit, the record's ends, while `snap` / `snap_r` hold the chain's in `fwd_epoch`); **OPEN** — the owner converted part of the way into the chain and its shares are `fwd_lag` ∈ {0, 1} above the chain's (the lag lemma: with the chain's shares `q = a·d + b` and the owner's `σ ≤ q + 1`, `conv_rem + σ ≤ (d − 1) + a·d + b + 1`, so the next `σ` is at most the next `q` + 1). The owner's conversion adds 0 at `E` in HOLD; at an epoch the chain accrued whole only the lag's part `⌊(q + lag)·A/WAD⌋ − ⌊q·A/WAD⌋` (one product); at `fwd_epoch` its own accrual less the chain's, `⌊σ·A/WAD⌋ − ⌊q·snap/WAD⌋`; then it drops the chain, as do the V2-12 jump, the parking and the current epoch. A third party continues a chain from `fwd_epoch` with the records it passes: the program's unsigned `sync_lender` takes up to `max_epochs` of them (a `u8`, bounded only by the transaction's size), and the web and the operator send at most `MAX_RECORDS` (8) per transaction; a lender's `LenderPosition` closes (KI-67) only without a chain.

`R_MAX = 10⁶` shares per raw token keeps `S ≤ 1.8·10²⁵ ≪ 2¹²⁸`; epoch records are per `(market, epoch)` and O(1) per lender per epoch; a lender whose shares convert to 0 jumps straight to the current epoch.

### 9.6 Fuzz results


The pool fuzz draws 18 operations, including time passing, governance changes of `rent_protocol_bps`, withdrawals of everything, repeated claims in one transaction, churn between withdrawals, sweeps, covers, shortfalls, partial and full wipes (a depositor bundles the post-wipe sweep 70 % of the time; a refused deposit is counted as `dep!` — since AIR-07's drained-pool gate (#117) also those refused `PoolDrained`: 26, 234 and 22 of the three runs' 6,653, 6,607 and 6,653 deposit attempts). After every operation it asserts I-P1–I-P7 — v0.4.1: I-P4 first on the state the program sees, each lender's allowance computed with only that lender touched, then after touching everyone; every 2,000 operations it runs the full wind-down on a copy and asserts the liveness bound. The residue it reports is rounding (no rebase-orphan shares since KI-65's cumulative conversion). The lines above are the pool model of the AI review's fixes (AIR-02, AIR-03, AIR-07, AIR-08, AIR-12; #117): the epochs rolled were 1,020 / 1,930 / 1,020 before AIR-07's drained-pool gate. The reviewers' lock-heavy mix (§9.3) runs separately with the same untouched-state check.

## 10. Bonding-curve specifics, graduation and venue changes

### 10.1 Graduation and the boost (C1-13, ADOPTED Q15)

★**Since short.win 1.0.1 (pump-final):** the keeper no longer completes curves: a position past `rt` completes the curve itself in its exit by pump's synthetic migration (§10.2), and anyone's completion — a third party's v2 buy of exactly `rt`, a v3 overshoot, or short.win's own exit — is followed by `migrate_v2`, which the keeper sends top-level in the same tick, and the `→ PumpSwap` transition. After a synthetic completion the pool opens at `(X₁, Y₁)` = (the live `curve_base_ata`, `rs + pcQ − fee`), above the curve's final price by ρ (1.063 on 98u8RJ's completion, 6.46 on E1bY's), and its boost is `⌊X₁·Y₁/10¹⁵⌋` (§3.1); `P_ema` carries the curve's final price into the warm-up, so after a large pool part the EMA leg can lag the pool for longer than 8 steps (the M5-SEC-4 class; known issues PUMP-2026-10-13-1 residual 1). The rows below are an exact completion's.

The curve completes when price reaches `(vt/279.9 M)²` times the current price (14.7× from 0 % progress, 5.84× from 50 %, 2.46× from 80 %, 1.65× from 90 %, 1.18× from 97 %). After migration the proportional boost adds a deterministic **+45.66 %** within ~6 minutes. Opens are refused while `boost_vault.amount > 0.05 SOL` and `now < graduated_at + 900 s`. Simulated with migration 2 s after completion and the boost spent over 360 s, **under the program's graduation rule since #105** (the normative sim models M6 as built: the market moves onto the pool in the prologue of its next instruction, after the roll of the curve's last bucket, the ring rewritten to the pool's `lp_supply` and the observation state restarted — the 8-bucket warm-up of §5.2 — with nothing recorded in that instruction; the boost at the ELF's rounding; §5.2, ARCH §2.5):

Positions opened at 80–90 % progress are liquidated on the completing buy or by the boost push that follows (D@90 %: at 2.34×, 302 s after completion), lenders made whole (+5.2–5.3 %); at 97 % the position survives graduation and is liquidated only if the price keeps rising; positions opened at ≤ 50 % progress go to S1 on the completing buy. The keeper's exit on a graduated position comes after the warm-up, 8–12 s after the migration (D@80 %: +12 s, the migration at +2 s) — accepted by the owner on 2026-09-30 (M6R-4, SPEC §14).

**The graduation as built (M6, #104; the sim since #105).** Until #104 this table carried v0.4.1's rule — `P_ema` carried over with the ring rewritten to the pool's `√(X·Y)` and no restart. Against the table published through #104 the outcome is the same in every row; what moved:

| market | scenario | published through #104 (v0.4.1 rule) | the program's rule (this table, #105) |
|---|---|---|---|
| D@50% | gap / +2x / +3x | S1 +50 s at 6.39× / 8.14× / 9.37×; lenders 0 % + 2.8738, keeper 0.0391 | S1 **+52 s** (no settlement is evaluated during the warm-up) at 6.39× / **8.33×** / **9.72×**; the same amounts |
| D@80% | gap, +2x, +3x | liquidation +15 s, 2.58×, CR 1.57/1.66, borrower −2.0910, lenders 100 % + 0.1449, keeper 0.0859 | liquidation **+12 s** (the warm-up seed reaches the pool's price faster than the carried EMA's blend), 2.58×, CR 1.57/1.58, borrower −2.0880, lenders 100 % + **0.1434**, keeper 0.0850 |
| D@90%, D@97% | every row | — | unchanged (the exits come after the gate) |

**On the fork** (`graduation_scenarios`, the dumped pump, pump-fees and PumpSwap ELFs, a real `migrate_v2` and pump's real `boost_buy_and_burn` on the sim's schedule) every row reproduces **to the lamport** — exit second, cranks, spot at the exit, `B`, `B̂_ref`, `max_in`, rent, the penalty and its split, the S1 take, the CRs — against this sim with one input changed, `R_min` at the pre-open 0 % utilisation the program prices (§11.2's known difference): D@80 % lenders 100 % + 0.1421, D@90 % gap +303 s (+302 s here) with lenders + 0.1591 (0.1598 at `9aade3e`; 0.1606 on the AI review's fixes, #117, which the fork has not re-run), +2x / +3x + 0.1764 / + 0.1863 (0.1780 / 0.1878), D@97 % + 0.2085 / + 0.2224 (0.2104 / 0.2242). The pool a real `migrate_v2` creates from ECON's completed curve equals `CPPool.graduate(rQ − 15,000,001)` to the lamport (`lp_supply` 4,193,388,282,654 = its `isqrt(X·(Y + VQR))`), whatever the completion path, and the whole boost moves the price ×1.4566 on the ELF too; the ELF burns `⌊X·(b − 1)/(Y + b − 1)⌋` per boost chunk of `b` lamports, which the sim's `CPPool.boost_tick` now does (≈ 2,400 tokens fewer per chunk than v0.4.1's `⌊X·b/(Y + b)⌋`; it moves market C's post-boost reserves in the last digits and none of this document's printed figures). The atomic, self-financed `[buy_v2(rT), migrate_v2]` and the straddle paths are §10.2–§10.3's.

**The warm-up's price seed after a graduation (M6-SEC-1, owner decision of 2026-09-30, #105; §5.2).** The carried positions come onto the fresh pool at their full size, with the boost's push certain in the next minutes, and the warm-up seed read the lowest post-change bucket low: one `[sell, crank_price, buy]` next to the keeper's crank in any warm-up second set `P_ema` for the rest of the warm-up and delayed the keeper's exit. Since #105 the seed falls by at most one v0.4.1 step per integration (`attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` part 1, D@80 %, the honest exit +12 s): a low at 0.25× the pool's price in the 4th / 8th warm-up second delays it to +22 / +13 s instead of +28 s (+22 / +14 s before AIR-22, #117); in the 1st second still +28 s, because the carried seed itself is then at 0.55× the pool and climbs at ≤ ×7/6 per integration (the residual, §12.12 (12)); the lenders are whole in every row (tokens back plus 0.1463 SOL against 0.1434 honest), the attacker's round trip costs 0.63–1.08 SOL. A genuine dump right after the transition is tracked at the steady-state rate (the seed at the gate 1.03–1.67× the dumped price) and forces nothing: every position stays open, the spot leg reading the live price.

**Third-party LP around the transition (M6R-1, #105; §1.2).** A deposit between `migrate_v2` and the transition crank raises the `lp_supply` anchor; as built after M6 `L_slow` then kept the curve's value, 1.353× the pool's depth, decaying over an hour (the reference credited it: D@80 % lenders 0.1410 against 0.1434 honest). Since #105 the first PumpSwap observation caps `L_slow` at the pool's locked depth (1.000; the lenders' figures of the honest rows; `pumpswap_lp.py` part 2).

**Ring liquidity across venue changes (R3B-7 a).** The curve's `√(vT·vQ)` at completion (≈ 5.7·10¹²) is ≈ 1.35× the new pool's (≈ 4.2·10¹²); a Raydium fast path could land on a pool with a quarter of the depth (since #99 only on one whose opening caps every carried position fits, §6.1: a position at the size cap of a λ 50 % incumbent needs a candidate whose locked depth covers the incumbent's whole depth, ≥ 111 % of its reserves at λ 90 %; a thinner pool carries only a proportionally smaller position). v0.4 rewrites every ring entry's `l` and `lobs` at the change (v0.4.1: `l`, `llo`, `lhi`; M4R: on Raydium to the anchor `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋`, then the 8-bucket warm-up; §5.2). The graduation rows run the program's rule since #105 (the ring rewritten to the pool's `lp_supply`, then the 8-bucket warm-up; the carried-over variant keeps the ring's `L` through the same restart). The Raydium rows replay `FAST_SCENARIOS[1]`, the 120 % candidate (λ 90 %, the one of the scenarios' candidates the capped position fits, #99; before #99 the 25 % one: the same 4 wide seconds, all in the gate), whose switch is the program's rule (`Market.adopt_pool`); a cell `n (g in the gate)` counts `g` of the `n` wide seconds inside the warm-up gate, during which the box decides nothing:

At graduation the outcomes of these genuine moves do not depend on it. Under v0.4.1's graduation (no restart) carry-over left the settlement box more than 1 % wide in `L` for the first 4 seconds after the change (3 with v0.4's median of the clamped observations) — long enough to block or defer an S2/S3 amount that depends on it; since M6 the graduation restarts the observation state as a Raydium switch does (the rows' "rewritten to lp_supply (M6)", #105), so a carried-over ring's wide seconds (5 as built at #105) all lie inside the warm-up gate, during which the box decides nothing, and the rewritten ring has none. After a Raydium switch (M4R) the warm-up re-seeds and replaces the whole ring before the gate opens, so a carried-over ring's 4 wide seconds lie inside the gate, and the exits are the ones v0.4.1 had (v0.4.1's rewrite to `min(√(X·Y)_new, L_ema)`: 0 wide seconds, the same exits). **Since AIR-15 (#117)** the box's `L_hi` is capped at the new pool's `D`: the graduation rows and the rewritten Raydium rows have no wide second left (the rewritten ring had 4, all inside the gate), and only a carried-over Raydium ring keeps its 4 — no exit changes.

**Raydium adoptions: M4 as built and M4R** (#92, #93, #96; `attacks/venue_switch.py` (b), (c)). M4 as built rewrote the ring to the adopted pool's bounded depth `L_new = min(L_rec, L_live, D/λ_min)`; M4R rewrites it to the anchor `D/λ_min` and lets the 8-bucket warm-up re-seed it (§5.2). (b) is an honest fast path — the incumbent halts, the window opens at +120 s and adopts at +180 s, then a 2×/120 s pump from +300 s — onto thinner, deeper and `λ < λ_min` candidates, under v0.4.1's rule, M4 as built and M4R. - **Thinner** candidates with `λ ≥ λ_min` got the same `L_ema` under v0.4.1 and M4 (their `√(X·Y)` lies below the old `L_ema` and below the cap); M4R anchors them at `D/λ_min` (1.8× their depth at λ 90 %) and the warm-up brings `L_ema` to their depth (1.000) at the gate; the 4 wide seconds lay inside the gate, and since AIR-15's cap on `L_hi` (#117) there are none. The exits are the same under every rule that adopts. *(The scripts run on the AI review's fixes since #117, and their comparison rules change only what they name, so the v0.4.1 and M4 columns also carry AIR-15's cap: v0.4.1's deeper-candidate rows show 5 wide seconds instead of the 18 below (17 in (c)), its λ 25 % rows 4 instead of 20.)* **Since #99** the capped RA / RB position no longer moves onto the 25 %-depth candidate (`D` = 45 % of `pool_d`: the position is 3.3 % of the `X₀` an open on it would have, the cap 0.75 %): no window, the position frozen on the halted pool ("no adoption (#99 …)"). The deeper 150 % candidate (`D` = 2.7× `pool_d`) carries it. The §10.3 table replays `FAST_SCENARIOS`, whose switch is M4R's (`Market.adopt_pool`) behind the window floor and the #99 fit, which `fast_path_events` now checks at the proposal and at the execution (before #99 it applied no floor).
- A **deeper** candidate kept the old, thinner `L_ema` under v0.4.1, and the first honest buckets of the new pool left the box wide for 18 s (M4-SEC-6); M4 as built started the ring at the pool's own depth; M4R reaches it at the gate, with the box narrow from then on.
- An eligible candidate with **`λ < λ_min`** was written at the cap `D/λ_min` under M4 (half its depth at λ = 25 %), which left the box wide for 20 s (M4V-3). M4R starts at the same anchor, but the warm-up seed reads the pool's own bucket highs and reaches its depth (1.000) at the gate: the box is narrow from then on, and M4V-3's delay is gone. (M4's written cap was also the start of a forced-liquidation band on such pools, §12.12.) Since #99 the capped position moves onto neither λ 25 % candidate (`D` = 50 % and 100 % of `pool_d`, i.e. a quarter and half of the incumbent's depth: 3.0 % and 1.5 % of `X₀`), in (b) and in (c) alike; a λ 25 % candidate carries it only with four times the incumbent's reserves (§10.5 (d1)).
- RC's 25 % candidate (21.2 SOL real, 19.1 SOL locked) is below the floor under M4 and M4R: no window opens and the halted market keeps its position until an eligible pool appears (the λ = 25 % candidate with RC's own reserves, 21.2 SOL locked, likewise). Before #99 the §10.3 table's `fast_path_events` applied no floor and showed its switch anyway; it now refuses it (and the fit refuses it too).

(c) LP moves around the two samples M4's window adoption read (RA; the round trip costs no swap fee): "deposit" adds as much again as the pool's LP counter around the instruction (λ halves, `D` stays), "withdraw" removes every unlocked LP around it (`√(X·Y)` falls to `D`). Cells: `L_ema` / `√(X·Y)` at the switch (M4R: → at the gate), seconds with the box wider than 1 % in `L` (first 60 s; M4R: of which inside the gate):

Under M4 a deposit — anyone with capital can make one, and the round trip costs nothing but capital — raised only the sample it wrapped: under the M4 review's record-only rule a deposit around the recording proposal wrote 1.995× the pool's depth into the ring and left the box wide for 24 s (the program reproducer: 1.996×, 24 of 30 s; M4V-2); as built, the execution's own sample bounded it. A withdrawal by an unlocked LP of the candidate lowered either sample to `D`, `1 − λ` below the pool: 0.2 % at λ 99.78 %, 75 % at λ 25 % (box wide 25 s) — and that written `D` was not only a delay: held with a withdraw-wrapped crank every second, it forced the liquidation of near-threshold positions (M4R-1, §12.12). M4R reads neither sample, so its column is the same in every row, and the exit is the same in every row under every rule that adopts; since #99 M4R does not adopt the λ 25 % candidate here (the capped position is 1.5 % of the `X₀` an open on it would have), so its rows show no adoption and the position frozen, whatever the LP does.

### 10.2 Buyback larger than `real_token_reserves`

- `q = rt`: allowed; the buy completes the curve and the market enters Migrating.
- ★**Since short.win 1.0.1 (pump-final; owner D1 (a), D2, D3 of 2026-10-13; #119): `q > rt` on a live curve is one instruction.** pump's 2026-10-08 build buys past `rt` by **synthetic migration**: `buy_v3(q)` takes `rt` from the curve and `q − rt` from the would-be pool `(curve_base_ata − rt, rs + n₁ − fee)`, both parts at the curve's fee, all or nothing, and completes the curve. short.win trades every curve leg on v3, so a close, liquidation or expiry past `rt` executes that buy, priced `B̂_v3` (§3.1), and the market enters Migrating in the same instruction; σ is judged on `B̂_v3` at once (§5.6). The bundle below, its helper and the keeper's straddle plan are retired, and the γ guard stays (G21). The sim prices and executes it so since P7 (`Params.synthetic`, default on); its first table row is the **synthetic v3** row: the borrower's close past `rt` costs **1.8837 SOL** (the bundle's 1.9227; borrower P&L −0.3550 SOL against −0.3939 at the sim's 80 %-utilisation `R_min`), and `graduation_e2e::econ_10_2` reproduces it to the lamport on the fork (`B − growth` = 1,883,736,744 lamports, `R_min` 720,630, payout 4,603,772,566, `pcB` 2,260,866,303,878, `pcQ` 938,978,951). The bundle rows stay as the 1.0.0 record; the text below is that record.
- `q > rt` on a live curve (1.0.0): pump rejects the buy (6021). Prevented at open (`γ = 50 %`, ADOPTED Q14) and executed through a **Jito bundle of ≥ 3 transactions** (keeper completes the curve with its own SOL; `migrate_v2`; the position buys all of `q` on the new pool), priced with `B̂_str` (§3.1). Because pump rejects any buy above `rT` and never fills partially, a curve trade landing between the keeper's read and the bundle reverts it; ARCH adopts a `complete_curve(max_sol_cost)` helper that reads `rT` at execution (R3B-11, no economic change). **M5:** the guard is judged on the curve's `rT` at the EMA price when that is the smaller (#103, §6.1), so a sale wrapped around the open no longer lifts it; the bundle's first two transactions and `B̂_str` equal to the executed PumpSwap buy are verified on the fork (604,827,436 lamports on the near-complete fixture, unchanged by a curve buy between the keeper's read and the bundle); pump's undocumented cap byte (§1.1) would fill exactly `rt` for a caller that sets it, which short.win and the helper never do. A borrower can still make its own position a straddle after opening by buying `rt − q + 1` itself: the position then exits only through the bundle, whose third transaction is M6's (ARCH §5.4). **M6 (#104):** the borrower's own three-transaction bundle — tx3 = the pool's accounts, the transition crank and its `close_short` — closes the first table row at the sim's `B` exactly (1,922,674,204 lamports; P&L −0.3917 SOL against −0.3939 at the sim's 80 %-utilisation `R_min`; `graduation_e2e::econ_10_2_*`). The keeper's tx3 cannot carry its exit: the transition starts the post-change warm-up, so a keeper `liquidate` there reverts the bundle, and the keeper sends the transition as tx3 and liquidates on the pool 8 s later (T + 8; accepted by the owner on 2026-09-30, M6R-4); a borrower's self-made straddle is liquidated that way 9 s after the fallback bundle. **The sim models both since #105** (`Market._straddle_to_pool`): a keeper that finds a straddled position liquidatable or expired sends tx1–tx3 and exits on the pool once the warm-up has passed; a borrower's close runs its whole bundle at once, the rows below (unchanged: they are closes).

| policy | scenario | outcome | exit vs event | price at exit | buyback cost (SOL) | B_open (SOL) | borrower P&L (SOL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| synthetic v3 (one transaction) | rt -> Q/2, borrower closes 30s later | close | +30s | 1.193x | 1.8837 | 1.5745 | -0.3550 |
| synthetic v3 (one transaction) | rt -> Q/2, then others complete curve at +10min, close after | close | +30s | 1.193x | 1.8837 | 1.5745 | -0.3550 |
| straddle quote B_str (bundle path, retired) | rt -> Q/2, borrower closes 30s later | close | +30s | 1.193x | 1.9227 | 1.5745 | -0.3939 |
| straddle quote B_str (bundle path, retired) | rt -> Q/2, then others complete curve at +10min, close after | close | +30s | 1.193x | 1.9227 | 1.5745 | -0.3939 |
| no straddle quote | rt -> Q/2, borrower closes 30s later | S1 | +56s | 1.193x | 0.0000 | 1.5745 | -4.9566 |
| no straddle quote | rt -> Q/2, then others complete curve at +10min, close after | S1 | +56s | 1.193x | 0.0000 | 1.5745 | -4.9566 |

Without a straddle quote the position looks unquotable and settles S1 (the "no straddle quote" rows), which is why every pricing account is mandatory (C0-2). (`docs/design/sim/out/full.txt` holds the table; the synthetic rows are the sim's default since pump-final P7, the bundle rows its `synthetic = False` comparison.)

### 10.3 Migration pause, the S3 timer and the Raydium fast path (SPEC §5.3; C0-16; ADOPTED Q5; R3A-4, R3B-8)

- While Migrating, liquidations and closes pause; rent accrues; the EMA keeps integrating the completed curve's (final) price. After `max_migration_pause_s = 3,600 s` a stuck migration counts as no route. **A migration PumpSwap stalls (M6-SEC-3; owner decision of 2026-09-30, #105):** while pAMM's `disable_flags` bit 0 (`create_pool`) is set, pump's `migrate_v2` fails, so a curve that completes stays Migrating; a liquidatable position settles S3 after the pause and `s3_wait_s`, a healthy one only at its deadline (the fork: S3 at 259,828 s ≈ 72 h after the open, the lenders 0.6224 SOL in SOL, no tokens back). New curve opens are therefore refused while the bit is set (`GraduationHalted`; ARCH §3.4); positions opened before it keep that exposure.
- **What the migration pause assumes (M5, as built).** The pause is bounded because the market leaves `PumpMigrating` for PumpSwap once the canonical pool exists (the `→ PumpSwap` transition rewrites the ring, §10.1). The M5 build has no such transition (M6), so a migrated market would stay `PumpMigrating` with the EMA frozen at the last curve price while the pool trades on: on the M5 fork a `migrate_v2` 1 s after completion froze `P_ema` at 0.643× the pool's price, and the D@80 % position the completion put below its threshold waited for its deadline and settled S3 at the frozen price — lenders 1.9409 SOL for `Q` worth 2.6821 SOL at the pool (−27.6 %), the borrower keeping 2.4975 SOL — and an atomic, self-financed `[complete, migrate_v2]` makes the same freeze deterministic (the pool at 3.79× the frozen EMA after one buy; M5-SEC-4). The M5 build therefore cannot enable the curve venue (ARCH §2.3.2: `VENUE_BITS_UNFINISHED`), and M6's transition must cover both cases, a borrower's self-made straddle (buying `rt − q + 1`) included (ARCH §5.4). **Closed in M6 (#104):** the transition runs in the first instruction that reads the valid pool, so no market stays `PumpMigrating` after `migrate_v2`; the atomic `[complete, migrate_v2]` with the pool at 3.63× the last curve EMA is liquidated on the pool 9 s after the transition, the lenders getting `Q` back plus 0.1018 SOL (`graduation_e2e`); the curve venue is enableable. Where no venue prices — `None`, a migrated or unparseable curve — M5 prices FV on a cap-only constant-product view of the EMA state (PROPOSED; ARCH §3.8: 2.9888 SOL against 3.0867 on the deterministic pool for that D@80 % position).
- **Halted venues (V2-5).** While a pool cannot execute, no observation is recorded; liquidatable positions reach S3 after the continuous chain, healthy positions stay frozen until the route returns or their deadline (owner item O14) — on Raydium also when no fast-path candidate fits them (#99, below). The settlement box of such a market is the EMA point (nothing can be pushed), and an S3 at that frozen mark vests all but `R_due`'s credited part (its rent top-up vests too, B2-2; AIR-09, #117: the tokens are priced at 0 for the immediate part, so a cheap-token holder's `[deposit, sol_settle, claim_sol, withdraw]` around the public S3 time realises nothing at the stale price; §8.6, §9.4).
- **Continuous S3 chain:** every observation records `s3_no_route = !venue_executable ∨ route_broken ∨ φ_live > min(global venue_fee_hard_cap_bps, mark)`, the S3 chain at `min(global, mark)` — the mark `Market.min_open_hard_cap_bps` being the lowest cap an open position locked since the market last emptied (AIR-19, #117; the global cap alone while unset) — a market-level predicate (ARCH §5.3, A26); `no_route_since` restarts if consecutive no-route observations are more than 60 s apart. S3 is allowed iff the position is liquidatable **or expired**, the position has no route now — its no-route at `min(global, its locked cap)` —, the chain is unbroken, and `now ≥ max(no_route_since, graduated_at + grad_grace_s, migration_seen_at + max_migration_pause_s·[migrating]) + s3_wait_s`, the three timers at the position's snapshot values (defaults 600, 3,600 and 600 s). A no-route caused only by the flag or the fee cap still needs the position to be liquidatable with the spot leg evaluated normally, or expired. S3 on an executable venue (flag or fee above the hard cap) is simulated (§8.6). `pool_fail` (below) keeps the global cap. **Why the locked caps (AIR-19):** a position's keeper exits are bounded at its own locked cap and its S1 legs priced there, so once Governance raised the global cap above a position's locked cap, a fee between the two was a route under the global cap alone: the chain never started, `expire` / `liquidate` refused `ExitBoundNotMet`, and an expired healthy position had no exit at all. The mark is conservative by design (a closed position may have set it): the chain can start at a fee that is still a route for every open position, which keep their route; the owner accepted that on 2026-10-12 rather than track each cap exactly (known issues). The normative sim has a single hard cap and nothing to mirror.
- **Raydium fast path on its own predicate (R3A-4, R3B-8).** `pool_fail = !venue_executable ∨ φ_live > venue_fee_hard_cap_bps` — venue-side evidence only, never the Emergency flag — drives a separate chain `pool_fail_since` / `last_pool_fail_obs` with the same 60 s gap rule. After `pool_fast_route_s` (120 s) of continuous failure, the first proposal of an eligible candidate with `D ≥ pool_fast_min_bps·pool_d` (25 %) that passes the window floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` (§6.2; M4R-2, PROPOSED, #96) opens a 60 s collection window and records its keys and `D` (M4 also recorded its `λ`; M4R does not); a strictly deeper one, judged the same way, replaces it. `execute_pool_switch` then adopts the window's candidate if it is still eligible and passes the window floor at `P_ema`, and the chain that armed the window is unbroken (`pool_fail` now and `pool_fail_since + pool_fast_route_s ≤ fp_t0`, M4-SEC-2); otherwise it cancels the window. A window whose candidate's own Raydium program has been flagged since is cancelled too (M4V-4), so that while the chain holds a proposal on the other program can open a new window at once. **Since #99 (OWNER, 2026-09-29) a proposal that opens or improves the window, and the execution, also need every open position to fit the candidate's opening caps** as an open on it right after the adoption would have them (§6.1: `X₀ ≤ D/√p`, at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` and at `P_ema`; the execution without the candidate's live `X`; `CarriedPositionsTooLarge` / cancelled); if no candidate fits, the positions stay on the halted pool and exit through S3 at fair value, a healthy one at its deadline. The adoption writes the anchor and restarts the observation state (§5.2); **exits resume after the warm-up** — at adoption + `EMA_WARMUP_BUCKETS` + 1 = +9 s with the keeper cranking every second (§5.5, §5.8 duty 9) — and positions then continue normally on the new pool, their `P_ema` and `L_ema` seeded from its own buckets. The rows below replay M4R on candidates at λ = 90 % whose reserves are 50 %, 120 % and 25 % of the incumbent's, with the position at the incumbent's size cap (0.75 % of its `X`): only the 120 % candidate carries it (its `D` covers the incumbent's `L_slow`: 0.744 % of the `X₀` an open on it would have); the 50 % and 25 % ones (1.65 % and 3.31 % of `X₀`, the cap 0.75 %) open no window, and RC's 25 % pool is also below the window floor (§10.1 (b)). A refused row stays "open" at the frozen 1.00× until the scenario's horizon. Before #99 the 50 % and 25 % candidates were adopted and liquidated much like the 120 % one (RA +391 / +388 s, lenders +2.2 %; RB +416 / +413 s, +3.5 %; RC's 50 % pool open at CR 1.50, its 25 % pool +426 s, +4.2 %):
