Lenders receive the fair value at the last confirmed price, plus rent for the whole term and the expiry fee: +6.9–7.1 % over `q` at the frozen `P_ema`. Against the price the token reached meanwhile (the scenario's 2×) that is −46 %: the D3 / O14 exposure of any halted market whose positions cannot move, which #99 accepts for positions no candidate can carry (a switch onto a pool too thin for them was what exposed them to M4F-1, M4G-1 and M4G-3; §12.12 (11), residual 12).

Self-test (a lender-keeper cranks only during outages; the route was back, unobserved, for 2 minutes):

```text
S3 with observation-gap rule off: first S3 600 s after the first outage (route was back, unobserved, from +300 s to +420 s)
S3 with observation-gap rule 60 s: first S3 1020 s after the first outage (route was back, unobserved, from +300 s to +420 s)
```

### 10.4 Other curve cases

Non-SOL quote curves and mayhem-mode curves are rejected at market creation (ADOPTED Q6); legacy SPL-Token pump coins are allowed. Venue fees, virtual reserves and the boost parameters are admin-changeable on pump's side: they are read on every quote, and opens are refused after a venue program upgrade until re-validated (ADOPTED Q13).

### 10.5 Raydium pools with `λ < λ_min`: the `L` push, drains and deferral (M4; #93; M4R #96)

From M4 on, eligibility does not read `λ` (§6.2), so a market can adopt a pool whose unlocked LP is most of its depth. The v0.4.1 argument for the settlement box's `L` side — `√(X·Y) ≥ D`, so any `L` push is bounded at `1 − λ ≤ 50 %` (ARCH §4.4) — held only for `λ ≥ λ_min`. Re-checked with `attacks/venue_switch.py` (d1)–(d4) on pools with the examples' locked depth `D` at λ = 50, 25 and 10 % (reserves scaled by `λ_min/λ`, so the sizing and the fully drained pool are the examples'):

**The push bound.**

- One instruction wrapped in an unlocked LP withdrawal reads `√(X·Y)` as low as `D`: a push of `1 − λ` (75 % at λ 25 %, 90 % at λ 10 %; no bound as λ → 0 other than the floor on locked SOL). A deposit pushes a sample up without bound, as before. Since M4R (#96) an adoption reads no sample at all, and the warm-up seed reads bucket highs for `L` (§5.2), so a withdraw-wrapped sample reaches `L_ema` only in a warm-up whose every bucket is attacker-only (§12.12); M4 as built read the smaller of two samples, which the downward push reached (§10.1 (c)).
- An integrated second moves `L_ema` no further than the price: the observation is clamped to `[L_ema/(1 + C), L_ema·(1 + C)]` (C = 100 %), so an attacker-only second lowers `L_ema` by at most 8.3 % whatever λ is; `D` stops the fall sooner the higher λ is: at λ = 50 % from the second attacker-only second on, at a lower λ only once `L_ema` has fallen below `2·D`. After 5 consecutive attacker-only seconds `L_ema` is 0.701 of the pool at λ 50 % and 0.647 at λ ≤ 25 %; after 8, 0.616 and 0.499; it reaches `D` only after tens of seconds.
- The box medians move as for the price: 4 of the 8 entries with one low observation each widen the low side (a contrary observation suffices), 5 attacker-only seconds lower the high side ((d2): `L_lo` reaches `D` at k = 4, `L_hi` follows `L_ema` from k = 5).

**No settlement can be forced** (near-threshold *liquidations* are another matter: right after a switch M4 as built forced them, §12.12, and in steady state a genuinely reached low `L_ema` frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks still can, §12.12's pre-existing residual). Every open is sized on at most `D/λ_min` (§6.1), and no instruction or observation reads an `L` below `D`, so the lowest state any push reaches — every unlocked LP withdrawn and held out — keeps at least `λ_min` of the depth a position opened on the pool was sized on, for every λ (since #99 a position carried over by the fast path fits the caps an open on the new pool has right after the adoption, which are sized on its locked depth `D` itself, so no drain takes the pool below the depth the position fits; before, a candidate with `D` = 25 % of `pool_d` could carry it and leave only `pool_fast_min_bps·λ_min` = 12.5 % of its basis, as in v0.4.1). At λ < λ_min the pool was deeper than that basis when the position opened, so the only difference from the λ = 50 % rows is the open's own smaller price impact (a slightly smaller `V`):

At a flat price and at ×1.5 nothing happens on any pool; a genuine 2×/120 s pump liquidates normally with lenders made whole (RC at λ ≤ 25 % is liquidated at 2.00× where the λ = 50 % pool stays open at CR 1.50 = its liquidation CR: the same genuine price, a CR 0.01–0.02 lower). The two RC pump rows at λ ≤ 25 % read M4F-1's reference cap (#98, §8.2) *(since AIR-14, #117, that cap is `D` itself, so after the drain the reference reads the drained pool and the rows show the same exits; the comparison that follows is M4F-1's `D/λ_min` against no cap, measured before AIR-14)*. After the drain the reference credits at most `D/λ_min`, twice the drained depth and the depth the position was sized on, where before it took `L_slow` (3.8× / 9.5× the drained depth). The exit is the same (second, price, buyback), and the penalty base `min(B̂_live, B̂_ref)` is higher: lenders +4.045 → +4.065 % (λ 25 %) and +4.035 → +4.069 % (λ 10 %). The borrower gets 0.52 / 0.86 millisol less; lenders get +0.26 / +0.43 of it and the keeper the rest. On pools with `λ ≥ λ_min` the cap sits at or above the pre-drain depth and changes no row. Since #99 a candidate with `D` = 25 % of `pool_d` (the `pool_fast_min_bps` minimum) no longer carries RA's capped position — an open on it right after the adoption is sized on `D`, an eighth of the incumbent's depth — so no window opens and the position stays frozen on the halted pool ("#99: … refused"; S3 at fair value at its deadline, §10.3); before, it was carried and drained to CR 1.92 and liquidated at 1.60× (+2.2 %). The thinnest candidates that carry it have `D` = the incumbent's whole depth (twice `pool_d`: 200 % of its reserves at λ 50 %, 400 % at λ 25 %) and, fully drained, keep it above the position opened on RA (CR 2.03 against 2.01; the pump liquidated at 1.71×, +2.2 %): the drained state depends on `D` alone, as in v0.4.1. S2 stays out of reach after the full drain ((d4): RA S1 at ×2.01 / ×1.97 / ×1.97 and S2 at ×125.9 / ×124.7 / ×124.7 at λ 50 / 25 / 10 %; RC S1 at ×2.96 / ×2.93 / ×2.90, S2 at the same multiples as RA; §8.6). Consecutive attacker-only seconds in which every observation is withdraw-wrapped, with the evaluating instruction wrapped too so that its live legs read `D`, leave RA's capped position at a flat price never liquidatable, with no S1 or S2 leg, and refuted:

| λ | k | `L_ema` | `L_lo` | `L_hi` | CR spot at D / EMA | liquidatable | S1 legs | S2 legs | refuted |

| 50% | 3 | 0.789 | 0.789 | 0.789 | 2.01 / 2.03 | no | no | no | yes |
| 50% | 4 | 0.741 | 0.500 | 0.741 | 2.01 / 2.02 | no | no | no | yes |
| 50% | 5 | 0.701 | 0.500 | 0.701 | 2.01 / 2.02 | no | no | no | yes |

| 50% | 40 | 0.500 | 0.500 | 0.500 | 2.01 / 2.01 | no | no | no | yes |
| 25% | 1 | 0.917 | 0.917 | 0.917 | 1.99 / 2.01 | no | no | no | yes |
| 25% | 3 | 0.770 | 0.770 | 0.770 | 1.99 / 2.01 | no | no | no | yes |

| 25% | 8 | 0.499 | 0.250 | 0.499 | 1.99 / 2.01 | no | no | no | yes |
| 25% | 40 | 0.251 | 0.250 | 0.251 | 1.99 / 1.99 | no | no | no | yes |

| 10% | 3 | 0.770 | 0.770 | 0.770 | 1.98 / 2.00 | no | no | no | yes |
| 10% | 4 | 0.706 | 0.100 | 0.706 | 1.98 / 2.00 | no | no | no | yes |
| 10% | 5 | 0.647 | 0.100 | 0.647 | 1.98 / 2.00 | no | no | no | yes |
| 10% | 8 | 0.499 | 0.100 | 0.499 | 1.98 / 2.00 | no | no | no | yes |
| 10% | 40 | 0.102 | 0.100 | 0.102 | 1.98 / 1.98 | no | no | no | yes |

(`L` values relative to the pool's `√(X·Y)`. Since AIR-15, #117, `L_hi` is capped at the pool's `D`, so it follows `L_ema` down instead of staying at the pre-drain 1.000; the decisions are the same.)

**Delay only.** What a push can do is widen the box's `L` side, which defers every decision that must agree across it (§5.6):

- after an adoption: nothing beyond the warm-up gate since M4R — the seed reaches the pool's depth at the gate for λ down to 10 % and the box is narrow from then on (§10.1 (b)); M4 as built left it wide 20 s for an honest `λ < λ_min` candidate written at the cap and 25 s after a withdrawal around one of its two samples (§10.1 (b), (c));
- a **contrary LP observation every second** — an unlocked LP holder's `[withdraw, crank, re-deposit]` next to the keeper's honest crank, which costs no swap fee — holds `L_lo` at `D` while `L_hi` stays honest. The S2/S3 amount then spreads across the box by ≈ `(1 − λ)·Q/X_D`, with `X_D` the token reserve at depth `D` and `Q/X_D ≤ min(3·φ, √(1 + ι_pos) − 1)/λ_min` at the size caps (bps; settles only at ≤ `fv_dev_bps` = 100; capped position, expired at a flat price or liquidatable after a pump):

| market | fee | state | λ 50% | λ 33% | λ 25% | λ 10% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RA | 0.25% | expired, flat | 75 | 100 | 113 | 136 |
| RA | 0.25% | liquidatable (x1.6) | 97 | 129 | 145 | 174 |
| RA | 1.00% | expired, flat | 253 | 340 | 384 | 464 |
| RA | 1.00% | liquidatable (x1.6) | 325 | 437 | 493 | 596 |
| RC | 0.25% | expired, flat | 75 | 100 | 113 | 136 |
| RC | 0.25% | liquidatable (x2.0) | 109 | 145 | 163 | 196 |
| RC | 1.00% | expired, flat | 253 | 340 | 384 | 464 |
| RC | 1.00% | liquidatable (x2.0) | 366 | 492 | 556 | 672 |

On a 25 bps pool an expired position at a flat price stays within `fv_dev_bps` for λ ≥ 33 %, but not at λ 25 % or 10 % (113, 136 bps), and a liquidatable one on RC exceeds it already at λ 50 % (109 bps); on a 1 % pool the spread is 253–672 bps at every λ. The §5.6 remark that an LP round trip "moves `L` too little to matter" therefore held only for low-fee pools with a high λ, and is corrected there. The deferral lasts while the round trip is repeated in every second — transaction fees only, but it needs most of the unlocked LP: withdrawing a share `s` of it lowers `L_lo` only to `√(X·Y)·(1 − s·(1 − λ))`. It concerns S3 on an executable venue (the Emergency flag or a fee above the hard cap; S2 is unreachable at capped sizes, §8.6), and the deferred position can still be liquidated or expired normally while the route works for short.win (§5.6). Disclosed with owner item O18 (§17).

**While the post-change reference guard is set** (M4R, §8.2) a low-λ pool's unlocked LP holder has one more lever, an extraction rather than a delay: withdraw-wrapping at least 4 of the 8 warm-up seconds and at least 4 of every 8 after them keeps `L_mlo` at `D`, so the guard stays set, and a withdraw-wrapped liquidation of a genuinely liquidatable position then prices its reference at `max(D, L_slow ≤ D)` instead of the pool's depth. The borrower overpays by at most `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 ≈ (1/λ − 1)·q/x` of `B` — ≈ 0 on λ ≈ 1 pools, +4.04 % (1.24 SOL) in `switch_warmup.py` (5) at λ 50 % with `q` 3 % of the candidate's `X` — and M4 as built allowed the same +4.04 % through its `D` seed. While the guard is set it forces no exit (§12.12). **In steady state** `L_b`'s `L_slow` term kept the reference at the undrained depth until AIR-14 (#117; since then the cap at `D` prices it on the drained pool, §8.2), but that did not stop withdraw-wrapped cranks and a withdraw-wrapped liquidation from forcing exits (FITS-2, §12.12 (5b)): where `δ` covers the drained cost — at 25 bps, and at 1 % below about half the cap — after a short drag, and at the impact cap once the drag had been held for about τ_slow (0.79 h at λ 50 %, 3.19 h at `λ_min` 2,000 and λ 20 %) and `L_slow` had followed it to `D`. Since #101 the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` and the keeper covers every second of a drag after its first (§5.5, §5.8 duty 2): no drag of attacker-only seconds forces anything in (5b), up to the keeper's missed covered seconds (at `9aade3e`; since AIR-14's cap at `D`, #117, one level at 0.79 × the cap, §12.12 (5b) (iii)); a low reached by a genuine held withdrawal, then frozen, still forces what the LP's withdrawal for good forces ((5b) (vii), FITX-1; §12.12 residual 10); the overpay of a due liquidation priced at a dragged `L_slow` (≤ `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1` of `B`) needs the same drag, now held at one step, or a held drain frozen as in (vii).

---

## 11. Simulation

### 11.1 Method

Venues: exact integer math of §1/§3 with the live pump fee schedules; Raydium with the generic fee-on-input model and tracked LP units (λ = 50 % = `λ_min`; pools with a lower λ are eligible since M4 and are covered in §10.5); PumpSwap pools canonical, with tracked LP units whose creation share is burned (`D` the whole depth; since #105 a third-party deposit is `CPPool.add_liquidity`, studied in §12.12 (12)), and the graduation as built (#105: the `lp_supply` anchor, the 8-bucket restart, the ELF's boost rounding). Example markets:

| Key | Venue |
|---|---|
| A | PumpSwap 150 M tokens / 2,500 SOL (90 bps) |
| B | PumpSwap 200 M / 1,000 SOL (100 bps) |
| C | fresh pump graduate after the boost: 171.4 M / 102.57 SOL effective (84.99 real + 17.58 virtual; 120 bps) |
| D@x | pump curve at x % progress (125 bps) |
| RA / RB / RC | Raydium AMM v4 150 M / 2,500 SOL; CPMM 200 M / 1,000 SOL; CPMM 206.9 M / 84.99 SOL (all 25 bps; λ = 50 %) |

Positions at the per-position cap, 80 % utilisation, event 1 h after open. The in-house keeper observes every second near thresholds (§5.8) and acts 1 s after a position becomes actionable, retrying every second. Borrower P&L = payout − M − open fee. **Lender P&L vs holding** = SOL received − value of the tokens not returned, at the scenario's **terminal** price. Tables come from `python3 docs/design/sim/shortwin_sim.py --mc 400 --fuzz 1000` (output `sim/out/full.txt`); the drain scenarios withdraw the unlocked half of the LP — all of it on these λ = 50 % pools; at a lower λ a full drain removes more of the pool but ends at the same depth relative to sizing (§10.5) — and the Emergency-flag rows set the route-broken flag one second before the move.

### 11.2 Deterministic scenarios

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

What changed against v0.3: every genuine outcome is unchanged (the fixed 2 % bound executes at the same second as v0.3's 1 % Dutch start; §12.2), except RA/RC figures that move in the last digit (conservative token reserve and `ert = 0` on Raydium). The 90–98 % drain rows are gone: a full drain of an adopted pool leaves at least `λ_min` of the depth the positions opened on it were sized on (sizing ≤ `D/λ_min`; since M4 also on pools with `λ < λ_min`, which may lose more than half of their depth, §10.5), so "drain all unlocked LP (50 %)" is the realistic worst case up to the open's own price impact (CR after the drain 2.01 at λ 50 %, 1.98 at λ 10 %), and it liquidates nothing on its own; 50 % drains followed by a pump liquidate normally (RA 1.69×, RB 1.95×; RC stays open at 2×). The Emergency flag leaves every liquidation unchanged (the flag is S3 evidence only, V2-1).

**On the real curve (M5 fork, `pump_curve_scenarios`, dumped ELFs):** the D@50 % rows gap 2× and 5×, the three pumps, dump and close and flat to expiry, the D@90 % rows dump and close and flat to expiry, and the stuck migrations of D@50 % (S3 at +4,200 s, `V − K_s`), D@80 % (§8.6: S3 at +4,200 s, FV binding) and D@90 % (stays open, CR_EMA 2.40) reproduce to the lamport — exit second, cranks, spot, `S`, `M`, `V`, `N_open`, fee, `B`, `B̂_ref`, `max_in`, the penalty split, `K_s` / `K_e`, S1 / S3 amounts, FV, `P_ema`, `L_ema` — with two recorded differences. (1) **The D@0 % rows cannot occur on a real curve:** at 0 % progress `rQ = 0` and pump pays a sale out of `rQ`, so the sim's D@0 % short (`S` = 0.4346 SOL) has nothing to sell into and the program refuses it (`ProceedsBelowMin`); the sim's `PumpCurve.sell` does not check `rQ`. The D@0 % rows are kept as the scale reference (their outcomes scale exactly to D@50 %'s, which reproduce). (2) **`R_min` is priced at a different utilisation:** the sim prices a position's minimum rent at its fixed 80 % utilisation, the program at the **pre-open** utilisation (ARCH §4.5, pre-instruction counters: 0 % for a market's first position); the fork adjusts the legs below the rent by exactly that difference, and in one row it moves the timing — D@50 % pump 10×/10 min liquidates at +231 s instead of +230 s (B 1.7296, lenders 0.0868, keeper 0.0519, borrower −1.2153 SOL; a sim run at the program's `R_min` gives exactly the fork's run). The rows whose path completes the curve (D@50 % gap 10× and spike, every D@90 % gap / pump / spike row, §10.1's graduation rows) continue on the migrated pool and need M6's `→ PumpSwap` transition. **M6 (#104):** §10.1's twelve graduation rows run on the fork (`graduation_scenarios`, §10.1 "As built"); the §11.2 rows that complete the curve are not replayed yet (`graduation_support::Grad::run` can drive them).

Calm regime: −13.6 % to +0.9 % of `N_open`, every cell within two standard errors of break-even (≈ 5 % of rent and fees against rare gaps); Tier C's mean (−13.6 % ± 8.6 %) is set by a handful of large gaps in 400 paths. Stressed regime: −72 % to −188 %. v0.4 draws each path from its own seeded random stream, so these cells no longer shift when an exit elsewhere moves by a second (v0.3's shared stream did; its figures are not comparable cell by cell). Both regimes are illustrative and uncalibrated; calibrating them is the top open question before mainnet (§17). **What is calibrated, and from what (M10, WP-9; premise corrected):** a *gap* here is a persistent price jump of at least 1.5× that the keeper cannot act inside — the Monte Carlo's per-tier `lam_up` / `lam_dn` (up and down gaps per market-day), `alpha` (their size's tail index) and `sigma` (the volatility between them) —, not an observation gap. v0.4.1 said "from indexer data"; that cannot work before launch, because the M8 indexer streams only short.win's own markets. The parameters are measured instead from a crawl of public mainnet RPC that reuses the M8 decoders, over markets chosen by on-chain eligibility (never by volume, which would select for the pumps being measured) and cohorts followed from birth (method: `docs/research/gap-calibration.md`; after M11 the indexer takes over on listed markets). The crawl started on 2026-10-02; **results are pending** at least 30 days of tapes, after which the Monte Carlo is re-run with a `calibrated` regime. On public RPC alone (the owner's decision of 2026-10-02) Tier A, and possibly B, can reach only an upper bound: the first census found 77 eligible Tier A pools against the ≈ 107 needed for 3,210 market-days in 30 days; the owner revisits the data source after 30 days of data. Organic inter-trade gaps (seconds without a trade) do not calibrate `COVERAGE_GAP_S`, `oracle_stale_s` or the pause thresholds, which guard against a missing keeper or oracle, not a quiet pool.

### 11.7 Conclusions

1. **Moves slower than the EMA are handled well** (10× over ≥ 2–3 min): liquidation within seconds of the threshold, lenders get `Q` + rent + half the penalty.
2. **Short spikes never liquidate**, and an atomic spike cannot move the EMA.
3. **Gaps larger than the cushion become S1 losses** shared pro-rata by lenders; the keeper must act within 1–2 s for Tier C/D fast pumps.
4. **Tier A lenders are the most exposed per unit of move** (cushion 1.6×).
5. **LP drains on adoptable pools do not liquidate** on their own, at any λ (§10.5); S2 is unreachable at capped sizes; the D3 cap matters for S3 (flag, fee hike, stuck migration), whose amount an unlocked LP holder's zero-fee round trip every second can defer on low-λ or high-fee pools (§10.5).
6. **Graduation includes the boost:** positions opened at 80–90 % curve progress are liquidated on the completing buy or by the +45.66 % push that follows, lenders made whole.
7. **Lender economics** still hinge on uncalibrated gap frequencies (OWNER; price jumps ≥ 1.5×, measured from a public-RPC crawl since 2026-10-02, results pending ≥ 30 days of data — §11.6, `docs/research/gap-calibration.md`).

---

## 12. Manipulation and MEV analysis

### 12.1 Attacker models

Every attack script is in `sim/attacks/` and was re-run on v0.4.1; "v0.3" rows come from the frozen model. All costs assume **no arbitrage** (round-trip venue fees only; lower bounds).

- **Atomic (C0-5):** once per second the attacker lands `[pump → crank_price → dump]`; the in-house keeper also observes every second (§5.8). The attacker is also the keeper of the final exit and sandwiches it.
- **Held (stronger):** the attacker holds the pool at the target price for whole seconds, so every observation of those seconds sees it. On-chain this is indistinguishable from a genuine pump. **It is also the exposure of a market in degraded mode** (§5.8): without an honest observer an atomic bundle alone in its second achieves the held effect at the atomic cost.
- **Contrary (V1-2):** one observation per second below the EMA or the liquidation price, to freeze `P_ema` or fail the robust-spot leg (§12.9).
- **Attacker-only second:** a second in which the only observation is the attacker's; the building block of the settlement attacks (§12.10–12.11). **Consecutive** attacker-only seconds (R4A-9) model a censored or absent keeper.
- **Frozen second (R4B-2, v0.4.1):** a second that holds the honest keeper's observation *and* an attacker observation just beyond `P_ema`; the bucket straddles `P_ema`, so the EMA does not move. After one attacker-only push, frozen seconds keep the pushed EMA in place ("push-then-freeze"); v0.4's box, built on the clamped observations, recorded it, v0.4.1's box on bucket extremes does not.
- **Widening (v0.4.1 residual):** a contrary observation in every second, frozen or not, which widens one side of the box without moving any decision; it can only defer (§5.6).
- **Noise (R4B-1):** not an attacker — deterministic per-second price noise of ±0.3 % / ±1 % in the §5.7 battery, so that the live quote and the one-second-old reference differ as they do on a real pool.

**Result.** The extraction is ≤ 2 % of the reference cost whether or not other keepers act: Tier A +1.50 SOL over an honest keeper (borrower −2.02), lenders never lose. v0.3's Dutch clock allowed +0.71 at the first second but +3.86 after 4 s (borrower −5.11).

**On the real venues (M10, WP-8b; `raydium_cpmm_attacks`, `pumpswap_attacks`, `raydium_v4_attacks`, figures printed with `-- --nocapture`, the structural claims asserted).** The atomic liquidation sandwich takes from the borrower only: CPMM USELESS (`V` 533.7 SOL, pool 23,310 SOL) +5.34 SOL over an honest keeper (borrower −6.96; +5.55 after 4 s), the CPMM creator-fee pool +0.157, PumpSwap (`V` 92.8 SOL, pool 1,777 SOL) +1.05 (borrower −1.21); the expiry sandwich +1.17 / +0.19 SOL, rent and the expiry fee senior. "Lenders never lose" holds to ≈ 10⁻⁷ of the reference: the front-run's own fees stay in the pool and deepen it, so `B̂_ref` drops by a hair (USELESS 38,524 lamports of 358.78 SOL) and the penalty computed on it with it — the lenders' and protocol's legs end 2,158 lamports (USELESS), 4,177 (AMM v4), 800 (PumpSwap) and 77 (the creator-fee pool) below the honest liquidation's.

**Why a fixed 2 %** (the reviewer's `dutch.py`, `dutch2.py`, `dutch3.py`, `sandwich_fixed.py`, re-run against the v0.3 model with its clock, with a fixed 1 % and 2 %, and against v0.4):

| keeper latency | variant | runs | cells different (outcome, exit price or lender P&L) | of which worse for lenders |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| latency 1 s | v0.3 fixed 1 % | 72 | 0 | 0 |
| latency 1 s | v0.3 fixed 2 % | 72 | 0 | 0 |
| latency 1 s | v0.4.1 (fixed 2 %) | 72 | 1 | 0 |
| latency 2 s | v0.3 fixed 1 % | 72 | 0 | 0 |
| latency 2 s | v0.3 fixed 2 % | 72 | 0 | 0 |
| latency 2 s | v0.4.1 (fixed 2 %) | 72 | 1 | 0 |

The v0.4.1 cell (since #105, both latencies) is D@90 %'s 10×/60 s pump, which completes the curve: under the graduation as built (§10.1) the keeper's liquidation waits for the pool's warm-up gate and lands 1 s later, at 3.415× instead of 3.286× (latency 1 s; latency 2 s: 3.548× instead of 3.415×), the lenders +1.8 % instead of +1.7 % (+1.9 % instead of +1.8 %); the bound plays no part. Since #103 the curve's basket is filled on the curve's reserves at the EMA price (§6.1): each open's own sale lowers the spot under `P_ema`, so the next cap is smaller (the held S1-forcing rows: D@0 % 3 positions / 4.23 SOL instead of 4 / 4.68, D@50 % 4 / 7.11 instead of 4 / 7.42 — the only rows that changed; S1 forcing stays unprofitable, −75.3 and −123.0 SOL). On the real curve (M10, `pump_curve_attacks`, `curve_s1_forcing_d50.py`) atomic S1 forcing arms nothing at ×4.4–×6.4, and the held one at ×4.4 on the program's basket — its fourth position smaller than the sim's, the program's OI cap the stricter at `t` = 3 — reaches its first S1 at the sim's 58 s with a best net of −123.14 SOL (sim −123.04); on AMM v4, CPMM and PumpSwap (a) and (b) are run atomic only and force nothing. **Result:** S1 forcing is unprofitable everywhere (40-update persistence on the settlement box costs more than the windfall). Basket liquidation is unprofitable on PumpSwap and the curve; on 25 bps Raydium pools it is marginally profitable (§6.1, §12.6). ### 12.6 Residual risk under the held model (and in degraded mode)

Liquidation, held model (the attacker holds the pool for 1–3 s at +2.5–30 % before sandwiching an exit that is already due — its best plans are 2-s holds since AIR-22's lower-median growth, #117, 3-s holds before; v0.4.1: a liquidation reserves nothing in its bound, §8.2):

| market | vault V (SOL) | honest: B / rent / penalty / borrower | attacked: B / rent / penalty / borrower | held pump before exit | attacker net incl. keeper reward (SOL) | extra over an honest keeper (SOL) | borrower loss (SOL) | lender+protocol loss (SOL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | 127.741 | 103.094 / 0.0035 / 5.1547 / 19.490 | 127.354 / 0.0035 / 0.3839 / 0.000 | 2 s at +20.0% | +9.910 | +8.363 | 19.490 | +3.3395 |
| B | 63.934 | 47.782 / 0.0028 / 3.3447 / 12.804 | 59.784 / 0.0028 / 4.1143 / 0.033 | 2 s at +20.0% | +5.092 | +4.089 | 12.772 | -0.5387 |
| C | 7.885 | 5.300 / 0.0006 / 0.4238 / 2.161 | 7.284 / 0.0006 / 0.5733 / 0.028 | 2 s at +30.0% | +0.618 | +0.491 | 2.133 | -0.1047 |
| D@0% | 1.836 | 1.058 / 0.0008 / 0.1058 / 0.672 | 1.079 / 0.0008 / 0.1058 / 0.651 | none | +0.041 | +0.009 | 0.021 | -0.0000 |
| D@50% | 2.913 | 1.678 / 0.0013 / 0.1677 / 1.066 | 1.709 / 0.0013 / 0.1677 / 1.035 | none | +0.064 | +0.014 | 0.031 | +0.0000 |
| D@90% | 5.485 | 3.159 / 0.0024 / 0.3158 / 2.009 | 4.360 / 0.0024 / 0.4275 / 0.696 | 2 s at +30.0% | +0.240 | +0.146 | 1.313 | -0.0782 |
| RA | 37.870 | 30.566 / 0.0011 / 1.5283 / 5.775 | 36.412 / 0.0011 / 1.4577 / 0.000 | 2 s at +15.0% | +2.839 | +2.381 | 5.775 | +0.0495 |
| RB | 18.939 | 14.146 / 0.0009 / 0.9902 / 3.802 | 18.660 / 0.0009 / 0.2783 / 0.000 | 2 s at +30.0% | +2.010 | +1.713 | 3.802 | +0.4984 |
| RC | 1.931 | 1.299 / 0.0001 / 0.1038 / 0.528 | 1.778 / 0.0001 / 0.1400 / 0.013 | 2 s at +30.0% | +0.283 | +0.252 | 0.516 | -0.0253 |

**Result.** The held attacker raises the reference with its held seconds and then buys the vault down to `V − K_s − ert`: it takes the whole remainder of the borrower being liquidated (Tier A +8.36 SOL over an honest keeper, borrower −19.49 SOL), and lenders and protocol lose the part of the penalty the vault no longer covers (Tier A −3.34 SOL; penalty 5.15 → 0.38 SOL). Elsewhere the lenders' penalty share on the lifted reference can leave them ahead (B, C, D@90 %, RC: +0.03 to +0.54 SOL) while the borrower still loses its remainder. **AIR-22 (#117; the owner accepted the trade-off on 2026-10-12):** with the sustained growth the lower median of the newest steps, one non-rising step in three is tolerated, so two held seconds open `κ_eff` where three were needed; the attacker saves a held second's push fees. Under the minimum the Tier A plan was 3 s at +15 %: +5.66 SOL over an honest keeper, lenders and protocol −2.06 SOL, penalty 5.15 → 2.22 SOL (RA +1.52 / −0.57, now 2 s, +2.38 / −0.05). v0.4's penalty reserve prevented the lenders' loss on these fresh positions under the minimum (+0.46 SOL on A); under the lower median it only trims it (−2.94 instead of −3.34 SOL on A). It also blocked honest liquidations in genuine fast pumps (§8.2) and did not hold on aged ones; v0.3's Dutch clock reached the same point more slowly. How the three reserve rules compare, on fresh positions and on positions aged at 90 % utilisation whose rent due is far above `K_s` (`sandwich.py`, last section; loss positive = lenders and protocol lose):

| market | position | rule | rent due / K_s (SOL) | lender+protocol loss vs honest (SOL) | attacker extra over an honest keeper (SOL) | borrower loss (SOL) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | fresh (1 h), 80 % | none (v0.4.1) | 0.004 / 0.250 | +3.340 | +8.363 | 19.490 |
| A | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent | 0.004 / 0.250 | +3.340 | +8.363 | 19.490 |
| A | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent+penalty | 0.004 / 0.250 | +2.938 | +8.084 | 19.490 |
| A | aged 25 d, 90 % | none (v0.4.1) | 6.043 / 0.250 | +4.543 | +8.510 | 18.603 |
| A | aged 25 d, 90 % | rent | 6.043 / 0.250 | +9.176 | +8.154 | 18.603 |
| A | aged 25 d, 90 % | rent+penalty | 6.043 / 0.250 | +9.176 | +8.154 | 18.603 |
| B | fresh (1 h), 80 % | none (v0.4.1) | 0.003 / 0.250 | -0.539 | +4.089 | 12.772 |
| B | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent | 0.003 / 0.250 | -0.539 | +4.089 | 12.772 |
| B | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent+penalty | 0.003 / 0.250 | -0.539 | +4.089 | 12.772 |
| B | aged 12 d, 90 % | none (v0.4.1) | 1.935 / 0.250 | +3.942 | +4.593 | 12.408 |
| B | aged 12 d, 90 % | rent | 1.935 / 0.250 | -0.522 | +3.842 | 12.384 |
| B | aged 12 d, 90 % | rent+penalty | 1.935 / 0.250 | -0.522 | +3.842 | 12.384 |
| RA | fresh (1 h), 80 % | none (v0.4.1) | 0.001 / 0.250 | +0.049 | +2.381 | 5.775 |
| RA | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent | 0.001 / 0.250 | +0.049 | +2.381 | 5.775 |
| RA | fresh (1 h), 80 % | rent+penalty | 0.001 / 0.250 | +0.827 | +2.298 | 5.775 |
| RA | aged 25 d, 90 % | none (v0.4.1) | 1.898 / 0.250 | +1.257 | +2.457 | 5.512 |
| RA | aged 25 d, 90 % | rent | 1.898 / 0.250 | +2.608 | +2.412 | 5.512 |
| RA | aged 25 d, 90 % | rent+penalty | 1.898 / 0.250 | +2.608 | +2.412 | 5.512 |

On fresh positions no reserve and the rent-only reserve are identical (rent due is ≈ 0). On aged positions the reserves no longer help under the lower-median growth: against a rent reserve the attacker's best plan costs lenders and protocol more on Tier A and RA (−9.18 instead of −4.54 SOL on A, −2.61 instead of −1.26 on RA), while on aged Tier B the rent-only reserve still keeps the rent out of reach (+0.52 instead of −3.94 SOL). Under the minimum it kept it out of reach on A too (−1.92 instead of −8.02 SOL). Either way a reserve has the dead band that costs honest lenders a whole position in a genuine fast pump (§8.2). The held model needs the attacker to own whole seconds with nobody trading against the pool: a degraded-mode exposure (§5.8), not a genuine-market one; v0.4.1 chooses liveness in genuine markets. Owner item O19 lists it.

| Residual | Size (capped positions, no arbitrage) | Why it remains | Levers |
|---|---|---|---|
| Keeper takes part of the remainder of a borrower being liquidated | Tier A +8.36 SOL over an honest keeper (borrower −19.5, all of it); B +4.09 (−12.8); C +0.49 (−2.1); RA +2.38 (−5.8, all of it); RB +1.71 (−3.8, all of it); RC +0.25 (−0.52); D@90 % +0.15 (−1.3); D@0 % / D@50 % ≤ 0.02 (no held plan pays). Under the minimum (before AIR-22): A +5.66, B +1.94, RA +1.52 | a price held for 2 s at +15–30 % (3 s at +10–30 % before AIR-22) opens the sustained-growth cap κ exactly like a genuine trend; the fixed 2 % then applies to that reference | lower `κ_max` (costs Tier C/D liveness, §5.7); competitive keepers; real arbitrage; `kappa_growth_n` ≥ 5 would keep the 3-s hold but is outside its compiled box (AIR-22) |
| Lenders + protocol lose penalty (and, on aged positions, rent) the bought-down vault no longer covers | Tier A −3.34 SOL, RB −0.50, RA −0.05 (fresh; B, C, D@90 % and RC end 0.03–0.54 SOL ahead); Tier A −4.54 SOL, B −3.94, RA −1.26 aged at 90 % (under the minimum: A −2.06 / −8.02, RA −0.57 / −2.27) | a liquidation reserves nothing (v0.4.1, §8.2): any reserve blocks honest liquidations in fast pumps | the rent-only reserve (one extra S1 in 1,655 battery runs) — an owner choice, O19; under the lower median it keeps the rent out of reach on aged B (+0.52 SOL) but costs more on aged A and RA (−9.18, −2.61) |
| Near-threshold push | ≤ +0.06 SOL (RC) | the push needed is small | as above |
| Raydium basket liquidation | RA +3.15, RB +8.51, RC +0.52 SOL at the OI cap | 25 bps makes held seconds cheap | `fee_oi_cap_x100` = 500 (§6.1); the 25 SOL beta cap bounds it |
| Borrower's paid veto (V1-2) | Tier A 0.35 SOL/s at the threshold, ≈ 4.6× that 5 % further (§12.9) | one contrary observation per second | cost grows with the move; disclose |
| Settlement amount push (V2-9) | ≤ `fv_dev_bps` of the take either way (§12.11) | the box lets a settlement through once within 1 % | tighter `fv_dev_bps` slows S2/S3 in trends |
| Settlement deferral | ≈ 13 s per attacker-only second (§12.11); indefinitely while a contrary observation is paid every second: 0.002 SOL/s (RC) to 0.21 SOL/s (A) for an S3 on an executable venue (v0.4.1, §5.6) | a pushed EMA, or a widened side of the box, makes the corners disagree | keeper duty 2; an executable venue still allows a normal expiry or liquidation |
| Consecutive attacker-only seconds (degraded mode) | 5 in a row flip a capped S2 into S1 on the drained-RA model cases: +2.37 to +5.58 SOL for 1.1–2.0 SOL (R4A-9) | 5 of the ring's 8 entries move a median | keeper duties 1–2; degraded mode pauses opens (§5.8) |
| Censoring the keeper's observations | not modelled | the attacker must fill the market account's write-lock budget every slot (ARCH §6.6) | priority fees, bundles |

Every held-model attack needs the manipulated price to sit on the pool for whole seconds, including slot boundaries, while every other trader can sell into it. On deep pools that is expensive in practice; the numbers are the no-arbitrage bound — and the per-market exposure in degraded mode.

**On the real venues (M10, WP-8b; O19's held sandwich accepted and disclosed by the owner on 2026-10-03, SPEC §14).** The §12.6 plan run on the program against the real pools, at each venue's capped test position, the attacker holding the pool at +15 % for 3 s (attacker-only seconds) before the best sandwich (SOL; net = the attacker's gain after its push's cost). *These runs predate AIR-22 (#117): under the lower-median growth the sim's best plan holds 2 s, which saves the attacker a second's push fees (RA +2.839 SOL instead of +1.981); the real venues have not been re-measured under it (O19).*

| venue (pool) | `V` | atomic sandwich, net | held 3 s at +15 %, net | borrower's remainder: honest → attacked | lenders + protocol: honest → attacked |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| AMM v4 (Fartcoin) | ≈ 869 | +22.72 | **+61.27** (push 56.01; +47.25 over an honest keeper) | 237.67 → 101.74 | 32.73 → 39.07 |
| CPMM (USELESS) | 533.7 | +13.95 | **+37.33** | 146.0 → 62.5 | 20.10 → 24.00 |
| PumpSwap (`mature`) | 92.8 | +2.55 | **+10.20** | 25.38 → 10.79 | at least the honest legs (asserted) |
| pump curve (D@50 %) | — | +0.064 | −0.350 (3 s at +30 %); +0.052 (1 s at +2.5 %) (AIR-22; −0.363 / +0.051 under the minimum) | — | — |

I11 holds in every run (the spend within `⌊B̂_ref·(1 + δ)⌋` of the lifted reference), the attacker's extra comes out of the borrower's remainder only, and the lenders' and protocol's legs are at least the honest ones — they grow, because the reference is lifted. Holding 1 s at +2.5 % does not pay on AMM v4 (+22.55 against the atomic +22.72), nor does anything on the curve, whose held runs stay below the atomic sandwich (the sim's "none"). The held **fresh** push (×2.5, held until the position is liquidatable) loses on both new venues: USELESS 9 s, push 404.29 SOL, net −156.71; PumpSwap 10 s, push 85.15, net −44.22. **Owner decision (2026-10-03):** accepted with no program change; ECON, the audit's known issues and the borrower disclosure state the per-venue figures above (V4 +61.27, CPMM +37.33, PumpSwap +10.20 SOL for a 3-s hold at cap size); keeper competition is the M11 lever.

### 12.8 Creator fees on short.win volume (R1, C0-18)

The coin creator earns the venue's creator fee on every short.win leg: 30 bps per leg on the curve; on canonical PumpSwap pools 30 bps below 420 SOL market cap, **95 bps** in the 420–1,470 SOL tier (where fresh graduates sit) and falling to 5 bps at the top. A position that opens and closes pays the creator ≈ 2 × creator bps of notional (0.6 % on the curve, 1.9 % on a fresh graduate). The R1 disclosure and the market page show it.

On a CPMM pool that enabled a creator fee at creation (§1.3) the same holds at its config's rate. **Since CPMM's 2026-10 redeploy (M10, WP-2; `raydium-cp-swap@bdeee23`, rebuilt byte for byte)** part of that fee can go to Raydium instead of the creator: `collect_creator_fee` pays the creator `fee − ⌊fee·creator_fee_share_rate/10⁶⌋` and moves the rest to the protocol counter. The rate is 0 on all 21 configs today (slot 452,488,420). It changes neither what a short.win leg pays nor the pricing reserves (both counters are excluded from them), and short.win never calls the collection, so short.win's costs and the R1 figure (what the borrower pays) are unchanged; only who receives a CPMM creator fee may change.

**Result.** Under v0.4 every blocking plan earns less than an unblocked first-second sandwich (Tier A +1.13 SOL after a 1 s block against +1.50 without one; +0.64 before AIR-22, #117, whose lower-median growth lets the reference follow a little more of the rise after the block); the V1-1 attack class is gone with the clock.

**Result.** The veto is cheap only at the threshold (Tier A 0.35 SOL/s, which the 19.5 SOL of remaining equity covers for ≈ 56 s); 5 % further into the pump it costs ≈ 4.6× as much. Settlement arming is not affected (§12.10). Run on the program (M10, `attacks_exit_it`, RA with the price flat, driver `tests/litesvm/sim/ra_veto.py`): a veto of 10 / 60 / 300 s from the EMA leg's start costs the sim's 0.950 / 6.073 / 41.983 SOL to the lamport (0.0939 SOL in its first second), the keeper's checked `liquidate` is refused in every vetoed second, and the liquidation lands 2 s after the veto stops with the sim's buyback, reference and remainder.

| RC | x1.05 | v0.4.1 (box on bucket extremes) | PTF | S1 | 65 | 1 | 4 | 0.202 |

**Result.** v0.3 refuted on the instantaneous `P_ema`: at 3 % beyond the boundary the borrower postponed S1 indefinitely (13 pushes in 600 s; Tier A 124.8 SOL, D@0 % 3.2 SOL against a 1.84 SOL vault). v0.4 refuted only on the box, which stopped the plain push (S1 at most 4 s later) but not push-then-freeze (no S1 within 600 s at 3 % beyond the boundary in any market). v0.4.1's box on bucket extremes stops both: the frozen seconds keep their honest highs, the refutation corner does not move, and S1 lands 8 s later. Vetoes longer than the 10 s arm gap no longer restart the clock (v0.3 +34–42 s; v0.4.1 +0–6 s: the veto's low observations widen the box's low side for the few seconds they stay among the ring's 8 entries).

(V, R4A-3) The Emergency flag is set and a genuine move makes the position expired **and** CR-liquidatable at its deadline; in the second before the honest keeper's `sol_settle(S3)` the borrower lands one contrary low observation `[sell → crank → buy back]` (lenders / penalty-part lump / borrower, SOL):

| market | move | rule | legs at the call (spot / EMA) | honest: lenders / lump / borrower | veto: lenders / lump / borrower | veto cost (SOL) |

**Result.**

- **Kind (R3A-1, R4B-2, R4A-9).** v0.3 let a lender-keeper turn a capped S2 into an uncapped S1 with 1–2 pushed seconds (+2.37 to +5.58 SOL); v0.4 required the S1 EMA leg on the whole box, but one push plus 4 frozen seconds did it again. v0.4.1's box on bucket extremes needs 5 attacker-only seconds of the ring's 8: no flip with any number of frozen seconds up to 10, none with pushes interleaved with honest seconds, and 5 consecutive attacker-only seconds — the degraded-mode case — do flip (+2.37 to +5.58 SOL for 1.1–2.0 SOL). On real pools (M10): AMM v4's thin S2 pool never reaches S1 with 2 pushed seconds, push-then-freeze 2 / 4 / 6, 1–6 consecutive attacker-only seconds or pushes between honest seconds (5–6 consecutive ones settle S2 there); CPMM's drained S2 (`raydium_cpmm_e2e`'s) reaches no S1 below 5 consecutive attacker-only seconds, and 5–6 flip it — R4A-9's limit; PumpSwap's Emergency-flag S3 reaches no S1 with up to 6. One pushed second ×1.3 / ×0.8 moves the amount +1.02 % / −0.87 % (CPMM S2) and +1.01 % / −0.89 % (PumpSwap S3) of the honest one.
- **`p'` (R4A-3).** v0.4 took `p'` from the raw flag, so one contrary observation cut the lenders' S3 take by 2.9 % (A) to 7.3 % (D@0 %) for 0.02–0.71 SOL; v0.4.1 decides it on the box corner and the take is unchanged. Since M10 the same holds on CPMM (USELESS) and PumpSwap: `p′` 800 / 800 bps and the amounts identical to the lamport with and without the contrary observation.
- **Amount (V2-9).** One pushed second moves an S2/S3 amount by at most `fv_dev_bps`: +0.35 to +0.70 SOL for a lender push up, −0.13 to −0.46 SOL for a borrower push down, against +10.48 / −5.23 SOL without the box on Tier A (E).
- **The rejected reading.** Evaluating every decision "on the lender-adverse side" (refutation and amount at `(P_lo, L_hi)`, no amount check) closes the lender's push but hands the borrower both the refutation push (§12.10: no S1 within 600 s) and a one-second FV cut of 5.23 SOL (8.4 %) on Tier A.
- **Deferral (residual).** A party that owns an attacker-only second can keep the box wider than 1 % and defer an S2/S3 amount by ≈ 13 s per push; repeated every time the settlement becomes due, it blocks S2/S3 for as long as it pays (A: 43 pushes in 600 s). It needs a whole second without an honest observation each time, which keeper duty 2 denies after the first. v0.4.1 adds a cheaper form: a contrary observation in every second, even one that also holds the keeper's, widens one side of the box and defers an S3 on an executable venue for 0.002 SOL/s (RC) to 0.21 SOL/s (A) at a 1 % round trip (`refute_push.py` (d), §5.6). A push-then-freeze on the S3 amount now only defers it the same way: re-running the reviewer's `ptf.py` on v0.4.1, no S3 settles while the freeze lasts (200 s, 1.5–600 SOL of bundles) and the honest amount follows when it stops (v0.4: the frozen box let the pushed amount through, +3.14 to +10.48 SOL for a lender push or −5.22 SOL for a borrower push on Tier A, unprofitable at 17.7–54 SOL of bundles). An expired position deferred this way can still be expired normally when short.win's route works. Measured on the program (M10, `attacks_settle_it`, the mock's RC-like pool, a 0.4 % position, the Emergency-flag S3 due at its deadline; driver `tests/litesvm/sim/s3_widen.py`): a −1 % contrary observation every second does not defer it; a −3 % one (0.0064 SOL/s) defers it for as long as it is paid, and the S3 lands 6 s after the last contrary second — 14 / 54 s after the deadline for 20 / 60 s of it, the seconds `refute_push.widen_s3` gives on RC's max-size position (where the sim defers at −1 % too) — with an amount within `2·fv_dev_bps` of the honest S3's (rent accrues meanwhile): deferral only.


### 12.12 Right after a venue switch (`switch_warmup.py`, `venue_switch.py`, `pumpswap_lp.py`; M4R-1, M4R-2; PROPOSED, #96; switch only if all fit, #99; PumpSwap and the graduation, #105)

*Since the AI review's fixes (#117) the sims run AIR-13 … AIR-17 and AIR-22, and `out/attacks/switch_warmup.out`, `venue_switch.out` and `pumpswap_lp.out` are regenerated on them. Most outcomes of the rules as adopted move by small amounts in this section (AIR-22's growth, AIR-14's penalty base). AIR-15's cap on the box's `L_hi` removes the warm-up's wide seconds (4 → 0). **AIR-14's reference cap at `D` changes more:** `L_b` no longer credits `L_slow` above the locked depth, so V2-14's layer is gone on Raydium, as it is on PumpSwap since M6E-1. For the rules as adopted, (4d)'s forced adoptions are liquidated under every keeper with the cap, and a genuine withdrawal at a 1 % pool's open cap is liquidated without waiting for `L_slow` ((5b) (vi)). Residual 10 widens: a drag of attacker-only seconds forces one level, and missed keeper seconds and a held drain, frozen, force more ((5b) (iii), (iv), (vii)). Every level a held drain forces is still one the LP's withdrawal for good liquidates too. A run of `switch_warmup.py` parts 4, 5 and 11 with the cap at 2D (the sim otherwise as fixed) restores every S1 and every forced level of the `9aade3e` output, except in (5b) (v)'s comparison columns, which the other fixes move too. So these moves are AIR-14's. The scripts' **historical and analysis rules** (M4R without the guard, `#100`, `#105`, the cap alone, the analysis fits of (11) (d), the withdrawn lows bound, …) change only the rule they name and otherwise run the fixed sim. They therefore inherit the cap at `D`, and several no longer reproduce the rule they name. **Every table or figure below that differs between the two outputs says which one it is:** the regenerated output (the committed `out/`), or the `9aade3e` output (`git show 9aade3e:docs/design/sim/out/attacks/<script>.out`), with the regenerated values in a note. A table without a mark reads the same in both.*

The M4 re-verification (M4R) asked what a Raydium venue switch leaves behind for the positions it carries. As built after M4, the adoption seeded `L_ema` and the ring from single-instruction samples, `min(L_rec, L_live, D/λ_min)`, and carried a `P_ema` frozen while the incumbent could not execute. Both are forcing paths, not delays: an unlocked LP of the candidate withdraws around a sampling instruction (`L_new = D`) and then holds `L_ema` at `D` with one withdraw-wrapped crank per second — directional aggregation freezes an EMA that has one observation on its own side every second — so a position within ≈ `q/x` of `cr_liq` that is healthy on the pool as it trades becomes liquidatable on the EMA leg and on a withdraw-wrapped spot leg (**M4R-1**; the LiteSVM reproducer `m4r_it::m4r_explore_liquidation_grid`: liquidated 24–26 s after the adoption when wrapped, never in 90 s when not); and the window floor judged at the stale `P_ema` admits, after a dump during the halt, a pool with only `floor·√(P_live/P_ema)` of real locked SOL (**M4R-2**). The post-change confirmation of §5.2 (anchor, 8-bucket warm-up, gate, reference guard; the floor at `min(P_ema, P_cand)`, §6.2) replaces that rule. `attacks/switch_warmup.py` compares "M4 as built" (`venue_switch.BuiltM4`) with "M4R" (the sim's `Market`) on three run engines ported from the redesign's harnesses (the M4R-1 reproducer's markets; the R100 / RA / RC incumbents with attacker `a2`, whose every sample is withdraw-wrapped and whose wrapped liquidation lands first in each second; a 60-s pre-halt variant); the self-test: an honest switch reaches `L_ema/√(X·Y)` = 1.000 at the gate for λ 50, 99.78 and 25 %, the box is narrow after it, the guard clears at the gate, and a genuinely liquidatable position is liquidated at +9 s. Every engine's keeper cranks every second, except in part 4d / 4e, which put it on its §5.8 coverage duties: the guard and M4X-2's input guard hold only while every second carries an honest observation (M4F-1, #98; (4)).

**Since #99 (the owner's rule of 2026-09-29, §6.1) a switch carries open positions only onto a pool whose opening caps every one of them fits.** Parts (1)–(10) study the adoption's mechanics and **force the adoption**: most of their carried positions exceed the candidate's caps, and under #99 no window would open for them — the M4R-1 grid's 0.40 / 0.74 % of the incumbent's `X` are 1.53 / 2.83 % of the `X₀` an open on the 52-SOL λ 50 % candidate would have (cap 0.75 %); RA → 630 / 314 / 320 SOL at the size cap 2.94 / 5.90 / 5.78 %; RA → 625 SOL 5.91 %; the big 1 %-fee position 10.0 % (cap 2.47 %); of part 10's 955 floor-admitted random runs, 651 fit ((11), `switch_warmup.py` 11a lists every part). The mechanisms — anchor, warm-up, gate, guard, M4X-2's input guard, the cap — stay in force for the positions that fit, and the tables show what they do. (11) applies the rule.

**(1) M4R-1 — no forced liquidation.** A λ 50 % candidate (52 SOL at the pre-halt price) whose every unlocked LP unit the attacker holds; the position carried from a 100-SOL fully locked AMM v4 (Tier C, `cr_liq` 1.5); the candidate trades at `m`× the pre-halt price, flat after the adoption; every second the keeper's crank and liquidation attempt, and the attacker's `[withdraw all, crank, deposit]` and `[withdraw all, liquidate, deposit]`. **(4) Capital deposit freeze.** Anyone with capital about the pool's size deposits around sampling instructions and around one crank every second from the adoption (no swap fee). *(Regenerated on the AI review's fixes, #117. AIR-15's cap on the box's `L_hi` leaves M4R no wide second, 4 before. The analysis column runs M4R without the guard on today's sim, which caps the reference's EMA terms at `D` on Raydium since AIR-14 (`D ≤ L_live` on the pool the exit reads), and that cap alone keeps a frozen `L_ema` from lifting the reference: the freeze rows are liquidated there too. Before AIR-14 the column read S1 +123 s at 3.000×, lenders −17.785, borrower 0 — the case the guard was built for, and the reason it stays: it also bounds `L_slow`'s input, M4X-2.)*

(RA → 314 SOL, q 6 %: the same pattern — as built S1 at +120 s, −17.281 SOL with both samples wrapped; M4R liquidation at +59 s at 1.701×, lenders +0.853; without the guard S1, −17.281, before AIR-14.) With the guard the freeze delays the liquidation by 2–4 s (2–4 % of price) instead of blocking it. A freeze keeps `L_ema` up only while it is repeated every second with the pool's size in capital; it delays the liquidation trigger by at most ≈ `(1 − H/L)·q/x` in price (2.01× and the big position at 2.0× stay open while it lasts, where M4 as built liquidated at +28 s / +14 s) and, **with the keeper observing every second**, cannot block the reference — for as long as it is held, since the guard also covers `L_slow`'s input (§5.2, §8.2). With the keeper on its 30-s cadence after the gate it did block it (M4F-1, below). The M4R verification (M4X-2) held the freeze for 10–30 min before the pump: with the guard on the `L_ema` term alone, `L_slow` integrated the frozen `L_ema` (`+1/τ_slow` of the gap per second, plus a first 60-s-weighted step after the gate) and the unguarded `L_slow` term of `L_b` blocked the reference again (engine P, `switch_warmup.py` part 4c; cells: outcome; `L_slow/√(X·Y)` at the end):

On the fork (`m4r_it::m4x2_a_held_deposit_freeze_no_longer_lifts_l_slow`: a 320-SOL λ 99.78 % candidate, the position at the size cap, 7× the pool deposited around a crank every second for 900 s, then a genuine 4× ramp) the rule before M4X-2 reached `L_slow/√(X·Y)` 2.621 and S1 at +1,025 s (lenders 29.946 SOL for `q` worth 40.002, the borrower 0); now `L_slow` stays at 0.998 with the guard still set and the position is liquidated at +948 s (2.419×) with `q` bought back and 5.216 SOL to the borrower. `L_slow` held at the lows is the conservative side for the other readers too: the size cap and `R_eff` (§6.1, §6.2) no longer grow with the freeze.

**The keeper's coverage after the gate (M4F-1, #98).** Every row above has the keeper crank every second, and the guard and M4X-2's input guard rely on that: both read `L_mlo`, which holds the honest depth only while at most 4 of the 8 newest buckets are attacker-only (§8.2). As built at M4R, the keeper's duties (§5.8) left a healthy carried position after the gate at one observation every 30 s, and its duty-2 bursts compared prices only, so a deposit-wrapped crank every second owned 29 of every 30 seconds. `switch_warmup.py` part 4d re-runs part 4c's freeze with the keeper on its duties (engine P, `Spec.keeper_rule`):

- **Old keeper.** Per second while `ema_warm > 0`, near a threshold or `|P_spot/P_ema − 1| > 2 %`, and in a price burst; otherwise every 30 s.
- **M4F-1 keeper** (#98's duties 2 and 9; its liquidity bursts reverted by #99). Also per second while `ema_guard = 1`, and a burst on a foreign bucket whose `llo` / `lhi` is more than 2 % off the keeper's own `√(X·Y)`.
- **#99 keeper** (§5.8 before #101). Per second while `ema_guard = 1`, bursts on price only; after the guard clears, the old keeper.
- **#101 keeper** (§5.8 as it stands). The #99 keeper, plus per-second coverage for 10 s after a foreign bucket liquidity deviation on a market with positions, extended while they recur (FITS-2 (b)).

The cap is §8.2's, at 2D (1.995× the depth) on these λ 99.78 % candidates. The flat price holds for W s after the adoption, then a genuine pump rises to the top over 120 s. The attacker deposit-wraps one crank every second from `from` (`depK` = K× the pool's LP counter deposited; from +40 s = after an honest guard clear at +9 s). These carried positions exceed the candidates' opening caps (2.94–5.90 % of `X₀` against 0.75 %), so the rows force the adoption; under #99 no window opens for them ((11)). *(The table is the `9aade3e` output, with the cap at `⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋` = 2D. Since AIR-14 (#117) the cap is `D`, and the regenerated part 4d differs in three places. First, the cap alone liquidates every row under the old keeper: the 5.9 % rows at +163–166 s (+643 s after 600 s flat), the 2.9 % rows at +166 s / +646 s @1.767× with lenders +0.852, and the 320-SOL row at +153 s @1.825×, with 80–573 attacker-only seconds. Second, the #99 keeper's freezes from +40 s are liquidated at +163 / +166 s, with 80 attacker-only seconds. Third, the freezes from +40 s under M4F-1's and the #101 keepers read lenders +0.845 instead of +0.835. The columns without the cap are unchanged.)*

With the old keeper the attacker's buckets lift `L_mlo` within about 5 entries, the guard clears at +15 s (a freeze from +40 s finds it already cleared honestly at +9 s and lifts `L_ema` directly), `L_ema` = `L_mlo` = the deposit's depth, and the genuine pump ends in S1 in every attacked row, as it did under M4 as built (the same cells). The fork reproduces it (`m4r_it::m4f1_*`, #97's M4X-2 market: a 320-SOL candidate, q/x 3.12 %, 7× the pool deposited, a 4× ramp): S1, lenders −10.056 SOL, the borrower zeroed, where the control is liquidated. The 320-SOL row here takes that candidate and ramp; its position is larger relative to the pool (5.8 %).

With M4F-1's keeper every row is liquidated as with the keeper cranking every second (without the cap the same cells). The freeze from the adoption keeps the guard set, and the reference at the honest lows, for as long as it lasts, at the price of a keeper observation every second throughout (153–646 s here). A freeze started after the clear gets one attacker-only second: `L_ema` moves by one step (7/6), then freezes at the burst's honest lows (+161 s at 1.683× against the honest +160 s at 1.667×) — but only while the keeper misses no covered second (M4G-1: each miss is another step, and four isolated misses in such a freeze ended in S1 on the fork), and at a burst cost anyone could trigger for a transaction fee (M4G-2); #99 reverted those bursts. The #99 keeper keeps the guard set under a freeze from the adoption (liquidated, as M4F-1's) and after an honest clear is the old keeper again: with the cap at 2D the freezes from +40 s end in S1 (80 attacker-only seconds). That is the case #99 removes: a candidate on which the carried position exceeds the opening caps is not adopted. The #101 keeper (FITS-2 (b)) brings the liquidity trigger back per second and on markets with positions only: every row is liquidated as under M4F-1's keeper (the freezes from +40 s at +161 s, 1 attacker-only second), at one keeper crank per attacker transaction instead of a 60-s per-slot burst; a missed covered second is still another step (M4G-1).

The cap alone (the old keeper), at 2D as built until AIR-14, bounds the pushed reference at 2× the depth. That liquidates the 2.9 % position (+169 s at 1.817×, lenders +0.859) but not the 5.8–5.9 % ones: their live buyback cost still exceeds `B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` when the reference is credited with 2× the depth. A position within the caps of these 25-bps pools (≤ 0.75 % of the live `X`) is below what a 2× reference can block ((11) (c)); a 25-bps incumbent's position at a 1 % candidate's impact cap was not (FIT-2, (11) (d)), which #100's carried fee caps exclude, nor one carried at a 1 %→1 % switch's impact cap (FITS-1), which #101's 25-bps cap on them excludes. Since AIR-14 (#117) the cap is `D` (§8.2), so `L_b = L_live` on these pools and a pushed `L_ema` no longer lifts the reference at all. In the regenerated part 4d every row is liquidated under every keeper with the cap (the note under the table); the rules without it still end in S1. The coverage duties still bound what a freeze delays: the freezes from +40 s exit at +163–166 s under the old and the #99 keepers, against +161 s under M4F-1's and the #101 keepers.

Steady-state controls (part 4e: no switch, positions at the open caps on 314–1,250-SOL pools at λ 99.78 / 50 %, and on λ 50 % pools of 60–314 SOL drained to their locked part, `q/x` 0.74–1.49 %, 3×/120 s and 2.5×/60 s pumps): under the old keeper and without the cap, a `dep`–`dep7` freeze delays the liquidation by 0–2 s (for example +46 s at 1.767× against +45 s at 1.750×). With M4F-1's keeper +0–1 s, and the same with the #101 keeper (1 attacker-only second instead of 116–580); the #99 keeper is the old keeper there (no switch sets the guard). The exposure was a switch's: a carried position that is large on the new pool, which #99 no longer adopts ((11)).

The unlocked LP holder's withdraw-wrapped liquidation, landing first, prices the reference at `D` while the guard is set: the borrower pays +4.04 % (1.24 SOL) more than in the honest liquidation — `(1/λ − 1)·q/x` at most, ≈ 0 on λ ≈ 1 pools — which M4 as built also allowed, through its `D` seed. It never forces an exit. The "blocker" rows are (4)'s freeze at a flat price: the position waits for as long as the freeze is paid (as built: +20 s, its cap binding at λ = λ_min).

**(5b) The steady-state withdraw drag under the keeper's real cadence (FITV-2, FITS-2; `switch_warmup.py` part 5b).** No switch: a Tier C Raydium market whose unlocked LP the attacker holds — FITV-2's fork pool, a 160-SOL λ 99.78 % CPMM at a 1 % fee plus the attacker's own deposit of 1× / 3× / 3.95× the LP counter (λ 49.9 / 24.9 / 20.2 %) — with a position at the open cap (in (iii) a share of it) and the EMA converged. The keeper follows its duties (§5.8): every 30 s in quiet periods, per second near a threshold or once `|P_spot/P_ema − 1| > 2 %`. Every second, before the keeper, the attacker sends `[withdraw all, crank_price, deposit]` — an attacker-only second's bucket is the drained depth `D`, so such seconds drag `L_ema` toward `D` (1/6 of the gap each), and a keeper observation in a dragged second cannot lift it (the observation is clamped into the bucket's `[D, H]`, where `L_ema` already is) — and `[withdraw all, liquidate, deposit]`. Flat for `W` s (the drag), then a genuine pump to the hold level over 60 s, held 300 s. **"#100"** is the rule set as #100 left it: the spot leg at the drained `L_live` and the #99 keeper (29 of every 30 quiet seconds are the attacker's, so `L_ema` reaches `D` within about 8 s); `L_b = max(L_live, min(max(L_ema', L_slow), cap))`, whose `L_slow` term (V2-14) credits the depth that was there until it has followed the dragged `L_ema` (τ_slow = 1 h). **"#101"** is the rule set as adopted: the spot leg at `max(L_live, L_ema')` (§5.5) and the #101 keeper, which covers every second for 10 s after a foreign liquidity deviation on a market with positions, extended while they recur (§5.8 duty 2); the attacker starts in the second after a keeper observation, so its first dragged second is attacker-only. "The withdrawn lows bound" is #100's first fix (§8.2), on #100's exits. (i) The drag held `W` s before a pump to the hold level. (iv) **What is left under #101: missed keeper seconds** (the M4G-1 class, downward). While the attacker wraps every second, a covered second whose keeper observation does not land is another attacker-only step, and a covered second's bucket holds `L_ema`, so the steps add up; isolated misses do not trigger degraded mode. Pausing until the 10-s window lapses gains nothing: the honest covered seconds give each step back. The 320-SOL 1 % pool, a 1-h drag:

| the open cap | 2.40 % | 2.078× | every second; 1 / 3 / 5 / 10 / 20 misses | 2.07× (1) / 2.06–2.07× (2) / 2.05–2.07× (3) / 2.03–2.07× (5) / 2.01–2.07× (7) | 0.847 / 0.920 … 0.510 / 0.735 |
| half the cap | 1.22 % | 2.039× | every second; 0 / 1 misses | none | 0.917 / 0.947, 0.847 / 0.920 |
| half the cap | 1.22 % | 2.039× | every second; 3 / 5 / 10 / 20 misses | 2.03× (1) / 2.03× (1) / 2.02–2.03× (2) / 2.01–2.03× (3) | 0.741 / 0.880 … 0.510 / 0.735 |
| either | | | one wrap every 2 s / every 11 s (keeper 3,959 / 3,632 observations for 1,980 / 360 attacker transactions at the cap) | none | 1.000 / 1.000, 0.903 / 0.971 |

(v) **The spot leg's own case: a genuine spike and retreat.** No drag. The price ramps to `k`× (over 5–20 s), holds 3–5 s and falls back over 2 s to `r`× below the honest threshold: `P_ema` lags the spike, so for a few seconds after the retreat the EMA leg is true while the robust spot at the pool's depth is not — the spot leg is the veto. The unlocked-LP holder sends only `[withdraw all, liquidate, deposit]` every second. Of the retreat levels 1.98–2.07× the honest keeper leaves open, forced:

| pool | honest threshold | spike | #100 | the #101 keeper alone | #101 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 320 SOL, λ 49.9 %, 1 % | 2.078× | 2.4× (10 s ramp, 5 s hold) | 2.01–2.07× (7 of 9) | 2.01–2.07× (7) | none |
| 640 SOL, λ 24.9 %, 1 % | 2.039× | 2.2× (20 s, 5 s) | 1.98–2.03× (5 of 5) | the same | none |
| 320 SOL, λ 49.9 %, 0.25 % | 2.026× | 2.3× (10 s, 5 s), 2.6× (5 s, 3 s) | 2.01–2.02× (2 of 4) | the same | none |

(the other spike shapes of the grid forced nothing under any rule at `9aade3e`, and force nothing under #101 on the fixed sim; where the honest keeper liquidates at the spike itself, the attacker only takes that due liquidation in the same second, under every rule).

(vi) **Liveness: a genuine withdrawal (FITS-2 (c)).** No attacker; every unlocked LP unit leaves the pool at second 90 and stays out, so a position healthy at the pool's full depth becomes liquidatable at its new depth. Under #101 the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')`, and `L_ema` follows the drained depth at 1/6 of the gap per integrated second. The EMA leg — the ring's reprojections at `L_ema` — already waited for that decay under #100: in a flat or rising market it binds, so the new spot leg costs nothing; it binds only while the robust spot is below `P_ema` (a dip at the withdrawal, noise), until `L_ema` has decayed. Positions with a band between the drained and the full-depth threshold (`h` its middle); cells: seconds after the withdrawal, #100 → #101:

| pool, position | thresholds (full / drained) | flat `h` | dip 0.25 % / 0.5 % at the withdrawal | `h` with ±1 % noise, seeds 1–3 | pump to 1.2 `h`, the withdrawal 30 s in |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 320 SOL, λ 49.9 %, 0.25 %, the open cap (0.74 %) | 2.0240× / 2.0025× | +8 → +8 s | +7 → +7 s / +4 → +6 s | +7 → +7, +9 → +9, +9 → +10 s | +21 → +21 s |
| 320 SOL, λ 49.9 %, 1 %, 0.3 of the cap (0.73 %) | 2.0240× / 2.0025× | +8 → +8 s | +7 → +7 s / +3 → +6 s | +7 → +7, +9 → +9, +9 → +10 s | +21 → +21 s |
| 640 SOL, λ 24.9 %, 1 %, 0.3 of the cap (0.37 %) | 2.0122× / 1.9811× | +13 → +13 s | +12 → +12 s / +12 → +12 s | +9 → +13, +13 → +21, +12 → +12 s | +21 → +21 s |

(The regenerated output of the AI review's fixes, #117; the second noise cell read +13 → +23 s at `9aade3e`.) The delay is 0–3 s after a dip and 0–8 s under ±1 % noise (0–10 s at `9aade3e`), at the same prices or higher; every run is a liquidation under both rule sets. At the open cap on the 1 % pools there was no such band at `9aade3e`, under either rule set: the drained buyback exceeded `B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` while `L_slow` still credited the pre-withdrawal depth (V2-14). After a genuine withdrawal the position waited for `L_slow`, and in a pump that followed at once it could gap into S1. On the 320-SOL pool a withdrawal followed by 3×/120 s gave S1 at +162 s under #100 and #101, against a liquidation at +72 s without the withdrawal; followed by 2.5×/300 s it stayed open for 900 s under both, against +223 s without it. That was the other side of V2-14's `L_slow` term, which #101 left unchanged (O18). **Since AIR-14 (#117)** the reference cap is `D` and `L_b` reads the drained pool, so the wait is gone. In the regenerated output the 320-SOL position is liquidated at +68 s @2.150× (3×/120 s) and +209 s @2.050× (2.5×/300 s). The 640-SOL position is liquidated at +64 s @2.083× and +194 s @1.975×. Each comes earlier than without the withdrawal (+72 / +223 s and +70 / +215 s).

The lows bound let the drag force the band at once: it capped `L_slow` at `L_mlo·1.01 = D` as soon as the lows were dragged, and the wrapped liquidation fit a bound at `D` (FITV-2; on the fork forced at 2.05× while the honest keeper never liquidates). Under #100's exits (the `9aade3e` output) `L_b`'s `L_slow` term still credits the depth the pool had, and wherever `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 > δ` — at 1 % and the impact cap — the wrapped liquidation's drained cost exceeds `B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` and a short drag forces nothing; but `L_slow` integrates the dragged `L_ema` with τ_slow = 1 h, and after 0.79 h (λ 50 %), 1.80 h (λ 25 %) or 3.19 h (λ 20 %, `λ_min` 2,000) the same band opens — 2.01–2.07× forced against the honest 2.078× (3.3 % in price), 1.94–2.03× against 2.039× (4.9 %). Where `δ` covers the drained cost — at 25 bps, and at 1 % below about half the cap — a 30-s drag already forces a band (FITS-2 (a): at half the cap 2.01–2.03× against 2.039×; on the fork 2.025×), and at 1.9 % of `X` a 25-min drag does (FITS-2 (b); the fork's cap-sized regression position). **Under #101 a drag of attacker-only seconds forces at most one level**: the drag gets one attacker-only second and holds `L_ema` at ≈ 0.917 of the depth, so the spot leg (at `L_ema'`) and the EMA leg both read nearly the pool's depth, and `L_slow` never follows below it. At `9aade3e` it forced nothing on any pool, size or drag of the tables, and the probed hold levels were not reached within 8 h. Since AIR-14 (#117) `L_b` no longer credits `L_slow` above the drained pool, so wherever both legs hold at that one step the wrapped liquidation fits its bound. In the regenerated output that is one level, 2.06× against 2.065×, at 0.79 × the open cap after any drag of 30 s to 1 h ((iii)); every other #101 cell of (i)–(iii) is as at `9aade3e`. Each of #101's two changes alone is not enough in general: the spot leg alone leaves the drag (which moves `L_ema'` itself), and the keeper alone leaves the spike-and-retreat (v). At λ 99.78 % the one level is the attacker's attempt landing first in its second (there is nothing to withdraw). The attacker pays one transaction a second and the keeper one crank a second for as long as the drag lasts (1:1). (A failed attacker liquidation keeps its observation in engine P; on chain the attacker bundles the wrapped crank shown here.)

(vii) **What is left under #101: a genuinely reached low, frozen (FITX-1, #102).** The per-second coverage denies the drag its attacker-only seconds, but a covered bucket `[D, H]` holds `L_ema` where it is and cannot lift it. The attacker withdraws its unlocked LP **for real** at second 1 and keeps it out for `held` s, cranking every second at the drained depth — the keeper reads the same depth, so nothing is foreign and no coverage starts — and `L_ema` genuinely falls toward `D` (λ 49.9 %: 0.741 / 0.667 / 0.566 / 0.510 of the full depth after 3 / 5 / 10 / 20 s). It then re-deposits and withdraw-wraps a crank every second: the first wrap starts the coverage, and every later bucket holds the drained and the keeper's honest observation, which leaves `L_ema` where it is — the freeze, one keeper crank per attacker transaction. `L_slow` follows the drained lows (its input is `min(lobs, L_mlo)`, every bucket's low `D`; τ_slow = 1 h), as after a withdrawal for good. After `W` s flat, the genuine pump to the hold level, with `[withdraw all, liquidate, deposit]` every second: the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` = the frozen low, the EMA leg the same low, and where `δ` covers the drained cost (or `L_slow` has followed) the exit bound passes. (The table is the regenerated output of the AI review's fixes, #117. At `9aade3e`, with `L_b` crediting `L_slow` up to 2D, the 640- and 792-SOL pools' cap-sized rows forced nothing. The 320-SOL pool forced nothing at 0.79–1 × the cap after a 60-s window, and 2–7 levels there only once `L_slow` had followed (25 min / 1 h). The last column read 69 of 69.) The held drain needs a few seconds of the attacker's capital genuinely out of the pool: 3 s force a level at every size on the 320-SOL pool, from the cap down. After it the freeze costs one transaction a second, as the drag did. Since AIR-14's cap at `D`, `L_b` reads the drained pool, so a held drain forces at once what #100's drag forced only after `L_slow` had followed, at every size. That is 1–7 levels on the 320-SOL pool after 3–20 s held, and 1–9 levels on the 640-SOL pool (1.95–2.03× against 2.039×). On the 792-SOL pool it is 11 levels after a 20-s hold (1.85–1.95× against 2.082×, 6–11 % in price). **Everything it forces is what the same LP forces by withdrawing for good** (the last column: 181 of 181 levels, never earlier): the frozen `L_ema` is at or above the depth a withdrawal for good reaches, `L_slow` follows the same drained lows, the wrapped liquidation buys at the same drained depth, and its bound is no looser (a deeper `L_b` only lowers `max_in`). What the attacker saves is having to keep its LP out of the pool after the held seconds. On the fork (`freeze::fitx1_*`, a 20-s held drain; the verifier's cases, measured before the AI review's fixes) the attacker's liquidation lands where the honest keeper leaves the position open — the 1 % position at 500 ‰ of its cap (1.232 % of `X`) at 2.025× (+71 s; penalty 0.6416 SOL, 0.1925 of it the attacker's; the borrower 3.3017 SOL; 111 keeper cranks for 111 wraps; held 10 / 5 s: +74 / +86 s), the 25-bps position at its open cap (0.469 %) at 2.010× (+76 s; at 2.005×, +82 s) and the cap-sized 1 % position (1.899 %) after a 25-min freeze at 2.05× (+67 s; `L_slow` 0.833 at the pump; penalty 1.0048 SOL; 1,547 / 1,547) — and the honest keeper liquidates each in the same second after the LP's withdrawal for good (at 2.005× one second earlier), at the same price and buyback cost (±9 lamports), the borrower's remainder 632–163,421 lamports lower than under the attack.

Closing it needs a new rule — an owner decision (O18), not adopted. Two candidate floors of the spot leg, as analysis rules (`MhiFloor`, `MhiSlowFloor`): `L_mhi`, the upper median of the ring's bucket liquidity highs (the box's `L_hi` term: every covered second's high is the keeper's honest depth, so lowering it needs 5 of 8 attacker-only buckets), and `min(L_mhi, L_slow)`, which deposit-wrapped cranks cannot raise. The `L_mhi` floor closes every held-drain and missed-second cell (the one left is a due level) and adds nothing to (vi)'s 21 genuine withdrawals (+0 s against #101 in each), but lets a deposit freeze hold a position 0.5–2 % above its threshold open for as long as the freeze lasts, where #101 liquidates 0–31 s after the control — residual 2's class, widened from the EMA leg's `(1 + C)` step to the deposit's whole depth in the spot leg. `min(L_mhi, L_slow)` behaves as #101 in every deposit row and adds nothing to (vi) either. It leaves the long freezes: 2–3 levels at 0.79–1 × the 320-SOL cap and 1–3 at the 640-SOL cap after 25 min – 1 h, one level at 0.79 × the cap after 60 s, and one level at half the cap and below and at 25 bps after 25 min. It also leaves the missed seconds: one level at the cap after 3 misses, three after 20. `L_slow` follows the drained lows under the freeze as after a withdrawal for good. (At `9aade3e` it left nothing at the 640-SOL cap, nothing after 60 s, and 2 levels after 20 misses.)

**(6) M4R-2.** The token dumps `f`× during the halt (or, in the M4X-3 rows, 5 s into the collection window, after the proposal that opened it); the only candidate has 30 SOL of locked SOL at the pre-halt price (λ 99.78 %), i.e. `30/√f` SOL at the live price:

| f | attacker | M4 as built | M4R |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4x | none | adopted; P_ema/P 3.99; 15.0 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL | not adopted (refused 310, cancelled 0); cost 0.00 SOL |
| 4x | pump-wrapped recording proposal | adopted; P_ema/P 3.99; 15.0 SOL locked; cost 0.04 SOL | adopted; P_ema/P 3.99 -> 1.86; 15.0 SOL locked; cost 0.04 SOL |
| 4x | none; the dump comes 5 s into the window (M4X-3) | adopted; P_ema/P 3.99; 15.0 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL | adopted; P_ema/P 3.99 -> 1.86; 15.0 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL |
| 16x | none | adopted; P_ema/P 15.97; 7.5 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL | not adopted (refused 310, cancelled 0); cost 0.00 SOL |
| 16x | pump-wrapped recording proposal | adopted; P_ema/P 15.97; 7.5 SOL locked; cost 0.03 SOL | adopted; P_ema/P 15.97 -> 7.44; 7.5 SOL locked; cost 0.03 SOL |
| 16x | none; the dump comes 5 s into the window (M4X-3) | adopted; P_ema/P 15.97; 7.5 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL | adopted; P_ema/P 15.97 -> 7.44; 7.5 SOL locked; cost 0.00 SOL |

An honest dump before the window-opening proposal is refused (no window; M4 as built adopted the 15- and 7.5-SOL pools); the thin pool's backer can pump-wrap its window-opening proposal (0.03–0.04 SOL) and restore M4's judgment at `P_ema`, never looser, and the warm-up then lowers `P_ema` towards the live price — since M6-SEC-1 (#105) at the steady-state rate, at most one v0.4.1 step per integration (the cells' "→ 1.86" / "→ 7.44": the seed at the gate after a 4× / 16× dump; before #105 "→ 1.00", the live price at once), and on through the steady-state EMA after the gate — so opens wait out the adopted pool's listing window (`PoolListWindow`, 1 h), by which time `P_ema` is on the pool, and then tiers see the pool as it is; the lagging-high seed forces nothing (the spot leg reads the live price, every other outcome of parts 1–11 is unchanged). The execution judges the floor at `P_ema` only, so an honest dump **inside** the 60-s window, after the proposal that opened it, is adopted at `P_ema` in the same way (M4X-3; `m4r_it::m4x3_a_dump_inside_the_window_is_adopted_at_p_ema_then_re_seeded`: 29.99 SOL locked at `P_ema`, 14.94 live, adopted; after the warm-up `P_ema` was the live price and a new open was refused `PoolTooThin` — since M6-SEC-1 the seed is 1.73× the live price at the gate (a fall of ×0.43 from the carried 4.03×: `(6/7)·(11/12)⁷`, then one steady step) and 1.00× a minute later; an open at the gate is refused `PoolListWindow` (the adopted pool's listing window), and once the window has run `PoolTooThin`). A re-check at the execution against the candidate's own spot was rejected: that spot is one instruction's sample, so an execution that failed on it would either revert — holding `PoolWindowClosed` for every other proposal while the pool stays thin, the freeze M4V-4 removed — or cancel, and then a dump-wrapped execution (a round trip, ≈ 0.1 SOL) could cancel every window until S3, a forced exit. A halted market whose only candidate is thin at the live price stays halted — its positions frozen until the incumbent returns, a deeper pool appears or S3 — which is the v0.4.1 behaviour for an ineligible pool (O14).

The seed reads only bucket highs and lows, so it needs **all 8** warm-up buckets attacker-only (the keeper absent for s < 9) or an 8-s genuine held drain before the M4R-1 band applies at +9 s; 7 attacker-only buckets leave it honest. (M4 as built was forced whatever the keeper did.) Row (f) is open because its attacker holds the pool (every observation sees the push) and withdraw-wraps its liquidation; an attacker who instead **pump-wraps** its cranks and its liquidation in attacker-only seconds (atomic `[buy, crank_price, sell]` and `[buy, liquidate, sell]` bundles, no keeper to observe the true price) writes bucket lows at its pump. Before the M4R verification (M4X-1) the warm-up seed read that low at once: 8 attacker-only warm-up seconds — 7 when the attacker executes the switch and cranks in its second — forced the liquidation of a position at **any** threshold at +9 s while the pool traded flat (the LiteSVM reproducer: a ≈ 3× threshold liquidated at a true 1.00×). With the bound, attacker-only seconds after a switch force exactly the thresholds they force in steady state and under M4 as built (whose `P_ema` obeys the same per-integration bound), never earlier (the gate; as built reached the 2.05× position at +7 s with 7 absent seconds); the attacker's crank pumps are smaller (its seed takes the whole step with a pump just above it, where the v0.4.1 step needs a `(1 + C)×` pump), which changes the fee cost, not the reach. That remaining exposure is §12.6's degraded mode: `N` attacker-only seconds move `P_ema` by at most `×(τ + dt·(1 + C))/(τ + dt)` each — 9/7·(7/6)⁵ ≈ 2.8× at the oldest of the EMA leg's three entries by the gate — and the keeper's duty 1 (§5.8) is what bounds it.

A held price push through the warm-up — every observation of each second, the keeper's included, sees it — is the §12.6 held model: (e) was liquidated at +9 s (as built +10 s) by a withdraw-wrapped liquidation once the push had ended and the pool was back at 2.00×, the EMA leg true from the seeded `P_ema` and the spot leg true only at the drained depth; since #101 the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` and the position stays open (the spike-and-retreat case of (5b) (v)); without a keeper to confirm the pushed spot leg nothing happens (f). That needs a steady-state change (for example, the EMA leg at `max(L_i, upper median of lhi)`) and is a follow-up (O18). #101's per-second coverage makes the freeze cost one keeper crank a second but does not lift the low: at the FITS-2 positions a 3–20-s held drain, frozen, forces 1–5 hold levels, and after a 25-min – 1-h freeze the cap-sized ones — each a liquidation the same LP's withdrawal for good also gets ((5b) (vii), residual 10; FITX-1, #102), where the spot-leg floors at `L_mhi` and `min(L_mhi, L_slow)` are evaluated as analysis rules.
