Every attacked run is a liquidation, lenders +0.102 to +0.835 SOL at the exit price; none is an S1. A thinner candidate carries a proportionally smaller position (an open on it right after the switch is sized on its locked depth, §6.1), so at the fit limit `q/x` is ≤ 0.75 % of the new pool's live `X` (0.37 % at λ 50 %): what a position opened there at the caps would be. The freezes delay the trigger by at most one second, the ≈ `(1 − H/L)·q/x` of residual 2 with `q/x` ≤ 0.75 %; the held deposits delay nothing when pulled at the pump's start (the lagging `L_ema` is back at the live depth well before the threshold) and at most one second when pulled just before it. The cap changes no exit second or price here, and the #101 keeper's per-second liquidity-anomaly coverage takes the freezes from +40 s from +0–1 s to +0 s (#99's keeper: +0–1 s; the worst delays, from the freezes while the guard is set and the deposit pulled late, are unchanged). They lie within the steady-state numbers of part 4e (0–2 s at the open caps): **at 25 bps, with no oversized carried position, M4F-1's freeze, M4G-1's missed seconds and M4G-3's held deposit only delay exits.** #99 generalised this to every fee from `(1 − c·λ_min)/(1 − c) − 1 < δ` (with the reference at the cap and the live depth drained to `D`: 0.38 % at `c` = 0.75 %); that bound reads `q/X` at the adoption and leaves out the pump's growth of `q/X_live`, the EMA lag and the margin a carried position posted on the old pool's cost — (d). The per-second duty while the guard is set still keeps the reference at the honest lows through a freeze from the adoption; after the clear it is the fit (and the cap), not keeper coverage, that bounds the push, so a missed keeper second (M4G-1) and the keeper's cost of bursts (M4G-2) no longer matter here.

(d) **FIT-2 and FITS-1: a carried position on a higher-fee candidate, and fast pumps** (`switch_warmup.py` 11d; the fork's `freeze::fit2_*`). On a 1 % pool the per-position impact cap binds, 2.47 % of `X₀` (25 bps: the fee-scaled 0.75 %), and a carried position's margin was posted on the incumbent's cost (on the fork V/B 2.93 on the new pool against 3.12 for the same size opened there). RAC = RA's 2,500-SOL λ 50 % 25-bps pool at Tier C (the fork's M4X-2 market), RAC1 the same pool at a 1 % fee, RA the 25-bps pool at Tier A; the candidates λ 99.78 % pools at a 1 % fee; `W` s flat after the adoption, then a genuine 4× pump over 30 / 45 / 60 / 120 s; the #101 keeper; the pushes are the freeze `dep7` after the clear (M4G-1's limit: every second but the keeper's attacker-only — since #101 the keeper covers it every second after its first), a 7× deposit held from +20 s and pulled at the pump's first second (M4G-3), and both. *(The analysis-fit cells are the `9aade3e` output, with the exits' reference cap at 2D. The analysis fits run with the exits as adopted, so since AIR-14 (#117) they read the cap at `D`, which no longer credits the pulled deposit. In the regenerated part 11d every S1 cell of that column is a liquidation at +618 s @2.800×: RAC at 100 % of #99's limit +1 s (+3.7 % in price, lenders +0.340), at 76 % +0 s (+0.328); RAC1 at 100 / 76 / 70 % +0 s (+0.431 / +0.397 / +0.381). RAC1 at 65 % reads +0 s (lenders +0.366) where it read +1 s. The adopted column is unchanged.)*

With the fee caps at the candidate's own fee (#99 as first built), the 25-bps incumbent's position moved onto the 1 % candidate at its impact cap, and in a 4×/30 s pump the pushes ended it in S1 where the control was liquidated: the held deposit is honest depth while held, so no coverage sees it, and after the pull `L_slow` credits it for about an hour (up to the 2D cap; since AIR-14 the cap at `D` credits nothing above the drained pool, and those rows are liquidated, the note above), while a freeze after the clear owned the idle seconds' lows (since #101 the keeper's liquidity-anomaly coverage holds that freeze at its first step, and it delays the exit by 0–1 s; the held deposit is never foreign and still ends in S1 at that size); the margin, posted on the deeper, cheaper pool, leaves no room for the lag. With `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` (#100) the same positions open no window and settle S3 at fair value at the deadline, and a position at the new fit limit — 0.75 % of `X₀`, the class it was margined at — is liquidated within a second of the control under every push, at every pump speed: FIT-2 is closed for a switch onto a higher-fee pool. **A 1 %→1 % switch** (FITS-1) kept the impact-cap fit limit under #100 (`min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` is the candidate's own fee), and a position margined on the deeper incumbent's cost ended in S1 under the held deposit in a 4×/30 s pump at 70–100 % of it in the sim with the cap at 2D (and at 60–100 % on the fork, where the freeze with the #99 keeper also did); **since #101 the carried fee caps take at most 25 bps**, so those positions open no window and settle S3 at fair value, and one at the new limit (0.749 % of `X₀`) is liquidated within 0–1 s of the control under every push. **Native controls** (the 320-SOL 1 % pool listed directly, a position at its own open cap, `q/x` 2.41 %): at Tier C liquidated in every row (30-s pump +19–20 s, lender +0.373 to +0.779; with the #101 keeper the freeze from +40 s gets 1 attacker-only second instead of 79 and delays nothing); at Tier A S1 at 4×/30 s and 4×/60 s with or without a push, like RA's carried row (the §11.5 gap class: the EMA leg lags the pump past the liquidation-to-S1 buffer). The fork's regressions: `freeze::fit2_a_position_at_the_one_percent_fit_limit_is_not_adopted_and_settles_in_s3` (FIT-2's position at 76 % of #99's cap: the keeper proposes nothing, a direct proposal is refused, S3 at the deadline with the lenders at FV), `freeze::fit2_a_carried_position_at_the_new_fit_limit_is_liquidated_under_the_pushes` (95 % of the new cap, a 4×/30 s pump: the control and the three pushes liquidated, no S1) and `freeze::fit2_a_native_position_at_the_open_cap_is_liquidated_under_the_same_pushes`; FITS-1's 1 %→1 % switch is the code's regression of #101 (ARCH §8.2).

(e) **FIT-3: a closed large position keeps the mark** (`switch_warmup.py` 11e; residual 14). RA's market (Tier A, 30-day terms): three honest positions of a quarter of the incumbent's size cap, opened on days 0, 10 and 20; the griefer opens at the incumbent's cap on day 20 and closes a second later; the incumbent halts on day 21; the only candidate has 40 % of its reserves at λ 99.78 % (a per-position cap of ≈ 0.4 of the incumbent's), which each honest position and their OI fit. The keeper cranks every 30 s (duty 5) and proposes the candidate whenever it fits; each position exits at its deadline. Cells: time after the halt; the lender's P&L against holding `q` (at the frozen `P_ema` for S3, else the exit price):

| event | without the griefer | with it (the griefer's round trip 0.140 SOL; 54,715 proposals refused) |
|---|---|---|
| the candidate adopted | +210 s, all three positions carried (`ΣQ` 0.75 of the cap) | +19.00 d, the last position (`ΣQ` 0.25) |
| position 1 (day 0) | expiry on the new pool at +9.00 d, +0.256 SOL (+5.5 %) | S3 on the halted pool at +9.00 d, +0.305 SOL (+6.6 %) |
| position 2 (day 10) | expiry at +19.00 d, +0.257 SOL (+5.5 %) | S3 at +19.00 d, +0.304 SOL (+6.6 %) |
| position 3 (day 20) | expiry at +29.00 d, +0.259 SOL (+5.5 %) | carried at +19.00 d, expiry at +29.00 d, +0.255 SOL (+5.5 %) |

Every proposal is refused while `min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)` exceeds the candidate's per-position cap: the griefer's closed position holds the first term at the incumbent's cap, so the S3 exits must bring `ΣQ` under it. Each healthy position on the halted pool settles S3 at fair value at its own deadline — the §10.3 timer — and the fit's `ΣQ` term then lets the next proposal carry the rest. The price is flat here, so the frozen positions cost the lenders nothing; with a move during the halt the exposure is residual 12's (−46 % against a 2× move).

**(12) PumpSwap: third-party LP and the graduation's warm-up seed (the owner's decisions of 2026-09-30, M6R-1 and M6-SEC-1, #105; `attacks/pumpswap_lp.py`).** Canonical PumpSwap pools take third-party deposits and withdrawals (§1.2), so this section's vectors apply to PumpSwap markets; "M6 as built" is the rule of #104 (no locked depth on PumpSwap, the seed bounded upward only), "#105" the owner's decisions of #105, "#106" those with OWNER-M6D-1 — `D` itself in sizing, the tier and (since the #106 verification's M6E-1) the exit reference's cap: the normative sim's rule. The #105 cells of (a)–(d) are #106's too: sizing on `D` changes none of them (the graduation's carried positions are sized on the curve, (c)'s before the deposit, and (d)'s positions are the same ones — the cap and half of it under #106, half and a quarter of #105's cap), nor does the reference cap at `D` but for (c)'s `L_b` read (the same exits); `pumpswap_lp.out` prints them under "#106".

(a) **One low in a graduation's warm-up second (M6-SEC-1).** ECON §10.1's D@80 % gap row (the honest liquidation +12 s); in the `k`-th second after the transition the attacker sends `[sell to f× the pool's spot, crank_price, buy back]` next to the keeper's crank (its round trip 0.628 SOL at 0.5×, 1.077 SOL at 0.25×). Cells: the exit (seconds after the completion, price), lenders' tokens + SOL:

| low | second | M6 as built (#104) | #105 |

| 0.25× | 1 / 4 / 8 | +28 s @2.61× / +28 s / +28 s; 100 % + 0.1463 | +28 s / **+22 s** / **+13 s** |

(The 8th-second cells read +14 s before AIR-22's lower-median growth, #117.)

A low recorded late in the warm-up now pulls the seed by one step per remaining integration instead of to the low; a low in the first second is reached as before, because the carried seed is then at 0.555× the pool (the completing buy took the price to 2.46× in one second) and itself climbs at ≤ ×7/6 per integration: the bound works from the running seed, not from the pool's price (the seed at the gate after a first-second low at 0.25× / 0.1×: 0.256 / 0.256 of the pool's price, as built 0.250 / 0.100; on D@97 %, whose carried seed is at 0.886×, 0.413 / 0.413 against 0.250 / 0.100). The lenders are whole in every row (the delayed exits pay them slightly more, the buyback at a higher price). On D@90 % (+3× ramp) and D@97 % (+2× ramp) the exits come 52–87 s after the completion and no low changes them. A genuine dump right after the transition is tracked at the steady-state rate — the seed at the gate 1.000 / 1.034 of the dumped price on D@80 % (0.5× / 0.25×), 1.000 / 1.670 on D@97 % — and every position stays open under both rules (nothing forced: the spot leg reads the live price). This is the residual of M6-SEC-1; the review's other options (a median seed; a carried-position bound at graduation) remain for the owner (O18).

(b) **A deposit between `migrate_v2` and the transition (M6R-1).** `[migrate_v2, deposit k× the pool, crank_price]` then a withdrawal raises the `lp_supply` anchor: as built `L_slow` kept the curve's value, 1.353× the pool's depth at the transition, 1.275× after 900 s, 1.130× after an hour (k = 1 or 3, withdrawn at once), and the reference credited it (D@80 % lenders + 0.1410, D@90 % +2x + 0.1768 (+ 0.1767 at `9aade3e`), D@97 % +2x + 0.2089, against + 0.1434 / 0.1780 / 0.2104 honest). Under #105 the first PumpSwap observation caps `L_slow` at the burned LP's depth: 1.000 at every probe, the honest lenders' figures (+ 0.1421 on D@80 %, whose exit comes 1 s earlier because the attacker's crank made the transition and the keeper's crank that second recorded the first bucket). The open caps after the boost window do not move in either rule (X_now binds).

As on Raydium ((5b)), the drag's attacker-only seconds move `L_ema'` itself, so the spot leg's floor alone does not stop it; the keeper's per-second liquidity-anomaly coverage on markets with positions does (FITS-2 (b)), and the owner's decision runs it on PumpSwap. What remains is Raydium's: a missed covered second is another step, and a low reached genuinely, then frozen, forces what the LP's withdrawal for good forces (FITX-1, residual 10).

(e) **A held deposit that sized the open, pulled at a genuine pump (M6D-1, found by the #105 verification; closed by the owner's decision of 2026-09-30, #106).** (c)'s table holds positions sized on the burned depth. Since AIR-14, #117, the sim's `Market.ref_depth_cap` returns `D` on every venue, and `pumpswap_lp.py`'s `#105` rule emulates #105 through `lock_min_bps` only, so its cap now reads `D` too: the regenerated `out/attacks/pumpswap_lp.out` shows that column's pulled rows liquidated (+21–65 s, the lenders +4.2–4.4 %). That is the script's comparison losing #105's 2D cap, not a property of #105, whose rows stay as above; the script would need its own `ref_depth_cap` for #105 to reproduce them.)*

M6 as built (#104) gaps every one of #105's S1 rows into S1 too, and the half-cap row as well, at larger sizes (1.43–5.05× the honest cap; the lenders from +5.3 % at the 3× pump to −49.5 % at 10×). The band was a position above ≈ 1.25–1.5× the honest cap whose deposit leaves at a genuine pump — S1 with the borrower zeroed at 3×/120 s, the lenders below `Q`'s value from 5×/120 s on; the third-party shares seen on mainnet (e/acc 7.02 %, §1.2) sized ≤ 1.25× and were liquidated. At `λ = λ_min` (the 1× row) #105's `D` term did not bind — the cap was the deposited pool's own depth — Raydium's V2-14 trade-off (residual 10: a genuine withdrawal just before a pump at the open cap), with one difference: on Raydium λ ≈ 1 pools have no unlocked LP, while on a canonical PumpSwap pool anyone can deposit and withdraw (§1.2). **On the fork** (`pamm_it::lock::part_3e_*`; `legacy261`, 3× held 1 h, the open at 95 % of the 2.000× cap, a 5×/120-s pump): the control (the deposit kept) is liquidated at +61 s; pulled, 20 liquidations fail `ExitBoundNotMet` (the others `NotLiquidatable` / `UseSolSettle`) and S1 settles at +124 s — the lenders 4.189 SOL against `Q`'s 6.743 SOL at the spot (−37.9 %), the borrower 0 of `V` 4.242 SOL; as decided (`D` in sizing and, since M6E-1, the reference cap) the held cap is 1.000× and the same pump liquidates at +59 s, the lenders `Q` back and 0.0632 SOL (+4.20 % of `Q`'s value at the spot; with the cap at `2D` the same second, +4.12 %, the borrower 2.2 M lamports more). **The owner's decision (2026-09-30, #106; SPEC §14 M6D-1): option (a)** — `λ_min` := 100 % in PumpSwap sizing only (`PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS`): `X₀`'s `D` term and the tier's bound on `D` itself (a canonical pool's lock is its burned LP, so `λ_min` has no eligibility role there); Raydium keeps `pool_lock_min_bps`; the reference cap, first kept at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋`, is `D` too since the #106 verification's M6E-1 ((f)). Every row above is liquidated; the cost is sizing a pool with genuine third-party LP at λ of its live depth (e/acc 0.93×, a pool with a third of its LP third-party 0.67×). The fork pin as built (S1) is inverted (`pamm_it::lock::part_3e_*`; ARCH §8.2 ★T-PAMM-LOCK).

(f) **The reference cap under #106 (M6E-1), and the margin (`attacks/pumpswap_lp.py` 3 (f)).** #106 caps the exit reference's EMA terms at `D` (`λ_min` = 100 %, as in sizing), so on a canonical pool (`D ≤ L_live`) `L_b = L_live`; `Ref2D` is #106 as first written, the cap kept at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋` = `2D`. With the positions sized on `D` the two caps give the same exit second and price in every liquidation row: (i) (e)'s run over a grid — a deposit of 0 / 1 / 3× the pool held 1 h, a position at the cap or half of it, the deposit pulled in the first second of a genuine pump to 2–20× over 1–300 s: 120 of 120 cells, the lenders' P&L within 0.07 points of `Q` (0.08 before the AI review's fixes, #117), the borrower's share within 0.11 points of `V`; (ii) (d)'s drag table with `Ref2D`'s cap in every column: 32 of 32 cells; (iii) a due liquidation wrapped in the attacker's own withdrawal (1× / 3× the pool of third-party LP, positions at the cap or half of it, 3× / 5× over 120 s): the attacker's liquidation lands in the keeper's own second under either cap, the borrower 0.7–2.3 points of `V` below the keeper's exit with the cap at `D`, 0.6–2.2 with it at `2D`, the lenders +4.08–4.21 % (+4.07–4.21 % before the AI review's fixes). (iv) **The expiry (M6E-1, found by the #106 verification):** `δ_exp` = 1 % does not cover the drained pool's `(1 − c/2)/(1 − c) − 1` above `c` = 1.96 % (≈ 80 % of the cap), so under `Ref2D` a deposit held for the hour before a deadline and pulled at it holds the expiry up until `L_slow` (τ 1 h) falls below twice the drained depth — C's market, Tier C (7 d), the position at 95 % of the cap, the keeper trying every second from the deadline:

| deposit (third-party share), pulled | size (of the cap) | #106 (cap `D`) | `Ref2D` (cap `2D`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| none | 0.95 | expiry +1 s | expiry +1 s |
| 1× (50 %), at the deadline | 0.95 | expiry +1 s | expiry +2 s (`L_b` 1.83× the depth) |
| 2× (67 %), at the deadline | 0.95 | expiry +1 s | **expiry +1,874 s** (2.00×) |
| 3× (75 %), at the deadline | 0.95 | expiry +1 s | **expiry +3,326 s** (2.00×) |
| 3× (75 %), 600 s / 1,800 s before | 0.95 | expiry +1 s | **+2,355 s** / +1 s |
| 3× (75 %), at the deadline | 0.88 / 0.80 / 0.50 | expiry +1 s | **+2,831 s** / +1 s / +1 s |

On the fork (`pamm_it::lock::part_3f_*`, `legacy261`, the position at 95 % of its cap, q/X 2.29 %): +1 s for the control and for 3× / 2× pulled at the deadline and 3× pulled 10 min before; the build with the cap at `2D` refused 3,346 / 1,909 / 2,535 expiries `ExitBoundNotMet` and expired at +3,347 / +1,910 / +2,536 s. The block is a free extension for the borrower with the lenders' tokens out (no lender loss measured: the liquidations pass), anyone's with capital alone — the borrower's own included, and a genuine LP leaving at the deadline does the same. (v) **A governed `λ_min`:** `pool_lock_min_bps` ∈ [2,000, 10,000] is a governance parameter; under #106 it reaches no PumpSwap term, while `Ref2D`'s cap followed it — at 3,333 / 2,000 (3D / 5D) (e)'s 3× deposit pulled at a 5× pump gapped a position sized on `D` into S1 again, the lenders −31.1 % of `Q`, the borrower zeroed (the fork at 2,000: S1 +125 s, −39.1 %), #106 +38 s, the lenders +4.2 %; at a 10× pump it did too before the AI review's fixes (−52.3 %), and on the fixed sim (#117) it is liquidated at +21 s like #106 (+4.1 %). **The cap is therefore `D`:** a cap at `D` retires V2-14's layer on PumpSwap — a buyback wrapped in an LP's own withdrawal is priced on the drained pool, at worst the burned depth the position was sized on, (iii)'s ≤ 0.1 points of `V` — and in exchange no third-party deposit is credited to an exit, as to a size or a tier, the expiry is never held up by one, and no parameter brings M6D-1 back. The grid at the cap (#106; exit, lenders' P&L against `Q`):

| pump | no deposit (control) | 1× the pool, pulled | 3× the pool, pulled |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2×/120 s | open | open | liquidation +125 s @2.00×; +4.2 % |
| 3×/1 s | liquidation +9 s @3.00×; +1.7 % (the borrower zeroed) | **S1** +63 s; +3.8 % | **S1** +60 s; +1.9 % |
| 3×/120 s | liquidation +73 s @2.22×; +4.2 % | liquidation +69 s @2.15×; +4.2 % | liquidation +67 s @2.12×; +4.2 % |
| 5×/1 s | S1 +49 s; −35.4 % | S1 +48 s; −37.7 % | S1 +49 s; −38.8 % |
| 5×/120 s | liquidation +41 s @2.37×; +4.2 % | liquidation +39 s @2.30×; +4.2 % | liquidation +38 s @2.27×; +4.2 % |
| 10×/120 s | liquidation +23 s @2.73×; +4.2 % | liquidation +22 s @2.65×; +4.2 % | liquidation +21 s @2.58×; +4.2 % |
| 20×/120 s | S1 +59 s; −68.8 % | S1 +59 s; −69.9 % | S1 +58 s; −70.0 % |

**What the pulled deposit still does (the margin, not the size).** The open's margin prices the buyback on the pool as it is (`max(B̂_spot, B̂_ema)` at the deposited depth, §4), so a position opened while a deposit is held posts a little less margin than the same `q` on `D`. Tier C's opening CR of 300 % puts the liquidation threshold at ≈ 2× and the V/B edge at ≈ 3×, and the thinner margin moves both a little lower: of the grid's 80 deposit cells, 12 differ from the control — a hold at 2.00× liquidates after a 3× deposit (the control stays open; every duration, both sizes), and a 3× one-second gap settles **S1** after a 1× or 3× deposit where the control is liquidated with the borrower zeroed — the lenders whole in all of them. Past the edge every row, the control's too, is the genuine gap class (S1, §11.5), and the pulled deposit adds 0.3–3.5 points of `Q` to the lenders' loss (5×/1 s: −35.4 → −37.7 / −38.8 %). This is residual 16's margin part; #105 and M6 as built have it too. The owner accepted it as built on 2026-10-09 (#115).

**Residuals (disclosed; owner item O18 — its rules adopted by the owner on 2026-10-09, #115, residuals 1–4 and 13 accepted as disclosed with them (known-issues KI-18 … KI-21, KI-25), and residual 16's margin part accepted).**

1. **Degraded mode.** If every one of the 8 warm-up buckets is attacker-only (keeper absent or censored for the whole warm-up; 7 keeper-absent seconds when the attacker executes the switch itself and cranks in its second), the attacker can seed `L_ema = D` with withdraw-wrapped cranks; an 8-s genuine held drain does the same; the M4R-1 band then applies at +9 s ((8)). On pools with `λ < 1/(1 + C)` (50 % at the defaults) 4 attacker-only warm-up buckets suffice, because the seed falls to `(1 + C)·D < H` ((P2)–(P3) of §5.2). Steady state needs 3 attacker-only seconds for the same band ((8), residual 10). On the price side, pump-wrapped cranks and a pump-wrapped liquidation in those seconds raise the seed by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration (M4X-1, (P5)): a position whose threshold is within ≈ 2.8× of the carried `P_ema` can be liquidated at the gate at the attacker's pumped price while the pool trades below it — exactly what the same attacker-only seconds force in steady state and forced under M4 as built (part 8d: the same thresholds, never earlier), not a new class. Before M4X-1 the seed read the pump itself, and 8 such seconds forced any threshold ((8)).
2. **Capital deposit freeze.** One deposit-wrapped warm-up crank, then deposit-wrapped cranks every second, hold `L_ema ≤ (1 + C)·H`, higher only if ≥ 5 of the 8 warm-up buckets are pushed; it needs about the pool's size in both assets re-deposited every second, and pays no fee. The EMA leg is delayed by at most ≈ `(1 − H/L)·q/x` in price, and at a flat price for as long as the freeze lasts ((4): 2.01× open where as built liquidated at +28 s; the big position open at 2.0× where as built liquidated at +14 s; (5): open at a flat 1.60× for 120 s, as built +20 s); the box stays wide, so S2/S3 amounts wait (the §10.5 class). While the guard is set and the keeper observes every second (§5.8 duty 9), the reference stays at the honest lows, however long the freeze is held (the guard on the `L_ema` term and on `L_slow`'s input, M4X-2: RA → 630 SOL liquidates at +61 s at 1.732×; after 10–30 min of freeze the genuine pump is still liquidated). After the guard clears — honestly at the gate, or once the keeper misses 5 of 8 guarded seconds — a push reaches the reference again, up to the §8.2 cap (`λ/λ_min` of the depth until AIR-14; since AIR-14, #117, the depth `D` itself, which a push cannot lift, and the regenerated (4d) is liquidated in every row with the cap). That blocked the liquidation of carried positions of 3–6 % of the new pool's `X` until S1: M4F-1 (the keeper's 30-s cadence after the gate: lenders −12.553 to −14.092 SOL in part 4d, −10.056 SOL on the fork, the borrower zeroed), M4G-1 (with #98's liquidity bursts, isolated keeper misses ratcheting `L_ema` up: S1 on the fork after four) and M4G-3 (a real deposit held and pulled at the pump: S1, −10.056 SOL on the fork). **Since #99 no carried position is larger than the new pool's opening caps**: at a 25-bps candidate's fit limit (`q/x` 0.37–0.75 % of the new pool's `X`: what an open on it right after the switch could be) the freeze from the adoption or after the clear, and the held deposit pulled at or just before the pump, delay the liquidation by 0–1 s (0–1.4 % of price), lenders +0.102 to +0.835 SOL, with or without the cap and whichever keeper ((11) (c)) — within the steady-state delays of positions opened at the caps (part 4e: 0–2 s). **That was not true at every fee (FIT-2):** with the fee-scaled caps at the candidate's own fee, a 25-bps incumbent's position at a 1 % candidate's fit limit (the 2.47 % impact cap, the margin posted on the incumbent's cost) ended in S1 in a 4×/30 s pump under a deposit held and pulled at the pump (`L_slow` crediting it for about an hour), the freeze after the clear and the freeze with keeper misses — on the fork (lenders −25 %) and in the sim ((11) (d), the cap at 2D: lenders −7.847 / −6.016 SOL at 100 / 76 % of that limit; with AIR-14's cap at `D` the same rows are liquidated). **Since #100 the carried fee caps take `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)`** (§6.1): those positions open no window and settle S3 at fair value, and at the new fit limit (0.75 % of `X₀`) the pushes delay the exit by 0–1 s at 30–120-s pumps, as for positions opened on the pool. A switch whose incumbent fee is at least the candidate's was the exception (residual 15), closed since #101 by the carried fee caps' 25-bps cap; and since #101 the keeper covers every second of a freeze after the clear after its first (§5.8 duty 2), which holds `L_ema` at one step. Faster pumps (4× in ≤ 25 s at Tier C, ≤ 60 s at Tier A) end in S1 without any push, for native positions as well (the §11.5 gap class). Oversized positions are not carried: they stay on the halted pool and settle S3 at fair value (residual 12).
3. **Withdraw contest while the guard is set.** An unlocked-LP holder who withdraw-wraps ≥ 4 of the 8 warm-up seconds and ≥ 4 of every 8 after, then withdraw-wraps a liquidation of a genuinely liquidatable position, prices its reference at `max(D, L_slow ≤ D)`: the borrower overpays by at most `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 ≈ (1/λ − 1)·q/x` of `B` (≈ 0 on λ ≈ 1 pools; (5): +4.04 %, 1.24 SOL, as built the same through its `D` seed). While the guard is set it forces no exit (the reference and the seed read the honest lows). #100 said "it never forces an exit" without that qualifier; in steady state it did (FITS-2; residual 10): with the spot leg at the drained `L_live`, a withdraw-wrapped liquidation forced positions healthy at the pool's depth after a drag — where `δ` covered the drained cost (at 1 % below about half the cap) after 30 s, at 1.9 % of `X` after 25 min on the fork, at the cap once `L_slow` had followed — and, with no drag, after a genuine spike's retreat ((5b) (v)). Since #101 the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` (§5.5) and the keeper's liquidity-anomaly coverage holds a drag at its first step (§5.8 duty 2). At `9aade3e` no drag of attacker-only seconds forced anything in (5b) (i)–(v); since AIR-14's reference cap at `D` (#117) one level does (2.06× against 2.065× at 0.79 × the cap, (5b) (iii)). The overpay bound above stands for a due liquidation. A genuinely reached low frozen by withdraw-wrapped cranks still forces — exactly what the same LP's withdrawal for good forces ((5b) (vii), residual 10; FITX-1, #102).
4. **M4R-2 is closed only for honest dumps before the window opens.** One pump-wrapped window-opening proposal by the thin pool's backer (0.03–0.04 SOL) restores M4's `P_ema` judgment, never looser ((6)). A genuine dump **inside** the 60-s collection window, after the proposal that opened it, is judged by the execution at `P_ema` alone and adopted (M4X-3; 15 SOL of locked SOL at the live price against the 25-SOL floor after a 4× dump, as under M4 as built); the warm-up then lowers `P_ema` towards the live price — since M6-SEC-1 (#105) by at most one v0.4.1 step per integration, 1.86× the live price at the gate after a 4× dump, the steady-state EMA following after it (on the fork ≈ 1.7× at the gate, 1.00 a minute later; `m4r_it::m4x3_*`) — so opens wait out the adopted pool's listing window (`PoolListWindow`), by which time `P_ema` is on the pool, and then see it as it is (a new open is refused `PoolTooThin`); no exit is forced while the seed lags; and the carried shorts are `√f`× further from liquidation. A re-check at the execution against the candidate's spot was not adopted: that spot is one instruction's sample, and a failing execution must either revert (holding every other proposal, the M4V-4 freeze) or cancel (a dump-wrapped execution then cancels windows until S3). A tracked-EMA execution floor (the redesign's alternative, 2.0–2.4 SOL of per-window freeze to beat) needs a window-observation path and was not adopted; a follow-up option is a `pool_fail` clause for an incumbent below the floor at the confirmed `P_ema`.
5. **Held push during the warm-up.** Holding the pool pumped for all 8 warm-up seconds (every observation, the keeper's included, sees the push) seeds `P_ema` high, by at most the largest v0.4.1 step per integration: the steady-state held class of §12.6 ((8) (e): liquidated at +9 s where as built took +10 s, by a withdraw-wrapped liquidation after the push had ended — since #101 open, the spot leg no longer reads the drained depth; with no keeper at all, for a held 3× push on a near-threshold position, a 9-s push and −0.3825 SOL for the attacker, as built 8 s and −0.2667). Atomic bundles in attacker-only seconds, which need no held price, are residual 1.
6. **Paid down-push of the price seed (bounded by M6-SEC-1, #105).** One dump-wrapped warm-up crank lowered the seed without bound (to the low at once), and after the gate `P_ema` recovered at ≤ +16.7 %/s, a delay of about `ln k/ln(7/6)` seconds for a `k`× dump. Since the owner's M6-SEC-1 decision (2026-09-30) the seed falls by at most one v0.4.1 step per integration (×11/12 at `dt` = 1), so one low pulls it by at most ×0.4986 over the warm-up and the delay is at most ≈ `ln 2/ln(7/6)` ≈ 4.5 s from a seed at the pool's price — **from the running seed**: after a graduation whose completing buy jumped the price the carried seed lies below the pool (0.55× on ECON's D@80 %), so a low recorded in the first one or two warm-up seconds is reached as before (§10.1; part (12) (a): +28 s against +12 s, as built +28 s; from the 4th second on +22 / +13 s, +22 / +14 s before AIR-22, #117). It is paid (0.63–1.08 SOL on the D@80 % pool), the §12.9 class, and the lenders are whole in every simulated row.
7. **Exit latency.** Adoption + 9 s with the keeper cranking every second, usually earlier than as built ((7)); later only where as built was already right (5× / 16× S1: +49 vs +48 s).
8. **A new pool that halts inside the warm-up.** Nothing is gated on a venue that cannot execute: positions are frozen until S3 or the next fast path, as built, and S3 reads the partly anchored ring ((8): the 2.2× position stays open under both rules). The price seed stays where the step bound left it (M4X-1), so after a genuine move during the halt larger than the seed reached before the new pool halted (≈ 1.9× for a halt at +3 s), an S3 that needs the position liquidatable waits for its deadline, as with M4 as built's carried `P_ema` (on the fork a halt at +6 s, the seed at the pool's 2.5×, settles S3 after `s3_wait_s`: `switch_confirm_it::a_new_pool_that_halts_in_the_warm_up_is_not_gated`).
9. **Rent during the warm-up** accrues on the running-minimum `P_ema` for ≤ 8 s: lender-unfavourable, negligible.
10. **The steady-state withdraw drag (FITV-2, FITS-2, FITX-1; closed by #101 for drags of attacker-only seconds, up to missed keeper seconds and, since AIR-14, one level; a genuinely reached low, frozen, is left — bounded by a withdrawal for good; O18).** Withdraw-wrapped cranks in the keeper's quiet seconds (29 of every 30 on a healthy market) took `L_ema` to the locked depth `D` within about 8 s and held it there through the keeper's own observations, and a withdraw-wrapped liquidation — its spot leg at the drained `L_live`, its EMA leg at the dragged `L_ema` — forced positions healthy on the pool. `L_b`'s `L_slow` term kept the wrap out of the bound only where `cost(D)/cost(H) − 1 > δ` and only until `L_slow` followed the drag (τ_slow = 1 h): at the cap on 1 % pools after 0.79 h (λ 50 %, 2.01–2.07× forced against the honest 2.078×), 1.80 h (λ 25 %) or 3.19 h (`λ_min` 2,000); below about half the cap, and at 25 bps, `δ` covers the drained cost and a 30-s drag forced a band (2.01–2.03× against 2.039× at half the cap; the fork: `FITV_QPM` 500, forced at 2.025×); at 1.9 % of `X` a 25-min drag did (the fork's cap-sized regression position; #100's "0.79 h" was the open cap's). **Since #101** the spot leg reads `max(L_live, L_ema')` (§5.5; the withdrawal no longer makes it true) and the keeper covers a market with open positions every second while foreign liquidity-deviating observations recur (§5.8 duty 2): the drag keeps its first attacker-only second and holds `L_ema` at ≈ 0.917 of the depth. At `9aade3e` no drag of attacker-only seconds in (5b) forced anything — every pool and size, drags of 30 s to 3 h, the probed hold level not within 8 h — for one attacker transaction and one keeper crank a second. Since AIR-14 (#117) `L_b` no longer credits `L_slow` above the drained pool, and the one step forces one level (2.06× against 2.065× at 0.79 × the cap, (5b) (iii)). Neither change alone is enough: the spot leg alone leaves the drag (it moves `L_ema'` itself), the keeper alone leaves the spike-and-retreat ((5b) (v)). **Left:** a covered second whose keeper observation does not land is another attacker-only step, and the steps add up while the attacker keeps wrapping (the M4G-1 class, downward): 3 isolated misses in a 1-h drag force a half-cap 1 % position at 2.03× against 2.039×. Since AIR-14 a single miss forces a cap-sized one at 2.07×, and 20 misses force it at 2.01–2.07× against 2.078× ((5b) (iv); at `9aade3e` only 20 misses forced it, at 2.06–2.07×). Pausing until the 10-s window lapses gains nothing. **A genuinely reached low, frozen (FITX-1, #102).** The per-second coverage holds `L_ema` where it is and cannot lift it: an unlocked LP that withdraws for real and keeps its LP out for 3–20 s (cranking at the drained depth, which the keeper reads too — nothing is foreign), re-deposits and then withdraw-wraps a crank every second holds `L_ema` at the low it genuinely reached (λ 49.9 %: 0.741–0.510 of the depth), and the spot leg (at `max(L_live, L_ema')`) and the EMA leg read it, `L_slow` following the drained lows (every bucket's low is `D`) over τ_slow. That forces what #100's drag forced — not the narrow "2.01× against 2.02×" band #101 cited from (8)'s steady-state table (which is engine W's near-threshold case, not (8) (c), the halt row): on the fork (`freeze::fitx1_*`, a 20-s held drain) both FITS-2 reproducers — the 1 % position at 500 ‰ of its cap (1.232 % of `X`) at 2.025× (+71 s, penalty 0.6416 SOL, 0.1925 of it the attacker's) and the cap-sized one (1.899 %) after a 25-min freeze at 2.05× (+67 s, penalty 1.0048 SOL) — and the 25-bps position at its open cap (0.469 %) at 2.005–2.010× (+76–82 s), where the honest keeper leaves all of them open; in the sim ((5b) (vii), regenerated on the AI review's fixes, #117) 1–5 hold levels (1.98–2.03×) under the honest threshold at half the cap and below and at 25 bps after a 3–20-s held drain. At 0.79–1 × the cap a 3-s held drain already forces 2 levels, and 20 s force 2.01–2.07×. The 640-SOL pool's cap is forced at 1–9 levels (1.95–2.03× against 2.039×), and the 792-SOL pool's at 11 after a 20-s hold (1.85–1.95× against 2.082×). At `9aade3e`, with `L_b` crediting `L_slow`, the cap-sized rows were forced only once `L_slow` had followed (2.05–2.06× / 2.01–2.06× at 0.79 × the cap after 25 min / 1 h, 2.01–2.07× at the cap after 1 h), and nothing on the 640- and 792-SOL pools. **It is bounded by the LP's withdrawal for good:** the frozen `L_ema` is at or above the depth such a withdrawal reaches, `L_slow` follows the same drained lows, the wrapped liquidation buys at the same drained depth and its bound is no looser, so every forced liquidation is one the honest keeper makes when the same LP withdraws at +1 s and stays out — the fork's three: the same second, price and buyback cost, the borrower's remainder 632–163,421 lamports higher under the attack; the sim: 181 of 181 forced levels, never earlier (69 of 69 at `9aade3e`). What the attacker saves is having to keep its capital out of the pool after the held seconds; it pays one transaction a second (the keeper one crank). Closing it needs a new rule, an owner decision (O18): (5b) (vii)'s analysis floors of the spot leg — `L_mhi` closes it (one level left, which the honest keeper liquidates too) and (iv)'s missed-second cells with +0 s on (vi)'s genuine withdrawals, but lets a capital deposit freeze hold a position 0.5–2 % above its threshold open for as long as the freeze lasts; `min(L_mhi, L_slow)` costs nothing measured but leaves the long freezes at 0.79–1 × the cap and the missed seconds. The attacker needs the pool's unlocked LP; λ ≈ 1 Raydium pools (the D2 coins' main pools, §1.3) have nothing to withdraw — not so a canonical PumpSwap pool, whose burned creation LP makes λ = 1 but which takes anyone's deposit, and a deposit can be withdrawn (§1.2; residual 16). A genuine withdrawal delays a due liquidation by 0–3 s after a dip and 0–8 s under ±1 % noise ((5b) (vi); 0–10 s at `9aade3e`). At the open cap on a 1 % pool a genuine withdrawal right before a pump could gap into S1 while `L_slow` credited the pre-withdrawal depth (V2-14's trade-off, unchanged by #101; O18). Since AIR-14's cap at `D` (#117) such a position is liquidated at the drained depth, earlier than without the withdrawal ((5b) (vi)). **AIR-14 moved this residual both ways.** With `L_b` at the drained pool, a genuine withdrawal no longer delays an exit or gaps it into S1. But V2-14's credit no longer keeps a wrapped liquidation out of its bound either, so missed keeper seconds and a held drain force more levels than at `9aade3e`, and a drag forces one. Every level a held drain forces is still one the LP's withdrawal for good liquidates too ((5b) (vii)). The owner accepted this residual (O18) on the `9aade3e` figures, and its widening by AIR-14 on 2026-10-13 (SPEC §14 fourteenth round).
11. **Evidence.** The sims (`switch_warmup.py`: parts 4d / 4e for M4F-1, part 11 for #99, parts 5b, 11d and 11e for #100, and for #101 part 5b (the drag under #100 and #101, smaller positions, missed keeper seconds, the spike and retreat, a genuine withdrawal's liveness; for #102 (vii), the held drain, frozen, and the candidate floors), 11d (iii) (the 1 %→1 % switch) and the #101 keeper's columns in 4d, 4e, 11c and 11d; `venue_switch.py`; the §10.3 fast-path rows) and, since the build, the LiteSVM regressions on the mainnet fork (`m4r_it`, `switch_confirm_it`, `m4v_it`, `m4_sec_it`, `pool_select_it::carried_fit`; ARCH §8.2 ★T-SWITCH-CONFIRM), including the M4R verification's reproducers M4X-1–3, M4F-1's (`m4r_it::m4f1_*`: the keeper's per-second coverage while the guard is set, and the cap under the finding's cadence), #99's (the fit at the proposal and the execution; ARCH §8.2) and #100's: FIT-1's residual (`carried_fit::fit1_*`, `freeze::fit1_*`), FIT-2's fix (`freeze::fit2_*`: FIT-2's position refused and settled in S3, a position at the new fit limit liquidated under the pushes, the native control), FIT-3's residual (`carried_fit::fit3_*`, the keeper scenario `pool_select::a_mark_left_by_a_closed_position_blocks_the_fast_path_and_the_positions_settle_in_s3`), FITV-2's withdraw contest under the keeper's cadence without the lows bound (`freeze::fitv2_*`, a 30-s drag) and `freeze::m4g2_*`; #101's FITS regressions (the 1 %→1 % switch refused and settled in S3, and liquidated at the new limit; FITS-2's small-position and long drags and the spike and retreat not forced; the keeper's per-second liquidity-anomaly coverage; ARCH §8.2); #102's pinned FITX-1 residual (`freeze::fitx1_*`: a 20-s held drain, frozen, forces the FITS-2 reproducers and the 25-bps open-cap position, each exactly as the LP's withdrawal for good does); CU measured in `m3_cu` (ARCH §6.1). Engine P records a failed attacker liquidation's observation (an upper bound on the attacker: on chain it bundles a wrapped `crank_price`).
12. **No candidate fits (#99).** A halted Raydium market whose positions no eligible candidate can carry stays halted: healthy positions are frozen at the pre-halt `P_ema` until their deadline and settle S3 at fair value then (D3; the expiry fee as `p'`, O15), liquidatable ones after the S3 chain. Lenders get `q`'s value at that price plus rent and the fee (+6.9–7.1 % in the §10.3 rows) — but not the price the token reached meanwhile (−46 % against the rows' 2× move), and borrowers keep a short that could not be liquidated at the true price. This is O14's halted-market exposure, which #99 accepts as the intended outcome for positions a thinner pool cannot carry: before #99 they were carried onto that pool and exposed to M4F-1, M4G-1 and M4G-3. The O14 remedies still apply: the route returning, or a deeper candidate appearing, which the next window adopts once every position fits it.
13. **Where the fit is judged (#99; FIT-1, #100).** Each check runs at `min(P_ema, P_cand)` and at `P_ema`, and a lower price credits more tokens to the same depth, so for the depth terms the frozen `P_ema` binds. After a genuine `k`× **pump** of the candidate during the halt its live `X_cand` binds: the honest proposal reads it and refuses a position above a native open's cap, but a dump wrapped around the proposal lifts `X_cand` inside the transaction (two swap fees on about the candidate's `X`), and the execution — which does not read `X_cand`, so that no withdrawal or buy around it cancels a window — adopts. The guarantee is the execution's `X₀ = min(L_slow', ⌊D·10⁴/λ_min⌋)/√P_ema`: a carried position can be up to `λ·√k` × a native open's cap on the adopted pool (the fork: 1.085 / 1.109 / 1.366 at `k` 1.2 / 1.5 / 1.9 against 1.094 / 1.223 / 1.377), ≈ 1.4× at the carried shorts' own ≈ 2× liquidation threshold, and it is that much nearer its liquidation. Under the M4F-1 / M4G pushes such a position is liquidated within 1 s of the control (`freeze::fit1_*`: `r` 1.5, a 1 % candidate, the carried fee caps at 25 bps since #100, a 4×/120 s pump). After a genuine **dump** the check at the pre-dump `P_ema` is the stricter one: a position the new pool could carry at its live price can still be refused, at the proposal and at the execution, and stay frozen until S3 at the pre-dump `P_ema` — lender-favourable, at the borrower's cost. No round trip around the execution cancels a window that fits (§6.1).
14. **The mark (FIT-3; #100).** `max_q_hwm` bounds every open `q` and resets only when `open_positions` reaches 0, so a borrower who opened a large position — at the incumbent's size cap, even one closed a second later (0.013401 SOL on the fork; 0.140 SOL in the sim's RA market) — keeps every candidate thinner than that position's cap refused (`CarriedPositionsTooLarge`) while any position stays open, even when each open position and their OI fit it (the fork: three positions of 0.6 of a 40 % candidate's cap). The positions then do what residual 12 describes: frozen at the pre-halt `P_ema`, each settles S3 at fair value at its own deadline — the §10.3 timer, `max(deadline, the S3 chain's start + s3_wait_s)` for a healthy position, i.e. at most the tier's term after its open (30 / 14 / 7 days at A / B / C) and at least `s3_wait_s` (600 s) after the halt — with rent for the term and the expiry fee (the lenders +6.6 % against `q` at the frozen price in (11) (e), a flat price; −46 % against a 2× move meanwhile, §10.3); a position that is liquidatable at the frozen price settles `s3_wait_s` after the chain starts. Because the fit bounds `q` by `min(max_q_hwm, ΣQ)`, the S3 exits also release the rest: once `ΣQ` is under the candidate's per-position cap the next proposal carries the remaining positions ((11) (e): adopted at +19.00 d instead of +210 s, after two of the three positions settled S3). The griefer pays its round trip and keeps no position; an honest large position that stays open has the same effect at the price of its rent and margin. #100's first answer, a permissionless `refresh_max_q` that lowered the mark to the open positions' largest `q` over a chunked scan, was withdrawn: its shared, unauthenticated scan state let anyone reset a running scan between the keeper's chunks for one transaction fee and keep the market pinned (FITV-1: 400 griefer transactions against 815 keeper transactions, no switch). The owner accepts this fallback (O18).
15. **A switch between pools of the same high fee (FIT-2 at the impact cap; #100) — closed by #101 (FITS-1).** `min(φ_inc, φ_cand)` keeps a position in the size class of the fee it was margined at, so it changed nothing when the incumbent's fee is at least the candidate's: a 1 %→1 % switch carried positions up to the candidate's 2.47 % impact cap, on a margin posted on the deeper incumbent's cost. #100 put the S1 band at 70–100 % of that limit; the fork's was wider, **60–100 %** (1.48–2.46 % of `X`: under the freeze after the clear and the freeze with misses at 60 %, every push at 65–100 %; lenders −4.716 to −7.824 SOL where the control was liquidated), and the sim's was 70–100 % under the held deposit with the cap at 2D (with the #101 keeper the freeze no longer ends in S1; with AIR-14's cap at `D`, #117, neither does the held deposit). **Since #101 the carried fee caps take at most `CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM` = 2,500** (§6.1): every carried position is in the 0.75 %-of-`X₀` class whatever the fees, the 1 %→1 % positions above it open no window and settle S3 at fair value at the deadline ((11) (d): +6.96 d, lenders +7.0–7.2 % against `q` at the frozen `P_ema`), and one at the new limit (0.749 % of `X₀`) is liquidated within 0–1 s of the control under every push. The cost is adoptions refused between high-fee pools for positions between 0.75 % of the candidate's `X₀` and its impact cap (residual 12's S3 fallback), which the owner accepts.
16. **PumpSwap's third-party LP (M6R-1 and OWNER-M6D-1; the owner's decisions of 2026-09-30, #105, #106).** The Raydium protections apply with the burned creation LP as the locked depth (§1.2, §6.1, §6.2, §8.2; (12)), so the Raydium residuals apply too: a missed covered keeper second and FITX-1's frozen genuine low (residual 10). **Sizing credits the burned depth only (#106):** a deposit held for any time adds nothing to a position's size, the market's caps or the tier ((12) (c): 1.00×). That closes M6D-1: under #105 sizing credited up to `λ/λ_min` of `D` — twice the burned depth at the default `λ_min` — and a position sized on a held deposit whose LP withdrew in the first second of a genuine pump gapped into S1, every liquidation failing its exit bound while `L_slow` kept the reference at the cap (the lenders −29.3 / −50.8 % of `Q` at 5× / 10× over 120 s, the fork −37.9 %, the borrower zeroed); under #106 every such row is liquidated at +21–67 s, the lenders +4.1–4.2 % ((12) (e)). **The exit reference's cap is `D` too (the #106 verification's M6E-1):** kept at `⌊D·10⁴/pool_lock_min_bps⌋` = `2D` it changed no liquidation but held a `D`-sized expiry up for up to an hour after a deposit pulled at the deadline, and at a governed `pool_lock_min_bps` below the default (3D–5D) gapped `D`-sized positions into S1 again ((12) (f) (iv), (v)); at `D` the reference is the live pool, and V2-14's layer is gone on PumpSwap — a buyback wrapped in an LP's own withdrawal is priced on the drained pool, in the same second, the borrower's share of `V` at most 0.1 points lower ((12) (f) (iii)). **Left:** the cost — a pool with genuine third-party LP is sized at λ of its live depth (e/acc 0.93×); and the margin — priced on the pool as it is, it is a little thinner for a position opened while a deposit is held, so a pulled deposit liquidates slightly lower (2.00× where the control stays open) and turns a gap to the position's V/B edge (Tier C: 3× in one second) from a liquidation with the borrower zeroed into S1, the lenders whole in both, and adds 0.3–3.5 points of `Q` to the lenders' loss in the genuine gap class beyond it ((12) (f)); pricing the open's margin on the burned depth as well was the owner's option (O18; not simulated), not taken: the owner accepted the margin as built on 2026-10-09 (eleventh round, #115; known-issues KI-26). Raydium's "λ ≈ 1 pools have nothing to withdraw" (residual 10) does not carry over: anyone can deposit into and withdraw from a canonical PumpSwap pool. A deposit between `migrate_v2` and the transition is undone at the first PumpSwap observation ((12) (b)). Cost (ARCH §6.4): the LP mint in every PumpSwap ReadSet (+33 B): the open that carries the largest extras set and the keeper's `[crank, liquidate]` with it exceed a v0 transaction (v1 or a per-market ALT), v0 crank batches hold 3 PumpSwap cranks.
17. **The graduation's warm-up seed (M6-SEC-1; the owner's decision, #105).** Bounded per integration from the running seed (residual 6; (12) (a)): a low in the first warm-up seconds after a graduation that jumped the price still delays the carried positions' exits (D@80 %: +28 s against +12 s, a 1.08-SOL round trip), the lenders whole.

## 13. Economic invariants

Asserted in `shortwin_sim.py` (every exit, every settlement, the exit fuzz) and in `pool_model.py` (after every fuzz operation; liveness every 2,000). ARCH carries them into LiteSVM/Crucible.

| ID | Invariant |
|---|---|
| I-E1 | Keeper-initiated buyback: measured spend `≤ max_in`, venue part `≤ B̂_ref·(1 + δ)` with the position's fixed `δ` (v0.4: no clock). |
| I-E2 | **Expiry:** rent and expiry fee are paid in full whenever `V − R − π_ref − ert ≥ B̂_ref·(1 + ε)`, and a sandwich never reduces an expiry's rent. **Liquidation (v0.4.1):** no reserve (§8.2). |
| I-E3 | Penalty / expiry fee `≤ ⌈rate·min(B̂_live, B̂_ref)/10⁴⌉`; where no reserve applies (every liquidation), a sandwich reduces rent + penalty + fee by at most `max_in − honest spend` (the bound's slack). |
| I-E4 | Waterfall legs are non-negative and sum to `V` (including the keeper top-up). |
| I-E5 | S1: lenders' take `= V − K_s`, and the S1 EMA leg holds at the box corner `(P_lo, L_hi)`; S2/S3: take `= min(V − K_s, FV(P_ema, L_ema))`, it lies within `[x(P_lo, L_hi), x(P_hi, L_lo)]` (±2 lamports of `reprice` rounding), and `x(P_hi, L_lo) − x(P_lo, L_hi) ≤ fv_dev_bps·x(P_lo, L_hi)/10⁴`, each corner with its own `p'` (v0.4.1). |
| I-E6 | The venue charge lies in `[B̂ − 5, B̂]` (quotes are upper bounds; `reprice` gives `Y/X ≥ P`). |
| I-E7 | EMA: at most one integration per second; single step within `[−C/(1+C), +C]·dt/(τ + dt)` (a post-change warm-up integration re-seeds instead: its rise within the same `+C·dt/(τ + dt)` (M4X-1), its fall within the same `−C/(1+C)·dt/(τ + dt)` since M6-SEC-1 (#105), I-E12); an instruction's own observation is never in the EMA it reads. |
| I-E8 | S1/S2 execute only after `settle_persist_n` EMA updates of one eligibility clock that only box-robust evidence resets; liquidation only with the EMA leg on the newest `liq_persist_n` ring entries. |
| I-E9 | S3 only after a continuous no-route chain (gaps ≤ 60 s) of ≥ `s3_wait_s`. |
| I-E10 | A liquidation that leaves the borrower equity pays the keeper at least `K_s` (v0.4). |
| I-E11 | **Liveness (v0.4.1, R4B-1):** a liquidatable, buyback-allowed position with a fresh completed bucket never waits while `B̂_live ≤ ⌊B̂_ref·(10⁴ + δ_liq)/10⁴⌋` (the liquidation bound has no other term below `V − K_s − ert`). The exit fuzz counts the waits an expiry's reserve causes. |
| I-E12 | **Post-change confirmation (PROPOSED, M4R, #96):** no liquidation, expiry or S1/S2/S3 settlement while `ema_warm > 0` on an executable venue; a warm-up integration sets `P_ema` = min of the post-change bucket lows and of the largest v0.4.1 step up from the previous `P_ema` (M4X-1), floored at the largest step down from it (`price_down_bound`, M6-SEC-1, #105), and `L_ema` = `min(max lhi, ⌊lomed(lhi)·(10⁴ + C)/10⁴⌋)`, leaving `L_slow` alone (on PumpSwap every executable observation of the warm-up also caps `L_slow` at the locked depth `D`, M6R-1); while `ema_guard = 1` the exit reference reads `min(L_ema, L_mlo)` and `L_slow` integrates `min(lobs, L_mlo)` (M4X-2). ARCH I18 checks it after every instruction; the sim implements it in `Market` and `attacks/switch_warmup.py` exercises it. |
| I-P1 | Σ token claims `≤ T + ΣB_Q`; Σ shares `≤ S`. |
| I-P2 | Claim side, strict per lender: `Σ(real − claimed) + unmatched + vest_bal ≤ lender_sol`, `claimed ≤ real`, and each lender's own allocation `real − claimed ≤ max(pending, 0)` after a touch; entitlement side, signed aggregate: `Σ pending ≤ lender_sol − vest_bal + receivable`; no lender with `pending < 0` reduces its shares. |
| I-P3 | `S + VS ≤ R_MAX·(T + ΣB_Q + VA)` (or `S = 0`); `S < 2¹²⁸`. |
| I-P4 | Every withdrawal `≤` the lender's pro-rata allowance; Σ allowances `≤ T` (+ dust) **on the state the program sees — each lender touched alone, no other lender synced** (v0.4.1); another position's open + close never reduces a lock; no lock is effective at or after its `lock_until`, and an expired lock leaves the aggregate at its `lock_until` (v0.4.1). |
| I-P5 | `claim_sol` never pays more than the lender's own entitlement; a second call in the same state pays 0. |
| I-P6 | **Liveness** (v0.4, R3A-2): after every position resolves, the vest is released and every lender is synced, sequential claims leave at most dust (rounding + rebase-orphan credits) in `lender_sol`. ★With lenders who never return (KI-66, KI-66 full): third parties' pot-only touches and their forward chains move an absent lender's surplus behind and after a rebase record to the pot, so the present lenders wait only for the latent rounding units (§9.4). A lender's KI-53 deficit (§9.4, the late depositor's exposure) is not a surplus a touch can move: the present lenders whose positive `pending` it backs stay owed it for as long as its owner does not return, since only that owner's `cover_deficit` pays it in, whether or not a rebase record lies between (FUZZ-M10-24H-D-B: 73,744,534,723 lamports in the 24 h fuzz's run D; known-issues KI-53). |
| I-P7 | `receivable = 0` and `A_j = 0` whenever no position is open, under any sequence of `rent_protocol_bps` changes (v0.4, R3B-9). |

Fuzz results. `pool_model.py`: 60,000 operations per configuration (continuous rent at 90 % and 100 % utilisation caps, and option (b)), all invariants held, I-P4 also on the untouched state (§9.6). Exit fuzz (1,000 random paths; attacker bundles in 30 % of seconds; a contrary observation every second in 20 % of runs; a lender-attacker landing attacker-only seconds before settlements in 30 % of runs; **v0.4.1: per-second price noise of ±0.3 % or ±1 % in half of the runs, and a freezer that lands an opposite observation in seconds that also hold the keeper's, around armed and expiring positions, in a quarter of the runs**; σ_max overrides in a third of runs so S2 is reachable; the Emergency flag in a fifth of runs; sandwiches on 60 % of exits):

```text
{'runs': 1000, 'exits': 697, 'sandwich_attempts': 8948, 'bundles': 253491, 'attacker_only_seconds': 772, 'blocker_runs': 192, 'noisy_runs': 490, 'freezer_runs': 267, 'frozen_seconds': 2905, 'penalty_truncated_by_sandwich': 22, 'max_loss_over_bref_bps': 102, 'max_settle_box_bps': 94, 'reserve_waits_liq': 0, 'reserve_waits_exp': 0, 'kinds': {'liquidation': 224, 'expiry': 281, 'S1': 147, 'S2': 10, 'S3': 35}}
```

`max_settle_box_bps` is the widest box an S2/S3 settled on, `(x(P_hi, L_lo) − x(P_lo, L_hi))/x(P_lo, L_hi)` (bound: `fv_dev_bps` = 100; every take lay inside its box, I-E5); `reserve_waits_liq = 0` is I-E11 (no liquidation ever waited for a reserve) and `reserve_waits_exp = 0` counts the expiry reserve's waits; every rent, penalty or fee truncated by a sandwich stayed within I-E3's slack (the largest, 102 bps of `B̂_ref`, against the bound's 200). Far fewer S3 exits than in v0.4 (35 against 49), and about as many S2 (10 against 9), because the freezer, blocker and noisy runs now defer settlements they used to let through, and more paths end still open within their horizon. *(Recomputed on the AI review's fixes, #117: AIR-22's lower-median growth changes the first growth-dependent exit, and the fuzz draws from one random stream, so the later paths redraw: 697 exits instead of 709, S1 / S2 / S3 147 / 10 / 35 instead of 174 / 8 / 28.)*

## 15. Parameter registry

Scope: G = global config, T = per tier, P = snapshot per position at open, M = market-level (params-version rule), C = compile-time constant. Every value is configurable within the compiled **hard bounds**, which v0.4 tightened to the simulated-safe region (R3B-6) and v0.4.1 re-derived on the 1,655-run battery for every parameter with a liveness effect (R4A-4, §5.7), and timelocked (SPEC §11). ARCH §2.3.3 carries the same table. **Bold** = changed in v0.4 or v0.4.1, or added after M4 (#91–#96).

**Removed in v0.4:** `ema_tau_s` as a parameter (now the constant `EMA_TAU_S`), `delta_liq_min_bps`, `delta_liq_slope_bps`, `delta_liq_max_bps`, `liq_persist_s`, `settle_persist_s`, `fv_liq`, `settle_s2_first`, `margin_ema_own_sale`, `ref_lo_k`, `pool_switch_delay_s` (and, ARCH-only, `venue_sync_max_age_slots` with the A7 mirror, decision 4). `fv_cap` is not a parameter (SPEC). The liquidation keeper floor, the expiry reserve and (v0.4.1) the absence of a liquidation reserve are rules, not parameters.

**M4R (#96):** no new tunable parameter. `pool_fast_*` and `pool_fast_route_s + pool_fast_window_s ≤ 540` are unchanged (M4R defers nothing); `C = ema_clamp_bps` also sets the warm-up seed's up-bounds: `(10⁴ + C)/10⁴` × the lower median of the highs for `L`, and for `P` the largest v0.4.1 step `(τ + dt·(1 + C))/(τ + dt)` from the previous `P_ema` (M4X-1, #97); the new constant is `REF_GUARD_TOL_BPS`. The state field `Market.fp_lambda_bps` (M4's recorded `λ`, #92) is retired with `adoption_liquidity`.

**#101:** one new compiled constant, `CARRIED_FEE_CAP_PPM`. The keeper's `liq_cover_s` (10 s, the per-second coverage after a foreign liquidity deviation, §5.8 duty 2) is off-chain configuration next to `burst_dev_bps` / `burst_s`, not a protocol parameter.

**#105 (the owner's decisions of 2026-09-30):** no new protocol parameter — M6R-1 reuses `pool_lock_min_bps` (λ_min) for PumpSwap's `D/λ_min` bounds (replaced on PumpSwap by the compile-time `PUMPSWAP_LOCK_MIN_BPS` = 10,000 — in sizing and the tier since #106, OWNER-M6D-1, in the reference cap and a price start's anchor since its verification's M6E-1), M6-SEC-1 reuses `ema_clamp_bps` (`C` of the step down), M6-SEC-3 appends one error code (`GraduationHalted`). **Keeper ops knobs recorded as PROPOSED under O17** (off-chain configuration, `services/keeper` `keeper.example.toml`; not protocol parameters, no timelock):

| Knob | Default | Unit | What it sets | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `[sweep] check_interval_s` | 60 | s | how often the keeper reads each market's trader side (`ATA(trader, WSOL)`, stray lamports, the trader's base ATA) and sends `sweep_trader` when something sits there (§5.8 duty 13) | PROPOSED (O17) |
| `[sweep] cashback_interval_s` | 604,800 | s | the cashback claim on cashback-coin markets at most once per interval (curve: pump `claim_cashback` then `sweep_trader`; PumpSwap: `[init_user_volume_accumulator if missing, create ATA(trader, WSOL), pAMM claim_cashback, sweep_trader]`) | PROPOSED (O17) |
| `[sweep] cashback_min_lamports` | 10,000,000 | lamports | … and only once the claimable cashback reaches this (a PumpSwap claim costs the keeper the rent of the `ATA(trader, WSOL)` it creates, which the sweep passes on to the lenders) | PROPOSED (O17) |
| PumpSwap warm-up cranks (the listing-window cranks) | 8 per graduation or listing | cranks | a PumpSwap market without positions observed every second while its warm-up runs (after a graduation, a PumpSwap listing or a re-detection), so the first open reads a `P_ema` seeded from honest buckets (§5.8 duty 1; M4V-5's Raydium listing-window cranks extended to PumpSwap) | PROPOSED (O17) |

**#107 (M7, the keeper as built; `services/keeper/keeper.example.toml`, ARCH §7.1):** no new protocol parameter (`set_coverage_pause` is an instruction; its role `coverage_authority` is an authority of `GlobalConfig`, set by the timelocked `Authorities` change). **#108 (the M7 security review's fixes, ARCH row 60):** one compile-time constant and five keeper knobs, none a protocol economics parameter:

| Knob | Default | Unit | What it sets | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `COVERAGE_CLEAR_DELAY_S` (program constant) | 5 | s | opens refuse `CoveragePaused` for this long after `set_coverage_pause(false)` clears a set flag (`Market.coverage_cleared_at`): the coverage key cannot bracket an open on a paused market (M7-SEC-3); an honest unpause reopens 5 s later | PROPOSED |
| `[coverage] gap_tolerance_s` | 4 | s | a `CoverageGap` in the keeper's own transaction degrades only above `max(idle_interval_s + gap_tolerance_s, COVERAGE_GAP_S)`: 35 s or more with the 30-s constant, where landing jitter of the idle cadence (0–2 s on mainnet) made 31–32-s gaps (M7-SEC-1 (a)); with `COVERAGE_GAP_S` = 40 (OWNER-M7-12, decided 2026-10-01) the program emits none at the idle cadence and every one it emits degrades | PROPOSED (ops) |
| `[coverage] tick_gap_tolerance_s` | 5 | s | a tick more than `1 + tick_gap_tolerance_s` s after the previous one is the keeper down (a recovery restarts); a shorter skip misses only the seconds that required per-second coverage (M7-SEC-1 (b)) | PROPOSED (ops) |

**#109 (the owner's decisions of 2026-10-01, ARCH changelog 61):** two changed defaults and the global keeper budget's knobs (off-chain configuration of the keeper, `[budget]`; ARCH §7.1 duty 8 has the exact algorithm), none a protocol economics parameter:

| Name | Default | Unit | Meaning | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `oracle_stale_s` (program parameter, above) | 600 | s | a cache older than this tiers every graduated market C; the step rule binds only against a cache at most this old | **OWNER** (2026-10-01, M7-15) |
| `COVERAGE_GAP_S` (program constant; ARCH §2.3.3) | 40 | s | an integration more than this after the last one on a market with open positions emits `CoverageGap`; no economic effect | **OWNER** (2026-10-01, M7-12) |
| `[budget] global_daily_lamports` (`G`) | 1,000,000,000 | lamports per UTC day | the one keeper budget: covered markets' entitlements in rank order, the rest the free pool; 0 = no budget (every charge admitted) | PROPOSED (decision A; OWNER-M7-17) |
| `[budget] var_floor_lamports` | 1,000,000,000 | lamports of `V = ⌊ΣB_Q·min(P_ema, P_ACC_MAX)/WAD⌋` | a market with open positions and `V` at or above it is covered (ranked by `V`); below it the 30-s idle cadence only; bounds [0, `market_max_notional`]; 0 = every market with positions covered | PROPOSED (decision A; OWNER-M7-17) |
| `[budget] reserve_margin_bps` | 10,000 | bps of a quiet day | a covered market's entitlement = its quiet day (one crank per `idle_interval_s` at its last per-crank charge) × (10⁴ + this)/10⁴: twice it by default; bounds [0, 100,000] | PROPOSED (decision A; OWNER-M7-17) |
| `[budget] tail_share_bps` | 2,000 | bps of `G` | the most the below-floor markets' idle cadence may draw from the free pool in a day (0.2 SOL at the defaults); bounds [0, 10,000] | PROPOSED (decision A; OWNER-M7-17) |

The priority ceilings, `market_cu_per_slot` / `payer_cu_per_slot` and the `[coverage]` knobs are unchanged. `COVERAGE_CLEAR_DELAY_S` (#108) now applies after a clear by either signer, `coverage_authority` or (since M7-3) the Emergency.

**Relational checks in `validate_params()`** (v0.4 additions in bold):

- `cr_open_bps ≥ cr_liq_bps + 2,000`; `cr_liq_bps ≥ 10,500`; `util_kink_bps ≤ util_cap_bps ≤ 9,500`; penalty split sums to 10,000;
- `market_warmup_s ≥ 10·EMA_TAU_S`; `venue_fee_cap_bps < venue_fee_hard_cap_bps`; `settle_keeper_fixed ≤ settle_keeper_cap`; `expiry_keeper_fixed ≤ expiry_keeper_cap`; `theta_B < theta_A`;
- **`kappa_base_bps ≤ kappa_liq_bps`**; **`delta_liq_bps ≥ delta_exp_bps`**; **`fee_size_cap_x100 ≤ fee_oi_cap_x100`**; **per tier, `cr_open_bps` and `cr_liq_bps` ≥ the SPEC §4 values** (v0.4.1); v0.4's `liq_persist_n + kappa_growth_n ≤ 7` is implied by the v0.4.1 bounds (3 + 4) and dropped;
- **`pool_fast_route_s + pool_fast_window_s ≤ 540`**, checked against the compiled minimum of `s3_wait_s` (600) minus the 60 s chain gap, so that no position — whatever `s3_wait_s` it snapshot — reaches S3 before the fast path can complete (R3A-11 b);
- `ring_len ≥ max(liq_persist_n, kappa_growth_n + 1)` (holds for all compiled values).

`shortwin_sim.validate_params()` is the executable reference of these bounds and checks for every parameter the sim models, including the penalty split's parts and every per-tier bound (M1 doc sync), and the program's `shortwin_math::params::validate` is the same function (T-CONFIG); the bounds of the parameters the sim does not model (`min_notional`, `market_max_notional`, `market_creation_fee`, the boost pair, `market_warmup_s` and its `≥ 10·EMA_TAU_S`, `ema_force_delay_s`, `lump_vest_s`, `lock_grace_s`, θ and `theta_B < theta_A`, the oracle limits, the Raydium selection parameters and `pool_fast_min_bps`) are `params::validate_econ`, transcribed from this table; the program runs both (ARCH §2.3.3). The default configuration validates. **CI gate** (R3B-6, v0.4.1): every `Params`, `TierParams` or `EmaParams` proposal runs `python3 sim/config_check.py <overrides>`, which applies `validate_params()` and then the 1,655-run liveness battery of §5.7 (core rows, per-second noise, the genuine-pump grid, aged positions), comparing sizing parameters at equal position size; it must print `OK`. A proposal that only lowers lender income (penalty, rates, expiry fee) reports "lender −1 %" rows and needs the owner's explicit sign-off. `--corners` re-checks every corner of the core box and a stress corner with every scanned parameter at its adverse extreme; `--scan` moves every parameter with a liveness effect to each compiled extreme alone.

## 17. UNVERIFIED items and open questions

**UNVERIFIED**

- ★**Superseded as a rule (pump-final, 2026-10-13):** on pump's 2026-10-08 / 10-13 ELFs the boost is `⌊X₁·Y₁/10¹⁵⌋` (`X₁` the live `curve_base_ata`), measured with and without synthetic pool parts and donations (§3.1); the proportional form below is its value at `X₁ = 206.9e12`. The program never prices the boost (it moves no price). The history: the proportional boost rule `VQR = ⌊0.2069·migrated⌋`: exact on all 130 non-mayhem migrations decoded (two checked by hand here), and on all 1,610 of 2026-09-27 – 09-29 (M5 re-verification); the program source is closed. What moves VQR after the boost — downward too, and below 0 from 2026-09-30 — is unknown (§1.2), and whether `migrate_v2` still books the boost as before after the 09-30 change is to be re-checked (M6). **M6 (#104):** the boost rule held on 147 of 147 non-mayhem migrations of 2026-09-29; the drift is third-party LP (§1.2); the 09-30 change was not deployed on 2026-09-30, and pump's unreleased binaries build the same pool and boost after a curve with fee buckets (R-PUMP Update K3) — re-checked when it lands.
- Pump's undocumented trailing cap byte on buys (§1.1): its name and whether pump keeps it. short.win relies on its absence meaning "revert above `rt`" (§10.2).
- Raydium: ~~CPMM exact-out with the creator fee on the output side (source only)~~ **verified in M3** on the dumped ELF, every config × creator-fee side (`ray_math_it`, ARCH U11); byte-identical builds of the deployed binaries (**CPMM closed in M10:** the 2026-10 deploy is `raydium-cp-swap@bdeee23`, rebuilt byte for byte, WP-2; AMM v4 remains a behavioural match), and whether Raydium's pool admin has historically disabled pools or raised fees.
- Whether held pumps of 1–3 s at +10–30 % on real pools are arbitraged (the §12.6 residuals assume they are not; since AIR-22 the best plans hold 2 s).
- ~~CU per instruction in the keeper cost model~~ **Closed by M7 (#107, U27):** the keeper's own transactions measured per venue and the crank batches per transaction measured (§5.8); still UNVERIFIED only whether v1 transactions land on mainnet (ARCH U25, the `shortwin-v1-probe` tool). As it stood: CU per instruction in the keeper cost model (ARCH §6.1 estimates until T-CU; measured on Raydium in M3 and on the curve in M5 — a steady curve crank 31.5 k, `sol_settle` ≤ 64.7 k, a liquidation ≤ 198.7 k, inside §5.8's 35 k / 90 k / 220 k, but a crank that moves the venue 36.6 k and the `None → PumpCurve` re-detection 50.7 k); the number of `crank_price` instructions that fit one transaction (v0.4.1 byte estimate: 4 PumpSwap / CPMM or 5 curve cranks per v0 transaction, up to 9 per v1 transaction; ARCH §6.4, T-SIZE; **#105:** PumpSwap's seventh read account (the LP mint, `P6`) adds 33 B per instruction — 3 PumpSwap cranks per v0 transaction — and, measured in `m6_cu` (ARCH §6.1), 2.7–2.9 k CU per PumpSwap crank (a steady roll + record 34.8 k, inside §5.8's 35 k; 37.9 k when it re-caches a changed coin creator) and +4.2–4.7 k per open; T-SIZE measured the 3-crank batch and the transactions over v0 (ARCH §6.4)).

## Appendix A. Review resolution — round 3 (economic side)

The full record, including the ARCH-only findings, is in `ARCHITECTURE.md` Appendix A. "Fixed" means fixed in this document and the sims; "ARCH" means the change is architectural and this document only aligns to it. The fourth review revised several of these fixes; the changelog v0.4 → v0.4.1 (#74–#89) lists the economic side of every round-4 finding, and ARCH Appendix A's "Review resolution — round 4" records them all.

| ID | Sev. | Finding (short) | Resolution | Where |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R3A-1 | MED | FV gate protects S2 only; a lender flips S2 → S1 with 1–2 pushed seconds | **FIXED**: the settlement box (one rule) — S1 needs its EMA leg at `(P_lo, L_hi)`; flip cases added to `fv_push.py` (none succeeds; v0.3 +2.37–5.58 SOL); exit fuzz now produces S2 (9) and S3 (49) and asserts I-E5 against the ring-median state (max deviation 90 bps ≤ 100) | §5.6, §12.11, §13 |
| R3A-2 | MED | Memoryful claim not live: realised SOL stranded | **FIXED**: MarketVault pot `unmatched`, drawable up to the own deficit; liveness invariant I-P6 asserted every 2,000 fuzz ops; reviewer's test 68.12 → 0.00002 SOL; "nothing is stranded" wording removed; `S == shares` needs no special case. ★Behind a rebase record (M10): a third party's pot-only touch and its forward chain move an absent lender's surplus without its owner (KI-66, KI-66 full); the latent rounding units' residual is disclosed | §9.4, §9.5, §9.6 |
| R3A-3 | MED | Contradictory differential targets; sim deviations (i)–(v) | **FIXED** (sim side): all five deviations and the rent-index tolerance removed, so differential tests need no exceptions; v0.4 sims are normative (ARCH §8.1/M0 point to v0.4) | changelog #67, §2 |
| R3A-4 | MED | Fast-path trigger undefined / no state | **FIXED** (with ARCH): explicit `pool_fail` predicate and chain, simulated; the flag never triggers it | §10.3 |
| R3A-5 | LOW | Global `rent_protocol_bps` breaks the true-up | **FIXED**: snapshot per position, folded into `A_j`; I-P7 asserted under random governance changes; receivable clamp stated | §7, §9.4 |
| R3A-6 | LOW | (a) refutation on raw `P_ema`; (b) spot-leg veto restarts the clock | **FIXED**: box-robust refutation and disarm; armed clock kept on every non-refuting call (a: S1 +4 s instead of never; b: +0–2 s instead of +34–42 s) | §5.6, §12.10 |
| R3A-7 | LOW | Dutch clock counted non-executable seconds; residual attribution | **RESOLVED by removal** of the clock (fixed δ); the held residual is re-attributed to κ opening plus the fixed 2 % | §8.2, §12.6 |
